## I / I

The Episode Called the Wedding Jest / 所谓婚礼戏言

===Original===
April 30, 1293—May 1, 1323

“Pus vezem de novelh florir
pratz, e vergiers reverdezir
rius e fontanas esclarzir,
ben deu quascus lo joy jauzir
don es jauzens.”

===Chinese===
1293年4月30日——1323年5月1日

（奥克语）
“Pus vezem de novelh florir
pratz, e vergiers reverdezir
rius e fontanas esclarzir,
ben deu quascus lo joy jauzir
don es jauzens.”
（意译）
既然我们重又看见草地绽出新花，
园圃重新转绿，
江河与泉源重焕清光，
那么每一个心怀喜悦的人，
理应纵情享有他那份喜悦。

===Original===
It would in ordinary circumstances be my endeavor to tell you, first of all, just whom the following tale concerns. Yet to do this is not expedient, since any such attempt could not but revive the question as to whose son was Florian de Puysange?

No gain is to be had by resuscitating the mouldy scandal: and, indeed, it does not matter a button, nowadays, that in Poictesme, toward the end of the thirteenth century, there were elderly persons who considered the young Vicomte de Puysange to exhibit an indiscreet resemblance to Jurgen the pawnbroker. In the wild youth of Jurgen, when Jurgen was a practising poet (declared these persons), Jurgen had been very intimate with the former Vicomte de Puysange, now dead, for the two men had much in common. Oh, a great deal more in common, said these gossips, than the poor vicomte ever suspected, as you can see for yourself. That was the extent of the scandal, now happily forgotten, which we must at outset agree to ignore.

All this was in Poictesme, whither the young vicomte had come a-wooing the oldest daughter of the Comte de la Forêt. The whispering and the nods did not much trouble Messire Jurgen, who merely observed that he was used to the buffets of a censorious world; young Florian never heard of this furtive chatter; and certainly what people said in Poictesme did not at all perturb the vicomte’s mother, that elderly and pious lady, Madame Félise de Puysange, at her remote home in Normandy. The principals taking the affair thus quietly, we may with profit emulate them. So I let lapse this delicate matter of young Florian’s paternity, and begin with his wedding.

===Chinese===
在寻常情况下，我本会力求首先向诸位交代，下面这段故事所关涉的究竟是何许人。然而此举并不妥当，因为任何这样的尝试，都免不了重新挑起那个老问题：弗洛里安·德·皮桑日（Florian de Puysange）究竟是谁的儿子？

把这样一桩发了霉的丑闻重新翻检出来，并无半分好处：况且说实在的，如今这也全然不值一提了——十三世纪末叶，普瓦泰斯姆（Poictesme）曾有些上了年纪的人，认为年轻的皮桑日子爵与当铺老板于尔根（Jurgen）长得未免太像。据这些人宣称，在于尔根放浪的青春岁月里，当他还操着诗人的营生时，他与先前的皮桑日子爵——如今已故——过从甚密，因为这两个人有太多的共同之处。哦，共同之处可多着呢，这些嚼舌根的人说，比那位可怜的子爵生前疑心的还要多得多，您诸位不妨自己掂量。这桩丑闻的全部内容不过如此，如今幸而早已被人遗忘，我们在开篇之际便须约定，对此置之不理。

这一切都发生在普瓦泰斯姆，年轻的子爵正是为着向拉福雷伯爵（Comte de la Forêt）的长女求婚而来。那些窃窃私语和挤眉弄眼，并不曾怎样惊动于尔根大人，他只淡淡说了一句：世人吹毛求疵的白眼，他早已司空见惯；年轻的弗洛里安则从未听见过这些鬼祟的闲话；至于人们在普瓦泰斯姆说了些什么，自然更搅扰不着子爵的母亲——那位上了年纪的虔敬夫人，费莉丝·德·皮桑日夫人（Madame Félise de Puysange），她远在诺曼底的家中。既然当事诸位都如此心平气和，我们不妨效法他们，从中讨些便宜。于是，关于年轻弗洛里安的生父这桩微妙公案，我便就此搁下不表，而从他的婚礼讲起。

### I / I

Concerning Several Compacts / 论种种盟约

===Original===
It is a tale which they narrate in Poictesme, telling how love began between Florian de Puysange and Adelaide de la Forêt. They tell also how young Florian had earlier fancied other women for one reason or another; but that this, he knew, was the great love of his life, and a love which would endure unchanged as long as his life lasted.

And the tale tells how the Comte de la Forêt stroked a gray beard, and said, “Well, after all, Puysange is a good fief—”

“As if that mattered!” cried his daughter, indignantly. “My father, you are a deplorably sordid person.”

“My dear,” replied the old gentleman, “it does matter. Fiefs last.”

So he gave his consent to the match, and the two young people were married on Walburga’s Eve, on the day that ends April.

===Chinese===
这是普瓦泰斯姆人讲述的一则故事，说的是弗洛里安·德·皮桑日与阿德莱德·德·拉福雷（Adelaide de la Forêt）之间的爱情如何开端。他们还讲到，年轻的弗洛里安从前也曾因这样那样的缘由倾心过别的女子；但这一番，他心里明白，才是他此生的大爱，是一段终其一生、至死不渝的爱。

故事里说，拉福雷伯爵捋着花白胡须，说道：「嗯，说到底，皮桑日总归是一块好封地——」

「好像这有什么要紧似的！」他的女儿愤然叫道，「父亲，您实在是个可悲的俗物。」

「亲爱的，」老绅士答道，「这确实要紧。封地是留得久的。」

于是他应允了这桩婚事，两个年轻人便在瓦尔普吉斯之夜（Walburga's Eve）——四月最后一天——成了亲。

===Original===
And they narrate how Florian de Puysange was vexed by a thought that was in his mind. He did not know what this thought was. But something he had overlooked; something there was he had meant to do, and had not done: and a troubling consciousness of this lurked at the back of his mind like a small formless cloud. All day, while bustling about other matters, he had groped toward this unapprehended thought.

Now he had it: Tiburce.

The young Vicomte de Puysange stood in the doorway, looking back into the bright hall where they of Storisende were dancing at his marriage feast. His wife, for a whole half-hour his wife, was dancing with handsome Etienne de Nérac. Her glance met Florian’s, and Adelaide flashed him an especial smile. Her hand went out as though to touch him, for all that the width of the hall severed them.

Florian remembered presently to smile back at her. Then he went out of the castle into a starless night that was as quiet as an unvoiced menace. A small and hard and gnarled-looking moon ruled over the dusk’s secrecy. The moon this night, afloat in a luminous gray void, somehow reminded Florian of a glistening and unripe huge apple.

===Chinese===
他们还讲到，弗洛里安·德·皮桑日被心里盘桓的一个念头搅得心神不宁。他不晓得这念头究竟是什么。只是有什么东西被他疏忽了；有什么他本打算要做的事，却始终没有做：对这一点的隐隐不安潜藏在他心底，像一小团无形无状的云。这一整天，他里里外外地忙着别的事务，却始终在摸索着那个未曾捉摸清楚的念头。

这下他捉住它了：蒂比尔丝。

年轻的皮桑日子爵立在门口，回望着灯火通明的厅堂，斯托里森德（Storisende）的众人正在他的婚宴上翩翩起舞。他的妻子——做了整整半个钟头的妻子——正与英俊的艾蒂安·德·内拉克（Etienne de Nérac）共舞。她的目光与弗洛里安相遇，阿德莱德朝他嫣然一笑，格外明媚。她的手伸了出来，仿佛要抚触他，尽管整个厅堂的宽度隔开了两人。

弗洛里安这才想起要回她一笑。随后他走出城堡，投入一个无星之夜，那夜色静得像一桩未曾出口的威吓。一弯小小的、硬扎扎、疙疙瘩瘩的月亮，君临着暮色的隐秘。今夜的月亮，漂浮在一片明晃晃的灰色虚空里，不知怎的让弗洛里安想起一只光泽莹然、却尚未长成的巨大苹果。

===Original===
The foliage about him moved at most as a sleeper breathes, while Florian descended eastward through walled gardens, and so came to the graveyard. White mists were rising, such mists as the witches of Amneran notoriously evoked in these parts on each Walburga’s Eve to purchase recreations which squeamishness leaves undescribed.

For five years now Tiburce d’Arnaye had lain there. Florian thought of his dead comrade and of the love which had been between them—a love more perfect and deeper and higher than commonly exists between men—and the thought came to Florian, and was petulantly thrust away, that Adelaide loved ignorantly where Tiburce d’Arnaye had loved with comprehension. Yes, he had known almost the worst of Florian de Puysange, this dear lad who, none the less, had flung himself between Black Torrismond’s sword and the breast of Florian de Puysange. And it seemed to Florian unfair that all should prosper with him, and Tiburce lie there imprisoned in dirt which shut away the color and variousness of things and the drollness of things, wherein Tiburce d’Arnaye had taken such joy. And Tiburce, it seemed to Florian—for this was a strange night—was struggling futilely under all that dirt, which shut out movement, and clogged the mouth of Tiburce, and would not let him speak; and was struggling to voice a desire which was unsatisfied and hopeless.

===Chinese===
他四周的枝叶至多像沉睡者的呼吸那般微微起伏。弗洛里安穿过一座座围墙环绕的花园向东走去，便到了墓园。白雾正在升起——众所周知，每逢瓦尔普吉斯之夜，阿姆内兰（Amneran）的女巫便在这一带唤起这样的雾，以换取种种消遣；至于那些消遣的详情，碍于体面，恕不加描述。

蒂比尔丝·达尔奈（Tiburce d'Arnaye）在此长眠，已经五年了。弗洛里安想起他亡故的挚友，想起两人之间存过的那份情谊——一种比世人寻常所有的更完美、更深挚、更高尚的情谊——随即一个念头浮上弗洛里安心头：阿德莱德的爱是无知的，而蒂比尔丝·达尔奈的爱却洞悉一切；这念头被他赌气似的挥开了。是的，这位亲爱的少年几乎深知弗洛里安·德·皮桑日最不堪的一面，却仍旧奋身挡在「黑」托里斯蒙德（Black Torrismond）的剑锋与弗洛里安·德·皮桑日的胸膛之间。如今万事于他弗洛里安如此顺遂，而蒂比尔丝却躺在那儿，被泥土囚禁着；泥土遮断了万物的色彩与纷繁，遮断了万物的谐趣，而蒂比尔丝·达尔奈生前在其中何等快意。在弗洛里安看来——因为这是一个离奇的夜晚——蒂比尔丝仿佛正在那重重泥土之下徒劳地挣扎，泥土禁锢了他的行动，堵塞了他的口，不让他说话；他挣扎着要喊出一个愿望，一个永无满足、永无指望的愿望。

===Original===
“O comrade dear,” said Florian, “you who loved merriment, there is a feast afoot on this strange night, and my heart is sad that you are not here to share in the feasting. Come, come, Tiburce, a right trusty friend you were to me; and, living or dead, you should not fail to make merry at my wedding.”

Thus he spoke. White mists were rising, and it was Walburga’s Eve.

So a queer thing happened, and it was that the earth upon the grave began to heave and to break in fissures, as when a mole passes through the ground. And other queer things happened after that, and presently Tiburce d’Arnaye was standing there, gray and vague in the moonlight as he stood there brushing the mold from his brows, and as he stood there blinking bright wild eyes. And he was not greatly changed, it seemed to Florian; only the brows and nose of Tiburce cast no shadows upon his face, nor did his moving hand cast any shadow there, either, though the moon was naked overhead.

===Chinese===
「哦，亲爱的伙伴，」弗洛里安说道，「你生前最爱欢笑，今夜这样一个离奇的夜里，正有一场欢宴，你不在这里同乐，我心头好生悲伤。来吧，来吧，蒂比尔丝，你曾是我最信得过的朋友；无论生死，你都不该不到我的婚宴上来凑趣啊。」

他如是说道。白雾正在升起，而这是瓦尔普吉斯之夜。

于是一件离奇的事发生了：坟上的泥土开始隆起、绽出一道道裂缝，仿佛有鼹鼠在地底穿行。随后又接连发生了别的离奇事，不多时，蒂比尔丝·达尔奈便站在了那里，月光下身形灰蒙而模糊，他站在那儿拂去眉宇间的腐土，站在那儿眨动着一双明亮而狂野的眼睛。在弗洛里安看来，他没有太大的改变；只是蒂比尔丝的眉与鼻不再往脸上投下影子，他移动的手也不曾在脸上投下影子，虽然月亮赤裸裸地悬在头顶。

===Original===
“You had forgotten the promise that was between us,” said Tiburce; and his voice had not changed much, though it was smaller.

“It is true. I had forgotten. I remember now.” And Florian shivered a little, not with fear, but with distaste.

“A man prefers to forget these things when he marries. It is natural enough. But are you not afraid of me who come from yonder?”

“Why should I be afraid of you, Tiburce, who gave your life for mine?”

“I do not say. But we change yonder.”

“And does love change, Tiburce? For surely love is immortal.”

===Chinese===
「你把我们之间的约定忘了，」蒂比尔丝说；他的嗓音也没大变，只是变小了。

「是啊。我是忘了。如今我想起来了。」弗洛里安微微打了个寒噤，不是出于恐惧，而是出于嫌恶。

「男人成亲的时候，总宁愿忘掉这些事。这也再自然不过。可你不怕我么——从那边来的人？」

「我为什么要怕你呢，蒂比尔丝？你可是为我舍过性命的人啊。」

「这我不说。只是在那边，我们是会变的。」

「那么爱也会变么，蒂比尔丝？要知道，爱可是不朽的呀。」

===Original===
“Living or dead, love changes. I do not say love dies in us who may hope to gain nothing more from love. Still, lying alone in the dark clay, there is nothing to do, as yet, save to think of what life was, and of what sunlight was, and of what we sang and whispered in dark places when we had lips; and of how young grass and murmuring waters and the high stars beget fine follies even now; and to think of how merry our loved ones still contrive to be, even now, with their new playfellows. Such reflections are not always conducive to philanthropy.”

“Tell me,” said Florian then, “and is there no way in which we who are still alive may aid you to be happier yonder?”

“Oh, but assuredly,” replied Tiburce d’Arnaye, and he discoursed of curious matters; and as he talked, the mists about the graveyard thickened. “And so,” Tiburce said, in concluding his tale, “it is not permitted that I make merry at your wedding after the fashion of those who are still in the warm flesh. But now that you recall our ancient compact, it is permitted I have my peculiar share in the merriment, and I may drink with you to the bride’s welfare.”

===Chinese===
「生也罢，死也罢，爱是会变的。我并不是说，爱会在我们这些再也别想从爱里得到什么的人心中死去。只是，独自躺在黑暗的黏土里，眼下一无事事，只能回想生是什么滋味，阳光是什么滋味，回想我们还有嘴唇的时候，在幽暗处唱过些什么、耳语过些什么；回想嫩草、淙淙的水声和高悬的星辰，即便此刻，仍在怎样孕育出种种美妙的痴愚；再想想我们所爱的人，即便此刻，又怎样和新欢玩伴照旧寻欢作乐。这样的沉思，可未必总能滋长仁爱之心。」

「告诉我，」弗洛里安于是问道，「难道没有任何法子，让我们这些还活着的人，能帮你在那边过得快活些？」

「哦，那自然是有的，」蒂比尔丝·达尔奈答道，接着他讲起种种诡异之事；随着他的讲述，墓园四周的雾愈来愈浓。「所以呢，」蒂比尔丝讲完他的故事，说道，「我不获准像那些还有温热血肉的人那样，到你的婚宴上寻欢作乐。不过，你既然想起了我们古老的盟约，我便获准以我独特的一份来凑这份热闹，也可以与你共饮，祝新娘安康。」

===Original===
“I drink,” said Florian, as he took the proffered cup, “to the welfare of my beloved Adelaide, whom alone of women I have really loved, and whom I shall love always.”

“I perceive,” replied the other, “that you must still be having your joke.”

Then Florian drank, and after him Tiburce. And Florian said, “But it is a strange drink, Tiburce, and now that you have tasted it you are changed.”

“You have not changed, at least,” Tiburce answered; and for the first time he smiled, a little perturbingly by reason of the change in him.

“Tell me,” said Florian, “of how you fare yonder.”

===Chinese===
「我饮这一杯，」弗洛里安接过递来的杯子，说道，「祝我心爱的阿德莱德安康，女人之中唯有她是我真正爱过的，我也将永远爱她。」

「我看得出，」对方答道，「你照旧免不了要说笑。」

于是弗洛里安饮了，蒂比尔丝随后也饮了。弗洛里安说：「可这是杯怪酒啊，蒂比尔丝，你一沾唇，就变了模样。」

「至少你倒没有变，」蒂比尔丝答道；他头一回笑了，只因他身上的变化，那笑叫人微微不安。

「给我讲讲吧，」弗洛里安说，「你在那边过得怎样。」

===Original===
So Tiburce told him of yet more curious matters. Now the augmenting mists had shut off all the rest of the world. Florian could see only vague rolling graynesses and a gray and changed Tiburce sitting there, with bright wild eyes, and discoursing in a small chill voice. The appearance of a woman came, and sat beside him on the right. She, too, was gray, as became Eve’s senior: and she made a sign which Florian remembered, and it troubled him.

Tiburce said then, “And now, young Florian, you who were once so dear to me, it is to your welfare I drink.”

“I drink to yours, Tiburce.”

Tiburce drank first: and Florian, having drunk in turn, cried out, “You have changed beyond recognition!”

“You have not changed,” Tiburce d’Arnaye replied again. “Now let me tell you of our pastimes yonder.”

===Chinese===
于是蒂比尔丝对他讲起更加诡异之事。此刻，愈积愈浓的雾已把世上其余的一切都隔绝了。弗洛里安只看得见一片翻涌的灰蒙，看见一个灰色的、变了样的蒂比尔丝坐在那里，睁着明亮狂野的眼睛，用细小阴冷的嗓音絮絮而谈。一个女人的形影走来，在他右侧坐下。她也是灰色的，不愧是夏娃的长辈；她打出一个手势，弗洛里安认得这手势，心里为之不安。

蒂比尔丝于是说：「现在，年轻的弗洛里安，你这曾经对我如此亲爱的人，我要为你的安康饮这一杯。」

「我为你的安康饮，蒂比尔丝。」

蒂比尔丝先饮了；弗洛里安跟着饮罢，失声叫道：「你变得认不出了！」

「你倒没有变，」蒂比尔丝·达尔奈又答道，「现在让我给你讲讲我们在那边的消遣。」

===Original===
With that he talked of exceedingly curious matters. And Florian began to grow dissatisfied, for Tiburce was no longer recognizable, and Tiburce whispered things uncomfortable to believe; and other eyes, as wild as his, but lit with red flarings from behind, like a beast’s eyes, showed in the mists to this side and to that side, for unhappy beings were passing through the mists upon secret errands which they discharged unwillingly. Then, too, the appearance of a gray man now sat to the left of that which had been Tiburce d’Arnaye, and this newcomer was marked so that all might know who he was: and Florian’s heart was troubled to note how handsome and how admirable was that desecrated face even now.

“But I must go,” said Florian, “lest they miss me at Storisende, and Adelaide be worried.”

“Surely it will not take long to toss off a third cup. Nay, comrade, who were once so dear, let us two now drink our last toast together. Then go, in Sclaug’s name, and celebrate your marriage. But before that let us drink to the continuance of human mirth-making everywhere.”

===Chinese===
说着，他讲起了极其诡异之事。弗洛里安渐渐生出不满，因为蒂比尔丝已再辨认不出，而且蒂比尔丝低声说着些令人难以置信、也令人不快的事情；还有别的眼睛，同他的一样狂野，却像野兽的眼睛似的，从后面透出红色的闪光，在雾中忽左忽右地显现——那是不幸的生灵们正穿行雾中，勉勉强强地办着各自的秘密差事。此外，一个灰色的男人的形影此刻坐在那个曾是蒂比尔丝·达尔奈之物的左手边，这新来者身上带着标记，人人一望便知他是谁；弗洛里安心惊地注意到，那张遭亵渎的脸，即便此刻，依然是何等俊美、何等可敬。

「可我必须走了，」弗洛里安说，「免得斯托里森德的人找我不到，阿德莱德也要着急了。」

「干了这第三杯，想必费不了多少工夫。不，伙伴，你这曾经如此亲爱的人，且让我们俩现在共饮最后一杯。然后，以斯克劳（Sclaug）之名，去吧，去庆贺你的婚礼。不过在此之前，让我们先为普天之下人类欢宴的绵延不朽干一杯。」

===Original===
Florian drank first. Then Tiburce took his turn, looking at Florian as Tiburce drank slowly. As he drank, Tiburce d’Arnaye was changed even more, and the shape of him altered, and the shape of him trickled as though Tiburce were builded of sliding fine white sand. So Tiburce d’Arnaye returned to his own place. The appearances that had sat to his left and to his right were no longer there to trouble Florian with memories. And Florian saw that the mists of Walburga’s Eve had departed, and that the sun was rising, and that the graveyard was all overgrown with nettles and tall grass.

He had not remembered the place being thus, and it seemed to him the night had passed with unnatural quickness. But he thought more of the fact that he had been beguiled into spending his wedding-night in a graveyard, in such questionable company, and of what explanation he could make to Adelaide.

===Chinese===
弗洛里安先饮。随后轮到蒂比尔丝，他一面缓缓饮着，一面望着弗洛里安。饮着饮着，蒂比尔丝·达尔奈变化愈甚，他的形貌开始扭曲，那身形涓涓淌散，仿佛蒂比尔丝是由流动的细白沙堆筑而成的。就这样，蒂比尔丝·达尔奈回到了他自己的归所。方才坐在他左右两侧的形影也不见了，不再用回忆来搅扰弗洛里安。弗洛里安看见瓦尔普吉斯之夜的雾已经散去，太阳正在升起，而墓园里长满了荨麻和很高的荒草。

他记忆里这地方并非这副模样，在他看来，这一夜流逝得快得反常。但他更挂心的，是自己竟被诓骗着在新婚之夜守着一座墓园、与那样一伙来历可疑的东西共度；还有，他该向阿德莱德如何解释。

### II / II

Of Young Persons in May / 五月里的年轻人

===Original===
The tale tells how Florian de Puysange came in the dawn through flowering gardens, and heard young people from afar, already about their maying. Two by two he saw them from afar as they went with romping and laughter into the tall woods behind Storisende to fetch back the Maypole with dubious old rites. And as they went they sang, as was customary, that song which Raimbaut de Vaqueiras made in the ancient time in honor of May’s ageless triumph.

Sang they:

“May shows with godlike showing
Today for each that sees
May’s magic overthrowing
All musty memories
In him whom May decrees
To be love’s own. He saith,
‘I wear love’s liveries
Until released by death.’

“Thus all we laud May’s sowing,
Nor heed how harvests please
When nowhere grain worth growing
Greets autumn’s questing breeze,
And garnerers garner these—
Vain words and wasted breath
And spilth and tasteless lees—
Until released by death.

“Unwillingly foreknowing
That love with May-time flees,
We take this day’s bestowing,
And feed on fantasies
Such as love lends for ease
Where none but travaileth,
With lean infrequent fees,
Until released by death.”

And Florian shook his sleek black head. “A very foolish and pessimistical old song, a superfluous song, and a song that is particularly out of place in the loveliest spot in the loveliest of all possible worlds.”

===Chinese===
故事里说，弗洛里安·德·皮桑日在黎明时分穿过繁花盛开的花园，远远听见年轻人已经开始张罗五月节采青（maying）了。他远远望见他们成双成对，嬉闹着、欢笑着走进斯托里森德背后高高的树林，去按那些古旧而暧昧的仪式抬回五月柱（Maypole）。一路走着，他们照例唱起那支歌——古时兰博·德·瓦凯拉斯（Raimbaut de Vaqueiras）为礼赞五月不朽的凯旋而作的歌。

他们唱道：

「五月今日以神祇般的仪态，
向每个有眼得见的人显现；
五月的魔法正摧翻
一切霉腐的记忆，
在那被五月钦定为
爱之所属的人心中。他说：
『我身披爱的号衣，
直到死亡方得释放。』

「于是我们齐声颂扬五月的播种，
却不问收成可否入眼——
当四处再无一粒值得栽种的谷实，
去迎候秋天探问的清风，
收割者收得的唯有这些——
虚言，与徒耗的气息，
泼洒的酒，无味的糟粕——
直到死亡方得释放。

「我们于心不甘地预知
爱情将随五月时光遁逃，
便领受今日的馈赠，
以幻梦为粮——
那是爱在这唯有辛劳之地
为宽慰而借出的幻梦，
酬报清瘦而稀少，
直到死亡方得释放。」

弗洛里安摇了摇他那乌亮英俊的头。「一支愚不可及、悲观透顶的老歌，一支多余的歌，而且尤其不合时宜——在这所有可能的世界之中最可爱的世界里、最可爱的地方。」

===Original===
Yet Florian took no inventory of the gardens. There was but a happy sense of green and gold, with blue topping all; of twinkling, fluent, tossing leaves and of the gray under side of elongated, straining leaves; a sense of pert bird noises, and of a longer shadow than usual slanting before him, and a sense of youth and well-being everywhere. Certainly it was not a morning wherein pessimism might hope to flourish.

Instead, it was of Adelaide that Florian thought: of the tall, impulsive, and yet timid, fair girl who was both shrewd and innocent, and of her tenderly colored loveliness, and of his abysmally unmerited felicity in having won her. Why, but what, he reflected, grimacing—what if he had too hastily married somebody else? For he had earlier fancied other women for one reason or another: but this, he knew, was the great love of his life, and a love which would endure unchanged as long as his life lasted.

===Chinese===
不过弗洛里安并未去清点花园里的种种。只是一种绿与金交叠的欢愉之感，头顶罩着一片蔚蓝；是闪闪的、流动的、翻腾的树叶，和细长绷紧的叶子背面的灰影；是鸟雀泼辣的啼鸣，是投在身前比往常更长的影子，还有一种青春与安乐无处不在的感觉。这实在不是一个悲观主义有望滋长的早晨。

恰恰相反，弗洛里安心头想的是阿德莱德：想那高挑、任性却又羞怯的美貌姑娘，她又精明又天真；想她柔彩焕发的可爱，想自己赢得她的那份深不可测、全然不配的幸福。哎呀，可是——他皱着脸暗想——倘若他一时仓促，娶了别人，那可怎么好？他从前也曾因这样那样的缘由倾心过别的女子：但这一番，他心里明白，才是他此生的大爱，是一段终其一生、至死不渝的爱。

### III / III

What Comes of Marrying Happily / 美满姻缘的下场

===Original===
The tale tells how Florian de Puysange found Adelaide in the company of two ladies who were unknown to him. One of these was very old, the other an imposing matron in middle life. The three were pleasantly shaded by young oak-trees; beyond was a tall hedge of clipped yew. The older women were at chess, while Adelaide bent her meek golden head to some of that fine needlework in which the girl delighted. And beside them rippled a small sunlit stream, which babbled and gurgled with silver flashes. Florian hastily noted these things as he ran laughing to his wife.

“Heart’s dearest—!” he cried. And he saw, perplexed, that Adelaide had risen with a faint wordless cry, and was gazing at him as though she were puzzled and alarmed a very little.

“Such an adventure as I have to tell you of!” says Florian then.

“But, hey, young man, who are you that would seem to know my daughter so well?” demands the lady in middle life, and she rose majestically from her chess-game.

Florian stared, as he well might. “Your daughter, madame! But certainly you are not Dame Melicent.”

At this the old, old woman raised her nodding head. “Dame Melicent? And was it I you were seeking, sir?”

===Chinese===
故事里说，弗洛里安·德·皮桑日见到阿德莱德正与两位他不认识的夫人相伴。其中一位十分苍老，另一位是气度庄严的中年贵妇。三人在幼橡树的怡人浓荫下坐着，后面是一排修剪齐整的紫杉高篱。年长的两位在下棋，阿德莱德则低垂着她温顺的金发头颅，做着那类姑娘家心爱的精细针线。她们身旁淌过一条沐着阳光的小溪，泛着银光，汩汩潺潺。弗洛里安一路笑着奔向妻子，匆匆把这些看在眼里。

「心肝儿——！」他喊道。随即他诧异地看见，阿德莱德已伴着一声轻微的无言惊呼站起身来，正凝视着他，仿佛又困惑、又微微惊惶。

「我有一桩奇遇要讲给你听呢！」弗洛里安于是说道。

「且慢，年轻人，你这般跟我女儿亲热，你是什么人？」那位中年夫人质问道，仪态万方地从棋局前站起身来。

弗洛里安愣住了，这也难怪他。「您的女儿，夫人！可您肯定不是梅利森特夫人（Dame Melicent）呀。」

听了这话，那位极老的夫人抬起她不住点动的头。「梅利森特夫人？您要找的，是我么，先生？」

===Original===
Now Florian looked from one to the other of these incomprehensible strangers, bewildered: and his eyes came back to his lovely wife, and his lips smiled irresolutely. “Is this some jest to punish me, my dear?”

But then a new and graver trouble kindled in his face, and his eyes narrowed, for there was something odd about his wife also.

“I have been drinking in queer company,” he said. “It must be that my head is not yet clear. Now certainly it seems to me that you are Adelaide de la Forêt, and certainly it seems to me that you are not Adelaide.”

The girl replied, “Why, no, messire; I am Sylvie de Nointel.”

“Come, come,” says the middle-aged lady, briskly, “let us make an end to this playacting, and, young fellow, let us have a sniff at you. No, you are not tipsy, after all. Well, I am glad of that. So let us get to the bottom of this business. What do they call you when you are at home?”

“Florian de Puysange,” he answered, speaking meekly enough. This capable large person was to the young man rather intimidating.

===Chinese===
此刻弗洛里安困惑地在这两个莫名其妙的外人之间看来看去：他的目光回到他可爱的妻子身上，唇边浮起一个踌躇的微笑。「亲爱的，这是罚我的什么玩笑么？」

但随即一种新的、更沉重的惶惑在他脸上燃起，他眯起了眼睛，因为他妻子身上也有些古怪。

「我方才在一伙古怪的同伴里喝过酒，」他说，「想必我的脑子还没清醒。如今我明明觉得你是阿德莱德·德·拉福雷，又明明觉得你不是阿德莱德。」

姑娘答道：「哎呀，不是的，大人；我是希尔薇·德·努安泰勒（Sylvie de Nointel）。」

「好了，好了，」中年夫人干脆利落地说，「这出戏别再演下去了。小伙子，让我闻闻你身上的酒气。不，你到底还没醉。嗯，这倒让我高兴。那么，咱们把这桩事查个水落石出。你在家里的时候，人家管你叫什么？」

「弗洛里安·德·皮桑日，」他答道，口气温顺得很。这位精明强干的大块头人物，让年轻人颇有几分怯惧。

===Original===
“La!” said she. She looked at him very hard. She nodded gravely two or three times, so that her double chin opened and shut. “Yes, and you favor him. How old are you?”

He told her twenty-four.

She said, inconsequently: “So I was a fool, after all. Well, young man, you will never be as good-looking as your father, but I trust you have an honester nature. However, bygones are bygones. Is the old rascal still living? and was it he that had the impudence to send you to me?”

“My father, madame, was slain at the battle of Marchfeld—”

“Some fifty years ago! And you are twenty-four. Young man, your parentage had unusual features, or else we are at cross-purposes. Let us start at the beginning of this. You tell us you are called Florian de Puysange and that you have been drinking in queer company. Now let us have the whole story.”

Florian told of last night’s happenings, with no more omissions than seemed desirable with feminine auditors.

===Chinese===
「哎呀！」她说。她死死盯着他打量，又神情郑重地点了两三下头，点得双下巴一开一合。「不错，你长得真像他。你多大了？」

他告诉她，二十四岁。

她答非所问地说：「这么说，我到底还是当了回傻子。好吧，年轻人，你永远不会有你父亲那么好看了，不过我指望你的天性比他老实。罢了，过去的事就让它过去。那老滑头还活着么？是他厚着脸皮把你打发到我这里来的？」

「我父亲，夫人，已战死于马希费尔德（Marchfeld）之役——」

「那可是五十年前的事了！而你才二十四岁。年轻人，你的家世可有些不寻常之处，要不然就是咱们话不投机、牛头不对马嘴。咱们从头说起吧。你说你叫弗洛里安·德·皮桑日，说你在一伙古怪的同伴里喝过酒。现在，把事情原原本本讲给我们听。」

弗洛里安讲了昨夜的种种遭遇，只略去了在他看来不宜对女性听众启齿的部分。

===Original===
Then the old woman said: “I think this is a true tale, my daughter, for the witches of Amneran contrive strange things, with mists to aid them, and with Lilith and Sclaug to abet. Yes, and this fate has fallen before to men that were over-friendly with the dead.”

“Stuff and nonsense!” said the stout lady.

“But, no, my daughter. Thus seven persons slept at Ephesus, from the time of Decius to the time of Theodosius—”

“Still, Mother—”

“—And the proof of it is that they were called Constantine and Dionysius and John and Malchus and Marcian and Maximian and Serapion. They were duly canonized. You cannot deny that this thing happened without asserting no less than seven blessed saints to have been unprincipled liars, and that would be a very horrible heresy—”

“Yet, Mother, you know as well as I do—”

“—And thus Epimenides, another excellently spoken-of saint, slept at Athens for fifty-seven years. Thus Charlemagne slept in the Untersberg, and will sleep until the ravens of Miramon Lluagor have left his mountains. Thus Rhyming Thomas in the Eildon Hills, thus Ogier in Avalon, thus Oisin—”

The old lady bade fair to go on interminably in her gentle resolute piping old voice, but the other interrupted.

===Chinese===
随后老夫人说道：「我看这故事是真的，我的女儿，因为阿姆内兰的女巫们借着浓雾相助，又有莉莉丝和斯克劳串通，是弄得出种种怪事的。是啊，与死者过分亲近的人，从前也遭过这样的命运。」

「胡说八道！」胖夫人说。

「不，我的女儿。当年以弗所便有七人长眠，从德基乌斯（Decius）的时代一直睡到狄奥多西乌斯（Theodosius）的时代——」

「可是，母亲——」

「——而且证据便是，他们名叫君士坦丁、狄奥尼修斯、约翰、马尔库斯、马尔基安、马克西米安和塞拉皮翁。他们都如期封了圣。你若否认此事曾发生，便无异于宣称不下七位受福的圣徒是无耻的骗子，那可是滔天的异端邪说——」

「可是，母亲，您同我一样清楚——」

「——又如埃庇米尼得斯，另一位口碑极好的圣徒，在雅典一睡便是五十七年。又如查理曼睡在翁特斯山中，还要一直睡到米拉蒙·柳阿戈尔（Miramon Lluagor）的渡鸦离开他的群山为止。又如押韵者托马斯睡在伊尔登山，奥吉耶睡在阿瓦隆，还有奥辛——」

老夫人用她那温和而执拗、细若笛音的苍老嗓音，眼看要滔滔不绝地数落下去，却被另一位打断了。

===Original===
“Well, Mother, do not excite yourself about it, for it only makes your asthma worse, and does no especial good to anybody. Things may be as you say. Certainly I intended nothing irreligious. Yet these extended naps, appropriate enough for saints and emperors, are out of place in one’s own family. So, if it is not stuff and nonsense, it ought to be. And that I stick to.”

“But we forget the boy, my dear,” said the old lady. “Now listen, Florian de Puysange. Thirty years ago last night, to the month and the day, it was that you vanished from our knowledge, leaving my daughter a forsaken bride. For I am what the years have made of Dame Melicent, and this is my daughter Adelaide, and yonder is her daughter Sylvie de Nointel.”

“La, Mother,” observed the stout lady, “but are you certain it was the last of April? I had been thinking it was some time in June. And I protest it could not have been all of thirty years. Let me see now, Sylvie, how old is your brother Richard? Twenty-eight, you say. Well, Mother, I always said you had a marvelous memory for things like that, and I often envy you. But how time does fly, to be sure!”

===Chinese===
「好了，母亲，别为这事动气，那只会加重您的气喘，对谁也没有特别的好处。事情也许正像您说的那样。我当然没有半分不敬神明的意思。只是这种酣然长眠，搁在圣徒和皇帝身上倒也相宜，出现在自己家里就未免不像话了。所以，这事就算不是胡说八道，也理应是胡说八道。这一点我绝不松口。」

「可我们把这孩子晾在一边了，亲爱的，」老夫人说，「听好，弗洛里安·德·皮桑日。恰恰是三十年前的昨夜，月份日子一分不差，你从我们的世界里消失了，撇下我的女儿做了弃妇。因为我便是岁月把梅利森特夫人雕琢成的模样，这是我的女儿阿德莱德，那边是她女儿希尔薇·德·努安泰勒。」

「哎呀，母亲，」胖夫人说，「您准记得是四月底么？我一直想着是六月里的哪一天呢。而且我敢说，怎么也凑不满三十年。让我想想，希尔薇，你弟弟理查多大了？你说，二十八岁。嗯，母亲，我总说您记这些事情的记性真是好得出奇，我常羡慕您呢。只是这光阴，也确实过得太快了！」

===Original===
And Florian was perturbed. “For this is an awkward thing, and Tiburce has played me an unworthy trick. He never did know when to leave off joking; but such posthumous frivolity is past endurance. For, see now, in what a pickle it has landed me! I have outlived my friends, I may encounter difficulty in regaining my fiefs, and certainly I have lost the fairest wife man ever had. Oh, can it be, madame, that you are indeed my Adelaide!”

“Yes, every pound of me, poor boy, and that says much.”

“—And that you have been untrue to the eternal fidelity which you vowed to me here by this very stream! Oh, but I cannot believe it was thirty years ago, for not a grass-blade or a pebble has been altered; and I perfectly remember the lapping of water under those lichened rocks, and that continuous file of ripples yonder, which are shaped like arrowheads.”

===Chinese===
弗洛里安心慌意乱起来。「这可是件棘手的事，蒂比尔丝跟我开了个不光彩的玩笑。他从来不知道玩笑该何时收场；可这种死后的轻狂，实在叫人忍无可忍。您瞧瞧，这把我弄到了什么田地！我活得比朋友们都久，要收回封地恐怕还有周折，而且我无疑失去了男人所能有过的最美满的妻子。哦，夫人，您果真会是我的阿德莱德么！」

「是的，我这一身每一磅都是，可怜的孩子，而这话说出来，分量可不轻。」

「——而且您竟背弃了您在这条溪边、正是在此地向我立誓的永恒忠贞！哦，可我无法相信那已是三十年前的事，因为这里一茎草、一颗石子都不曾变动；我还清清楚楚记得那些生满地衣的岩石下水的潋滟，记得那边一列连绵不断的涟漪，形状恰似箭镞。」

===Original===
Adelaide rubbed her nose. “Did I promise eternal fidelity? I can hardly remember that far back. But I remember I wept a great deal, and my parents assured me you were either dead or a rascal, so that tears could not help either way. Then Ralph de Nointel came along, good man, and made me a fair husband, as husbands go—”

“As for that stream,” then said Dame Melicent, “it is often I have thought of that stream, sitting here with my grandchildren where I once sat with gay young men whom nobody remembers now save me. Yes, it is strange to think that instantly, and within the speaking of any simple word, no drop of water retains the place it had before the word was spoken: and yet the stream remains unchanged, and stays as it was when I sat here with those young men who are gone. Yes, that is a strange thought, and it is a sad thought, too, for those of us who are old.”

===Chinese===
阿德莱德揉了揉鼻子。「我许诺过永恒的忠贞么？那么远的事我简直记不得了。可我记得我哭了好久，我父母让我确信，你要么死了，要么是个恶棍，所以哭也罢不哭也罢，横竖无济于事。后来拉尔夫·德·努安泰勒（Ralph de Nointel）来了，是个好人，做我的丈夫还算像样——就照一般丈夫的标准——」

「至于那条溪水，」梅利森特夫人随即说道，「我时常想起它，坐在这里同我的孙儿孙女们一处，坐在当年我同欢快青年们同坐的地方，那些青年如今除了我再无人记得。是啊，想起来真奇怪：转瞬之间，随便说一个最简单的字眼的工夫，便没有一滴水还留在字出口之前的位置上了：然而溪水依旧未变，还同我当年与那些如今已逝的青年同坐时一模一样。是的，这是个奇怪的念头，也是个伤心的念头，对我们这些老朽的人来说。」

===Original===
“But, Mother, of course the stream remains unchanged,” agreed Dame Adelaide. “Streams always do except after heavy rains. Everybody knows that, and I can see nothing very remarkable about it. As for you, Florian, if you stickle for love’s being an immortal affair,” she added, with a large twinkle, “I would have you know I have been a widow for three years. So the matter could be arranged.”

Florian looked at her sadly. To him the situation was incongruous with the terrible archness of a fat woman. “But, madame, you are no longer the same person.”

She patted him upon the shoulder. “Come, Florian, there is some sense in you, after all. Console yourself, lad, with the reflection that if you had stuck manfully by your wife instead of mooning about graveyards, I would still be just as I am today, and you would be tied to me. Your friend probably knew what he was about when he drank to our welfare, for we would never have suited each other, as you can see for yourself. Well, Mother, many things fall out queerly in this world, but with age we learn to accept what happens without flustering too much over it. What are we to do with this resurrected old lover of mine?”

===Chinese===
「可是，母亲，溪水当然是不会变的，」阿德莱德夫人表示同意，「溪水向来如此，除非下过大雨。这谁都知道，我看不出有什么特别可怪之处。至于你嘛，弗洛里安，倘若你非要讲究爱情乃不朽之事，」她添了一句，眼睛大大地一闪，「那我倒要让你知道，我守寡已经三年了。所以，这事未尝不可从长计议。」

弗洛里安悲伤地望着她。在他看来，眼前这局面，与一个胖女人那叫人吃不消的卖弄风情报不成个搭配。「可是，夫人，您已不是从前那个人了。」

她拍了拍他的肩膀。「好了，弗洛里安，你到底还有几分见识。孩子，这样宽慰自己吧：倘若当年你像个男子汉似的守着妻子，而不是在墓园里游荡神思，我今天也还是这副模样，你便还同我拴在一处。你朋友为我们俩的安康举杯的时候，多半是心中有数的，因为你我本不般配，这你自己也看得出来。唉，母亲，这世上许多事情的结局都离奇古怪，可人上了年纪，就学会处变不惊、不再自乱阵脚了。我这个死而复生的旧情人，咱们该怎么发落呢？」

===Original===
It was horrible to Florian to see how prosaically these women dealt with his unusual misadventure. Here was a miracle occurring virtually before their eyes, and these women accepted it with maddening tranquillity as an affair for which they were not responsible. Florian began to reflect that elderly persons were always more or less unsympathetic and inadequate.

“First of all,” says Dame Melicent, “I would give him some breakfast. He must be hungry after all these years. And you could put him in Adhelmar’s room—”

“But,” Florian said wildly, to Dame Adelaide, “you have committed the crime of bigamy, and you are, after all, my wife!”

She replied, herself not untroubled: “Yes, but, Mother, both the cook and the butler are somewhere in the bushes yonder, up to some nonsense that I prefer to know nothing about. You know how servants are, particularly on holidays. I could scramble him some eggs, though, with a rasher. And Adhelmar’s room it had better be, I suppose, though I had meant to have it turned out. But as for bigamy and being your wife,” she concluded more cheerfully, “it seems to me the least said the soonest mended. It is to nobody’s interest to rake up those foolish bygones, so far as I can see.”

===Chinese===
这些女人如此平淡无奇地打发他那不寻常的灾祸，弗洛里安看得心头发怵。一场奇迹几乎就在她们眼前发生，她们却以一种令人发狂的镇定接受了它，当作一桩与己无干的闲事。弗洛里安开始认定：上了年纪的人，总是多少有些缺少同情心，有些不中用。

「头一件事，」梅利森特夫人说，「得给他弄点早饭。这么些年下来，他准饿了。你可以把他安置到阿代尔玛（Adhelmar）的房里去——」

「可是，」弗洛里安狂乱地对阿德莱德夫人说，「您犯了重婚之罪，您到底还是我的妻子呀！」

她答道，自己也不是全无惶惑：「是，可是，母亲，厨娘和管家这会儿正躲在那边的树丛里，干些什么糊涂勾当，我宁可一无所知。您知道下人都是什么德行，尤其是逢年过节。不过嘛，我可以给他炒几个鸡蛋，配上一片腌肉。我想，也还是安置到阿代尔玛的房里去为好，虽说本打算把那间房腾空另作用处。至于重婚、还做你的妻子，」她的口气欢快了些，总结道，「依我看，这话越少提，了结得越快。就我所见，翻这些陈年糊涂账，对谁都没有好处。」

===Original===
“Adelaide, you profane equally love, which is divine, and marriage, which is a holy sacrament.”

“Florian, do you really love Adelaide de Nointel?” asked this terrible woman. “And now that I am free to listen to your proposals, do you wish to marry me?”

“Well, no,” said Florian: “for, as I have just said; you are no longer the same person.”

“Why, then, you see for yourself. So do you quit talking nonsense about immortality and sacraments.”

“But, still,” cried Florian, “love is immortal. Yes, I repeat to you, precisely as I told Tiburce, love is immortal.”

===Chinese===
「阿德莱德，您这可是双重亵渎：亵渎了爱——爱是神圣的；也亵渎了婚姻——婚姻是圣事。」

「弗洛里安，你当真爱这位阿德莱德·德·努安泰勒么？」这位可怕的女人问道，「如今我既可自由聆听你的求婚了，你可愿意娶我？」

「这个，不愿，」弗洛里安说，「因为，正像我方才所言；您已不是从前那个人了。」

「那么，你自己也看明白了。那就请你别再喋喋不休地念叨什么不朽呀圣事呀的蠢话。」

「可是，话又说回来，」弗洛里安嚷道，「爱是不朽的。是的，我向你再说一遍，正如我对蒂比尔丝说过的：爱是不朽的。」

===Original===
Then says Dame Melicent, nodding her shriveled old head: “When I was young, and was served by nimbler senses and desires, and was housed in brightly colored flesh, there were a host of men to love me. Minstrels yet tell of the men that loved me, and of how many tall men were slain because of their love for me, and of how in the end it was Perion who won me. For the noblest and the most faithful of all my lovers was Perion of the Forest, and through tempestuous years he sought me with a love that conquered time and chance: and so he won me. Thereafter he made me a fair husband, as husbands go. But I might not stay the girl he had loved, nor might he remain the lad that Melicent had dreamed of, with dreams be-drugging the long years in which Demetrios held Melicent a prisoner, and youth went away from her. No, Perion and I could not do that, any more than might two drops of water there retain their place in the stream’s flowing. So Perion and I grew old together, friendly enough; and our senses and desires began to serve us more drowsily, so that we did not greatly mind the falling away of youth, nor greatly mind to note what shriveled hands now moved before us, performing common tasks; and we were content enough. But of the high passion that had wedded us there was no trace, and of little senseless human bickerings there were a great many. For one thing”—and the old lady’s voice was changed—“for one thing, he was foolishly particular about what he would eat and what he would not eat, and that upset my housekeeping, and I had never any patience with such nonsense.”

===Chinese===
于是梅利森特夫人点着她那干瘪苍老的头，说道：「我年轻的时候，有更灵便的感官与欲念供我驱遣，栖身于一具有着鲜艳色彩的皮囊，那时爱我的男人多得很。吟游诗人至今还在传唱爱过我的那些人，传唱多少魁伟的男子因爱我而丧命，传唱到头来是佩里翁赢得了我。因为我所有情人中最高贵、最忠贞的，便是林间的佩里翁（Perion of the Forest），他历经风雨岁月，以一种战胜了时间与机缘的爱苦苦寻我：于是他赢得了我。此后，他做我的丈夫还算像样——就照一般丈夫的标准。可我没法永驻他爱过的那个少女，他也没法永做梅利森特梦见的那个少年——在德米特里奥斯（Demetrios）囚禁梅利森特、青春从她身上流逝的漫长岁月里，正是那些梦麻醉着她。不，佩里翁和我做不到，正如那边溪流里的两滴水也留不住各自的位置。于是佩里翁和我一同老去，彼此还算和睦；我们的感官与欲念开始倦怠地伺候我们，于是青春的消殒我们也不很放在心上，也不很留意如今在我们眼前挪动、操持着家常杂务的是怎样一双干枯的手；我们也就心满意足了。只是，那桩曾把我们结成夫妻的炽烈至情，已寻不见一丝痕迹，而无谓的、鸡毛蒜皮的人间口角，倒多得很。就说一件吧」——老夫人的声音变了——「就说一件吧，他对入口的东西挑剔得荒唐，这个不吃、那个不吃，害得我的家政一团糟，对这种没道理的事，我从来就没有耐性。」

===Original===
“Well, none the less,” said Florian, “it is not quite nice of you to acknowledge it.”

Then said Dame Adelaide: “That is a true word, Mother. All men get finicky about their food, and think they are the only persons to be considered, and there is no end to it if once you begin to humor them. So there has to be a stand made. Well, and indeed my poor Ralph, too, was all for kissing and pretty talk at first, and I accepted it willingly enough. You know how girls are. They like to be made much of, and it is perfectly natural. But that leads to children. And when the children began to come, I had not much time to bother with him: and Ralph had his farming and his warfaring to keep him busy. A man with a growing family cannot afford to neglect his affairs. And certainly, being no fool, he began to notice that girls here and there had brighter eyes and trimmer waists than I. I do not know what such observations may have led to when he was away from me: I never inquired into it, because in such matters all men are fools. But I put up with no nonsense at home, and he made me a fair husband, as husbands go. That much I will say for him gladly: and if any widow says more than that, Florian, do you beware of her, for she is an untruthful woman.”

===Chinese===
「嗯，尽管如此，」弗洛里安说，「您肯把它说出口，总归有失厚道。」

随后阿德莱德夫人说：「这话不假，母亲。男人个个都在吃食上挑三拣四，只当自己是唯一要紧的人，一旦开始迁就，便没完没了。所以非得拿出个章程来不可。唉，说真的，我那可怜的拉尔夫，起初也尽是亲吻呀、甜言蜜语呀那一套，我也相当乐意地受了。你知道姑娘家是什么样：她们喜欢被人疼宠，这也是天经地义。可那就生出孩子来了。孩子们接连出世，我便没多大工夫去理会他：拉尔夫也有他的庄稼和刀兵之事，忙个不停。一个家业渐大、儿女成群的男人，怠慢不得自己的营生。自然，他不是傻子，渐渐便留意到，这里那里的小娘们，眼睛比我亮，腰身比我细。我不晓得他远离我的时候，这类观察会引出什么结果：我从不追究，因为在这种事上，男人全是傻子。可是在家里，我容不得半句浑话，而他做我的丈夫还算像样——就照一般丈夫的标准。这句话我乐意替他说；弗洛里安，倘若有哪个寡妇把这话说得更满，你可得提防她，因为她是个不老实的女人。」

===Original===
“Be that as it may,” replied Florian, “it is not quite becoming to speak thus of your dead husband. No doubt you speak the truth: there is no telling what sort of person you may have married in what still seems to me unseemly haste to provide me with a successor: but even so, a little charitable prevarication would be far more edifying.”

He spoke with such earnestness that there fell a silence. The women seemed to pity him. And in the silence Florian heard from afar young persons returning from the woods behind Storisende, and bringing with them the Maypole. They were still singing.

Sang they:

“Unwillingly foreknowing
That love with May-time flees,
We take this day’s bestowing,
And feed on fantasies—”

===Chinese===
「纵然如此，」弗洛里安答道，「这样谈论您亡故的丈夫，终究有失厚道。您说的无疑是实情：您究竟嫁了个什么样的人——在我看来，您为我安排后任时那份仓促，至今仍嫌失体——这我无从置喙：但即便如此，发一点慈悲心，说几句饰词，岂不更有教益。」

他说得如此恳切，一时间众人都沉默了。两位妇人似乎在怜悯他。就在这片静默中，弗洛里安远远听见年轻人从斯托里森德背后的树林里归来，抬着他们的五月柱。他们还在唱着。

他们唱道：

「我们于心不甘地预知
爱情将随五月时光遁逃，
便领受今日的馈赠，
以幻梦为粮——」

### IV / IV

Youth Solves It / 青春来解题

===Original===
The tale tells how lightly and sweetly, and compassionately, too, then spoke young Sylvie de Nointel.

“Ah, but, assuredly, Messire Florian, you do not argue with my pets quite seriously! Old people always have some such queer notions. Of course love all depends upon what sort of person you are. Now, as I see it, Mama and Grandmama are not the sort of persons who have real love-affairs. Devoted as I am to both of them, I cannot but perceive they are lacking in real depth of sentiment. They simply do not understand or care about such matters. They are fine, straightforward, practical persons, poor dears, and always have been, of course, for in things like that one does not change, as I have often noticed. And Father, and Grandfather Perion, too, as I remember him, was kindhearted and admirable and all that, but nobody could ever have expected him to be a satisfactory lover. Why, he was bald as an egg, the poor pet!”

And Sylvie laughed again at the preposterous notions of old people. She flashed an especial smile at Florian. Her hand went out as though to touch him, in an unforgotten gesture. “Old people do not understand,” said Sylvie de Nointel, in tones which took this handsome young fellow ineffably into confidence.

===Chinese===
故事里说，年轻的希尔薇·德·努安泰勒于是开了口，说得又轻盈、又甜美，还带着怜惜。

「啊，不过，弗洛里安大人，您总不至于当真同我这两位宝贝计较吧！上了年纪的人总免不了有些这样那样的怪念头。当然啦，爱情这东西，全看你是什么样的人。依我看，妈妈和外婆都不是会有真正爱情的那路人。尽管我对她们一片孝心，却也看得出她们缺乏真正的深情。她们简直不懂、也不关心这些事。她们是善良、直爽、务实的人，可怜见的，一向如此——这种事上人是不会变的，这我时常留意到。还有我父亲，外加外公佩里翁，就我记忆中的他而言，心肠好，可敬，诸般都好，可谁也不能指望他做个像样的情人。哎呀，他头秃得像个蛋，那可怜的宝贝！」

希尔薇又为老人们的荒唐念头笑了起来。她朝弗洛里安嫣然一笑，格外明媚。她的手伸了出来，仿佛要抚触他，那是个不曾遗忘的手势。「老年人不懂的，」希尔薇·德·努安泰勒说，那声调把这位英俊青年当作知己，亲密得难以言传。

===Original===
“Mademoiselle,” said Florian, with a sigh that was part relief and all approval, “it is you who speak the truth, and your elders have fallen victims to the cynicism of a crassly material age. Love is immortal when it is really love and when one is the right sort of person. There is the love—known to how few, alas! and a passion of which I regret to find your mother incapable—that endures unchanged until the end of life.”

“I am so glad you think so, Messire Florian,” she answered demurely.

“And do you not think so, mademoiselle?”

“How should I know,” she asked him, “as yet?” He noted she had incredibly long lashes.

“Thrice happy is he that convinces you!” says Florian. And about them, who were young in the world’s recaptured youth, spring triumphed with an ageless rural pageant, and birds cried to their mates. He noted the red brevity of her lips and their probable softness.

===Chinese===
「小姐，」弗洛里安说，叹了一口气，半是宽慰，全是赞许，「您说的才是真理，您的长辈们则是沦为了一个粗鄙唯物时代的犬儒主义的牺牲品。当爱真正是爱、而当一个人又是那种对的人时，爱便是不朽的。世上有这样一种爱——唉，能识得它的何其少！很遗憾，令堂于这样一种至情是无缘的——它至死不渝，直到生命的尽头也不改变。」

「您这样想，我真高兴，弗洛里安大人，」她娴静地答道。

「那么您不这样想么，小姐？」

「眼下，」她问他，「我又怎么知道呢？」他注意到，她的睫毛长得难以置信。

「能说服您的人，是三重的有福啊！」弗洛里安说。在他们四周——他们正年轻，置身于这世界重拾的青春里——春天以一场永不衰老的田园盛典君临一切，鸟雀啼唤着各自的伴侣。他注意到她唇上的红是那般短促，而那双唇想必何等柔软。

===Original===
Meanwhile the elder women regarded each other.

“It is the season of May. They are young and they are together. Poor children!” said Dame Melicent. “Youth cries to youth for the toys of youth, and saying, ‘Lo, I cry with the voice of a great god!’”

“Still,” said Madame Adelaide, “Puysange is a good fief—”

But Florian heeded neither of them as he stood there by the sunlit stream, in which no drop of water retained its place for a moment, and which yet did not alter in appearance at all. He did not heed his elders for the excellent reason that Sylvie de Nointel was about to speak, and he preferred to listen to her. For this girl, he knew, was lovelier than any other person had ever been since Eve first raised just such admiring, innocent, and venturesome eyes to inspect what must have seemed to her the quaintest of all animals, called man. So it was with a shrug that Florian remembered how he had earlier fancied other women for one reason or another; since this, he knew, was the great love of his life, and a love which would endure unchanged as long as his life lasted.

===Chinese===
与此同时，两位年长的妇人相视一眼。

「这正是五月的时节。他们又年轻，又在一处。可怜的孩子们！」梅利森特夫人说，「青春向着青春索要青春的玩物，还宣称：『看哪，我是以一位大神的嗓音在呼喊！』」

「话虽如此，」阿德莱德夫人说，「皮桑日总归是一块好封地——」

可是弗洛里安两位的话都没有听进耳去，他立在那条沐着阳光的溪水旁；溪中不曾有一滴水多留片刻，溪水的容颜却丝毫未改。他不理会长辈们，理由再充分不过：希尔薇·德·努安泰勒就要开口了，而他情愿听她说话。因为他知道，这姑娘比自夏娃以来世上任何人都更可爱——当年夏娃初次抬起正是这样一双又倾慕、又天真、又大胆的眼睛，端详着在她看来必是万物中最古怪的动物，所谓「人」。于是，弗洛里安只耸了耸肩，想起自己从前也曾因这样那样的缘由倾心过别的女子；因为这一番，他心里明白，才是他此生的大爱，是一段终其一生、至死不渝的爱。

## II / II

===Original===
The Episode Called Adhelmar at Puysange

April 14, 1355﻿—October 23, 1356

“D’aquest segle flac, plen de marrimen
						
						S’amor s’en vai, son jot teinh mensongier.”

===Chinese===
题为《阿代尔玛在皮桑日》的一篇

1355年4月14日——1356年10月23日

（奥克语）
“D’aquest segle flac, plen de marrimen
						
						S’amor s’en vai, son jot teinh mensongier.”
意译：这孱弱的世代，满蓄忧惶——爱情既自去，它的轭，我且当作一场诳语。

===Original===
So Florian married Sylvie, and made her, they relate, a fair husband, as husbands go. And children came to them, and then old age, and, lastly, that which comes to all.

Which reminds me that it was an uncomfortable number of years ago, in an out-of-the-way corner of the library at Allonby Shaw, that I first came upon *Les Aventures d’Adhelmar de Nointel*. This manuscript dates from the early part of the fifteenth century and is attributed﻿—though on no very conclusive evidence, says Hinsauf﻿—to the facile pen of Nicolas de Caen (circa 1450), until lately better known as a lyric poet and satirist.

===Chinese===
于是弗洛里安娶了希尔薇（Sylvie）；据人们讲，他还做了个不坏的丈夫——以丈夫们的水准而论。儿女们相继来到他们身边，随后是垂暮之年，末了，便是人人终将轮到的那一桩事。

这倒提醒了我：许多年前——多得令人不自在的许多年前——在阿隆比肖（Allonby Shaw）宅邸图书馆一个僻静的角落里，我初次撞见《阿代尔玛·德·努安泰尔历险记》（*Les Aventures d’Adhelmar de Nointel*）的抄本。这份手稿成于十五世纪早期，据信出自尼古拉·德·卡昂（Nicolas de Caen，约1450年）那支流利的笔——虽说欣绍夫（Hinsauf）声称，证据并不怎么确凿。直到不久之前，尼古拉还更多以抒情诗人与讽刺诗人的身份为人所知。

===Original===
The story, told in decasyllabic couplets, interspersed after a rather unusual fashion with innumerable lyrics, seems in the main authentic. Sir Adhelmar de Nointel, born about 1332, was once a real and stalwart personage, a younger brother to that Henri de Nointel, the fighting Bishop of Mantes, whose unsavory part in the murder of Jacques van Arteveldt history has recorded at length; and it is with the exploits of this Adhelmar that the romance deals, not, it may be, without exaggeration.

===Chinese===
这个故事以十音节对句讲述，其间又以相当别致的方式穿插了无数歌诗，大体看来似属信史。阿代尔玛·德·努安泰尔爵士生于约1332年，当年确曾是血肉俱全的健旺人物：他是亨利·德·努安泰尔（Henri de Nointel）——那位好斗的芒特主教——的幼弟，而那位主教在谋杀雅克·范·阿特维尔德（Jacques van Arteveldt）一事中扮演的不光彩角色，史书已有连篇记载。这部罗曼司所敷衍的，正是这位阿代尔玛的功业——或许不无夸饰之处。

===Original===
In any event, the following is, with certain compressions and omissions that have seemed desirable, the last episode of the *Aventures*. The tale concerns the children of Florian and Sylvie: and for it I may claim, at least, the same merit that old Nicolas does at the very outset; since as he veraciously declares﻿—yet with a smack of pride:

Cette bonne ystoire n’est pas usée,
						
						Ni guère de lieux jadis trouvée,
						
						Ni ècrite par clercz ne fut encore.

===Chinese===
无论如何，下面这段——略作了些看来相宜的压缩与删节——便是《历险记》的末篇。故事讲的是弗洛里安与希尔薇的儿女；而它至少有一点好处，与老尼古拉在开篇处自诩的相同；因为他曾如此言之凿凿地宣称——却又带着一丝得意：

（古法语）
Cette bonne ystoire n’est pas usée,
						
						Ni guère de lieux jadis trouvée,
						
						Ni ècrite par clercz ne fut encore.
意译：这段美好的故事未曾磨旧，/ 亦非旧日里随处可求，/ 更还不曾被书记官笔录于纸。

===Original===
I

April-Magic

When Adhelmar had ended the tale of Dame Venus and the love which she bore the knight Tannhäuser (here one overtakes Nicolas midcourse in narrative), Adhelmar put away the book and sighed. The Demoiselle Mélite laughed a little﻿—her laughter, as I have told you, was high and delicate, with the resonance of thin glass﻿—and demanded the reason of his sudden grief.

===Chinese===
I

四月的魔法

当阿代尔玛讲完维纳斯夫人（Dame Venus）与她钟爱骑士唐怀瑟（Tannhäuser）的那段故事时（此处我们追上了叙事中途的尼古拉），他把书搁在一边，叹了口气。梅莉特小姐（Demoiselle Mélite）轻轻笑了——她的笑声，如我先前已告诉过您的，清越高雅，带一点薄玻璃的鸣响——便问他骤然生悲，是何缘故。

===Original===
“I sigh,” he answered, “for sorrow that this Dame Venus is dead.”

“Surely,” said she, wondering at his glum face, “that is no great matter.”

“By Saint Vulfran, yes!” Adhelmar protested; “for the same Lady Venus was the fairest of women, as all learned clerks avow; and she is dead these many years, and now there is no woman left alive so beautiful as she﻿—saving one alone, and she will have none of me. And therefore,” he added, very slowly, “I sigh for desire of Dame Venus and for envy of the knight Tannhäuser.”

===Chinese===
“我叹息，”他答道，“是伤怀这位维纳斯夫人已经死了。”

“说真的，”她说，对他那副阴沉面孔颇为诧异，“这可算不得什么了不得的大事。”

“凭圣伍尔弗兰起誓，正是了不得的大事！”阿代尔玛抗辩道；“因为这位维纳斯夫人是绝世的美人，凡有学问的书记官都这般作证；而她已死去多年，如今世上再没有一个活着的女人及得上她的美——只除了一位，而她偏不要我。所以，”他极慢地补上一句，“我为渴慕维纳斯夫人而叹息，为嫉羡骑士唐怀瑟而叹息。”

===Original===
Again Mélite laughed, but she forbore﻿—discreetly enough﻿—to question him concerning the lady who was of equal beauty with Dame Venus.

It was an April morning, and they set in the hedged garden of Puysange. Adhelmar read to her of diverse ancient queens and of the love-business wherein each took part, relating the histories of the Lady Heleine and of her sweethearting with Duke Paris, the Emperor of Troy’s son, and of the Lady Melior that loved Parthénopex of Blois, and of the Lady Aude, for love of whom Sieur Roland slew the pagan Angoulaffre, and of the Lady Cresseide that betrayed love, and of the Lady Morgaine la Fée, whose Danish lover should yet come from Avalon to save France in her black hour of need. All these he read aloud, suavely, with bland modulations, for he was a man of letters, as letters went in those days. Originally, he had been bred for the Church; but this vocation he had happily forsaken long since, protesting with some show of reason that France at this particular time had a greater need of spears than of *ave*’s.

===Chinese===
梅莉特又笑了，但她忍住了——忍得够审慎——没有去追问那位与维纳斯夫人同样美丽的女子究竟是谁。

那是个四月的早晨，他们坐在皮桑日围篱环抱的园中。阿代尔玛给她诵读各代古代王后的故事，以及每一位牵涉其中的情爱纠葛：他讲海莱娜夫人（Lady Heleine）与她同特洛伊皇帝之子帕里斯公爵（Duke Paris）的私情密约，讲爱上了布卢瓦的帕尔特诺佩克斯（Parthénopex of Blois）的梅利奥夫人（Lady Melior），讲奥德夫人（Lady Aude）——罗兰大人（Sieur Roland）为爱她手刃了异教徒安古拉弗尔——讲背弃了爱情的克蕾西德夫人（Lady Cresseide），又讲仙姬摩根（Lady Morgaine la Fée）——她的丹麦情郎终将从阿瓦隆（Avalon）降临，在法兰西至暗的危时拯救法兰西。这一切他都朗声诵读，音调温文，抑扬圆熟，因为他是位文人——以那个年代的文人标准而论。他起初本是被养来侍奉教会的；但这份前程他久已欣然抛却，还振振有词地分辩说，值此非常之世，法兰西更需要长矛，而非一遍遍的《圣母经》（*ave*）。

===Original===
For the rest, Sir Adhelmar de Nointel was known as a valiant knight, who had won glory in the wars with the English. He had lodged for a fortnight at Puysange, of which castle the master, Sire Reinault (son to the late Vicomte Florian) was Adhelmar’s cousin: and on the next day Adhelmar proposed to set forth for Paris, where the French King﻿—Jehan the Luckless﻿—was gathering his lieges about him to withstand his kinsman, Edward of England.

Now, as I have said, Adhelmar was cousin to Reinault, and, in consequence, to Reinault’s sister, the Demoiselle Mélite; and the latter Adhelmar loved, at least, as much as a cousin should. That was well known; and Reinault de Puysange had sworn very heartily that this was a great pity when he affianced her to Hugues d’Arques. Both Hugues and Adhelmar had loved Mélite since boyhood﻿—so far their claims ran equally. But while Adhelmar had busied himself in the acquisition of some scant fame and a vast number of scars, Hugues had sensibly inherited the fief of Arques, a snug property with fertile lands and a stout fortress. How, then, should Reinault hesitate between them?

===Chinese===
除去这些不谈，阿代尔玛·德·努安泰尔爵士号称骁勇的骑士，在对英格兰人的战事中挣得过荣耀。他在皮桑日已下榻半月；这座城堡的主人雷诺大人（Sire Reinault，故子爵弗洛里安之子）是阿代尔玛的表亲：次日阿代尔玛便打算启程赴巴黎，法兰西国王——“倒霉的约翰”（Jehan the Luckless）——正在那里聚拢封臣，以抵御他的亲戚、英格兰的爱德华。

前已说过，阿代尔玛是雷诺的表亲，因而也是雷诺之妹梅莉特小姐的表亲；而这一位，阿代尔玛爱她——至少，爱到一个表亲应有的分量。这是人尽皆知的事；所以雷诺·德·皮桑日把她许给于格·达尔克（Hugues d’Arques）的时候，曾痛心疾首地赌咒说这真是一大憾事。于格与阿代尔玛两人自幼便都爱着梅莉特——到此为止，两人资格相当。只是当阿代尔玛忙着挣取薄薄一点名声和满身伤疤的时候，于格却清醒务实地继承了达尔克的封地，一份膏腴遍野、坚堡矗立的可观产业。如此，雷诺在两人之间还有什么可犹豫的呢？

===Original===
He did not. For the Château d’Arques, you must understand, was builded in Lower Normandy, on the fringe of the hill-country, just where the peninsula of Cotentin juts out into the sea; Puysange stood not far north, among the level lands of Upper Normandy: and these two being the strongest castles in those parts, what more natural and desirable than that the families should be united by marriage? Reinault informed his sister of his decision; she wept a little, but did not refuse to comply.

So Adhelmar, come again to Puysange after five years’ absence, found Mélite troth-plighted, fast and safe, to Hugues. Reinault told him. Adhelmar grumbled and bit his nails in a corner, for a time; then laughed shortly.

“I have loved Mélite,” he said. “It may be that I love her still. Hah, Saint Vulfran! why should I not? Why should a man not love his cousin?”

Adhelmar grinned, while the vicomte twitched his beard and wished Adhelmar at the devil.

===Chinese===
他并没有犹豫。因为您得明白，达尔克城堡（Château d’Arques）坐落在下诺曼底，正筑在丘陵地带的边沿、科唐坦半岛（Cotentin）探入海中的那一角；皮桑日则矗立在北面不远的上诺曼底平川之上：这两座是该地带最坚固的城堡，两姓联姻，还有比这更自然、更相宜的事么？雷诺把他的决定告知妹妹；她哭了一小会儿，却没有拒绝依从。

于是，阔别五年重回皮桑日的阿代尔玛，发现梅莉特已经同于格订下婚约，钉是钉、铆是铆，稳稳当当。是雷诺告诉他的。阿代尔玛缩在角落里咕哝、咬了一阵指甲；随即短促地笑了一声。

“我爱过梅莉特，”他说。“如今兴许还爱着。哈，圣伍尔弗兰！我为什么不该爱？一个男人为什么不该爱自己的表妹？”

阿代尔玛咧嘴而笑，子爵则捻着胡子，直盼魔鬼把阿代尔玛收了去。

===Original===
But the young knight stuck fast at Puysange, for all that, and he and Mélite were much together. Daily they made parties to dance, and to hunt the deer, and to fish, but most often to rehearse songs. For Adhelmar made good songs.1

Today, the summer already stirring in the womb of the year, they sat, as I have said, in the hedged garden; and about them the birds piped and wrangled over their nest-building, and daffodils danced in spring’s honor with lively saltations, and overhead the sky was colored like a robin’s egg. It was very perilous weather for young folk. By reason of this, when he had ended his reading about the lady of the hollow hill, Sir Adhelmar sighed again, and stared at his companion with hungry eyes, wherein desire strained like a hound at the leash.

===Chinese===
然而年轻的骑士照旧死赖在皮桑日不走，他和梅莉特朝夕相伴。他们每日结伙跳舞、猎鹿、垂钓，但最多的还是演练歌诗。因为阿代尔玛素来会作好歌。1

这一天，夏日已在年光的母腹中躁动，如前所述，他们坐在围篱的园中；四周鸟雀啁啾，为筑巢吵嚷不休；黄水仙为敬奉春天婆娑起舞，跳得兴高采烈；头顶的天空，颜色恰似知更鸟的卵。对年轻人来说，这是凶险至极的天气。正因如此，读完那段空山之腹夫人的故事之后，阿代尔玛爵士又叹了口气，用一双饥渴的眼睛盯住身边人，那眼中欲望绷紧，恰似皮带扣紧的猎犬。

===Original===
Said Mélite, “Was this Lady Venus, then, exceedingly beautiful?”

Adhelmar swore an oath of sufficient magnitude that she was.

Whereupon Mélite, twisting her fingers idly and evincing a sudden interest in her own feet, demanded if this Venus were more beautiful than the Lady Ermengarde of Arnaye or the Lady Ysabeau of Brieuc.

“Holy Ouen!” scoffed Adhelmar; “these ladies, while well enough, I grant you, would seem to be callow howlets blinking about that Arabian Phoenix which Plinius tells of, in comparison with this Lady Venus that is dead!”

===Chinese===
梅莉特说：“那么，这位维纳斯夫人真是美极了么？”

阿代尔玛发了个足够分量的誓，说是。

于是梅莉特懒懒地绞着手指，忽然对自己的双脚大感兴趣，又问：这位维纳斯，是否比阿尔奈的埃尔芒加德夫人（Lady Ermengarde of Arnaye）或布里厄克的伊莎博夫人（Lady Ysabeau of Brieuc）更美。

“圣乌安在上！”阿代尔玛讥诮道；“这几位夫人嘛，我承认，也还过得去，可比之这位已死的维纳斯夫人，她们不过是几只乳毛未褪的小猫头鹰，对着普林尼（Plinius）笔下的那只阿拉伯不死鸟瞎眨眼睛罢了！”

===Original===
“But how,” asked Mélite, “was this lady fashioned that you commend so highly?﻿—and how can you know of her beauty who have never seen her?”

Said Adhelmar: “I have read of her fairness in the chronicles of Messire Stace of Thebes, and of Dares, who was her husband’s bishop. And she was very comely, neither too little nor too big; she was fairer and whiter and more lovely than any flower of the lily or snow upon the branch, but her eyebrows had the mischance of meeting. She had wide-open, beautiful eyes, and her wit was quick and ready. She was graceful and of demure countenance. She was well-beloved, and could herself love well, but her heart was changeable﻿—”

“Cousin Adhelmar,” declared Mélite, flushing somewhat, for the portrait was like enough, “I think that you tell of a woman, not of a goddess of heathenry.”

“Her eyes,” said Adhelmar, and his voice shook, and his hands, lifting a little, trembled﻿—“her eyes were large and very bright and of a color like that of the June sunlight falling upon deep waters. Her hair was of a curious gold color like the Fleece that the knight Jason sought, and it curled marvellously about her temples. For mouth she had but a small red wound; and her throat was a tower builded of ivory.”

===Chinese===
“可是，”梅莉特问，“您如此盛赞的这位夫人，究竟生得如何？——您又从未见过她，怎么会知道她的美？”

阿代尔玛说：“她的美貌，我是在底比斯的斯塔斯大人（Messire Stace of Thebes）的编年史里读到的，还有达雷斯（Dares）的书——他做过她丈夫的主教。她生得十分标致，不高不矮，恰到好处；她比百合的任何一朵、枝头的任何一片雪都更白皙、更明艳、更可爱，只是两道眉毛不幸长在了一处。她有一双睁得大大的、美丽的眼睛，心思敏捷、灵台清明。她体态窈窕，神色端凝。她广被爱宠，自己也能深挚地去爱，只是她的心，易变——”

“表哥，”梅莉特说着，微微红了脸，因为这幅画像实在像得太过了，“我看您讲的分明是个女人，不是什么异教的仙子。”

“她的眼睛，”阿代尔玛说，声音发颤，抬起的手也在抖——“她的眼睛又大又亮，颜色像六月的日光泻在深水上。她的头发是一种奇异的金色，像骑士伊阿宋（Jason）寻取的金羊毛，在她鬓边卷得美妙已极。她的嘴，是一道小小的红伤；她的颈项，是一座象牙筑成的塔。”

===Original===
But now, still staring at her feet and glowing with the even complexion of a rose, (though not ill-pleased), the Demoiselle Mélite bade him desist and make her a song. Moreover, she added, beauty was but a fleeting thing, and she considered it of little importance; and then she laughed again.

Adhelmar took up the lute that lay beside them and fingered it for a moment, as though wondering of what he would rhyme. Afterward he sang for her as they sat in the gardens.

Sang Adhelmar:

“It is in vain I mirror forth the praise
						
						In pondered virelais
						
						Of her that is the lady of my love;
						
						Far-sought and curious phrases fail to tell
						
						The tender miracle
						
						Of her white body and the grace thereof.

“Thus many and many an artful-artless strain
						
						Is fashioned all in vain:
						
						Sound proves unsound; and even her name, that is
						
						To me more glorious than the glow of fire
						
						Or dawn or love’s desire
						
						Or opals interlinked with turquoises,
						
						Mocks utterance.

“So, lacking skill to praise
						
						That perfect bodily beauty which is hers,
						
						Even as those worshippers
						
						Who bore rude offerings of honey and maize,
						
						Their all, into the gold-paved ministers
						
						Of Aphrodite, I have given her these
						
						My faltering melodies,
						
						That are Love’s lean and ragged messengers.”

===Chinese===
可这会儿，梅莉特小姐依旧盯着自己的脚，脸上泛起玫瑰般匀净的红晕（心里倒并非不喜），她叫他还是打住，给她作一支歌罢。况且她又说，美貌原是转瞬即逝的东西，她看它无足轻重；说罢她又笑了。

阿代尔玛拿起搁在手边的琉特琴，拨弄了片刻，仿佛在盘算该押什么韵。随后，当他们坐在园中时，他为她们唱了起来。

阿代尔玛唱道：

“我徒然把赞颂
						
						织进苦吟的维勒莱，
						
	献给我的爱人、我倾心的夫人；
						
						苦心孤诣、猎奇搜僻的辞句，终究道不尽
						
						那温柔的奇迹——
						
						她雪白的身躯，与其中的风姿。

“于是多少亦巧亦拙的诗行
						
						尽数织成徒劳：
						
						声响验成空响；连她的名字——于我，它比火焰的光辉、比黎明、比爱的渴想、比绿松石串起的猫眼石更加辉煌——也嘲弄着言语。

“所以，既无才力去赞颂
						
						那属于她的、完美的肉身之美，
						
						我便如那些崇拜者，
						
						捧着蜂蜜与玉米粗陋的供物——
						
						那是他们的全部——走进阿芙罗狄忒金铺石砌的殿堂，
						
	我也把这些献给了她：
						
						我这些踉跄的曲调，
						
						它们是爱神精瘦而褴褛的信使。”

===Original===
When he had ended, Adhelmar cast aside the lute, and caught up both of Mélite’s hands, and strained them to his lips. There needed no wizard to read the message in his eyes.

Mélite sat silent for a moment. Presently, “Ah, cousin, cousin!” she sighed, “I cannot love you as you would have me love. God alone knows why, true heart, for I revere you as a strong man and a proven knight and a faithful lover; but I do not love you. There are many women who would love you, Adhelmar, for the world praises you, and you have done brave deeds and made good songs and have served your King potently; and yet”﻿—she drew her hands away and laughed a little wearily﻿—“yet I, poor maid, must needs love Hugues, who has done nothing. This love is a strange, unreasoning thing, my cousin.”

===Chinese===
唱罢，阿代尔玛把琉特琴撂在一边，抓起梅莉特的双手，紧紧压到自己唇上。无需术士，也读得出他眼中的讯息。

梅莉特静坐了片刻。半晌，她叹道：“啊，表哥，表哥！我没法照您希望的那样爱您。唯有天主知道为什么，真心人，因为我敬您是个刚强的男子、身经百验的骑士、忠贞不渝的情人；可我不爱您。有许多女人会爱您的，阿代尔玛，因为世人称许您，您立过殊勋、作过好歌、为您的王上出过大力；然而”——她抽回双手，略带倦意地笑了笑——“然而我，可怜的姑娘，偏要去爱于格，虽然他一事无成。爱是件古怪的、没有道理的东西啊，表哥。”

===Original===
“But do you in truth love Hugues?” asked Adhelmar, in a harsh voice.

“Yes,” said Mélite, very softly, and afterward flushed and wondered dimly if she had spoken the truth. Then, somehow, her arms clasped about Adhelmar’s neck, and she kissed him, from pure pity, as she told herself; for Mélite’s heart was tender, and she could not endure the anguish in his face.

This was all very well. But Hugues d’Arques, coming suddenly out of a pleached walk, at this juncture, stumbled upon them and found their postures distasteful. He bent black brows upon the two.

“Adhelmar,” said he, at length, “this world is a small place.”

Adhelmar rose. “Indeed,” he assented, with a wried smile, “I think there is scarce room in it for both of us, Hugues.”

“That was my meaning,” said the Sieur d’Arques.

“Only,” Adhelmar pursued, somewhat wistfully, “my sword just now, Hugues, is vowed to my King’s quarrel. There are some of us who hope to save France yet, if our blood may avail. In a year, God willing, I shall come again to Puysange; and till then you must wait.”

Hugues conceded that, perforce, he must wait, since a vow was sacred; and Adhelmar, who suspected Hugues’ natural appetite for battle to be lamentably squeamish, grinned. After that, in a sick rage, Adhelmar struck Hugues in the face, and turned about.

The Sieur d’Arques rubbed his cheek ruefully. Then he and Mélite stood silent for a moment, and heard Adhelmar in the courtyard calling his men to ride forth; and Mélite laughed; and Hugues scowled.

===Chinese===
“可你当真爱于格么？”阿代尔玛问，嗓音发涩。

“是的。”梅莉特说，声音极轻；随即红了脸，隐隐纳罕自己方才说的可是真话。接着，不知怎么，她的双臂竟搂住了阿代尔玛的脖颈，吻了他——纯粹出于怜悯，她自己对自己这样说；因为梅莉特心肠柔软，受不住他脸上那份痛楚。

这本也罢了。偏偏这时候，于格·达尔克从一条编枝凉径上蓦地转出来，撞见了他们，觉得二位的姿势颇不入眼。他朝两人压低了乌黑的眉毛。

“阿代尔玛，”他终于开口，“这世界是个小地方。”

阿代尔玛站起身。“可不是，”他附和道，扭出一个歪斜的笑，“我看这世界小得容不下我们两个，于格。”

“我正是这个意思。”达尔克大人说。

“只是，”阿代尔玛接着说，颇有些怅惘，“我的剑，此刻，于格，已经许给我王上的事业去了。我们中间还有些人指望救下法兰西——倘若我们的血还有点用处的话。一年之内，若天主愿意，我会再回皮桑日；在那之前，您只好等。”

于格承认，他势必要等，因为誓约是神圣的；而阿代尔玛素来疑心于格天生的那点对厮杀的胃口未免娇气得可怜，闻言咧嘴一笑。随后，阿代尔玛带着一腔令人作呕的狂怒，一拳打在于格脸上，转身而去。

达尔克大人揉着腮帮子，好生懊恼。随后他与梅莉特默默立了片刻，听着阿代尔玛在院子里呼喝部属备马出发；梅莉特笑了；于格沉着脸。

===Original===
II

Nicolas as Chorus

The year passed, and Adhelmar did not return; and there was much fighting during that interval, and Hugues began to think the knight was slain and would never return to fight with him. The reflection was borne with equanimity.

So Adhelmar was half-forgot, and the Sieur d’Arques turned his mind to other matters. He was still a bachelor, for Reinault considered the burden of the times in ill-accord with the chinking of marriage-bells. They were grim times for Frenchmen: right and left the English pillaged and killed and sacked and guzzled and drank, as if they would never have done; and Edward of England began, to subscribe himself *Rex Franciae* with some show of excuse.

In Normandy men acted according to their natures. Reinault swore lustily and looked to his defences; Hugues, seeing the English everywhere triumphant, drew a long face and doubted, when the will of God was made thus apparent, were it the part of a Christian to withstand it? Then he began to write letters, but to whom no man at either Arques or Puysange knew, saving One-eyed Peire, who carried them.

===Chinese===
II

尼古拉充作歌队

一年过去了，阿代尔玛没有回来；其间战事频仍，于格渐渐觉得那骑士已死于非命，再不会回来同他决斗了。这个念头，他处之泰然。

于是阿代尔玛被淡忘了一半，达尔克大人把心思转到别的事务上。他依旧是单身汉，因为雷诺认为，时局的沉重与婚钟的叮当实在格格不入。对法兰西人来说，那是严酷的年代：英格兰人左右开弓地劫掠、屠戮、洗城、暴饮纵酒，仿佛永无餍足；英格兰的爱德华已开始署名“法兰西之王”（*Rex Franciae*），而且颇有些借口可循。

在诺曼底，人人各依本性行事。雷诺豪气地赌咒，加意修缮守备；于格眼见英格兰人处处得胜，便拉长了脸，疑心起来：天主的旨意既已昭然若此，基督徒违抗它，岂是本分？于是他开始写信，至于写给谁，阿尔克与皮桑日两处无人知晓——除了独眼的佩尔（One-eyed Peire），因为信是他递的。

===Original===
III

Treats of Huckstering

It was in the dusk of a rain-sodden October day that Adhelmar rode to the gates of Puysange, with some score men-at-arms behind him. They came from Poictiers, where again the English had conquered, and Adhelmar rode with difficulty, for in that disastrous business in the field of Maupertuis he had been run through the chest, and his wound was scarce healed. Nevertheless, he came to finish his debate with the Sieur d’Arques, wound or no wound.

But at Puysange he heard a strange tale of Hugues. Reinault, whom Adhelmar found in a fine rage, told the story as they sat over their supper.

It had happened, somehow, (Reinault said), that the Marshal Arnold d’Andreghen﻿—newly escaped from prison and with his disposition unameliorated by Lord Audley’s gaolership﻿—had heard of these letters that Hugues wrote so constantly; and the Marshal, being no scholar, had frowned at such doings, and waited presently, with a company of horse, on the road to Arques. Into their midst, on the day before Adhelmar came, rode Peire, the one-eyed messenger; and it was not an unconscionable while before Peire was bound hand and foot, and d’Andreghen was reading the letter they had found in Peire’s jerkin. “Hang the carrier on that oak,” said d’Andreghen, when he had ended, “but leave that largest branch yonder for the writer. For by the Blood of Christ, our common salvation! I will hang him there on Monday!”

===Chinese===
III

论市侩的营生

那是十月里一个被雨水泡透的黄昏，阿代尔玛率着二十来名武装扈从，来到皮桑日城门下。他们来自普瓦捷（Poictiers）——英格兰人又在那里打赢了；阿代尔玛骑在马上颇为吃力，因为在莫佩尔蒂（Maupertuis）原野那场大祸里，他被一枪捅穿了胸口，伤势才刚刚收口。然而不管有伤没伤，他都要回来同达尔克大人把那场争执了结。

可在皮桑日，他听到了关于于格的一桩离奇事。雷诺——阿代尔玛见到他时正气得七窍生烟——在两人对坐进晚餐的时候讲了这段故事。

事情是这样发生的（雷诺说）：阿诺·德·安德雷根元帅（Marshal Arnold d’Andreghen）——新从狱中逃脱，又经奥德利勋爵（Lord Audley）的狱政一番调理，脾气毫无改善——不知怎么听说了于格频频写的那些信；元帅不是读书人，见了这等行径不由皱眉，便即刻带着一队骑兵，在通往阿尔克的路上候着。就在阿代尔玛到来的前一天，独眼的信使佩尔骑进了他们中间；没过多一会儿，佩尔就被捆了个结实，手脚都不闲着，安德雷根正在读从他紧身衣里搜出的那封信。“把那送信的吊上那棵橡树，”安德雷根读完说，“不过旁边那根最粗的枝子，给你们那位写信的留着。因为凭基督的血、我们共同的救赎起誓！星期一我必吊死他！”

===Original===
So Peire swung in the air ere long and stuck out a black tongue at the crows, who cawed and waited for supper; and presently they feasted while d’Andreghen rode to Arques, carrying a rope for Hugues.

For the Marshal, you must understand, was a man of sudden action. Only two months ago, he had taken the Comte de Harcourt with other gentlemen from the Dauphin’s own table to behead them that afternoon in a field behind Rouen. It was true they had planned to resist the *gabelle*, the King’s immemorial right to impose a tax on salt; but Harcourt was Hugues’ cousin, and the Sieur d’Arques, being somewhat of an epicurean disposition, esteemed the dessert accorded his kinsman unpalatable.

===Chinese===
于是没过多久，佩尔便荡在了半空，向乌鸦们吐出一条黑舌头；鸦群聒噪着等开饭，不久便大快朵颐，而安德雷根已策马直奔阿尔克，给于格带去了一根绳子。

因为您得明白，这位元帅是个雷厉风行的人物。就在两个月前，他把阿尔库尔伯爵（Comte de Harcourt）连同另几位绅士，从王太子本人的餐桌上拿走，当天下午便在鲁昂（Rouen）城后的一块地里斩了首。诚然，那几位的图谋是抗缴盐税（*gabelle*）——王上对盐征税的古来特权；可阿尔库尔是于格的表亲，而达尔克大人天性颇有伊壁鸠鲁之风，觉得奉给他表亲的那道甜点，滋味实在难以下咽。

===Original===
There was no cause for great surprise to d’Andreghen, then, to find that the letter Hugues had written was meant for Edward, the Black Prince of England, now at Bordeaux, where he held the French King, whom the Prince had captured at Poictiers, as a prisoner; for this prince, though he had no particular love for a rogue, yet knew how to make use of one when kingcraft demanded it﻿—and, as he afterward made use of Pedro the Castilian, he was now prepared to make use of Hugues, who hung like a ripe pear ready to drop into Prince Edward’s mouth. “For,” as the Sieur d’Arques pointed out in his letter, “I am by nature inclined to favor you brave English, and so, beyond doubt, is the good God. And I will deliver Arques to you; and thus and thus you may take Normandy and the major portion of France; and thus and thus will I do, and thus and thus must you reward me.”

Said d’Andreghen, “I will hang him at dawn; and thus and thus may the devil do with his soul!”

Then with his company d’Andreghen rode to Arques. A herald declared to the men of that place how the matter stood, and bade Hugues come forth and dance upon nothing. The Sieur d’Arques spat curses, like a cat driven into a corner, and wished to fight, but the greater part of his garrison were not willing to do so in such a cause: and so d’Andreghen took him and carried him off.

In anger having sworn by the Blood of Christ to hang Hugues d’Arques to a certain tree, d’Andreghen had no choice in calm but to abide by his oath. This day being the Sabbath, he deferred the matter; but the Marshal promised to see to it that when morning broke the Sieur d’Arques should dangle side by side with his messenger.

===Chinese===
所以，当安德雷根发现于格写的那封信原来是呈给英格兰黑太子爱德华的——太子此时驻在波尔多（Bordeaux），被他 在普瓦捷俘获的法兰西国王，正囚在那里做他的阶下之囚——他丝毫不必惊讶；因为这位太子虽然对无赖并没有什么特别的偏爱，却深谙在王术需要时如何用一个无赖——正如他后来利用卡斯蒂利亚的佩德罗（Pedro the Castilian）一样，他此刻正打算利用于格，此人活像一只熟透的梨，只待落进爱德华太子的口中。“因为，”正如达尔克大人在信中指出的，“我生性倾向于偏袒你们勇敢的英格兰人，而且毫无疑问，仁慈的天主也是如此。我愿把阿尔克献给您；如此这般，您便可拿下诺曼底和法兰西的大半；我如此这般效劳，您也须如此这般酬报我。”

安德雷根说：“天一亮我就吊死他；至于他的灵魂，如此这般，且由魔鬼去发落！”

随后安德雷根率队直趋阿尔克。一名传令官向当地众人宣明案情，唤于格出来，在那“空无一物”之上跳一回舞。达尔克大人像被逼进角落的猫，一口一口啐出咒骂，想要一战；可他的守军大半不愿为这等事由卖命：于是安德雷根擒了他，带走了。

盛怒之下既已凭基督的血起誓要把于格·达尔克吊上某棵树，安德雷根冷静下来也别无选择，只得信守誓言。这一天是安息日，他把事情推迟了；但元帅保证，天一亮，达尔克大人必将与他的信使并肩荡起来。

===Original===
Thus far the Vicomte de Puysange. He concluded his narrative with a dry chuckle. “And I think we are very well rid of him, Adhelmar. Holy Maclou! that I should have taken the traitor for a true man, though! He would sell France, you observe﻿—chaffered, they tell me, like a peddler over the price of Normandy. Heh, the huckster, the triple-damned Jew!”

“And Mélite?” asked Adhelmar, after a little.

Again Reinault shrugged. “In the White Turret,” he said; then, with a short laugh: “Oy Dieus, yes! The girl has been caterwauling for this shabby rogue all day. She would have me﻿—me, the King’s man, look you!﻿—save Hugues at the peril of my seignory! And I protest to you, by the most high and pious Saint Nicolas the Confessor,” Reinault swore, “that sooner than see this huckster go unpunished, I would lock Hell’s gate on him with my own hands!”

For a moment Adhelmar stood with his jaws puffed out, as if in thought, and then he laughed like a wolf. Afterward he went to the White Turret, leaving Reinault smiling over his wine.

===Chinese===
皮桑日子爵讲到这里为止。他收束叙述，干笑了一声。“依我看，咱们正好摆脱了他，阿代尔玛。圣马库在上！想不到我竟把那奸贼当过正人君子！您瞧，他要把法兰西卖了——听说他讨价还价，像个走乡串巷的货郎，为诺曼底的价钱磨了半天嘴皮。呸，这市侩，这该死三遍的犹太佬！”

“梅莉特呢？”过了片刻，阿代尔玛问。

雷诺又耸了耸肩。“在白塔楼里。”他说；随即短促一笑：“‘哎呀天哪’（Oy Dieus），是啊！那姑娘为这个破落无赖嚎哭了一整天。她要我——我，王上的人，您想想！——冒着丢失领地的风险去救于格！我可向您声明，凭着至高至虔的坚信者圣尼古拉起誓，”雷诺赌咒道，“与其眼看这市侩逍遥法外，我宁可亲手拿钥匙替他锁死地狱的门！”

阿代尔玛鼓着腮帮子立了片刻，仿佛在盘算，随即像狼一般笑起来。之后他便往白塔楼去了，留下雷诺对着酒微笑。

===Original===
IV

Folly Diversely Attested

He found Mélite alone. She had robed herself in black, and had gathered her gold hair about her face like a heavy veil, and sat weeping into it for the plight of Hugues d’Arques.

“Mélite!” cried Adhelmar; “Mélite!” The Demoiselle de Puysange rose with a start, and, seeing him standing in the doorway, ran to him, incompetent little hands fluttering before her like frightened doves. She was very tired, by that daylong arguing with her brother’s notions about honor and knightly faith and such foolish matters, and to her weariness Adhelmar seemed strength incarnate; surely he, if anyone, could aid Hugues and bring him safe out of the grim marshal’s claws. For the moment, perhaps, she had forgotten the feud which existed between Adhelmar and the Sieur d’Arques; but in any event, I am convinced, she knew that Adhelmar could refuse her nothing. So she ran toward him, her cheeks flushing arbutus-like, and she was smiling through her tears.

===Chinese===
IV

痴愚的种种见证

他发现梅莉特独自一人。她一身黑衣，金发拢在脸侧，像一幅厚重的面纱，正伏在里面为于格·达尔克的处境垂泪。

“梅莉特！”阿代尔玛喊道；“梅莉特！”皮桑日小姐惊起而立，看见他立在门口，便朝他奔去，一双笨拙的小手像受惊的鸽子在她胸前扑动。她已被那一整天的争辩——同她哥哥关于荣誉、骑士信义诸如此类蠢事的争辩——弄得疲惫不堪，而在她的倦怠里，阿代尔玛俨然是力量的化身；无疑，他，如果还有人能，必能救出于格，把他从那铁面元帅的爪下安然带出。或许那一刻，她把阿代尔玛与达尔克大人之间的那段过节忘了个干净；但无论如何，我确信，她知道阿代尔玛什么也不会拒绝她。于是她朝他跑去，双颊晕出粉红，宛如初绽的五月花，她含着泪微笑。

===Original===
Oh, thought Adhelmar, were it not very easy to leave Hugues to the dog’s death he merits and to take this woman for my own? For I know that she loves me a little. And thinking of this, he kissed her, quietly, as one might comfort a sobbing child; afterward he held her in his arms for a moment, wondering vaguely at the pliant thickness of her hair and the sweet scent of it. Then he put her from him gently, and swore in his soul that Hugues must die, so that this woman might be Adhelmar’s.

“You will save him?” Mélite asked, and raised her face to his. There was that in her eyes which caused Adhelmar to muse for a little on the nature of women’s love, and, subsequently, to laugh harshly and make vehement utterance.

“Yes!” said Adhelmar.

===Chinese===
哦，阿代尔玛想，把于格丢给他应得的那场狗死，把这女子据为己有，岂非易如反掌？因为我知道她多少有点爱我。想着这个，他吻了她，轻轻地，像人安慰一个抽泣的孩子；随后他把她搂在怀里片刻，隐隐惊异于她头发的柔顺浓密和它的甜香。然后他把她轻轻推开，在灵魂里发誓：于格必须死，好让这女子归于阿代尔玛。

“您会救他的，是么？”梅莉特问，仰起脸来望他。她眼中有什么东西，令阿代尔玛默想了一会儿女人之爱的本性，随后粗声一笑，激烈地说起话来。

“是的！”阿代尔玛说。

===Original===
He demanded how many of Hugues’ men were about. Some twenty of them had come to Puysange, Mélite said, in the hope that Reinault might aid them to save their master. She protested that her brother was a coward for not doing so; but Adhelmar, having his own opinion on this subject, and thinking in his heart that Hugues’ skin might easily be ripped off him without spilling a pint of honest blood, said, simply: “Twenty and twenty is twoscore. It is not a large armament, but it may serve.”

He told her his plan was to fall suddenly upon d’Andreghen and his men that night, and in the tumult to steal Hugues away; whereafter, as Adhelmar pointed out, Hugues might readily take ship for England, and leave the marshal to blaspheme Fortune in Normandy, and the French King to gnaw at his chains in Bordeaux, while Hugues toasts his shins in comfort at London. Adhelmar admitted that the plan was a mad one, but added, reasonably enough, that needs must when the devil drives. And so firm was his confidence, so cheery his laugh﻿—he managed to laugh somehow, though it was a stiff piece of work﻿—that Mélite began to be comforted somewhat, and bade him go and Godspeed.

So then Adhelmar left her. In the main hall he found the vicomte still sitting over his wine of Anjou.

===Chinese===
他问于格手下还有多少人可用。梅莉特说，来了约莫二十个，到皮桑日来，指望雷诺帮他们救主人。她抗议说，她哥哥不肯出力，是个懦夫；但阿代尔玛对此自有看法，心里想着于格那张皮，不用洒一品脱正经血便能剥下来，只简单地说：“二十加二十，四十。算不得一支大军，但也尽够用了。”

他告诉她，他的计策是当夜突袭安德雷根一行，趁乱把于格劫走；此后，正如阿代尔玛指出的，于格大可扬帆去英格兰，任元帅在诺曼底咒骂命运女神，任法兰西国王在波尔多咬他的锁链，而于格在伦敦舒舒服服烤他的小腿。阿代尔玛承认这计策是个疯狂的计策，但又颇在理地补了一句：魔鬼赶车，也只好由它。他的信心那样坚定，笑声那样爽朗——他好歹笑出来了，虽然费了好一番功夫——梅莉特渐渐得了些安慰，便祝他前去，一路蒙天主保佑。

于是阿代尔玛离开了她。在大厅里，他见子爵还坐在那儿喝他的安茹葡萄酒。

===Original===
“Cousin,” said Adhelmar, “I must ride hence tonight.”

Reinault stared at him: a mastering wonder woke in Reinault’s face. “Ta, ta, ta!” he clicked his tongue, very softly. Afterward he sprang to his feet and clutched Adhelmar by both arms. “No, no!” Reinault cried. “No, Adhelmar, you must not try that! It is death, lad﻿—sure death! It means hanging, boy!” the vicomte pleaded, for, hard man that he was, he loved Adhelmar.

“That is likely enough,” Adhelmar conceded.

“They will hang you,” ’ Reinault said again: “d’Andreghen and the Count Dauphin of Vienna will hang you as blithely as they would Iscariot.”

“That, too,” said Adhelmar, “is likely enough, if I remain in France.”

===Chinese===
“表哥，”阿代尔玛说，“我今夜必须骑马离开此地。”

雷诺瞪着他：一脸压不住的骇异在他脸上泛起。“啧、啧、啧！”他极轻地咂着舌。随后他跳起身，双手抓住阿代尔玛的臂膀。“不，不！”雷诺喊道。“不行，阿代尔玛，你可不能去试！那是死，孩子——必死无疑！那是要上绞架的，孩子！”子爵恳求道，因为别看他心肠硬，他是爱阿代尔玛的。

“这倒很可能。”阿代尔玛承认。

“他们会绞死你，”雷诺又说：“安德雷根和维埃纳的太子伯爵（Count Dauphin of Vienna）绞死你，会像绞加略人犹大（Iscariot）一样眉开眼笑。”

“那也很有可能，”阿代尔玛说，“——假使我留在法兰西的话。”

===Original===
“Oy Dieus! will you flee to England, then?” the vicomte scoffed, bitterly. “Has King Edward not sworn to hang you these eight years past? Was it not you, then, cousin, who took Almerigo di Pavia, that Lombard knave whom he made governor of Calais﻿—was it not you, then, who delivered Edward’s loved Almerigo to Geoffrey de Chargny, who had him broken on the wheel? Eh, holy Maclou! but you will get hearty welcome and a chaplain and a rope in England.”

Adhelmar admitted that this was true. “Still,” said he, “I must ride hence tonight.”

“For her?” Reinault asked, and jerked his thumb upward.

“Yes,” said Adhelmar﻿—“for her.”

===Chinese===
“‘哎呀天哪’！那么你要逃去英格兰？”子爵辛辣地讥诮道。“爱德华王发誓要绞死你，不是已经发了八年誓了么？当年拿下阿尔梅里戈·迪·帕维亚（Almerigo di Pavia）——他封作加来总督的那个伦巴第无赖——的，不就是您么，表哥？把爱德华的心肝阿尔梅里戈交到若弗鲁瓦·德·夏尔尼（Geoffrey de Chargny）手里、教他上了轮刑的，不也正是您么？呃，圣马库在上！您去英格兰，管保有一场热热闹闹的迎接，外带一位随行神父和一根绳子。”

阿代尔玛承认此话不假。“不过，”他说，“我今夜必须骑马离开此地。”

“为了她？”雷诺问，拇指往上一戳。

“是的，”阿代尔玛说——“为了她。”

===Original===
Reinault stared in his face for a while. “You are a fool, Adhelmar,” said he, at last, “but you are a brave man, and you love as becomes a chevalier. It is a great pity that a flibbertigibbet wench with a towhead should be the death of you. For my part, I am the King’s vassal; I shall not break faith with him; but you are my guest and my kinsman. For that reason I am going to bed, and I shall sleep very soundly. It is likely I shall hear nothing of the night’s doings﻿—*ohimé*, no! not if you murder d’Andreghen in the courtyard!” Reinault ended, and smiled, somewhat sadly.

Afterward he took Adhelmar’s hand and said: “Farewell, lord Adhelmar! O true knight, sturdy and bold! terrible and merciless toward your enemies, gentle and simple toward your friends, farewell!”

He kissed Adhelmar on either cheek and left him. In those days men encountered death with very little ado.

===Chinese===
雷诺盯着他的脸看了半晌。“你是个傻子，阿代尔玛，”他终于说，“可你是个勇士，你爱得像个真正的骑士。可惜呀，一个亚麻色头发的轻浮丫头，竟要送掉你的性命。至于我，我是王上的封臣；我不会对他负约；可你是我的客人，我的骨肉。为此，我就要去睡了，而且会睡得很沉。今夜的事，多半我什么也不会听见——‘唉’（*ohimé*），不会的！哪怕你们在院子里谋杀安德雷根！”雷诺说到这里收了尾，微微一笑，笑里带几分凄然。

随后他握住阿代尔玛的手说：“别了，阿代尔玛爵爷！哦，真正的骑士，刚毅而豪勇！对仇敌可怖而无情，对朋友温良而纯直——别了！”

他吻了阿代尔玛的双颊，离他而去。在那个年代，人们同死亡打照面，是从容不过的。

===Original===
Then Adhelmar rode off in the rain with thirty-four armed followers. Riding thus, he reflected upon the nature of women and upon his love for the Demoiselle de Puysange; and, to himself, he swore gloomily that if she had a mind to Hugues she must have Hugues, come what might. Having reached this conclusion, Adhelmar wheeled upon his men, and cursed them for tavern-idlers and laggards and flea-hearted snails, and bade them spur.

Mélite, at her window, heard them depart, and heard the noise of their going lapse into the bland monotony of the rain’s noise. This dank night now divulged no more, and she turned back into the room. Adhelmar’s glove, which he had forgotten in his haste, lay upon the floor, and Mélite lifted it and twisted it idly.

“I wonder﻿—?” said she.

She lighted four wax candles and set them before a mirror that was in the room. Mélite stood among them and looked into the mirror. She seemed very tall and very slender, and her loosened hair hung heavily about her beautiful shallow face and fell like a cloak around her black-robed body, showing against the black gown like melting gold; and about her were the tall, white candles tipped with still flames of gold. Mélite laughed﻿—her laughter was high and delicate, with the resonance of thin glass﻿—and raised her arms above her, head, stretching tensely like a cat before a fire, and laughed yet again.

“After all,” said she, “I do not wonder.”

Mélite sat before the mirror, and braided her hair, and sang to herself in a sweet, low voice, brooding with unfathomable eyes upon her image in the glass, while the October rain beat about Puysange, and Adhelmar rode forth to save Hugues that must else be hanged.

===Chinese===
随后阿代尔玛带着三十四名武装随从，冒雨上马而去。一路行去，他思量着女人的天性，思量着他对皮桑日小姐的爱；他阴郁地暗自发誓：她既然心在于格，那就让她得到于格，天塌下来也随它。得了这个结论，阿代尔玛猛地回身对着部下，骂他们是酒馆里的懒汉、拖沓鬼、长着跳蚤心的蜗牛，喝令他们打马疾驰。

梅莉特在窗前听他们远去，听那行进的声息渐渐没入雨声温和的单调里。这湿漉漉的夜再没有泄漏什么，她转身回到房中。阿代尔玛匆忙间遗落的一只手套躺在地板上，梅莉特拾起来，闲闲地绞着。

“不晓得——？”她说。

她点起四支蜡炬，摆在房中一面镜子前。梅莉特立在烛间，望着镜中。她显得又高又纤瘦，散开的长发沉沉地垂在她那张浅淡的丽脸两侧，像一袭斗篷裹住她黑衣的身子，映着黑裙，宛如熔金；她四周是高高的白烛，烛尖挑着一簇簇纹丝不动的金色火苗。梅莉特笑了——她的笑声清越高雅，带一点薄玻璃的鸣响——她把双臂举过头顶，像炉前的猫一样绷紧了伸展，又笑了一声。

“说到底，”她说，“我才没有什么不晓得。”

梅莉特坐到镜前，编着头发，用甜润的低声自歌自唱，一双深不可测的眼睛凝望着镜中的影像；十月之雨四下敲打着皮桑日，而阿代尔玛正纵马出城，去救那个不救便要上绞架的于格。

===Original===
Sang Mélite:

“Rustling leaves of the willow-tree
						
						Peering downward at you and me,
						
						And no man else in the world to see,

“Only the birds, whose dusty coats
						
						Show dark in the green﻿—whose throbbing throats
						
						Turn joy to music and love to notes.

“Lean your body against the tree,
						
						Lifting your red lips up to me,
						
						Mélite, and kiss, with no man to see!

“And let us laugh for a little:﻿—Yea,
						
						Let love and laughter herald the day
						
						When laughter and love will be put away.

“Then you will remember the willow-tree
						
						And this very hour, and remember me,
						
						Mélite﻿—whose face you will no more see!

“So swift, so swift the glad time goes,
						
						And Eld and Death with their countless woes
						
						Draw near, and the end thereof no man knows,

“Lean your body against the tree,
						
						Lifting your red lips up to me,
						
						Mélite, and kiss, with no man to see!”

===Chinese===
梅莉特唱道：

“柳树沙沙的叶，
						
						俯身望着我与你，
						
						世上再无别人瞧见，

“只有鸟儿，它们蒙尘的外衣
						
						在绿荫里显出深色——它们颤动的歌喉
						
						把欢乐化作乐音，把爱化作歌吟。

“把身子倚上树来，
						
						抬起你红红的唇迎着我，
						
						梅莉特，吻吧，无人瞧见！

“且让我们笑这一晌：——是呀，
						
						让爱与笑迎来那一天，
						
						那时笑与爱都要收起。

“那时你会记得这棵柳树，
						
						记得此时此刻，记得我，
						
						梅莉特——你再也见不到的脸！

“韶光去得多快，多快，
						
						老暮与死亡带着数不尽的灾殃
						
						逼近来，结局无人知晓，

“把身子倚上树来，
						
						抬起你红红的唇迎着我，
						
						梅莉特，吻吧，无人瞧见！”

===Original===
Mélite smiled as she sang; for this was a song that Adhelmar had made for her upon a May morning at Nointel, before he was a knight, when both were very young. So now she smiled to remember the making of the verses which she sang while the October rain was beating about Puysange.

===Chinese===
梅莉特边唱边微笑；因为这支歌，是阿代尔玛在努安泰尔（Nointel）一个五月的早晨为她作的，那时他还没有受封骑士，两人都年纪轻轻。所以此刻，当十月的雨敲打着皮桑日，她一面唱着这些诗句，一面想起当年作歌的情形，便微笑起来。

===Original===
V

Night-Work
