It was not long before they came upon d’Andreghen and his men camped about a great oak, with One-eyed Peire a-swing over their heads for a lamentable banner. A shrill sentinel, somewhere in the dark, demanded the newcomers’ business, but without receiving any adequate answer, for at that moment Adhelmar gave the word to charge.

Then it was as if all the devils in Pandemonium had chosen Normandy for their playground; and what took place in the night no man saw for the darkness, so that I cannot tell you of it. Let it suffice that Adhelmar rode away before d’Andreghen had rubbed sleep well out of his eyes; and with Adhelmar were Hugues d’Arques and some half of Adhelmar’s men. The rest were dead, and Adhelmar was badly hurt, for he had burst open his old wound and it was bleeding under his armor. Of this he said nothing.

===Chinese===
V

夜间的活计

没过多久，他们便撞见了安德雷根一行，正围着一棵大橡树扎营，独眼佩尔荡在他们头顶，权当一面凄凉的军旗。黑暗里某处，一个尖嗓门的哨兵喝问来者何干，却没有得到任何像样的回答，因为就在那一刻，阿代尔玛下令冲锋了。

随后那光景，仿佛万魔殿（Pandemonium）里所有的魔鬼都选中了诺曼底做它们的游乐场；夜里的情形，因为天黑，无人看见，所以我也就无从奉告。只需这样说就够了：不等安德雷根把睡意从眼里揉净，阿代尔玛已经驰远了；与他同行的是于格·达尔克和他带来的人马的大约一半。其余的都死了；阿代尔玛自己也伤得不轻，因为旧创迸裂，血正从甲胄底下渗出来。这个，他一句也没提。

===Original===
“Hugues,” said he, “do you and these fellows ride to the coast; thence take ship for England.”

He would have none of Hugues’ thanks; instead, he turned and left Hugues to whimper out his gratitude to the skies, which spat a warm, gusty rain at him. Adhelmar rode again to Puysange, and as he went he sang.

Sang Adhelmar:

“D’Andreghen in Normandy
						
						Went forth to slay mine enemy;
						
						But as he went
						
						Lord God for me wrought marvellously;

“Wherefore, I may call and cry
						
						That am now about to die,
						
						‘I am content!’

“Domine! Domine!
						
						Gratias accipe!
						
						Et meum animum
						
						Recipe in coelum!”

===Chinese===
“于格，”他说，“你带上这几个人骑到海边去；从那儿乘船去英格兰。”

于格的千恩万谢他一概不受；他调转马头，撇下于格，任他对着天空抽抽噎噎地道谢——那天空正朝他啐着一阵温热的、时紧时缓的雨。阿代尔玛重又驰回皮桑日，一路唱着歌。

阿代尔玛唱道：

“安德雷根在诺曼底
						
						出发去杀我的对头；
						
						但他才行至半途，
						
						主上帝已为我行了奇功；

“因此，我这行将就死之人，
						
						尽可呼喊、尽可哭祈：
						
						‘我心满足无遗！’

“Domine! Domine!——主啊！主啊！
						
						Gratias accipe!——请受我的感恩！
						
						Et meum animum——并将我的灵魂
						
						Recipe in coelum!——接入天庭！”

===Original===
VI

They Kiss at Parting

When he had come to Puysange, Adhelmar climbed the stairs of the White Turret﻿—slowly, for he was growing very feeble now﻿—and so came again to Mélite crouching among the burned-out candles in the slate-colored twilight which heralded dawn.

“He is safe,” said Adhelmar. He told Mélite how Hugues was rescued and shipped to England, and how, if she would, she might straightway follow him in a fishing-boat. “For there is likely to be ugly work at Puysange,” Adhelmar said, “when the marshal comes. And he will come.”

“But what will you do now, my cousin?” asked Mélite.

“Holy Ouen!” said Adhelmar; “since I needs must die, I will die in France, not in the cold land of England.”

“Die!” cried Mélite. “Are you hurt so sorely, then?”

===Chinese===
VI

吻别

到了皮桑日，阿代尔玛攀上白塔楼的阶梯——慢慢地，因为他此刻已极虚弱——就这样又来到梅莉特面前；她蜷在燃尽的残烛之间，四周是预告黎明的石板色微明。

“他安全了。”阿代尔玛说。他告诉梅莉特，于格如何被救出、如何已上船去了英格兰；又说，只要她愿意，尽可即刻乘一艘渔船去追他。“因为元帅一来，皮桑日多半要有一场难看的差事，”阿代尔玛说，“而他是一定会来的。”

“可您如今怎么办呢，表哥？”梅莉特问。

“圣乌安在上！”阿代尔玛说；“我横竖是死，那就死在法兰西，不死于英格兰那片冷地方。”

“死！”梅莉特叫道。“那么，您伤得这样重？”

===Original===
He grinned like a death’s-head. “My injuries are not incurable,” said he, “yet must I die very quickly, for all that. The English King will hang me if I go thither, as he has sworn to do these eight years, because of that matter of Almerigo di Pavia: and if I stay in France, I must hang because of this night’s work.”

Mélite wept. “O God! O God!” she quavered, two or three times, like one hurt in the throat. “And you have done this for me! Is there no way to save you, Adhelmar?” she pleaded, with wide, frightened eyes that were like a child’s.

“None,” said Adhelmar. He took both her hands in his, very tenderly. “Ah, my sweet,” said he, “must I, whose grave is already digged, waste breath upon this idle talk of kingdoms and the squabbling men who rule them? I have but a brief while to live, and I wish to forget that there is aught else in the world save you, and that I love you. Do not weep, Mélite! In a little time you will forget me and be happy with this Hugues whom you love; and I?﻿—ah, my sweet, I think that even in my grave I shall dream of you and of your great beauty and of the exceeding love that I bore you in the old days.”

===Chinese===
他咧开嘴，笑得像个骷髅头。“我的伤并非医不得，”他说，“可尽管如此，我也很快就要死了。我去英格兰，英王会绞死我——为着阿尔梅里戈·迪·帕维亚那桩事，这誓他已发了八年；我留在法兰西，就为今夜这场活计，我也得上绞架。”

梅莉特哭了。“哦，天主！哦，天主！”她颤声说，一连两三次，像喉咙里受了伤。“您这一切都是为了我！就没有法子救您了么，阿代尔玛？”她哀求道，一双惊惶的眼睛睁得大大的，像孩子的眼睛。

“没有。”阿代尔玛说。他十分温柔地把她的双手握在自己手里。“啊，我亲爱的人，”他说，“坟墓既已替我掘好，我难道还要把气息浪费在这些关于王国、关于那班治国之争闹之人的空谈上么？我活不了多久了，我只想忘掉这世上除了你还有别的东西，只想忘掉除了我爱你还有别的事。别哭，梅莉特！过不多时，你就会忘了我，同你爱的这个于格快快活活过日子；至于我？——啊，亲爱的人，我想，纵然在坟墓里，我也会梦见你，梦见你的绝代容颜，梦见旧日里我对你的这份深爱。”

===Original===
“Ah, no, I shall not ever forget, O true and faithful lover! And, indeed, indeed, Adhelmar, I would give my life right willingly that yours might be saved!”

She had almost forgotten Hugues. Her heart was sad as she thought of Adhelmar, who must die a shameful death for her sake, and of the love which she had cast away. Beside it, the Sieur d’Arques’ affection showed somewhat tawdry, and Mélite began to reflect that, after all, she had liked Adhelmar almost as well.

“Sweet,” said Adhelmar, “do I not know you to the marrow? You will forget me utterly, for your heart is very changeable. Ah, Mother of God!” Adhelmar cried, with a quick lift of speech; “I am afraid to die, for the harsh dust will shut out the glory of your face, and you will forget!”

“No; ah, no!” Mélite whispered, and drew near to him. Adhelmar smiled, a little wistfully, for he did not believe that she spoke the truth; but it was good to feel her body close to his, even though he was dying, and he was content.

===Chinese===
“啊，不，我永远不会忘，哦，真诚而忠贞的情人！而且真的，真的，阿代尔玛，只要能救您的命，我心甘情愿交出我自己的！”

她几乎把于格忘掉了。她想着阿代尔玛——他为她要受一场羞辱的死——又想着她亲手抛掉的那份爱，心里悲苦。两相比较，达尔克大人的情意便显得颇为俗气寒碜；梅莉特开始思量，说到底，她对阿代尔玛的喜欢，也差不了多少。

“亲爱的，”阿代尔玛说，“我岂不是把你认识到了骨髓里？你会把我忘个干净，因为你的心非常易变。啊，圣母！”阿代尔玛喊道，语声陡然一扬；“我怕死啊，因为无情的尘土会遮断你容颜的光辉，而你会忘记！”

“不；啊，不！”梅莉特耳语着，向他偎近。阿代尔玛略带怅惘地笑了，因为他并不信她说的是真话；可即便在垂死之际，感到她的身子贴着自己的身子也是好的，他心满意足。

===Original===
But by this time the dawn had come completely, flooding the room with its first thin radiance, and Mélite saw the pallor of his face and so knew that he was wounded.

“Indeed, yes,” said Adhelmar, when she had questioned him, “for my breast is quite cloven through.” And when she disarmed him, Mélite found a great cut in his chest which had bled so much that it was apparent he must die, whether d’Andreghen and Edward of England would or no.

Mélite wept again, and cried, “Why had you not told me of this?”

“To have you heal me, perchance?” said Adhelmar. “Ah, love, is hanging, then, so sweet a death that I should choose it, rather than to die very peacefully in your arms? Indeed, I would not live if I might; for I have proven traitor to my King, and it is right that traitors should die; and, chief of all, I know that life can bring me naught more desirable than I have known this night. What need, then, have I to live?”

===Chinese===
可到这时天已大亮，最初的一线清辉漫进房来，梅莉特看见他脸上的死白，这才知道他受了伤。

“真的，是的，”她盘问他之后，阿代尔玛说，“我的胸口整个儿劈穿了。”待她卸下他的甲胄，梅莉特见他胸膛上有一道大伤，血流得那样多，显然，不管安德雷根与英格兰的爱德华乐意不乐意，他都是必死无疑的了。

梅莉特又哭了，喊道：“您为什么不早告诉我？”

“好教您救我么，兴许？”阿代尔玛说。“啊，爱人，绞死难道是那样甜美的死法，教我在它和安安稳稳死在你怀里之间挑它么？说真的，能活我也不要活了；因为我已证实自己是王上的叛臣，叛臣该死，这是天经地义；而首要的是，我知道这一夜我所得到的，已是此生再好不过的东西，我活着还有什么可求？”

===Original===
Mélite bent over him; for as he spoke he had lain back in a tall carven chair by the east window. She was past speech. But now, for a moment, her lips clung to his, and her warm tears fell upon his face. What better death for a lover? thought Adhelmar.

Yet he murmured somewhat. “Pity, always pity!” he said, wearily. “I shall never win aught else of you, Mélite. For before this you have kissed me, pitying me because you could not love me. And you have kissed me now, pitying me because I may not live.”

But Mélite, clasping her arms about his neck, whispered into his ear the meaning of this last kiss, and at the honeyed sound of her whispering his strength came back for a moment, and he strove to rise. The level sunlight through the open window smote full upon his face, which was very glad. Mélite was conscious of her nobility in causing him such delight at the last.

===Chinese===
梅莉特俯下身去；因为说话间他已仰倒在东窗边一张高高的雕花椅里。她说不出话来。可就在这时，有一刻，她的唇贴住了他的唇，温热的泪落在他脸上。对一个情人，还有比这更好的死么？阿代尔玛想。

可他还是低语了几句。“怜悯，永远是怜悯！”他倦倦地说。“梅莉特，我从你这里永远赢不到别的东西了。从前你吻过我，是因为不能爱我而怜我。如今你又吻我，是因为我活不成了而怜我。”

可梅莉特搂住他的脖颈，把这最后一吻的含义悄悄吐进他耳中；听到那蜜也似的低语，他的气力一时间竟回来了，他挣扎着要起身。敞开的窗口里平射进来的阳光，正照满他那张欢欣无比的脸。梅莉特自觉高贵，因自己在最后一刻还能给他这般欢喜。

===Original===
“God, God!” cried Adhelmar, and he spread out his arms toward the dear, familiar world that was slowly taking form beneath them﻿—a world now infinitely dear to him; “all, my God, have pity and let me live a little longer!”

As Mélite, half frightened, drew back from him, he crept out of his chair and fell prone at her feet. Afterward his hands stretched forward toward her, clutching, and then trembled and were still.

Mélite stood looking downward, wondering vaguely when she would next know either joy or sorrow again. She was now conscious of no emotion whatever. It seemed to her she ought to be more greatly moved. So the new day found them.

===Chinese===
“天主，天主！”阿代尔玛喊道，向着那正在他们脚下缓缓成形的、亲爱而熟稔的世界张开了双臂——如今在他眼里，这世界无限可亲；“全能的天主啊，发发慈悲，让我再多活一会儿吧！”

梅莉特半是惊惶地从他面前退开，他从椅子里爬出来，面朝下扑在她脚边。随后他的双手朝她伸出去，抓挠着，继而颤抖，继而僵然不动了。

梅莉特站着向下望，隐隐想着自己下一次再尝到欢乐或悲愁会是什么时候。此刻她什么情绪也感觉不到。她只觉得，自己似乎应当更加悲恸一些。新的一天降临，照见的就是这样一对人。

## III / III

===Original===
The Episode Called Love-Letters of Falstaff

===Chinese===
题为《福斯塔夫情书》的一篇

===Original===
March 2, 1414

===Chinese===
1414年3月2日

===Original===
“Jack, how agrees the devil and thee about thy soul, that thou soldest him for a cup of Madeira and a cold capon’s leg?”

===Chinese===
「杰克，你拿一杯马德拉酒和一只冷阉鸡腿把灵魂卖给了魔鬼——如今你们双方可谈妥了它的归置？」

===Original===
In the chapel at Puysange you may still see the tomb of Adhelmar; but Mélite’s bones lie otherwhere. “Her heart was changeable,” as old Nicolas says, justly enough; and so in due time it was comforted.

===Chinese===
在皮桑日的礼拜堂里，如今仍可见阿代尔玛之墓；但梅莉特的骸骨另葬他处。「她的心是易变的，」老尼古拉如是说，说得也够公道；于是这颗心到时自有人抚慰。

===Original===
For Hugues d’Arques—or Hugh Darke, as his name was Anglicized—presently stood high in the favor of King Edward. A fief was granted to Messire Darke, in Norfolk, where Hugues shortly built for himself a residence at Yaxham, and began to look about for a wife: it was not long before he found one.

===Chinese===
原来于格·达尔克——他的名字英国化之后，唤作休·达克（Hugh Darke）——不久便深得爱德华王的宠眷。达克大人获赐诺福克的一处封地；于格旋即在亚克斯姆为自己营建宅邸，并开始物色一位妻子：没过多久，他便觅得了。

===Original===
This befell at Brétigny when, in 1360, the Great Peace was signed between France and England, and Hugues, as one of the English embassy, came face to face with Reinault and Mélite. History does not detail the meeting; but, inasmuch as the Sieur d’Arques and Mélite de Puysange were married at Rouen the following October, doubtless it passed off pleasantly enough.

===Chinese===
此事发生于布雷蒂尼：1360年，法英两国在那儿缔结了那场大和约，于格作为英国使团的一员，与雷诺和梅莉特迎面相逢。史书未曾细载那次会面；不过，既然达尔克老爷与皮桑日的梅莉特于翌年十月在鲁昂成婚，想必那次会面过得还算愉快。

===Original===
The couple had sufficient in common to have qualified them for several decades of mutual toleration. But by ill luck, Mélite died in childbirth three years after her marriage. She had borne, in 1361, twin daughters, of whom Adelais died a spinster; the other daughter, Sylvia, circa 1378, figured in an unfortunate love-affair with one of Sir Thomas Mowbray’s attendants, but subsequently married Robert Vernon of Winstead. Mélite left also a son, Hugh, born in 1363, who succeeded to his father’s estate of Yaxham in 1387, in which year Hugues fell at the battle of Radcot Bridge, fighting in behalf of the ill-fated Richard of Bordeaux.

===Chinese===
夫妇二人共同之处颇多，本足以使他们相安无事地厮守上几十年。无奈运气不济，梅莉特婚后三年死于产褥。她在1361年生下一对孪生女儿：阿德莱丝终身未嫁；另一个女儿西尔维娅，约在1378年卷入一场不幸的情事，对象是托马斯·莫布雷爵士的一名随从，但她后来嫁给了温斯特德的罗伯特·弗农。梅莉特还遗下一子休，生于1363年；1387年，于格在拉德科特桥之战中为那命途多舛的波尔多的理查效力时阵亡，休遂继承了父亲在亚克斯姆的家业。

===Original===
Now we turn to certain happenings in Eastcheap, at the Boar’s Head Tavern.

===Chinese===
如今我们转去看东市野猪头酒店里的几桩事。

===Original===
I

===Chinese===
I

===Original===
“That Gray Iniquity”

===Chinese===
「那灰白的老罪愆」

===Original===
There was a sound of scuffling within as Sir John Falstaff—much broken since his loss of the King’s favor, and now equally decayed in wit and health and reputation—stood fumbling at the door of the Angel room. He was particularly shaky this morning after a night of particularly hard drinking.

===Chinese===
约翰·福斯塔夫爵士——自失了圣眷以来早已形容大损，如今才智、康健与名声一齐败落——正在「天使厅」门前摸索门闩，屋里传出一阵扭打之声。昨夜他灌得格外凶，今晨也就格外地脚下虚浮。

===Original===
But he came into the apartment singing, and, whatever the scuffling had meant, found Bardolph in one corner employed in sorting garments from a clothes-chest, while at the extreme end of the room Mistress Quickly demurely stirred the fire; which winked at the old knight rather knowingly.

===Chinese===
但他还是唱着歌走进厅来；那阵扭打究竟为了什么不得而知，只见巴道夫在墙角忙着从衣箱里挑拣衣裳，而厅堂另一头，快嘴桂嫂正端庄地拨着炉火；炉火朝老爵士挤了挤眼，那神情煞是心照不宣。

===Original===
“*Then came the bold Sir Caradoc*,” carolled Sir John. “Ah, mistress, what news?—*And eke Sir Pellinore*.—Did I rage last night, Bardolph? Was I a Bedlamite?”

===Chinese===
「*这时来了勇武的卡拉多克爵士*」，约翰爵士欢声唱道。「啊，老板娘，有什么新闻？——*还跟着珀利诺爵士*。——巴道夫，我昨夜可曾撒疯？可曾像个疯人院里放出来的？」

===Original===
“As mine own bruises can testify,” Bardolph assented. “Had each one of them a tongue, they would raise a clamor beside which Babel were as an heir weeping for his rich uncle’s death; their testimony would qualify you for any madhouse in England. And if their evidence go against the doctor’s stomach, the watchman at the corner hath three teeth—or, rather, hath them no longer, since you knocked them out last night—that will, right willingly, aid him to digest it.”

===Chinese===
「我身上的青伤便是明证，」巴道夫表示赞同。「它们若个个都长出舌头，那嚷起来的声势，准叫巴别塔的混乱相比之下，就像嗣子哭吊他那有钱的叔父——继承在望，哭声有气无力；它们的证词，足以为您在英格兰任何一家疯人院里谋个床位。倘若医生觉得这证据难以下咽，街角的更夫还有三颗牙——不如说，自打您昨夜把它们敲落之后，他就没有这三颗牙了——他必定十二分乐意帮他消化。」

===Original===
“Three, say you?” asked the knight, rather stiffly lowering his great body into his great chair set ready for him beside the fire. “I would have my valor in all men’s mouths, but not in this fashion, for it is too biting a jest. Three, say you? Well, I am glad it was no worse; I have a tender conscience, and that mad fellow of the north, Hotspur, sits heavily upon it, so that thus this Percy, being slain by my valor, is per se avenged, a plague on him! Three, say you? I would to God my name were not so terrible to the enemy as it is; I would I had ’bated my natural inclination somewhat, and had slain less tall fellows by some threescore. I doubt Agamemnon slept not well o’ nights. Three, say you? Give the fellow a crown apiece for his mouldy teeth, if thou hast them; if thou hast them not, bid him eschew this vice of drunkenness, whereby his misfortune hath befallen him, and thus win him heavenly crowns.”

===Chinese===
「你说三颗？」骑士问道，一面颇为僵硬地把那庞大的身躯沉进炉边替他备好的大椅里。「我原想我的英名传遍众人之口，却不想是这般传法，这玩笑未免咬人太狠。你说三颗？唔，幸而没有更糟；我这颗良心是软的，北边那个疯子霍茨波还沉沉压在它上头；于是这位潘西既已丧命于我的神威，他这一死本身就抵了仇，让他遭瘟去吧！你说三颗？我巴不得我的名字在敌人听来不这么可怕；我恨不得当初稍稍收敛起天生的脾性，少杀上六十来条大汉。我看阿伽门农夜里也睡不安稳呢。你说三颗？你若手头有，就给那家伙的每颗霉牙赏一枚克朗；若是没有，就劝他戒了酗酒的恶习——他这场不幸正是打那上头来的——好教他挣下天国的冠冕。」

===Original===
“Indeed, sir,” began Bardolph, “I doubt—”

===Chinese===
「说真的，老爷，」巴道夫开口道，「我怕——」

===Original===
“Doubt not, sirrah!” cried Sir John, testily; and continued, in a virtuous manner: “Was not the apostle reproved for that same sin? Thou art a Didymus, Bardolph;—an incredulous paynim, a most unspeculative rogue! Have I carracks trading in the Indies? Have I robbed the exchequer of late? Have I the Golden Fleece for a cloak? Nay, it is paltry gimlet, and that augurs badly. Why, does this knavish watchman take me for a raven to feed him in the wilderness? Tell him there are no such ravens hereabout; else had I ravenously limed the housetops and set springes in the gutters. Inform him that my purse is no better lined than his own broken skull: it is void as a beggar’s protestations, or a butcher’s stall in Lent; light as a famished gnat, or the sighing of a new-made widower; more empty than a last year’s bird-nest, than a madman’s eye, or, in fine, than the friendship of a king.”

===Chinese===
「不许疑，小子！」约翰爵士焦躁地喝道；随即又端出一副端方面孔，继续说道：「使徒不也为了这同样的罪受过申斥么？你是个多疑的多马（Didymus），巴道夫；——一个不肯信的异教徒，一个全无灵气的无赖！我在印度群岛有商船队么？我近来洗劫过国库么？我拿金羊毛当大氅穿么？没有，这不过是把下三滥的锥子，钻出的是凶兆。怎么，这无赖更夫把我当成旷野里给他送食的乌鸦了？告诉他，这一带不出产那样的乌鸦；否则我早像饿鸦一般，把粘胶涂遍了屋顶，在檐沟里布满圈套。告诉他，我的钱袋，衬里不比他那打破的脑袋厚实：空得像乞丐的赌咒，像大斋期的肉摊；轻得像饿瘪的蚊蚋，像新鳏汉子的叹息；比去年的鸟窝还空，比疯子的眼睛还空，说到底，比国王的友情还空。」

===Original===
“But you have wealthy friends, Sir John,” suggested the hostess of the Boar’s Head Tavern, whose impatience had but very hardly waited for this opportunity to join in the talk. “Yes, I warrant you, Sir John. Sir John, you have a many wealthy friends; you cannot deny that, Sir John.”

===Chinese===
「可您有的是阔朋友啊，约翰爵士，」野猪头酒店的老板娘进言道，她好不容易才按捺住性子，挨到这个插嘴的机会。「可不是么，我跟您说，约翰爵士。约翰爵士，您有的是阔朋友；这话您可否认不了，约翰爵士。」

===Original===
“Friends, dame?” asked the knight, and cowered closer to the fire, as though he were a little cold. “I have no friends since Hal is King. I had, I grant you, a few score of acquaintances whom I taught to play at dice; paltry young blades of the City, very unfledged juvenals! Setting my knighthood and my valor aside, if I did swear friendship with these, I did swear to a lie. But this is a censorious and muddy-minded world, so that, look you, even these sprouting aldermen, these foul bacon-fed rogues, have fled my friendship of late, and my reputation hath grown somewhat more murky than Erebus. No matter! I walk alone, as one that hath the pestilence. No matter! But I grow old; I am not in the vaward of my youth, mistress.”

===Chinese===
「朋友么，太太？」骑士问道，往火边又偎了偎，仿佛有点畏冷。「自打哈尔做了国王，我就没有朋友了。我承认，从前有过几十号相识，是我教会他们掷骰子的；城里那些轻薄少年，羽毛未丰的雏儿！就算把我的骑士身份和神威都搁在一边，我若同这班人结交为友，那也是结了一个谎。偏生这世道惯会吹毛求疵、满脑子烂泥，于是你瞧，连这些刚冒头的市政官、这些腌肉喂肥的臭流氓，近来也躲避起我的交情来，我的名声比厄瑞玻斯又晦暗了几分。不要紧！我独自个儿走路，像个染了瘟疫的人。不要紧！只是我老了；我已不在青春的前卫上了，老板娘。」

===Original===
He nodded his head with extreme gravity; then reached for a cup of sack that Bardolph held at the knight’s elbow.

===Chinese===
他极郑重地点了点头；随后伸手去接巴道夫擎在他肘边的白葡萄酒。

===Original===
“Indeed, I know not what your worship will do,” said Mistress Quickly, rather sadly.

===Chinese===
「说真的，我都不知道老爷您往后可怎么办呢，」快嘴桂嫂说道，颇有些伤感。

===Original===
“Faith!” answered Sir John, finishing the sack and grinning in a somewhat ghastly fashion; “unless the Providence that watches over the fall of a sparrow hath an eye to the career of Sir John Falstaff, Knight, and so comes to my aid shortly, I must needs convert my last doublet into a mask, and turn highwayman in my shirt. I can take purses yet, ye Uzzite comforters, as gaily as I did at Gadshill, where that scurvy Poins, and he that is now King, and some twoscore other knaves did afterward assault me in the dark; yet I peppered some of them, I warrant you!”

===Chinese===
「说真的！」约翰爵士答道，把酒干了，咧嘴一笑，那笑相颇为吓人；「除非那看顾麻雀坠地的天意，也肯照拂约翰·福斯塔夫骑士大人的前程，赶快来搭把手，我就只得把最后一件紧身衣改成面罩，单穿着衬衣去做剪径的买卖。我如今还能劫钱包呢，你们这些乌斯地来的「安慰者」，还能像当年在盖兹山那样痛快——就在那儿，那个下作的波因斯，还有如今做了国王的那一位，还有二十来个别的恶棍，事后在黑地里围攻我；可我也给几个人尝了辣味，我担保！」

===Original===
“You must be rid of me, then, master,” Bardolph interpolated. “I for one have no need of a hempen collar.”

===Chinese===
「那您就得把我打发了，老爷，」巴道夫插嘴道。「拿我来说，可不需要麻绳项圈。」

===Original===
“Ah, well!” said the knight, stretching himself in his chair as the warmth of the liquor coursed through his inert blood; “I, too, would be loth to break the gallows’ back! For fear of halters, we must alter our way of living; we must live close, Bardolph, till the wars make us Croesuses or food for crows. And if Hal but hold to his bias, there will be wars: I will eat a piece of my sword, if he have not need of it shortly. Ah, go thy ways, tall Jack; there live not three good men unhanged in England, and one of them is fat and grows old. We must live close, Bardolph; we must forswear drinking and wenching! But there is lime in this sack, you rogue; give me another cup.”

===Chinese===
「啊，可不是！」骑士说道，酒的暖意在他呆滞的血液里周流，他就在椅子里舒展开来；「我也不乐意去压断绞架的横梁！为了怕套索，咱们得改改活法；咱们得收敛着过日子，巴道夫，等打仗把咱们打成克罗伊斯，或者打成乌鸦的口粮。只要哈尔照他那点脾性走下去，仗是不愁没得打的：他要不很快就用上剑，我就把自己这口剑啃一段吃。啊，去你的，大个子杰克；英格兰没吊死的好汉凑不满三个，其中一个还是胖子，而且越来越老。咱们得收敛着过日子，巴道夫；咱们得戒酒戒色！不过这白葡萄酒里掺了胶，你这流氓；再给我来一杯。」

===Original===
The old knight drained this second cup, and unctuously sucked at and licked his lips. Thereafter,

===Chinese===
老骑士干了这第二杯，油光光地咂着、舔着嘴唇。随后——

===Original===
“I pray you, hostess,” he continued, “remember that Doll Tearsheet sups with me tonight; have a capon of the best, and be not sparing of the wine. I will repay you, upon honor, when we young fellows return from France, all laden with rings and brooches and such trumperies like your Norfolkshire peddlers at Christmas-tide. We will sack a town for you, and bring you back the Lord Mayor’s beard to stuff you a cushion; the Dauphin shall be your tapster yet; we will walk on lilies, I warrant you, to the tune of ‘Hey, Then Up Go We!’ ”

===Chinese===
「求你了，老板娘，」他接着说，「记着今晚桃儿·贴席要来同我进晚餐；备一只上好的阉鸡，酒也别吝惜。我以名誉担保，等我们这些小伙子从法兰西回来，个个满载着戒指、胸针和你那些圣诞节赶集的诺福克小贩叫卖的花哨玩意儿，我一定还你。我们要替你洗劫一座城，把市长大人的胡子带回来给你做个靠垫芯子；王太子早晚得来给你当酒保；我们还要在百合花上行走，我担保，踏着《嘿，咱们往上冲！》（Hey, Then Up Go We!）的调子。」

===Original===
“Indeed, sir,” said Mistress Quickly, in perfect earnest, “your worship is as welcome to my pantry as the mice—a pox on ’em!—think themselves; you are heartily welcome. Ah, well, old Puss is dead; I had her of Goodman Quickly these ten years since;—but I had thought you looked for the lady who was here but now;—she was a roaring lion among the mice.”

===Chinese===
「说真的，老爷，」快嘴桂嫂十分当真地说，「您光临我的食品柜，就跟那些老鼠——让瘟病缠了它们！——自个儿心里想的那么受欢迎；您是打心眼里受欢迎的。啊，对了，老「猫咪」死了；它是打桂掌柜手里跟了我十年的；——我方才还当您是在等先前在这儿的那位太太呢；——她在这群老鼠中间，可是一头吼叫的狮子。」

===Original===
“What lady?” cried Sir John, with great animation. “Was it Flint the mercer’s wife, think you? Ah, she hath a liberal disposition, and will, without the aid of Prince Houssain’s carpet or the horse of Cambuscan, transfer the golden shining pieces from her husband’s coffers to mine.”

===Chinese===
「哪位太太？」约翰爵士嚷道，登时来了精神。「你说是布商弗林特的老婆？啊，那一位手头最松，不用劳驾侯赛因王子的飞毯，也不用坎布司干的神马，就能把她丈夫钱柜里金晃晃的金币，搬进我的口袋。」

===Original===
“No mercer’s wife, I think,” Mistress Quickly answered, after consideration. “She came with two patched footmen, and smacked of gentility;—Master Dumbleton’s father was a mercer; but he had red hair;—she is old;—and I could never abide red hair.”

===Chinese===
「我想不是布商的老婆，」快嘴桂嫂想了想，答道。「她带着两个穿补丁号衣的仆从，一股上等人家气派；——邓布尔顿先生的父亲倒是个布商；可他是红头发；——她上了年纪；——红头发我可从来受不住。」

===Original===
“No matter!” cried the knight. “I can love this lady, be she a very Witch of Endor. Observe, what a thing it is to be a proper man, Bardolph! She hath marked me;—in public, perhaps; on the street, it may be;—and then, I warrant you, made such eyes! and sighed such sighs! and lain awake o’ nights, thinking of a pleasing portly gentleman, whom, were I not modesty’s self, I might name;—and I, all this while, not knowing! Fetch me my Book of Riddles and my Sonnets, that I may speak smoothly. Why was my beard not combed this morning? No matter, it will serve. Have I no better cloak than this?” Sir John was in a tremendous bustle, all a-beam with pleasurable anticipation.

===Chinese===
「不要紧！」骑士嚷道。「哪怕她是隐多珥的女巫本尊，我也能爱这位太太。你瞧，巴道夫，做个像样的男人是何等造化！她已经看中我了；——兴许是当众看中的；也许是在街上；——跟着，我担保，她便丢那样的眼色！叹那样的长气！夜里翻来覆去合不上眼，一心想着一位讨人喜欢、富富态态的绅士——若不是我本人便是谦逊的化身，我几乎可以点出他的名姓；——而我呢，这半日全都蒙在鼓里！把我的《谜语书》和《十四行诗集》取来，好让我谈吐圆熟些。今早怎么没人替我梳胡子？不要紧，将就使得。我就没有一件好些的斗篷么？」约翰爵士登时忙作一团，喜气盈盈，满心打着如意算盘。

===Original===
But Mistress Quickly, who had been looking out of the window, said, “Come, but your worship must begin with unwashed hands, for old Madam Wish-for’t and her two country louts are even now at the door.”

===Chinese===
可快嘴桂嫂一直望着窗外，这时说道：「喂，老爷这回怕是只好连手都不洗就开工了，「巴不得」老太太和她的两个乡下粗汉这会儿已经到了门口。」

===Original===
“Avaunt, minions!” cried the knight. “Avaunt! Conduct the lady hither, hostess; Bardolph, another cup of sack. We will ruffle it, lad, and go to France all gold, like Midas! Are mine eyes too red? I must look sad, you know, and sigh very pitifully. Ah, we will ruffle it! Another cup of sack, Bardolph;—I am a rogue if I have drunk today. And avaunt! vanish! for the lady comes.”

===Chinese===
「退下，小厮们！」骑士喊道。「退下！把夫人请进来，老板娘；巴道夫，再斟一杯白葡萄酒。咱们要抖起威风来，孩子，浑身金光灿灿地上法兰西去，赛过迈达斯！我的眼睛太红了么？你知道，我得显得愁苦些，还得哀哀切切地叹气。啊，咱们要抖起威风来！再来一杯，巴道夫；——今天我要是喝过酒，我就是个恶棍。退下！消失！夫人驾到了。」

===Original===
He threw himself into a gallant attitude, suggestive of one suddenly palsied, and with the mien of a turkey-cock strutted toward the door to greet his unknown visitor.

===Chinese===
他摆出一个英武的架势，活像忽然中风瘫痪的人，带着火鸡公的神气，大摇大摆地向门口走去，迎接他那位素不相识的客人。

===Original===
II

===Chinese===
II

===Original===
“Then was Jack Falstaff, now Sir John, a Boy”

===Chinese===
「那时杰克·福斯塔夫——如今的约翰爵士——还是个孩子」

===Original===
The woman who entered was not the jolly City dame one looked for: and, at first sight, you estimated her age as a trifle upon the staider side of sixty. But to this woman the years had shown unwonted kindliness, as though time touched her less with intent to mar than to caress; her form was still unbent, and her countenance, bloodless and deep-furrowed, bore the traces of great beauty; and, whatever the nature of her errand, the woman who stood in the doorway was unquestionably a person of breeding.

===Chinese===
进来的那位妇人，并不是人们料想中的城里快活太太：乍一看去，你估量她的年纪约莫六十开外，还偏向老成稳重那边。可是岁月待这位妇人却显出难得的仁慈，仿佛时光落在她身上，与其存心摧折，不如说是爱抚；她的身姿依然挺直，那苍白而刻着深纹的面容上，犹见绝色的痕迹；而且，不论她此来所为何事，立在门口的这位妇人，确凿无疑是有教养人家出身。

===Original===
Sir John advanced toward her with as much elegance as he might muster; for gout when coupled with such excessive bulk does not beget an overpowering amount of grace.

===Chinese===
约翰爵士竭力攒起他拿得出的全部风雅，向她迎上去；因为痛风配上这等超凡的块头，实在生不出多少叫人心折的雍容来。

===Original===
“*See, from the glowing East, Aurora comes*,” he chirped. “Madam, permit me to welcome you to my poor apartments; they are not worthy—”

===Chinese===
「*看，奥罗拉从霞光灿烂的东方来了*」，他尖着嗓子唱道。「夫人，容我欢迎您光临寒舍；这里实在配不上——」

===Original===
“I would see Sir John Falstaff, sir,” declared the lady, courteously, but with some reserve of manner, and looking him full in the face as she said this.

===Chinese===
「我要见约翰·福斯塔夫爵士，先生，」那位夫人说道，客气之中带着几分矜持，说这话时直直地望着他的脸。

===Original===
“Indeed, madam,” suggested Sir John, “if those bright eyes—whose glances have already cut my poor heart into as many pieces as the man in the front of the almanac—will but desist for a moment from such butcher’s work and do their proper duty, you will have little trouble in finding the bluff soldier you seek.”

===Chinese===
「说真的，夫人，」约翰爵士进言道，「只要那双明眸——它们的顾盼已把我这颗可怜的心切成了碎块，切得同历书卷首那人身上画的部位一样多——肯暂且歇下这屠户营生，尽一尽它们的正差，您要寻那位直爽豪气的老兵，便不费什么力气。」

===Original===
“Are you Sir John?” asked the lady, as though suspecting a jest. “The son of old Sir Edward Falstaff, of Norfolk?”

===Chinese===
「您就是约翰爵士？」夫人问话的口气仿佛疑心他在打趣。「诺福克的老爱德华·福斯塔夫爵士的儿子？」

===Original===
“His wife hath frequently assured me so,” Sir John protested, very gravely; “and to confirm her evidence I have about me a certain villainous thirst that did plague Sir Edward sorely in his lifetime, and came to me with his other chattels. The property I have expended long since; but no Jew will advance me a *maravedi* on the Falstaff thirst. It is a priceless commodity, not to be bought or sold; you might as soon quench it.”

===Chinese===
「他的太太屡次三番拿这一点向我担保，」约翰爵士分辩道，神色极其庄重；「为佐证她的证词，我身上还带着一宗可恶的酒渴，那东西生前把爱德华爵士折磨得不轻，又随他别的动产一并传到了我名下。产业我早已荡尽了；可再没有哪个犹太人肯凭这福斯塔夫家的酒渴，预支我一个*马拉维迪*（maravedi）。那是件无价之宝，买不来也卖不脱；倒不如想法子把它浇灭，反倒容易些。」

===Original===
“I would not have known you,” said the lady, wonderingly; “but,” she added, “I have not seen you these forty years.”

===Chinese===
「我几乎认不得您了，」夫人惊讶地说；「不过，」她又补了一句，「我已经四十年没见过您了。」

===Original===
“Faith, madam,” grinned the knight, “the great pilferer Time hath since then taken away a little from my hair, and added somewhat (saving your presence) to my belly; and my face hath not been improved by being the grindstone for some hundred swords. But I do not know you.”

===Chinese===
「说真的，夫人，」骑士咧嘴笑道，「那个大扒手时光，从那以后从我的头发上偷去了几根，又（恕我直言）往我的肚子上添了些分量；至于这张脸，给上百口剑当过磨刀石，就更不见起色了。可我不认得您。」

===Original===
“I am Sylvia Vernon,” said the lady. “And once, a long while ago, I was Sylvia Darke.”

===Chinese===
「我是西尔维娅·弗农，」夫人说。「而在很久很久以前，我是西尔维娅·达克。」

===Original===
“I remember,” said the knight. His voice was altered. Bardolph would hardly have known it; nor, perhaps, would he have recognized his master’s manner as he handed Dame Sylvia to the best chair.

===Chinese===
「我记得，」骑士说。他的嗓音变了。巴道夫多半听不出来；他把西尔维娅夫人让到上座时那副做派，恐怕他也认不得了。

===Original===
“A long while ago,” she repeated, sadly, after a pause during which the crackling of the fire was very audible. “Time hath dealt harshly with us both, John;—the name hath a sweet savor. I am an old woman now. And you—”

===Chinese===
「很久很久以前，」她凄然重复道；停了半晌，其间只听见炉火毕毕剥剥地响。「时光待你我两个都苛刻，约翰；——这名字叫起来还有几分甜味。如今我是个老太婆了。而你——」

===Original===
“I would not have known you,” said Sir John; then asked, almost resentfully, “What do you here?”

===Chinese===
「我也认不得你了，」约翰爵士说；随即又问，口气近乎怨怼：「你来此何干？」

===Original===
“My son goes to the wars,” she answered, “and I am come to bid him farewell; yet I should not tarry in London, for my lord is feeble and hath constant need of me. But I, an old woman, am yet vain enough to steal these few moments from him who needs me, to see for the last time, mayhap, him who was once my very dear friend.”

===Chinese===
「我儿子要去打仗了，」她答道，「我来送他一程；本不该在伦敦多耽搁，我家老爷身子虚弱，时刻离不得我。可我这个老婆子，还虚荣到肯从需要我的人身边偷出这一点工夫，来或许最后看一眼那个人——他曾经是我顶顶亲爱的朋友。」

===Original===
“I was never your friend, Sylvia,” said Sir John.

===Chinese===
「我从来就不是你的朋友，西尔维娅，」约翰爵士说。

===Original===
“Ah, the old wrangle!” said the lady, and smiled a little wistfully. “My dear and very honored lover, then; and I am come to see him here.”

===Chinese===
「啊，又是老口角！」夫人说着，怅然微微笑了。「那么，是我亲爱的、极受尊崇的旧日情人；我就是来这儿看看他的。」

===Original===
“Ay!” interrupted Sir John, rather hastily; and he proceeded, glowing with benevolence: “A quiet, orderly place, where I bestow my patronage; the woman of the house had once a husband in my company. God rest his soul! he bore a good pike. He retired in his old age and ’stablished this tavern, where he passed his declining years, till death called him gently away from this naughty world. God rest his soul, say I!”

===Chinese===
「正是！」约翰爵士忙不迭地打断；接着便满面慈悲地往下说：「一个清静、规矩的地方，我时常照应照应生意；这房子里的女人，她的男人从前在我连里当过差。愿上帝安他的灵魂！他扛得一手好长矛。他年老告退，开了这爿酒店，在此安度残年，直到死神客客气气地把他从这个顽劣的世界上唤走。愿上帝安他的灵魂，我说！」

===Original===
This was a somewhat euphemistic version of the taking-off of Goodman Quickly, who had been knocked over the head with a joint-stool while rifling the pockets of a drunken guest; but perhaps Sir John wished to speak well of the dead, even at the price of conferring upon the present home of Sir John an idyllic atmosphere denied it by the London constabulary.

===Chinese===
对于桂掌柜的死法，这番话未免过于粉饰——他是在掏一个醉客口袋的时候，叫人用板凳砸破了头的；不过约翰爵士大概存心称道亡者，哪怕代价是给他如今的居所平添一层田园诗意，而伦敦的巡警们是从不肯承认这里有这情调的。

===Original===
“And you for old memories’ sake yet aid his widow?” the lady murmured. “That is like you, John.”

===Chinese===
「而您还念着旧日情分，扶助他的遗孀？」夫人喃喃道。「这倒像您会的，约翰。」

===Original===
There was another silence, and the fire crackled more loudly than ever.

===Chinese===
又是一场沉默，炉火也炸得比先前更响。

===Original===
“And are you sorry that I come again, in a worse body, John, strange and time-ruined?”

===Chinese===
「我如今又来了，带着一副更不济的皮囊，约翰，又陌生，又叫时光毁败了的——你见了难过么？」

===Original===
“Sorry?” echoed Sir John; and, ungallant as it was, he hesitated a moment before replying: “No, faith! But there are some ghosts that will not easily bear raising, and you have raised one.”

===Chinese===
「难过？」约翰爵士应声道；虽则对夫人这般未免失礼，他还是迟疑了片刻才回答：「不难过，真的！只是有些鬼魂，轻易招惹不得，而你招来了一只。」

===Original===
“We have summoned up no very fearful spectre, I think,” replied the lady; “at most, no worse than a pallid, gentle spirit that speaks—to me, at least—of a boy and a girl who loved each other and were very happy a great while ago.”

===Chinese===
「我想我们招来的并非什么可怕的幽灵，」夫人答道；「至多不过是个苍白温柔的魂灵，它说起的——至少对我是如此——是一对彼此相爱、在很久以前非常快活的少男少女。」

===Original===
“Are you come hither to seek that boy?” asked the knight, and chuckled, though not merrily. “The boy that went mad and rhymed of you in those far-off dusty years? He is quite dead, my lady; he was drowned, mayhap, in a cup of wine. Or he was slain, perchance, by a few light women. I know not how he died. But he is quite dead, my lady, and I had not been haunted by his ghost until today.”

===Chinese===
「你是来寻那个少年的么？」骑士问，干笑了几声，却无欢意。「那个在那遥远尘封的岁月里为你发疯、为你押韵的少年？他早死透了，夫人；他兴许是淹死在一杯酒里的。又兴许是叫几个轻薄女人结果了性命。他怎么死的，我不知道。只是他确实死透了，夫人，而在今天以前，他的鬼魂倒还不曾来缠过我。」

===Original===
He stared at the floor as he ended; then choked, and broke into a fit of coughing which unromantic chance brought on just now, of all times.

===Chinese===
说完，他两眼盯着地板；随即喉头一哽，迸出一阵咳嗽——这不懂风情的巧劲儿，偏偏挑了这么个时候来。

===Original===
“He was a dear boy,” she said, presently; “a boy who loved a young maid very truly; a boy that found the maid’s father too strong and shrewd for desperate young lovers—Eh, how long ago it seems, and what a flood of tears the poor maid shed at being parted from that dear boy!”

===Chinese===
「他是个可爱的少年，」她过了一晌才说；「一个真心真意爱着一个年轻姑娘的少年；一个发现那姑娘的父亲太精明强干、不是走投无路的少年情侣对付得了的少年——唉，说起来是多么久远的事了，那可怜的姑娘被从那可爱的少年身边拆开时，淌了多少眼泪啊！」

===Original===
“Faith!” admitted Sir John, “the rogue had his good points.”

===Chinese===
「说真的！」约翰爵士承认道，「那小子也有他的好处。」

===Original===
“Ah, John, you have not forgotten, I know,” the lady said, looking up into his face, “and, you will believe me that I am very heartily sorry for the pain I brought into your life?”

===Chinese===
「啊，约翰，我知道，你没有忘记，」夫人仰起脸望着他说，「你也该相信，我给你一生带来的那些苦痛，我是打心底里抱愧的，是不是？」

===Original===
“My wounds heal easily,” said Sir John.

===Chinese===
「我的伤好得快，」约翰爵士说。

===Original===
“For though my dear dead father was too wise for us, and knew it was for the best that I should not accept your love, believe me, John, I always knew the value of that love, and have held it an honor that any woman must prize.”

===Chinese===
「虽然我那亲爱的亡父比我们两个都明智，知道我不该接受你的爱才是最好的，可你相信我，约翰，我一直懂得那份爱的分量，一向把它看作任何女子都必得珍视的荣耀。」

===Original===
“Dear lady,” the knight suggested, with a slight grimace, “the world is not altogether of your opinion.”

===Chinese===
「亲爱的夫人，」骑士微微做了个怪相，进言道，「世人可未必全盘同意您的见解。」

===Original===
“I know not of the world,” she said; “for we live away from it. But we have heard of you ever and anon; I have your life quite letter-perfect for these forty years or more.”

===Chinese===
「我不理会世人怎么想，」她说；「因为我们住在远离尘世的地方。不过我们时常听到你的消息；这四十多年来，你的生平我背得一字不差。」

===Original===
“You have heard of me?” asked Sir John; and, for a seasoned knave, he looked rather uncomfortable.

===Chinese===
「你们听说过我？」约翰爵士问；以一个老练的恶棍而论，他这会儿的神情未免相当局促。

===Original===
“As a gallant and brave soldier,” she answered; “of how you fought at sea with Mowbray that was afterward Duke of Norfolk; of your knighthood by King Richard; of how you slew the Percy at Shrewsbury; and captured Coleville o’ late in Yorkshire; and how the Prince, that now is King, did love you above all men; and, in fine, of many splendid doings in the great world.”

===Chinese===
「听说的是一位豪侠英勇的军人，」她答道；「说你在海上同后来做了诺福克公爵的莫布雷交过锋；说理查王封了你骑士；说你在什鲁斯伯里斩了潘西；新近又在约克郡擒了科尔维尔；说如今做了国王的那位亲王爱你胜过世上一切人；总之，都是你在那大千世界里的许多辉煌业绩。」

===Original===
Sir John raised a protesting hand. He said, with commendable modesty: “I have fought somewhat. But we are not Bevis of Southampton; we have slain no giants. Heard you naught else?”

===Chinese===
约翰爵士举起一只手，表示不敢当。他说道，谦虚得可嘉：「打过几仗。可我们又不是南安普顿的比维斯；我们没杀过巨人。旁的就没听说什么了么？」

===Original===
“Little else of note,” replied the lady; and went on, very quietly: “But we are proud of you at home in Norfolk. And such tales as I have heard I have woven together in one story; and I have told it many times to my children as we sat on the old Chapel steps at evening, and the shadows lengthened across the lawn, and I bid them emulate this, the most perfect knight and gallant gentleman that I have known. And they love you, I think, though but by repute.”

===Chinese===
「旁的值得说的就少了，」夫人答道；接着又十分平静地说下去：「可在诺福克家乡，我们都为你骄傲。我把听来的这些故事编成了一个整本；许多个傍晚，我们坐在老礼拜堂的石阶上，影子在草地上越拉越长，我把它讲给孩子们听，一遍又一遍，嘱咐他们要效法这位人物——我所识得的最完美的骑士、最豪侠的君子。我想他们也是爱你的，虽然不过是凭名声爱。」

===Original===
Once more silence fell between them; and the fire grinned wickedly at the mimic fire reflected by the old chest, as though it knew of a most entertaining secret.

===Chinese===
两人之间又落下一场沉默；炉火冲着旧衣箱上那描画的假火邪邪地笑着，仿佛肚里揣着一桩再有趣不过的秘密。

===Original===
“Do you yet live at Winstead?” asked Sir John, half idly.

===Chinese===
「你还住在温斯特德么？」约翰爵士问道，一半是心不在焉。

===Original===
“Yes,” she answered; “in the old house. It is little changed, but there are many changes about.”

===Chinese===
「是的，」她答道；「还住老宅子。宅子倒没怎么变，四下里却变了许多。」

===Original===
“Is Moll yet with you that did once carry our letters?”

===Chinese===
「从前替我们传信的莫尔还在你那儿么？」

===Original===
“Married to Hodge, the tanner,” the lady said; “and dead long since.”

===Chinese===
「嫁给制革匠霍奇了，」夫人说；「也过世多年了。」

===Original===
“And all our merry company?” Sir John demanded. “Marian? And Tom and little Osric? And Phyllis? And Adelais? Zounds, it is like a breath of country air to speak their names once more.”

===Chinese===
「那我们那一伙快活伙伴呢？」约翰爵士追问。「玛丽安呢？还有汤姆和小奥斯里克？菲莉丝呢？阿德莱丝呢？天晓得，再把这几个名字说上一遍，就像吸了一口乡下的新鲜空气。」

===Original===
“All dead,” she answered, in a hushed voice, “save Adelais, and even to me poor Adelais seems old and strange. Walter was slain in the French wars, and she hath never married.”

===Chinese===
「都死了，」她压低了声音答道，「只剩阿德莱丝，可连我看，可怜的阿德莱丝也显得又老又陌生了。沃尔特死在法兰西战争里，她始终没有嫁人。」

===Original===
“All dead,” Sir John informed the fire, as if confidentially; then he laughed, though his bloodshot eyes were not merry. “This same Death hath a wide maw! It is not long before you and I, my lady, will be at supper with the worms. But you, at least, have had a happy life.”

===Chinese===
「都死了，」约翰爵士像交心底话似的告知炉火；随后他笑了，虽然那双充血的眼睛里并无笑意。「这位死神先生的胃口可真不小！要不了多久，你和我，夫人，就该同蛆虫共进晚餐了。不过你总算有过幸福的一生。」

===Original===
“I have been content enough,” she said, “but all that seems run by; for, John, I think that at our age we are not any longer very happy nor very miserable.”

===Chinese===
「我素来也算知足，」她说，「可这一切仿佛都流过去了；因为，约翰，我想我们到了这个年纪，已经谈不上十分快活，也谈不上十分悲惨了。」

===Original===
“Faith!” agreed Sir John, “we are both old; and I had not known it, my lady, until today.”

===Chinese===
「说真的！」约翰爵士附和道，「咱们俩都老了；而这，夫人，直到今天我才明白。」

===Original===
Again there was silence; and again the fire leapt with delight at the jest.

===Chinese===
又是一阵沉默；炉火又一次为这句妙语乐得直蹿。

===Original===
Sylvia Vernon arose suddenly and cried, “I would I had not come!”

===Chinese===
西尔维娅·弗农霍地站起身，喊道：「我但愿我不曾来！」

===Original===
Then said Sir John: “Nay, this is but a feeble grieving you have wakened. For, madam—you whom I loved once!—you are in the right. Our blood runs thinner than of yore; and we may no longer, I think, either sorrow or rejoice very deeply.”

===Chinese===
于是约翰爵士说：「不，你唤起的不过是一点微弱的愁绪。因为，夫人——你，我曾经爱过的那个人！——你说的不错。咱们的血比从前稀薄了；我想，咱们再也不能深切地悲，也不能深切地喜了。」

===Original===
“It is true,” she said; “but I must go; and, indeed, I would to God I had not come!”

===Chinese===
「不错，」她说；「可我该走了；说真的，只求上帝，我真不该来！」

===Original===
Sir John was silent; he bowed his head, in acquiescence perhaps, in meditation it may have been; but he stayed silent.

===Chinese===
约翰爵士不作声；他低下头，或许是默许，也可能是沉思；总之他一直沉默着。

===Original===
“Yet,” said she, “there is something here which I must keep no longer: for here are all the letters you ever writ me.”

===Chinese===
「不过，」她说，「有样东西我不能再留了：这里是你写给我的全部书信。」

===Original===
Whereupon she handed Sir John a little packet of very old and very faded papers. He turned them awkwardly in his hand once or twice; then stared at them; then at the lady.

===Chinese===
说着，她递给约翰爵士一小扎年深日久、字迹黯淡的信纸。他把那扎纸在手里笨拙地翻了两翻；继而盯着纸看；又盯着这位夫人看。

===Original===
“You have kept them—always?” he cried.

===Chinese===
「你留着它们——始终留着？」他喊道。

===Original===
“Yes,” she responded, wistfully; “but I must not be guilty of continuing such follies. It is a villainous example to my grandchildren,” Dame Sylvia told him, and smiled. “Farewell.”

===Chinese===
「是啊，」她怅然应道；「可我再不该把这等痴事继续下去了。给我的孙儿孙女们立个坏榜样可不行，」西尔维娅夫人对他说，随即笑了。「别了。」

===Original===
Sir John drew close to her and took her hands in his. He looked into her eyes for an instant, holding himself very erect—and it was a rare event when Sir John looked anyone squarely in the eyes—and he said, wonderingly, “How I loved you!”

===Chinese===
约翰爵士凑近她，把她的双手握在自己手里。有一瞬，他直挺挺地立着，直望进她的眼睛——约翰爵士肯正眼看人，本是稀罕事——他说，语气里满是惊叹：「我那时多么爱你！」

===Original===
“I know,” she murmured. Sylvia Vernon gazed up into his bloated old face with a proud tenderness that was half-regretful. A quavering came into her gentle voice. “And I thank you for your gift, my lover—O brave true lover, whose love I was not ever ashamed to own! Farewell, my dear; yet a little while, and I go to seek the boy and girl we know of.”

===Chinese===
「我知道，」她低声道。西尔维娅·弗农仰望着他那虚肿的老脸，目光里是一种带着一半怅憾的高傲温柔。她那温和的嗓音起了颤。「我谢谢你的馈赠，我的爱人——啊，勇敢真挚的爱人，你的爱，我从不忘却承认它而羞愧！别了，亲爱的；再过些时日，我就要去寻我们都知道的那对少年和姑娘了。」

===Original===
“I shall not be long, madam,” said Sir John. “Speak a kind word for me in Heaven; for I shall have sore need of it.”

===Chinese===
「我去不了太久，夫人，」约翰爵士说。「替我在天堂美言几句；因为我会很用得着的。」

===Original===
She had reached the door by this. “You are not sorry that I came?”

===Chinese===
这时她已走到门边。「你不难过我来过？」

===Original===
Sir John answered, very sadly: “There are many wrinkles now in your dear face, my lady; the great eyes are a little dimmed, and the sweet laughter is a little cracked; but I am not sorry to have seen you thus. For I have loved no woman truly save you alone; and I am not sorry. Farewell.” And for a moment he bowed his unreverend gray head over her shrivelled fingers.

===Chinese===
约翰爵士回答得极凄然：「你亲爱的脸上如今起了许多皱纹，夫人；那双大眼睛有些昏花了，那甜美的笑声也有些发哑了；可我见了这样的你，并不难过。因为我真心爱过的女人，惟你一人；我不难过。别了。」说着，他那不甚可敬的白头，在她枯瘦的手指上低垂了片刻。

===Original===
III

===Chinese===
III

===Original===
“This Pitch, as Ancient Writers do Report, doth Defile”

===Chinese===
「这沥青，正如古代作家所记，终要玷人」

===Original===
“Lord, Lord, how subject we old men are to the vice of lying!” chuckled Sir John, and leaned back rheumatically in his chair and mumbled over the jest.

===Chinese===
「天哪，天哪，我们老头子多么容易犯说谎这门罪过啊！」约翰爵士暗笑着，风湿僵硬地往椅背上一靠，把这句笑话又咕哝了一遍。

===Original===
“Yet it was not all a lie,” he confided, as if in perplexity, to the fire; “but what a coil over a youthful greensickness ’twixt a lad and a wench more than forty years syne!

===Chinese===
「不过那也不全是谎话，」他像是颇为困惑，向炉火吐露心曲；「可为了四十多年前一个小伙子同一个姑娘之间的一场青春痴症，闹出这么大动静！

===Original===
“I might have had money of her for the asking,” he presently went on; “yet I am glad I did not; which is a parlous sign and smacks of dotage.”

===Chinese===
「我原可以开口就问她要钱的，」过了一会儿他又说；「可幸而我没那么做；这是个不妙的征兆，透着老糊涂的气味。」

===Original===
He nodded very gravely over this new and alarming phase of his character.

===Chinese===
他对自己性子里这一崭新而骇人的侧面，极郑重地点了点头。

===Original===
“Were it not a quaint conceit, a merry tickle-brain of Fate,” he asked of the leaping flames, after a still longer pause, “that this mountain of malmsey were once a delicate stripling with apple cheeks and a clean breath, smelling of civet, and as mad for love, I warrant you, as any Amadis of them all? For, if a man were to speak truly, I did love her.

===Chinese===
「这岂不是一个古怪的念头，命运老人斟上的一杯上头烈酒么，」又停了更久之后，他问那蹿跳的火苗，「这一座马姆齐甜酒堆成的大山，当年竟曾是个面若苹果、气息清新的娇弱少年，浑身灵猫香的味道，而且——我担保——为爱情发疯，发得同他们随便哪个阿马迪斯一样？因为，人若要说真话，我那时确实爱她。

===Original===
“I had the special marks of the pestilence,” he assured a particularly incredulous—and obstinate-looking coal—a grim, black fellow that, lurking in a corner, scowled forbiddingly and seemed to defy both the flames and Sir John. “Not all the flagons and apples in the universe might have comforted me; I was wont to sigh like a leaky bellows; to weep like a wench that hath lost her grandam; to lard my speech with the fag-ends of ballads like a man milliner; and did, indeed, indite sonnets, canzonets, and whatnot of mine own elaboration.

===Chinese===
「我害的瘟疫，种种特有征候样样齐全，」他向一块特别不肯相信——而且一脸执拗——的煤块担保；那是个阴沉的黑家伙，蛰伏在角落里，满脸凶相，仿佛公然同火苗和约翰爵士两个为敌。「普天之下所有的酒和苹果加起来，也安慰不了我；我惯常像漏风的破风箱那样叹气；像死了祖母的小姑娘那样哭；像女帽铺里的男伙计那样，说话夹七夹八全是歌谣的尾巴；而且当真亲笔炮制过十四行诗、小坎佐纳，以及诸如此类的自家精心之作。

===Original===
“And Moll did carry them,” he continued; “plump brown-eyed Moll, that hath married Hodge the tanner, and reared her tannerkins, and died long since.”

===Chinese===
「而莫尔就负责传送，」他接着说；「那个褐色眼睛的丰腴的莫尔，她嫁了制革匠霍奇，拉扯大一窝小制革匠，也过世多年了。」

===Original===
But the coal remained incredulous, and the flames crackled merrily.

===Chinese===
可那块煤还是不信，火苗们快活地毕剥作响。

===Original===
“Lord, Lord, what did I not write?” said Sir John, drawing out a paper from the packet, and deciphering by the firelight the faded writing.

===Chinese===
「天哪，天哪，我还有什么没写过？」约翰爵士说着，从那扎纸里抽出一张，就着火光辨认那褪了色的字迹。

===Original===
Read Sir John:

===Chinese===
约翰爵士读道：

===Original===
“Have pity, Sylvia? Cringing at thy door

Entreats with dolorous cry and clamoring,

That mendicant who quits thee nevermore;

Now winter chills the world, and no birds sing

In any woods, yet as in wanton Spring

He follows thee; and never will have done,

Though nakedly he die, from following

Whither thou leadest.

“Canst thou look upon

His woes, and laugh to see a goddess’ son

Of wide dominion, and in strategy

“More strong than Jove, more wise than Solomon,

Inept to combat thy severity?

Have pity, Sylvia! And let Love be one

Among the folk that bear thee company.”

===Chinese===
「西尔维娅啊，垂怜吧？蜷缩在你门畔、

以哀音与喧呼苦苦哀恳的，

是那再不肯离你而去的乞儿；

如今寒冬冻彻了世界，任凭哪座林子

里都没有鸟儿歌唱，他却像在恣意的春天

一般追随你；而且永不甘休，

纵使赤条条死去，也绝不停止追随

你的脚步所向。

「你忍看他的

苦难，而笑着看一位女神之子——他辖土辽阔，论韬略

比朱庇特更强，比所罗门更智，

却无力抵挡你的严酷么？

西尔维娅啊，垂怜吧！也让爱神算得一个

陪在你身畔的人们中间的一个。」

===Original===
“Is it not the very puling speech of your true lover?” he chuckled; and the flames spluttered assent. “*Among the folk that bear thee company*,” he repeated, and afterward looked about him with a smack of gravity. “Faith, Adam Cupid hath forsworn my fellowship long since; he hath no score chalked up against him at the Boar’s Head Tavern; or, if he have, I doubt not the next street-beggar might discharge it.”

===Chinese===
「这不正是天下痴情种子哭腔哭调的口吻么？」他轻笑道；火苗噼啪有声，表示赞同。「*陪在你身畔的人们中间的一个*，」他重复了一遍，随后四下里张望，神色颇为庄重。「说真的，亚当·丘比特早就同我绝了交；他在野猪头酒店没有粉笔记的赊账；即便有，我看随便哪个街边的叫花子都替他还得起。」

===Original===
“And she hath commended me to her children as a very gallant gentleman and a true knight,” Sir John went on, reflectively. He cast his eyes toward the ceiling, and grinned at invisible deities. “Jove that sees all hath a goodly commodity of mirth; I doubt not his sides ache at times, as if they had conceived another wine-god.”

===Chinese===
「而她竟把我当作一位十足豪侠的君子、一位真正的骑士，荐给她的儿女们，」约翰爵士若有所思地说下去。他把眼睛望向天花板，冲着看不见的众神咧嘴一笑。「朱庇特什么都看得见，攒下了好一大批笑料；我毫不怀疑他的两肋时常发酸，像是肚里又孕育出一位酒神。」

===Original===
“Yet, by my honor,” he insisted to the fire; then added, apologetically—“if I had any, which, to speak plain, I have not—I am glad; it is a brave jest; and I did love her once.”

===Chinese===
「然而，凭我的名誉，」他向炉火坚称；随即又道歉似的添了一句——「如果我还有名誉的话，说句老实话，我是没有了——我很高兴；这是个妙的玩笑；而我当年确实爱过她。」

===Original===
Then the time-battered, bloat rogue picked out another paper, and read:

===Chinese===
于是那被时光捶打得臃肿的老无赖又抽出一张纸，念道：

===Original===
“My dear lady—That I am not with thee tonight is, indeed, no fault of mine, for Sir Thomas Mowbray hath need of me, he saith. Yet the service that I have rendered him thus far is but to cool my heels in his antechamber and dream of two great eyes and of that net of golden hair wherewith Lord Love hath lately snared my poor heart. For it comforts me—”

===Chinese===
「我亲爱的夫人——今夜我不能陪伴在你身边，实在非我之过，因为托马斯·莫布雷爵士有事要用我，他这样说。然而迄今为止，我为他效的劳，不过是陪着脚后跟在他的前厅里受冷，梦想着一双大大的眼睛，和那张金发的网——爱神大人新近正是用它网住了我可怜的心。因为这安慰了我——」

===Original===
And so on, and so on, the pen trailing most juvenal sugar, like a fly newly crept out of the honeypot. And ending with a posy, filched, I warrant you, from some ring.

===Chinese===
如此等等，如此等等，那支笔拖出一路少年人的蜜糖，活像刚从蜜罐里爬出来的苍蝇。末了还缀上一句铭诗，我担保，是从哪枚戒指上偷来的。

===Original===
“I remember when I did write her this,” he explained to the fire. “Lord, Lord, if the fire of grace were not quite out of me, now should I be moved. For I did write it; and it was sent with a sonnet, all of Hell, and Heaven, and your pagan gods, and other tricks of speech. It should be somewhere.”

===Chinese===
「我记得我写这封信的时候，」他向炉火解释道。「天哪，天哪，倘若我身上的恩典之火还没有全然熄灭，此刻我该感动了。因为那是我亲手写的；寄出时还附了一首十四行诗，满纸的地狱、天堂、异教的神祇，以及种种说话的花腔。它总该在哪一处。」

===Original===
He fumbled with uncertain fingers among the papers. “Ah, here it is,” he said at last, and he again began to read aloud.

===Chinese===
他用不听使唤的手指在纸扎里摸索。「啊，在这儿，」他终于说道，便又朗声念起来。

===Original===
Read Sir John:

===Chinese===
约翰爵士读道：

===Original===
“Cupid invaded Hell, and boldly drove

Before him all the hosts of Erebus,

Till he had conquered: and grim Cerberus

Sang madrigals, the Furies rhymed of love,

Old Charon sighed, and sonnets rang above

The gloomy Styx; and even as Tantalus

Was Proserpine discrowned in Tartarus,

And Cupid regnant in the place thereof.

“Thus Love is monarch throughout Hell today;

In Heaven we know his power was always great;

And Earth acclaimed Love’s mastery straightway

When Sylvia came to gladden Earth’s estate:—

Thus Hell and Heaven and Earth his rule obey,

And Sylvia’s heart alone is obdurate.”

===Chinese===
「丘比特兴兵侵入地狱，勇猛地把

厄瑞玻斯的大军一路驱赶，

直到大获全胜：狰狞的刻耳柏洛斯

唱起了情歌，复仇众女神把爱情押上韵脚，

老卡戎声声叹息，十四行诗在阴惨的

斯提克斯河上空回响；恰如坦塔罗斯

在塔耳塔洛斯失过冠冕，普洛塞庇娜

也被废去了后位，丘比特君临其上。

「于是爱情今日做了遍地狱的君王；

在天堂，我们知道他的权能素来宏大；

而尘世一见西尔维娅来为人间添彩，

便即刻拥戴了爱情的主宰：——

于是地狱、天堂与尘世齐奉他的律令，

惟独西尔维娅的心，独自顽梗。」

===Original===
“Well, well,” sighed Sir John, “it was a goodly rogue that writ it, though the verse runs but lamely! A goodly rogue!

===Chinese===
「好了，好了，」约翰爵士叹道，「写下这诗的虽说是个好小子，诗行却跛得可怜！一个好小子！

===Original===
“He might,” Sir John suggested, tentatively, “have lived cleanly, and forsworn sack; he might have been a gallant gentleman, and begotten grandchildren, and had a quiet nook at the ingleside to rest his old bones: but he is dead long since. He might have writ himself *armigero* in many a bill, or obligation, or quittance, or whatnot; he might have left something behind him save unpaid tavern bills; he might have heard cases, harried poachers, and quoted old saws; and slept in his own family chapel through sermons yet unwrit, beneath his presentment, done in stone, and a comforting bit of Latin: but he is dead long since.”

===Chinese===
「他原可以，」约翰爵士试探着说，「活得清清白白，戒了白葡萄酒；原可以做个体面君子，生下一群孙儿孙女，在炉膛边占个安静的角落安放老骨头：可他早已死透了。他原可以在许多契据、债券、清账上，端端正正写上*armigero*（拉丁文，「执盾之士」，即乡绅）；原可以在身后留下些什么，而不止一叠没付的酒账；原可以坐堂问案，缉治偷猎，念念老格言；原可以在自家家堂里，听着尚未出世的布道词酣睡，睡在他石头刻的座像底下，像前还缀着一行慰藉人心的拉丁文：可他早已死透了。」

===Original===
Sir John sat meditating for a while; it had grown quite dark in the room as he muttered to himself. He rose now, rather cumbrously and uncertainly, but with a fine rousing snort of indignation.

===Chinese===
约翰爵士坐着沉思了片刻；他自言自语间，屋里已经相当暗了。这时他站起身来，动作颇为笨重迟疑，鼻子里却愤然喷出一声响亮的气。

===Original===
“Zooks!” he said, “I prate like a death’s-head. A thing done hath an end, God have mercy on us all! And I will read no more of the rubbish.”

===Chinese===
「咄！」他说，「我唠叨得像个骷髅头。做了的事自有完结，上帝怜悯我们众人！这些破烂我也不再念了。」

===Original===
He cast the packet into the heart of the fire; the yellow papers curled at the edges, rustled a little, and blazed; he watched them burn to the last spark.

===Chinese===
他把那扎信丢进炉膛正中；黄纸边缘蜷曲起来，窸窣响了几声，随即窜起火苗；他看着它们烧到最后一星火花。

===Original===
“A cup of sack to purge the brain!” cried Sir John, and filled one to the brim. “And I will go sup with Doll Tearsheet.”

===Chinese===
「来一杯白葡萄酒，清一清脑子！」约翰爵士喊道，把酒杯斟得满满的。「然后我去同桃儿·贴席吃晚饭。」

## IV / IV

The Episode Called “Sweet Adelais” / 所谓「甜蜜的阿德莱丝」

===Original===
September 29, 1422

“Anoon her herte hath pitee of his wo,

And with that pitee, love com in also;

Thus is this quene in pleasaunce and in loye.”

===Chinese===
1422年9月29日

（中古英语，乔叟《特洛伊罗斯与克丽西达》）
“Anoon her herte hath pitee of his wo,
And with that pitee, love com in also;
Thus is this quene in pleasaunce and in loye.”
（意译）
「她的心随即怜悯他的悲苦，
而随着怜悯，爱也悄然潜入；
这位王后便这样沉浸在欢愉与喜悦之中。」

===Original===
Meanwhile had old Dome Sylvia returned contentedly to the helpmate whom she had accepted under compulsion, and who had made her a fair husband, as husbands go. It is duly recorded, indeed, on their shared tomb, that their forty years of married life were of continuous felicity, and set a pattern to all Norfolk. The more prosaic verbal tradition is that Lady Vernon retained Sir Robert well in hand by pointing out, at judicious intervals, that she had only herself to blame for having married such a selfish person in preference to a hero of the age and an ornament of the loftiest circles.

I find, on consultation of the Allonby records, that Sylvia Vernon died of a quinsy, in 1419, surviving Sir Robert by some three months. She had borne him four sons and four daughters: of these there remained at Winstead in 1422 only Sir Hugh Vernon, the oldest son, knighted by Henry V at Agincourt, where Vernon had fought with distinction, and Adelais Vernon, the youngest daughter, with whom the following has to do.

===Chinese===
与此同时，老夫人西尔维娅·弗农（Sylvia Vernon）已心满意足地回到她当初被迫接受的那位配偶身边——此人做她的丈夫还算像样，就照一般丈夫的标准。的确，两人合葬的墓碑上郑重铭刻着：他们四十年的婚姻生活美满连绵，为全诺福克（Norfolk）树立了楷模。口头流传的说法则平实得多：弗农夫人之所以把罗伯特爵士管得服服帖帖，靠的是不失时机地提醒他——她当初放着时代的英雄、最高贵圈子的翘楚不嫁，偏挑了这么一个自私自利的人，只能怪她自己。

我查考阿隆比（Allonby）家的记录发现，西尔维娅·弗农死于喉痈，时在1419年，比罗伯特爵士多活了约莫三个月。她为他生了四子四女：到1422年，仍留在温斯特德（Winstead）的，只剩长子休·弗农爵士（Sir Hugh Vernon）——他曾在阿金库尔（Agincourt）奋勇作战，由亨利五世册封为骑士——以及幼女阿德莱丝·弗农（Adelais Vernon），下文要讲的便是她的事。

### I / I

Gruntings at Aeaea / 埃埃亚岛上的哼哼声

===Original===
It was on a clear September day that the Marquis of Falmouth set out for France. John of Bedford had summoned him posthaste when Henry V was stricken at Senlis with what bid fair to prove a mortal distemper; for the marquis was Bedford’s comrade-in-arms, veteran of Shrewsbury, Agincourt and other martial disputations, and the Duke-Regent suspected that, to hold France in case of the King’s death, he would presently need all the help he could muster.

“And I, too, look for warm work,” the marquis conceded to Mistress Adelais Vernon, at parting. “But, God willing, my sweet, we shall be wed at Christmas for all that. The Channel is not very wide. At a pinch I might swim it, I think, to come to you.”

He kissed her and rode away with his men. Adelais stared after them, striving to picture her betrothed rivalling Leander in this fashion, and subsequently laughed. The marquis was a great lord and a brave captain, but long past his first youth; his actions went somewhat too deliberately ever to be roused to the high lunacies of the Sestian amorist. So Adelais laughed, but a moment later, recollecting the man’s cold desire of her, his iron fervors, Adelais shuddered.

===Chinese===
那是一个晴朗的九月天，法尔茅斯侯爵（Marquis of Falmouth）启程赴法。亨利五世在桑利斯（Senlis）染疾、眼看凶多吉少之际，贝德福德的约翰（John of Bedford）火速传召他前去；因为侯爵是贝德福德的袍泽战友，历经什鲁斯伯里、阿金库尔等诸般兵戎论战的老将，而这位摄政公爵心里明白：一旦国王不讳，要保住法兰西，他眼下就得把能搜罗的帮手尽数搜罗齐备。

「我也料到有一场热仗要打，」临别时，侯爵对阿德莱丝·弗农小姐承认道，「不过，天从人愿的话，我的甜心，尽管如此，咱们圣诞便可成婚。海峡不算太宽。事急了，我想我兴许还能游过海来见你。」

他吻了她，带着随从策马而去。阿德莱丝目送他们，努力想象她的未婚夫这般效法利安得（Leander）泅海的模样，随即笑出声来。侯爵是位大贵人、勇将，只是青春年少早成过往；他行事过于四平八稳，断不会被撩起塞斯托斯情种那般的高热狂痴。于是阿德莱丝笑了；可片刻之后，一想到此人对她的欲念何等冷硬、他的热忱何等铁铸，她又打了个寒噤。

===Original===
This was in the courtyard at Winstead. Roger Darke of Yaxham, the girl’s cousin, standing beside her, noted the gesture, and snarled.

“Think twice of it, Adelais,” said he.

Whereupon Mistress Vernon flushed like a peony. “I honor him,” she said, with some irrelevance, “and he loves me.”

Roger scoffed. “Love, love! O you piece of ice! You gray-stone saint! What do you know of love?” Master Darke caught both her hands in his. “Now, by Almighty God, our Saviour and Redeemer, Jesus Christ!” he said, between his teeth, his eyes flaming; “I, Roger Darke, have offered you undefiled love and you have mocked at it. Ha, Tears of Mary! how I love you! And you mean to marry this man for his title! Do you not believe that I love you, Adelais?” he whimpered.

===Chinese===
这是在温斯特德的庭院里。姑娘的表兄、亚克斯姆的罗杰·达克（Roger Darke of Yaxham）立在她身旁，把这一颤看在眼里，从鼻子里哼出一声冷笑。

「阿德莱丝，这事你可得三思。」他说。

弗农小姐闻言登时红得像朵芍药。「我敬重他，」她说，答得有些文不对题，「他爱我。」

罗杰嗤之以鼻。「爱，爱！哦，你这块冰！你这灰石的圣像！你知道什么叫爱？」达克先生一把攥住她的双手。「如今我以全能的天主、我们的救主、救赎者耶稣基督起誓！」他从牙缝里挤出这一句，两眼喷火；「我罗杰·达克献给你的是未曾沾污的爱，你却拿来嘲弄。哈，圣母的眼泪啊！我多么爱你！而你竟要为一个爵位嫁给那个人！阿德莱丝，你难道不信我爱你么？」他呜咽道。

===Original===
Gently she disengaged herself. This was of a pattern with Roger’s behavior any time during the past two years. “I suppose you do,” Adelais conceded, with the tiniest possible shrug. “Perhaps that is why I find you so insufferable.”

Afterward Mistress Vernon turned on her heel and left Master Darke. In his fluent invocation of Mahound and Termagaunt and other overseers of the damned he presently touched upon eloquence.

===Chinese===
她轻轻地抽出身来。两年来，罗杰的行径随便哪一回都是这个路数。「我信。」阿德莱丝让步道，耸了耸肩，耸得几乎看不出来。「也许正因如此，我才觉得你这么叫人受不了。」

随后弗农小姐转身便走，撇下了达克先生。他对着穆罕默德、特尔玛冈特及沉沦者们的诸位监管念念有词，辞气如流水般顺畅，不多时竟念出了几分雄辩的味道。

### II / II

Comes One with Moly / 携摩吕草者来

===Original===
Adelais came into the walled garden of Winstead, aflame now with autumnal scarlet and gold. She seated herself upon a semicircular marble bench, and laughed for no apparent reason, and contentedly waited what Dame Luck might send.

She was a comely maid, past argument or (as her lovers habitually complained) any adequate description. Circe, Colchian Medea, Viviane du Lac, were their favorite analogues; and what old romancers had fabled concerning these ladies they took to be the shadow of which Adelais Vernon was the substance. At times these rhapsodists might have supported their contention with a certain speciousness, such as was apparent today, for example, when against the garden’s hurly-burly of color, the prodigal blazes of scarlet and saffron and wine-yellow, the girl’s green gown glowed like an emerald, and her eyes, too, seemed emeralds, vivid, inscrutable, of a clear verdancy that was quite untinged with either blue or gray. Very black lashes shaded them. The long oval of her face (you might have objected), was of an absolute pallor, rarely quickening to a flush; but her petulant lips burned crimson, and her hair mimicked the dwindling radiance of the autumn sunlight and shamed it. All in all, the aspect of Adelais Vernon was, beyond any questioning, spiced with a sorcerous tang; say, the look of a young witch shrewd at love-potions, but ignorant of their flavor; yet before this the girl’s comeliness had stirred men’s hearts to madness, and the county boasted of it.

===Chinese===
阿德莱丝走进温斯特德的围墙花园，园中此刻秋深似火，猩红与金黄烧成一片。她在一张半圆形的大理石凳上坐下，无缘无故地笑了，然后心满意足地等着命运夫人（Dame Luck）会送来什么。

她是个标致姑娘，这一点无可置辩，也（如她的爱慕者们惯常抱怨的那样）非言语所能描摹。喀耳刻、科尔基斯的美狄亚、湖上的薇薇安，是他们最爱用的比拟；古时传奇作家关于这几位夫人编织的种种奇谈，在他们看来不过是影子，而阿德莱丝·弗农便是那实体。有时这些狂想家倒也能以几分似是而非的理由支撑其论点，譬如今天便是明证：在花园那一片闹嚷嚷的色彩、猩红与橙黄与酒金的泼天烈焰映衬下，姑娘的绿裙熠熠生辉宛如翡翠，她的眼睛也似翡翠，鲜活、深不可测，澄净的翠色里不掺一丝蓝或灰。乌黑的睫毛遮蔽着它们。她那长长的鹅蛋脸（您也许要挑剔了）白得全无血色，难得泛起一阵红潮；可她任性的双唇烧着绯红，她的头发则戏仿着秋阳将残未残的光焰，并使它自惭形秽。总而言之，阿德莱丝·弗农的容态，毫无疑问，是带有一股妖冶的意味的；譬如说，像一个年轻的巫女，调配媚药的手艺精明老练，却不知媚药自己的滋味；只是在此之前，姑娘的美貌早已把男人们的心搅得发狂，全郡也都引以为夸耀。

===Original===
Presently Adelais lifted her small imperious head, and then again she smiled, for out of the depths of the garden, with an embellishment of diverse trills and roulades, came a man’s voice that carolled blithely.

Sang the voice:

“Had you lived when earth was new

What had bards of old to do

Save to sing in praise of you?

“Had you lived in ancient days,

Adelais, sweet Adelais,

You had all the ancients’ praise—

You whose beauty would have won

Canticles of Solomon,

Had the sage Judean king

Gazed upon this goodliest thing

Earth of Heaven’s grace hath got.

“Had you gladdened Greece, were not

All the nymphs of Greece forgot?

“Had you trod Sicilian ways,

Adelais, sweet Adelais,

“You had pilfered all their praise:

Bion and Theocritus

Had transmitted unto us

Honeyed harmonies to tell

Of your beauty’s miracle,

Delicate, desirable,

And their singing skill were bent

You-ward tenderly—content,

While the world slipped by, to gaze

On the grace of you, and praise

Sweet Adelais.”

===Chinese===
不多时，阿德莱丝昂起她那小小的、骄矜的头，又笑了，因为花园深处传来一个男人引吭高歌的嗓音，缀满了各色颤音与花腔。

那嗓音唱道：

「你若生在天地初开之时，
古代的歌人还有什么事可做，
除了歌唱对你的赞颂？

「你若生在远古岁月，
阿德莱丝，甜蜜的阿德莱丝，
古人的赞美便尽归你一身——
你这美人，本该赢得
所罗门的雅歌，
只要那位睿智的犹地阿君王
曾一睹这天恩所赐
人间至美之物。

「你若曾照亮希腊，希腊的
众宁芙岂不尽数被人遗忘？

「你若曾行经西西里的路途，
阿德莱丝，甜蜜的阿德莱丝，

「你便把他们的一切赞美窃取：
比翁与忒奥克里托斯
将遗赠给我们
蜜一样甘美的乐音，
传颂你美的奇迹，
纤巧动人，令人渴慕，
他们的歌艺将殷殷
向你倾注——心满意足，
任世界悄然流逝，只管凝望
你的风姿，赞美
甜蜜的阿德莱丝。」

===Original===
Here the song ended, and a man, wheeling about the hedge, paused to regard her with adoring eyes. Adelais looked up at him, incredibly surprised by his coming.

This was the young Sieur d’Arnaye, Hugh Vernon’s prisoner, taken at Agincourt seven years earlier and held since then, by the King’s command, without ransom; for it was Henry’s policy to release none of the important French prisoners. Even on his deathbed he found time to admonish his brother, John of Bedford, that four of these—Charles d’Orleans and Jehan de Bourbon and Arthur de Rougemont and Fulke d’Arnaye—should never be set at liberty. “Lest,” as the King said, with a savor of prophecy, “more fire be kindled in one day than all your endeavors can quench in three.”

Presently the Sieur d’Arnaye sighed, rather ostentatiously; and Adelais laughed, and demanded the cause of his grief.

===Chinese===
歌声到此为止。一个男人绕过树篱，驻足用倾慕的目光望着她。阿德莱丝抬头看他，对他的到来惊讶得难以置信。

来者是年轻的达尔奈大人（Sieur d’Arnaye），休·弗农的俘虏，七年前在阿金库尔被俘，此后奉国王之命一直囚禁在此，不索赎金；因为亨利的方针是：法国的要俘一个也不放。即便到了临终的病榻上，他还抽空告诫弟弟贝德福德的约翰：其中有四个——查理·德·奥尔良、让·德·波旁、阿蒂尔·德·鲁热蒙和富尔克·达尔奈（Fulke d’Arnaye）——永不得开释。用国王的话说，那口气颇有几分预言的意味：「免得一日之间燃起的大火，叫你竭尽全力三天也扑灭不了。」

不多时，达尔奈大人叹了口气，叹得未免有些做作；阿德莱丝笑了，问他有何伤心事。

===Original===
“Mademoiselle,” he said—his English had but a trace of accent—“I am afflicted with a very grave malady.”

“What is the name of this malady?” said she.

“They call it love, mademoiselle.”

Adelais laughed yet again and doubted if the disease were incurable. But Fulke d’Arnaye seated himself beside her and demonstrated that, in his case, it might not ever be healed.

“For it is true,” he observed, “that the ancient Scythians, who lived before the moon was made, were wont to cure this distemper by bloodletting under the ears; but your brother, mademoiselle, denies me access to all knives. And the leech Aelian avers that it may be cured by the herb agnea; but your brother, mademoiselle, will not permit that I go into the fields in search of this herb. And in Greece—hé, mademoiselle, I might easily be healed of my malady in Greece! For in Greece is the rock, Leucata Petra, from which a lover may leap and be cured; and the well of the Cyziceni, from which a lover may drink and be cured; and the river Selemnus, in which a lover may bathe and be cured: but your brother will not permit that I go to Greece. You have a very cruel brother, mademoiselle; seven long years, no less, he has penned me here like a starling in a cage.”

===Chinese===
「小姐，」他说——他的英语只带一丝口音——「我正害着一场极沉重的病。」

「这病叫什么名字？」她说。

「人们管它叫爱，小姐。」

阿德莱丝又笑了，怀疑这病未必不可救治。可富尔克·达尔奈在她身旁坐下，条分缕析地论证：就他而言，此病永远无药可医。

「因为，千真万确，」他说，「远在月亮尚未造出的年代就住着的古斯基泰人，惯用在耳后放血的法子治这病；可是小姐，令兄不许我沾任何刀子。又有医生埃利安断言，此病可用埃格尼亚草医治；可小姐，令兄不许我到田野里去找这种草。再说希腊——嗳，小姐，到了希腊，我这病轻易就能治好！因为希腊有那块留卡塔岩（Leucata Petra），情人从上一跃而下便可痊愈；有库齐科斯人的井，情人饮了井水便可痊愈；还有塞姆诺斯河，情人在河里洗个澡便可痊愈：可令兄不许我去希腊。小姐，您这位兄长实在狠心；整整漫长的七年哪，他把我圈在这里，像笼中的椋鸟。」

===Original===
And Fulke d’Arnaye shook his head at her reproachfully.

Afterward he laughed. Always this Frenchman found something at which to laugh; Adelais could not remember in all the seven years a time when she had seen him downcast. But while his lips jested of his imprisonment, his eyes stared at her mirthlessly, like a dog at his master, and her gaze fell before the candor of the passion she saw in them.

“My lord,” said Adelais, “why will you not give your parole? Then you would be free to come and go as you elected.” A little she bent toward him, a covert red showing in her cheeks. “Tonight at Halvergate the Earl of Brudenel holds the feast of Saint Michael. Give your parole, my lord, and come with us. There will be in our company fair ladies who may perhaps heal your malady.”

===Chinese===
富尔克·达尔奈朝她摇头，一脸责备。

随后他笑了。这个法国人总能找到可笑的东西；七年之中，阿德莱丝不记得哪一回见他垂头丧气。可是，他的唇上虽拿囚禁打趣，他的眼睛却望着她，毫无笑意，像一条狗望着主人；在他眼中那坦白的至情面前，她的目光低垂了下去。

「大人，」阿德莱丝说，「您为何不肯具结假释？那样您便可自由来去了。」她微微朝他俯身，脸颊上透出一抹隐约的红。「今夜布鲁德内尔伯爵在哈尔弗盖特设圣米迦勒节的宴会。大人，具结假释，同我们一道去吧。我们一行里有美貌的夫人小姐，说不定能医治您的病呢。」

===Original===
But the Sieur d’Arnaye only laughed. “I cannot give my parole,” he said, “since I mean to escape for all your brother’s care.” Then he fell to pacing up and down before her. “Now, by Monseigneur Saint Médard and the Eagle that sheltered him!” he cried, in half-humorous self-mockery; “however thickly troubles rain upon me, I think that I shall never give up hoping!” After a pause, “Listen, mademoiselle,” he went on, more gravely, and gave a nervous gesture toward the east, “yonder is France, sacked, pillaged, ruinous, prostrate, naked to her enemy. But at Vincennes, men say, the butcher of Agincourt is dying. With him dies the English power in France. Can his son hold that dear realm? Are those tiny hands with which this child may not yet feed himself capable to wield a sceptre? Can he who is yet beholden to nurses for milk distribute sustenance to the law and justice of a nation? Hé, I think not, mademoiselle! France will have need of me shortly. Therefore, I cannot give my parole.”

“Then must my brother still lose his sleep, lord, for always your safekeeping is in his mind. Today at cockcrow he set out for the coast to examine those Frenchmen who landed yesterday.”

At this he wheeled about. “Frenchmen!”

“Only Norman fishermen, lord, whom the storm drove to seek shelter in England. But he feared they had come to rescue you.”

Fulke d’Arnaye shrugged his shoulders. “That was my thought, too,” he admitted, with a laugh. “Always I dream of escape, mademoiselle. Have a care of me, sweet enemy! I shall escape yet, it may be.”

===Chinese===
可达尔奈大人只是笑。「我不能具结假释，」他说，「因为尽管令兄看得紧，我还是打算逃走。」说罢他在她面前踱来踱去。「如今我以圣梅达尔大人及庇荫过他的那只鹰起誓！」他半带自嘲地喊道，「任凭灾祸如雨点般往我身上浇，我想我也永远不会放弃希望！」停了停，「听着，小姐，」他接着说，口气郑重了些，朝东边打了一个激动的手势，「那边是法兰西，被劫掠、被蹂躏，残破、俯伏，赤裸裸任凭敌人宰割。可人们说，在万塞讷，那阿金库尔的屠夫正在死去。他一死，英格兰在法兰西的权势也随之而亡。他的儿子守得住那片亲爱的国土么？那双连自己吃饭都还不利索的小手，握得住权杖么？一个还要靠奶妈喂奶的人，能给一国的法律与正义分发滋养么？嗳，我看不能，小姐！法兰西不久就要用得着我了。所以，我不能具结假释。」

「那么大人，家兄只怕还得继续睡不安稳，因为您的看守时刻挂在他心上。今天鸡叫头遍，他就动身往海岸去了，盘查昨天登陆的那几个法国人。」

听了这话他猛地转身。「法国人！」

「不过是几个诺曼渔夫，大人，被风暴吹到英格兰来避难的。可他生怕他们是来救您的。」

富尔克·达尔奈耸了耸肩。「我也是这么想的，」他笑着承认，「我做梦都在盘算逃走，小姐。看住我吧，甜蜜的仇敌！说不定我还是要逃的。」

===Original===
“But I will not have you escape,” said Adelais. She tossed her glittering little head. “Winstead would not be Winstead without you. Why, I was but a child, my lord, when you came. Have you forgotten, then, the lank, awkward child who used to stare at you so gravely?”

“Mademoiselle,” he returned, and now his voice trembled and still the hunger in his eyes grew more great, “I think that in all these years I have forgotten nothing—not even the most trivial happening, mademoiselle—wherein you had a part. You were a very beautiful child. Look you, I remember as if it were yesterday that you never wept when your good lady mother—whose soul may Christ have in his keeping!—was forced to punish you for some little misdeed. No, you never wept; but your eyes would grow wistful, and you would come to me here in the garden, and sit with me for a long time in silence. ‘Fulke,’ you would say, quite suddenly, ‘I love you better than my mother.’ And I told you that it was wrong to make such observations, did I not, mademoiselle? My faith, yes! but I may confess now that I liked it,” Fulke d’Arnaye ended, with a faint chuckle.

Adelais sat motionless. Certainly it was strange, she thought, how the sound of this man’s voice had power to move her. Certainly, too, this man was very foolish.

===Chinese===
「可我不要你逃走，」阿德莱丝说。她把她那亮闪闪的小脑袋一扬。「没有你，温斯特德就不成其为温斯特德了。唉，大人，你来的时候我还是个孩子呢。你忘了那个瘦长笨拙、老是一本正经盯着你看的孩子了么？」

「小姐，」他答道，此刻他的声音颤抖着，眼中的饥渴愈发深重，「我想这些年来我不曾忘掉任何事——凡是有您份儿的事，小姐，连最琐碎的也不曾忘。您那时是个非常好看的孩子。您瞧，我记得清清楚楚，仿佛就在昨天：您那位贤德的母亲——愿基督看顾她的灵魂！——为一点小过失不得不责罚您的时候，您从不哭。是的，您从来不哭；可您的眼睛会变得幽幽惹怜，您会到花园里来找我，陪我默默坐上许久。『富尔克，』您会冷不丁地说，『我爱你胜过爱我母亲。』而我告诉您，说这种话是不对的，我说过吧，小姐？我的天，说过！可如今我不妨招认：我心里是欢喜的。」富尔克·达尔奈说到这里，轻声低笑了一下。

阿德莱丝坐着不动。这实在奇怪，她想，这个人的嗓音竟有这般摇动她的力量。同样确实的是，这个男人也非常愚蠢。

===Original===
“And now the child is a woman—a woman who will presently be Marchioness of Falmouth. Look you, when I get free of my prison—and I shall get free, never fear, mademoiselle—I shall often think of that great lady. For only God can curb a man’s dreams, and God is compassionate. So I hope to dream nightly of a gracious lady whose hair is gold and whose eyes are colored like the summer sea and whose voice is clear and low and very wonderfully sweet. Nightly, I think, the vision of that dear enemy will hearten me to fight for France by day. In effect, mademoiselle, your traitor beauty will yet aid me to destroy your country.”

The Sieur d’Arnaye laughed, somewhat cheerlessly, as he lifted her hand to his lips.

And certainly also (she concluded her reflections) it was absurd how this man’s touch seemed an alarm to her pulses. Adelais drew away from him.

“No!” she said: “remember, lord, I, too, am not free.”

“Indeed, we tread on dangerous ground,” the Frenchman assented, with a sad little smile. “Pardon me, mademoiselle. Even were you free of your trothplight—even were I free of my prison, most beautiful lady, I have naught to offer you yonder in that fair land of France. They tell me that the owl and the wolf hunt undisturbed where Arnaye once stood. My château is carpeted with furze and roofed with God’s Heaven. That gives me a large estate—does it not?—but I may not reasonably ask a woman to share it. So I pray you pardon me for my nonsense, mademoiselle, and I pray that the Marchioness of Falmouth may be very happy.”

And with that he vanished into the autumn-fired recesses of the garden, singing, his head borne stiff. Oh, the brave man who esteemed misfortune so lightly! thought Adelais. She remembered that the Marquis of Falmouth rarely smiled; and once only—at a bull-baiting—had she heard him laugh. It needed bloodshed, then, to amuse him, Adelais deduced, with that self-certainty in logic which is proper to youth; and the girl shuddered.

But through the scarlet coppices of the garden, growing fainter and yet more faint, rang the singing of Fulke d’Arnaye.

===Chinese===
「如今孩子成了妇人——一位转眼就要做法尔茅斯侯爵夫人的妇人。您瞧，等我逃出我的牢狱——我会逃出去的，小姐，放心——我会时常想起那位贵妇人。因为只有天主能约束人的梦，而天主是慈悲的。所以我希望夜夜梦见一位优雅的夫人，她的头发是金的，眼睛染着夏海的颜色，嗓音清越、低柔、甜美得奇妙。我想，那位亲爱的仇敌的幻影会夜夜激励我，教我白天为法兰西而战。说到底，小姐，您这叛逆的美貌，终究要帮着我去毁灭您的国家。」

达尔奈大人举起她的手吻了吻，笑声里带着几分落寞。

而同样确实的是（她的沉思到此收束），这个人的触碰竟像警报一般惊动她的血脉，也实在荒唐。阿德莱丝从他身边挪开。

「不！」她说：「记住，大人，我也是有约束在身的人。」

「确实，我们踏在危险的地界上了，」法国人应道，带着一丝凄然的小小的微笑。「原谅我，小姐。即便您解除了婚约——即便我逃出了牢狱，至美的夫人，在那边美丽的法兰西国土上，我也一无所有可以奉献给您。人们告诉我，在阿尔奈（Arnaye）旧日的所在，猫头鹰和狼如今自在游猎。我的城堡以荆豆铺地，以上帝的天空作顶。这倒给了我一份偌大的产业——不是么？——可我没有道理请一位妇人同我分享。所以，小姐，请原谅我的胡言乱语，也请让我祈愿，愿法尔茅斯侯爵夫人幸福美满。」

说完他便唱着歌隐入花园深处的秋火之中，头颅昂得笔直。哦，这把厄难看得如此轻微的勇敢的人！阿德莱丝想。她记起法尔茅斯侯爵难得一笑；她只听他笑过一回——那是在纵犬噬牛的时候。原来，非得见血才能取悦他，阿德莱丝推论道，带着青春所特有的那种逻辑上的自信；于是姑娘打了个寒噤。

可是，富尔克·达尔奈的歌声仍穿过花园绯红的树丛传来，愈来愈轻，愈来愈远。

===Original===
Sang the Frenchman:

“Had you lived in Roman times

No Catullus in his rhymes

Had lamented Lesbia’s sparrow:

He had praised your forehead, narrow

As the newly-crescent moon,

White as apple-trees in June;

He had made some amorous tune

Of the laughing light Eros

Snared as Psyche-ward he goes

By your beauty—by your slim,

White, perfect beauty.

“After him

Horace, finding in your eyes

Horace limned in lustrous wise,

Would have made you melodies

Fittingly to hymn your praise,

Sweet Adelais.”

===Chinese===
那法国人唱道：

「你若生在罗马年代，
卡图卢斯便不会在诗行里
哀悼莱斯比亚的麻雀：
他要去赞美你的额头，窄窄的
像一弯新生的月牙，
白得像六月的苹果树；
他要做一支恋歌，
歌唱欢笑的光明厄洛斯——
他正往普赛克处去时，
被你的美擒住——被你纤秀、
皎白、无瑕的美擒住。

「在他之后，
贺拉斯若在你的眼中发现
贺拉斯自己，被描画得光彩焕然，
他定要为你谱下旋律，
恰如其分地咏赞
甜蜜的阿德莱丝。」

### III / III

Roger is Explicit / 罗杰把话挑明

===Original===
Into the midst of the Michaelmas festivities at Halvergate that night, burst a mud-splattered fellow in search of Sir Hugh Vernon. Roger Darke brought him to the knight. The fellow then related that he came from Simeon de Beck, the master of Castle Rising, with tidings that a strange boat, French-rigged, was hovering about the north coast. Let Sir Hugh have a care of his prisoner.

Vernon swore roundly. “I must look into this,” he said. “But what shall I do with Adelais?”

“Will you not trust her to me?” Roger asked. “If so, cousin, I will very gladly be her escort to Winstead. Let the girl dance her fill while she may, Hugh. She will have little heart for dancing after a month or so of Falmouth’s company.”

“That is true,” Vernon assented; “but the match is a good one, and she is bent upon it.”

So presently he rode with his men to the north coast. An hour later Roger Darke and Adelais set out for Winstead, in spite of all Lady Brudenel’s protestations that Mistress Vernon had best lie with her that night at Halvergate.

===Chinese===
当夜，哈尔弗盖特的米迦勒节盛筵正酣，一个浑身泥浆的汉子闯了进来，要找休·弗农爵士。罗杰·达克把他领到骑士跟前。那人禀报说，他奉里辛城堡（Castle Rising）城主西蒙·德·贝克（Simeon de Beck）之命而来，带来消息说，一条法国装束的陌生船只正在北岸一带游弋。请休爵士对他的俘虏多加小心。

弗农痛痛快快骂了一句粗话。「这事我得亲自去查，」他说，「可阿德莱丝怎么办？」

「把她托付给我不好么？」罗杰问，「果真如此，表哥，我十分乐意护送她回温斯特德。让丫头趁现在跳个够吧，休。跟法尔茅斯过上一个月，她就没多少心思跳舞了。」

「这话不假，」弗农表示同意，「不过这门亲事是门好亲，她自己一门心思要嫁。」

于是他随即带着手下人往北岸去了。一个钟头后，罗杰·达克和阿德莱丝便动身回温斯特德，任凭布鲁德内尔夫人力劝，说弗农小姐那晚最好在哈尔弗盖特她那里歇宿。

===Original===
It was a clear night of restless winds, neither warm nor chill, but fine September weather. About them the air was heavy with the damp odors of decaying leaves, for the road they followed was shut in by the autumn woods, that now arched the way with sere foliage, rustling and whirring and thinly complaining overhead, and now left it open to broad splashes of moonlight, where fallen leaves scuttled about in the wind vortices. Adelais, elate with dancing, chattered of this and that as her gray mare ambled homeward, but Roger was moody.

Past Upton the road branched in three directions; here Master Darke caught the gray mare’s bridle and turned both horses to the left.

“Why, of whatever are you thinking!” the girl derided him. “Roger, this is not the road to Winstead!”

He grinned evilly over his shoulder. “It is the road to Yaxham, Adelais, where my chaplain expects us.”

In a flash she saw it all as her eyes swept these desolate woods. “You will not dare!”

“Will I not?” said Roger. “Faith, for my part, I think you have mocked me for the last time, Adelais, since it is the wife’s duty, as Paul very justly says, to obey.”

===Chinese===
那是个风声不定、天宇澄澈的夜，不暖也不寒，正是九月的好天气。四周的空气饱含着腐叶潮湿的气息，因为他们走的那条路夹在秋林之间：林子时而以枯黄的枝叶在头顶搭成拱廊，飒飒猎猎地作响，发出细弱的怨诉；时而又让开道路，敞向大片大片泼洒的月光，落叶在风的旋涡里四散奔逃。阿德莱丝跳罢舞余兴未消，灰骒马缓步归家，她一路东拉西扯地说着话，罗杰却闷闷不乐。

过了厄普顿（Upton），道路分作三股；就在此地，达克先生一把攥住灰骒马的缰绳，把两匹马都拨向了左边。

「哎呀，你到底在想什么呀！」姑娘讥笑他，「罗杰，这不是去温斯特德的路！」

他回头狞笑。「这是去亚克斯姆的路，阿德莱丝，我的司铎正在那儿候着我们呢。」

目光扫过这片荒凉的树林，她霎时间全明白了。「谅你不敢！」

「我不敢？」罗杰说。「说真的，依我看，阿德莱丝，你嘲弄我这回是最后一回了，因为正如保罗说得再公道不过的：妻子的本分，就是顺服。」

===Original===
Swiftly she slipped from the mare. But he followed her. “Oh, infamy!” the girl cried. “You have planned this, you coward!”

“Yes, I planned it,” said Roger Darke. “Yet I take no great credit therefor, for it was simple enough. I had but to send a feigned message to your blockhead brother. Ha, yes, I planned it, Adelais, and I planned it well. But I deal honorably. Tomorrow you will be Mistress Darke, never fear.”

He grasped at her cloak as she shrank from him. The garment fell, leaving the girl momentarily free, her festival jewels shimmering in the moonlight, her bared shoulders glistening like silver. Darke, staring at her, giggled horribly. An instant later Adelais fell upon her knees.

“Sweet Christ, have pity upon Thy handmaiden! Do not forsake me, sweet Christ, in my extremity! Save me from this man!” she prayed, with entire faith.

“My lady wife,” said Darke, and his hot, wet hand sank heavily upon her shoulder, “you had best finish your prayer before my chaplain, I think, since by ordinary Holy Church is skilled to comfort the sorrowing.”

“A miracle, dear lord Christ!” the girl wailed. “O sweet Christ, a miracle!”

“Faith of God!” said Roger, in a flattish tone; “what was that?”

For faintly there came the sound of one singing.

===Chinese===
她敏捷地从骒马上溜下来。可他追了上来。「哦，无耻！」姑娘喊道，「这是你安排好的，你这懦夫！」

「不错，是我安排的，」罗杰·达克说，「不过我也不居什么大功，这事简单得很。只需给你那木头脑壳的哥哥捎个假信就够了。哈，是的，阿德莱丝，是我计划的，而且计划得周全。但我行事光明正大。明天你就是达克夫人了，放心。」

她往后退缩，他一把抓向她的斗篷。斗篷脱落，姑娘一时得了脱身之机，节庆的珠翠在月光下闪烁，裸露的双肩莹莹如银。达克盯着她，发出可怕的窃笑。转瞬间，阿德莱丝双膝跪倒在地。

「甜蜜的基督，怜悯你的使女！别在急难中抛弃我，甜蜜的基督！救我脱离这个人！」她怀着全然的虔信祈祷。

「我的夫人太太，」达克说，他那只湿热的手沉沉落上她的肩头，「依我看，你这祷文最好留到我的司铎面前去念完，因为照例说来，圣教会安慰伤心人是最拿手的。」

「一个神迹，亲爱的主基督！」姑娘哀哭道。「哦，甜蜜的基督，赏一个神迹吧！」

「天主的信实！」罗杰用平板的调子说，「那是什么声音？」

因为远处隐隐传来一个人的歌声。

===Original===
Sang the distant voice:

“Had your father’s household been

Guelfic-born or Ghibelline,

Beatrice were unknown

On her star-encompassed throne.

“For, had Dante viewed your grace,

Adelais, sweet Adelais,

You had reigned in Bice’s place—

Had for candles, Hyades,

Rastaben, and Betelguese—

And had heard Zachariel

Chant of you, and, chanting, tell

All the grace of you, and praise

Sweet Adelais.”

===Chinese===
那远处的嗓音唱道：

「你父亲的门第
若生为归尔甫或吉伯林，
贝雅特丽齐便籍籍无名，
登不上那群星环拱的宝座。

「因为，倘若但丁曾亲见你的风姿，
阿德莱丝，甜蜜的阿德莱丝，
你便登临碧切的宝座——
以毕宿星团、
拉斯塔本与参宿四为烛——
还要听撒迦列
为你吟唱，且边唱边诉说
你全部的丰姿，赞美
甜蜜的阿德莱丝。」

### IV / IV

Honor Brings a Padlock / 荣誉带来一把挂锁

===Original===
Adelais sprang to her feet. “A miracle!” she cried, her voice shaking. “Fulke, Fulke! to me, Fulke!”

Master Darke hurried her struggling toward his horse. Darke was muttering curses, for there was now a beat of hoofs in the road yonder that led to Winstead. “Fulke, Fulke!” the girl shrieked.

Then presently, as Roger put foot to stirrup, two horsemen wheeled about the bend in the road, and one of them leapt to the ground.

“Mademoiselle,” said Fulke d’Arnaye, “am I, indeed, so fortunate as to be of any service to you?”

“Ho!” cried Roger, with a gulp of relief, “it is only the French dancing-master taking French leave of poor cousin Hugh! Man, but you startled me!”

Now Adelais ran to the Frenchman, clinging to him the while that she told of Roger’s tricks. And d’Arnaye’s face set mask-like.

“Monsieur,” he said, when she had ended, “you have wronged a sweet and innocent lady. As God lives, you shall answer to me for this.”

===Chinese===
阿德莱丝一跃而起。「神迹！」她喊道，声音发颤。「富尔克，富尔克！到我这里来，富尔克！」

达克先生拖着她挣扎的身子，匆匆往他的马那边去。达克嘴里咒骂个不停，因为通往温斯特德的那条路上，此刻已传来得得的马蹄声。「富尔克，富尔克！」姑娘尖声叫喊。

随后，罗杰刚一脚踏进马镫，两骑人马便从路的转弯处绕了出来，其中一个翻身下马。

「小姐，」富尔克·达尔奈说，「我当真如此走运，能为您效劳么？」

「嗬！」罗杰喊道，如释重负地咽了口气，「原来只是那位法国跳舞师傅，撇下可怜的休表哥不辞而别跑啦！老兄，你可吓了我一跳！」

阿德莱丝当即奔向那法国人，一边紧攀着他，一边诉说罗杰的奸计。达尔奈的脸绷成了一副面具。

「先生，」等她讲完，他说，「您玷辱了一位甜美无辜的夫人。上帝在上，您须为此向我交代。」

===Original===
“Look you,” Roger pointed out, “this is none of your affair, Monsieur Jackanapes. You are bound for the coast, I take it. Very well—ka me, and I ka thee. Do you go your way in peace, and let us do the same.”

Fulke d’Arnaye put the girl aside and spoke rapidly in French to his companion. Then with mincing agility he stepped toward Master Darke.

Roger blustered. “You hop-toad! you jumping-jack!” said he, “what do you mean?”

“Chastisement!” said the Frenchman, and struck him in the face.

“Very well!” said Master Darke, strangely quiet. And with that they both drew.

The Frenchman laughed, high and shrill, as they closed, and afterward he began to pour forth a voluble flow of discourse. Battle was wine to the man.

===Chinese===
「你听着，」罗杰指出，「这与你毫不相干，猴儿先生。我看你这是要往海边去吧。很好——你帮我一把，我帮你一把。你安安稳稳走你的路，也让我们走我们的。」

富尔克·达尔奈把姑娘推到一旁，用法语对他的同伴急速说了几句。随后他迈着轻巧玲珑的步子，朝达克先生走去。

罗杰咋呼起来。「你这跳蛤蟆！你这提线木偶！」他说，「你想干什么？」

「惩戒！」法国人说，扬手击在他脸上。

「好得很！」达克先生说，出奇地平静。说罢两人双双拔剑。

交手时，法国人高声尖利地笑着，接着便滔滔不绝地说起话来。战斗于这个人，如同美酒。

===Original===
“Not since Agincourt, Master Coward—hé, no!—have I held sword in hand. It is a good sword, this—a sharp sword, is it not? Ah, the poor arm—but see, your blood is quite black-looking in this moonlight, and I had thought cowards yielded a paler blood than brave men possess. We live and learn, is it not? Observe, I play with you like a child—as I played with your tall King at Agincourt when I cut away the coronet from his helmet. I did not kill him—no!—but I wounded him, you conceive? Presently, I shall wound you, too. My compliments—you have grazed my hand.
