But I shall not kill you, because you are the kinsman of the fairest lady earth may boast, and I would not willingly shed the least drop of any blood that is partly hers. Ohé, no! Yet since I needs must do this ungallant thing—why, see, monsieur, how easy it is!”

Thereupon he cut Roger down at a blow and composedly set to wiping his sword on the grass. The Englishman lay like a log where he had fallen.

“Lord,” Adelais quavered, “lord, have you killed him?”

Fulke d’Arnaye sighed. “Hélas, no!” said he, “since I knew that you did not wish it. See, mademoiselle—I have but made a healthful and bloodletting small hole in him here. He will return himself to survive to it long time—Fie, but my English fails me, after these so many years—”

===Chinese===
「自阿金库尔以来，懦夫先生，我还未曾握过剑——嗳，没有！这是把好剑，这一把——是把快剑，可不是么？啊，可怜的胳膊——不过您瞧，在这月光下，您的血看上去乌黑乌黑的，我原以为懦夫的血要比勇士的血颜色淡些呢。活到老学到老，是不是？看好了，我逗您玩就像逗孩子——就像当年在阿金库尔，我逗你们那位高个子国王玩、削掉他头盔上的王冠那样。我没有杀他——没有！——可我伤了他，您明白么？待一会儿，我也要伤您了。承情——您擦着了我的手。可我不会杀您，因为您是大地所能夸耀的最美妇人的亲属，凡是沾着她一份的血，我一滴也不愿流。哦嗬，不！不过，既然我非得做这件有失风度的事——那您瞧着，先生，这有多容易！」

说罢他一剑将罗杰砍翻，从容不迫地在草地上擦起剑来。那英格兰人像段木头似的倒在原地。

「大人，」阿德莱丝声音发颤，「大人，您杀了他么？」

富尔克·达尔奈叹了口气。「唉，没有！」他说，「因为我知道您不愿如此。您瞧，小姐——我不过在他身上这儿开了个放血的、有益健康的小孔。他会自己缓过来，靠这小孔长命百岁的——呸，这么多年下来，我的英语要不济了——」

===Original===
D’Arnaye stood for a moment as if in thought, concluding his meditations with a grimace. After that he began again to speak in French to his companion. The debate seemed vital. The stranger gesticulated, pleaded, swore, implored, summoned all inventions between the starry spheres and the mud of Cocytus to judge of the affair; but Fulke d’Arnaye was resolute.

“Behold, mademoiselle,” he said, at length, “how my poor Olivier excites himself over a little matter. Olivier is my brother, most beautiful lady, but he speaks no English, so that I cannot present him to you. He came to rescue me, this poor Olivier, you conceive. Those Norman fishermen of whom you spoke today—but you English are blinded, I think, by the fogs of your cold island. Eight of the bravest gentlemen in France, mademoiselle, were those same fishermen, come to bribe my gaoler—the incorruptible Tompkins, no less. Hé, yes, they came to tell me that Henry of Monmouth, by the wrath of God King of France, is dead at Vincennes yonder, mademoiselle, and that France will soon be free of you English. France rises in her might—” His nostrils dilated, he seemed taller; then he shrugged. “And poor Olivier grieves that I may not strike a blow for her—grieves that I must go back to Winstead.”

===Chinese===
达尔奈站了片刻，似在沉思，末了以一个鬼脸结束了他的冥想。此后他又用法语同同伴说起话来。这场争论看去非同小可。那陌生人比手画脚，又是恳求，又是赌咒，又是哀告，把星空诸天与科奇土斯泥渊之间的一切神明都请来评理；可富尔克·达尔奈毫不动摇。

「您瞧，小姐，」他终于说道，「我这可怜的奥利维耶，为这么一点小事激动成什么样子。奥利维耶是我的兄弟，至美的夫人，可他不会说英语，我没法把他引见给您。他此来是要救我，这可怜的奥利维耶，您明白。就是您今天说起的那些诺曼渔夫——不过你们英格兰人，我看，是被你们那寒冷海岛上的雾迷了眼。法兰西最英勇的八位绅士，小姐，就是那几个渔夫，来收买我的狱卒——正是那位不受贿的汤普金斯。嗳，是的，他们来告诉我：蒙茅斯的亨利——蒙天主之怒的法兰西国王——已在那边的万塞讷死了，小姐，法兰西不久便要摆脱你们英格兰人了。法兰西正以她的伟力奋起——」他鼻翼张大，人仿佛也高大了些；随即他耸了耸肩。「可可怜的奥利维耶伤心的是，我不能为法兰西出一把力——伤心的是，我必须回温斯特德去。」

===Original===
D’Arnaye laughed as he caught the bridle of the gray mare and turned her so that Adelais might mount. But the girl, with a faint, wondering cry, drew away from him.

“You will go back! You have escaped, lord, and you will go back!”

“Why, look you,” said the Frenchman, “what else may I conceivably do? We are some miles from your home, most beautiful lady—can you ride those four long miles alone? in this night so dangerous? Can I leave you here alone in this so tall forest? Hé, surely not. I am desolated, mademoiselle, but I needs must burden you with my company homeward.”

Adelais drew a choking breath. He had fretted out seven years of captivity. Now he was free; and lest she be harmed or her name be smutched, however faintly, he would go back to his prison, jesting. “No, no!” she cried aloud.

===Chinese===
达尔奈笑着牵过灰骒马的缰绳，把它转过来好让阿德莱丝上马。可姑娘发出一声轻微的、惊疑的呼喊，从他身边退开了。

「您要回去！您已经逃出来了，大人，您却要回去！」

「哎呀，您瞧，」法国人说，「我还能做什么呢？我们离您家有好几英里，至美的夫人——这漫漫四英里路，您能独自骑回去么？在这样危险的夜里？我能把您独自留在这高深的林子里么？嗳，自然不能。我难过极了，小姐，可我只得委屈您，容忍我一路奉陪到家了。」

阿德莱丝抽了一口噎住的气。他已把七年囚禁熬磨了过去。如今他自由了；而为了她不受伤、她的名声不受哪怕一丝一毫的玷污，他竟要谈笑自若地回到他的牢狱里去。「不，不行！」她高声喊道。

===Original===
But he raised a deprecating hand. “You cannot go alone. Olivier here would go with you gladly. Not one of those brave gentlemen who await me at the coast yonder but would go with you very, very gladly, for they love France, these brave gentlemen, and they think that I can serve her better than most other men. That is very flattering, is it not? But all the world conspires to flatter me, mademoiselle. Your good brother, by example, prizes my company so highly that he would infallibly hang the gentleman who rode back with you. So, you conceive, I cannot avail myself of their services. But with me it is different, hein? Ah, yes, Sir Hugh will merely lock me up again and for the future guard me more vigilantly. Will you not mount, mademoiselle?”

His voice was quiet, and his smile never failed him. It was this steady smile which set her heart to aching. Adelais knew that no natural power could dissuade him; he would go back with her; but she knew how constantly he had hoped for liberty, with what fortitude he had awaited his chance of liberty; and that he should return to captivity, smiling, thrilled her to impotent, heart-shaking rage. It maddened her that he dared love her thus infinitely.

===Chinese===
可他举起一只手，拦住她的话头。「您不能独自走。这里的奥利维耶会乐意送您。在那边海岸上等候我的那些勇敢绅士，无论哪一位都会十分十分乐意与您同行的，因为他们爱法兰西，这些勇敢的绅士，而且他们认为我侍奉法兰西的本领比大多数人都强。这真是天大的抬举，是不是？只是全世界都在合谋奉承我罢了，小姐。譬如说，您那位好兄长就把我做伴看得如此金贵，谁陪着您骑马回去，他准会把那位绅士送上绞架。所以，您明白，我不能借重诸位的好意。至于我嘛，情形就不同了，嗯？啊，是的，休爵士不过再把我锁起来，往后看守得更严些罢了。您请上马吧，小姐？」

他的声音平静，他的微笑从未离开过他的脸。正是这纹丝不动的微笑，让她一阵阵心酸。阿德莱丝知道，没有任何世间的力量能劝转他；他会陪她回去；可她也知道，他曾怎样始终不渝地盼着自由，以怎样的坚忍等候着自由的时机；而他竟要微笑着重回囚笼——这使她陷入一种无可奈何、撼动心魄的狂怒。他竟敢这样无边无际地爱她，简直要把她逼疯了。

===Original===
“But, mademoiselle,” Fulke d’Arnaye went on, when she had mounted, “let us proceed, if it so please you, by way of Filby. For then we may ride a little distance with this rogue Olivier. I may not hope to see Olivier again in this life, you comprehend, and Olivier is, I think, the one person who loves me in all this great wide world. Me, I am not very popular, you conceive. But you do not object, mademoiselle?”

“No!” she said, in a stifled voice.

Afterward they rode on the way to Filby, leaving Roger Darke to regain at discretion the mastership of his faculties. The two Frenchmen as they went talked vehemently; and Adelais, following them, brooded on the powerful Marquis of Falmouth and the great lady she would shortly be; but her eyes strained after Fulke d’Arnaye.

Presently he fell a-singing; and still his singing praised her in a desirous song, yearning but very sweet, as they rode through the autumn woods; and his voice quickened her pulses as always it had the power to quicken them, and in her soul an interminable battling dragged on.

===Chinese===
「不过，小姐，」等她上了马，富尔克·达尔奈又说道，「要是您愿意，咱们不妨取道菲尔比。那样还能同这个小无赖奥利维耶同路走上一程。您明白，此生此世，我怕是再见不着奥利维耶了，而奥利维耶，我想，是这茫茫大千世界里唯一爱我的人。我么，我人缘不大好，您明白。您不反对吧，小姐？」

「不反对！」她说，嗓音噎住。

随后他们便取道菲尔比而行，任凭罗杰·达克自行慢慢收复他神智的管辖权。两个法国人一路走一路热烈地交谈；阿德莱丝随在后面，心里盘算着权势赫赫的法尔茅斯侯爵，盘算着她不久将成为的贵妇；可她的眼睛，却始终追着富尔克·达尔奈的背影。

不多时他唱起歌来；他们骑行穿过秋林，他的歌依旧把赞颂献给她，一支渴慕的歌，缠绵而极甜美；他的嗓音照旧撩动她的血脉，一如既往；而在她灵魂里，一场没完没了的交战仍在艰难地拖着。

===Original===
Sang Fulke d’Arnaye:

“Had you lived when earth was new

What had bards of old to do

Save to sing in praise of you?

“They had sung of you always,

Adelais, sweet Adelais,

As worthiest of all men’s praise;

Nor had undying melodies,

Wailed soft as love may sing of these

Dream-hallowed names—of Héloïse,

Ysoude, Salomê, Semelê,

Morgaine, Lucrece, Antiopê,

Brunhilda, Helen, Mélusine,

Penelope, and Magdalene:

—But you alone had all men’s praise,

Sweet Adelais”

===Chinese===
富尔克·达尔奈唱道：

「你若生在天地初开之时，
古代的歌人还有什么事可做，
除了歌唱对你的赞颂？

「他们便要世世代代歌颂你，
阿德莱丝，甜蜜的阿德莱丝，
把你当作万人赞颂之最；
那些不朽的旋律也将不复存在——
那些爱在咏叹这些梦中圣名时
低回如泣的乐章——爱洛漪丝、
伊索尔德、莎乐美、塞墨勒、
摩根、卢克蕾西亚、安提俄佩、
布伦希尔德、海伦、美露辛、
佩涅罗佩与抹大拉的马利亚：
——唯独你一人，独享万人之赞，
甜蜜的阿德莱丝」

### V / V

“Thalatta!” / 「塔拉塔！」

===Original===
When they had crossed the Bure, they had come into the open country—a great plain, gray in the moonlight, that descended, hillock by hillock, toward the shores of the North Sea. On the right the dimpling lustre of tumbling waters stretched to a dubious skyline, unbroken save for the sail of the French boat, moored near the ruins of the old Roman station, Garianonum, and showing white against the unresting sea, like a naked arm; to the left the lights of Filby flashed their unblinking, cordial radiance.

Here the brothers parted. Vainly Olivier wept and stormed before Fulke’s unwavering smile; the Sieur d’Arnaye was adamantean: and presently the younger man kissed him on both cheeks and rode slowly away toward the sea.

D’Arnaye stared after him. “Ah, the brave lad!” said Fulke d’Arnaye. “And yet how foolish! Look you, mademoiselle, that rogue is worth ten of me, and he does not even suspect it.”

His composure stung her to madness.

===Chinese===
他们渡过布尔河，便到了开阔的乡野——一片月光下灰蒙蒙的大平原，一个土丘接一个土丘地降下去，直降到北海之滨。右手边，翻涌的水面泛着酒窝似的粼粼波光，延展到影影绰绰的天际线，中间只有那艘法国船的一叶帆——船泊在古罗马戍站加里亚农努姆（Garianonum）的废墟近旁，在永不宁静的海面映衬下白得耀眼，像一条赤裸的手臂；左手边，菲尔比的灯火闪烁着毫不眨眼的热诚光辉。

兄弟俩就在此诀别。奥利维耶又哭又闹，在富尔克那纹丝不动的微笑面前终究徒劳；达尔奈大人坚如磐石：不多时，弟弟吻了他的两颊，缓缓策马向海边去了。

达尔奈目送他远去。「啊，勇敢的小伙子！」富尔克·达尔奈说，「可又多么傻！您瞧，小姐，那小无赖抵得上十个我，而他自己连想都没想过。」

他的镇定把她激得快要发狂。

===Original===
“Now, by the passion of our Lord and Saviour!” Adelais cried, wringing her hands in impotence; “I conjure you to hear me, Fulke! You must not do this thing. Oh, you are cruel, cruel! Listen, my lord,” she went on with more restraint, when she had reined up her horse by the side of his, “yonder in France the world lies at your feet. Our great King is dead. France rises now, and France needs a brave captain. You, you! it is you that she needs. She has sent for you, my lord, that mother France whom you love. And you will go back to sleep in the sun at Winstead when France has need of you. Oh, it is foul!”

But he shook his head. “France is very dear to me,” he said, “yet there are other men who can serve France. And there is no man save me who may tonight serve you, most beautiful lady.”

“You shame me!” she cried, in a gust of passion. “You shame my worthlessness with this mad honor of yours that drags you jesting to your death! For you must die a prisoner now, without any hope. You and Orleans and Bourbon are England’s only hold on France, and Bedford dare not let you go. Fetters, chains, dungeons, death, torture perhaps—that is what you must look for now. And you will no longer be held at Winstead, but in the strong Tower at London.”

“Hélas, you speak more truly than an oracle,” he gayly assented.

And hers was the ageless thought of women. “This man is rather foolish and peculiarly dear to me. What shall I do with him? and how much must I humor him in his foolishness?”

D’Arnaye stayed motionless: but still his eyes strained after Olivier.

Well, she would humor him. There was no alternative save that of perhaps never seeing Fulke again.

===Chinese===
「如今我以我主救主的受难起誓！」阿德莱丝喊道，无可奈何地绞着双手，「我恳求你听我说完，富尔克！这件事你做不得。哦，你狠心，狠心！听着，大人，」她把马缰到他的马旁，口气收敛了些，接着说，「那边的法兰西，整个世界都躺在你的脚边。我们伟大的国王死了。法兰西如今奋起了，法兰西需要一位勇将。是你，是你！她需要的是你。她派人来接你了，大人，你热爱的法兰西母亲。而法兰西正需要你的时候，你却要回温斯特德晒着太阳睡大觉。哦，这太卑劣了！」

可他摇了摇头。「法兰西于我是极亲爱的，」他说，「可还有别的人能侍奉法兰西。而今晚能侍奉您的，除我之外却没有第二个人，至美的夫人。」

「你让我羞愧！」她一阵激愤，喊道，「你用你这疯狂的荣誉，羞煞我这不值一文的人——这荣誉拖着你谈笑风生地去送死！因为如今你必须作为囚徒死去了，毫无指望。你、奥尔良和波旁，是英格兰钳制法兰西的唯一凭借，贝德福德不敢放你。镣铐、锁链、地牢、死亡，也许还有酷刑——这就是你如今所能指望的一切。而且你不会再被关在温斯特德，而是关进伦敦那座坚固的高塔。」

「唉，您说得比神谕还准。」他快活地表示同意。

而她怀有的，是女人那千古不易的心思。「这个男人相当愚蠢，又对我格外亲爱。我该拿他怎么办？他的愚蠢，我又该迁就几分？」

达尔奈一动不动：可他的眼睛仍追着奥利维耶远去的方向。

也罢，她便迁就他。除此之外别无出路，除非从此再见不着富尔克。

===Original===
Adelais laid her hand upon his arm. “You love me. God knows, I am not worthy of it, but you love me. Ever since I was a child you have loved me—always, always it was you who indulged me, shielded me, protected me with this fond constancy that I have not merited. Very well,”—she paused, for a single heartbeat—“go! and take me with you.”

The hand he raised shook as though palsied. “O most beautiful!” the Frenchman cried, in an extreme of adoration; “you would do that! You would do that in pity to save me—unworthy me! And it is I whom you call brave—me, who annoy you with my woes so petty!” Fulke d’Arnaye slipped from his horse, and presently stood beside the gray mare, holding a small, slim hand in his. “I thank you,” he said, simply. “You know that it is impossible. But yes, I have loved you these long years. And now—Ah, my heart shakes, my words tumble, I cannot speak! You know that I may not—may not let you do this thing. Why, but even if, of your prodigal graciousness, mademoiselle, you were so foolish as to waste a little liking upon my so many demerits—” He gave a hopeless gesture. “Why, there is always our brave marquis to be considered, who will so soon make you a powerful, rich lady. And I?—I have nothing.”

===Chinese===
阿德莱丝把手放在他臂上。「你爱我。天知道，我配不上，可你爱我。打我还是孩子的时候起，你就一直爱我——向来，向来是你娇惯我、庇护我、以这份我不配领受的痴心护持我。好吧，」——她停了停，只一心跳的工夫——「走吧！把我带上。」

他抬起的那只手抖得像患了麻痹。「哦，至美的人！」法国人喊道，倾慕到了极处；「您竟肯这样！您竟肯出于怜悯来救我——救我这不配的人！而您管我叫勇敢的人——我，拿我这一肚子鸡毛蒜皮的愁苦来烦扰您的人！」富尔克·达尔奈翻身下马，不多时便立在灰骒马旁，把一只纤秀的小手握在自己手里。「谢谢您，」他简单地说。「您知道这是不可能的。是的，我爱了您这许多年。而如今——啊，我的心在颤，我的话乱作一团，我说不出来了！您知道我不能——不能让您做这样的事。哎呀，即便小姐您慷慨恩慈到这般地步，竟痴愚得在我这一身数不清的短处上浪费几分喜欢——」他做了个绝望的手势。「您瞧，总还得顾着我们那位勇敢的侯爵呢，他不久便要让您成为有财有势的贵妇了。而我呢？——我一无所有。」

===Original===
But Adelais had rested either hand upon a stalwart shoulder, bending down to him till her hair brushed his. Yes, this man was peculiarly dear to her: she could not bear to have him murdered when in equity he deserved only to have his jaws boxed for his toplofty nonsense about her; and, after all, she did not much mind humoring him in his foolishness.

“Do you not understand?” she whispered. “Ah, my paladin, do you think I speak in pity? I wished to be a great lady—yes. Yet always, I think, I loved you, Fulke, but until tonight I had believed that love was only the man’s folly, the woman’s diversion. See, here is Falmouth’s ring.” She drew it from her finger, and flung it awkwardly, as every woman throws. Through the moonlight it fell glistening. “Yes, I hungered for Falmouth’s power, but you have shown me that which is above any temporal power. Ever I must crave the highest, Fulke—Ah, fair sweet friend, do not deny me!” Adelais cried, piteously. “Take me with you, Fulke! I will ride with you to the wars, my lord, as your page; I will be your wife, your slave, your scullion. I will do anything save leave you. Lord, it is not the maid’s part to plead thus!”

Fulke d’Arnaye drew her warm, yielding body toward him and stood in silence. Then he raised his eyes to heaven. “Dear Lord God,” he cried, in a great voice, “I entreat of Thee that if through my fault this woman ever know regret or sorrow I be cast into the nethermost pit of Hell for all eternity!” Afterward he kissed her.

And presently Adelais lifted her head, with a mocking little laugh. “Sorrow!” she echoed. “I think there is no sorrow in all the world. Mount, my lord, mount! See where brother Olivier waits for us yonder.”

===Chinese===
可阿德莱丝已把两只手都搭上一副坚实的肩膀，向他俯下身去，直到她的头发拂着他的头发。是的，这个男人于她格外亲爱：他满口关于她的高调浑话，按公道论罪不至死，只该吃一记耳光，她不能眼睁睁看他送命；再说，迁就一下他的愚蠢，她到底也不怎么介意。

「你还不明白么？」她低语道。「啊，我的游侠骑士，你以为我说话是出于怜悯么？我想做贵妇人——是的。可我一直，我想，是爱你的，富尔克，只是直到今夜之前，我一直以为爱情不过是男人的痴愚、女人的消遣。瞧，这是法尔茅斯的戒指。」她把它从手指上褪下，笨拙地掷出去——女人掷东西都是那样笨拙。戒指闪着光，穿过月色落了下去。「是的，我曾渴慕法尔茅斯的权势，可你让我看见了凌驾于一切尘世权势之上的东西。我永远渴求至高之物，富尔克——啊，美好甜蜜的朋友，别拒绝我！」阿德莱丝哀婉地喊道。「带我走吧，富尔克！我要随你出征，大人，做你的扈从；我要做你的妻子、你的奴婢、你的厨下粗使丫头。什么都行，只要不离开你。大人，女儿家这样哀求，本不是分内的事啊！」

富尔克·达尔奈把她温暖顺从的身躯揽向自己，默默立着。随后他举目向天。「亲爱的主上帝，」他朗声喊道，「我恳求您：倘若因我的过错，这女人他日得知悔恨或悲伤，就把我掷入地狱最深处的深渊，直到永永远远！」之后，他吻了她。

不多时，阿德莱丝抬起头来，带着一丝嘲弄的轻笑。「悲伤！」她应声道，「我看这世上根本就没有悲伤这回事。上马吧，大人，上马！瞧，奥利维耶兄弟正在那边等我们呢。」

## V / V

The Episode Called in Necessity’s Mortar / 所谓必要之臼

===Original===
June 5, 1455—August 4, 1462

“Fortune fuz par clercs jadis nominée,
Qui toi, François, crie et nomme meurtrière.”

===Chinese===
1455年6月5日——1462年8月4日

（古法语）
“Fortune fuz par clercs jadis nominée,
Qui toi, François, crie et nomme meurtrière.”
（意译）
「命运女神昔日曾被学子们如此命名；
如今她向你，弗朗索瓦，高声呼号，指你是凶手。」

===Original===
So it came about that Adelais went into France with the great-grandson of Tiburce d’Arnaye: and Fulke, they say, made her a very fair husband. But he had not, of course, much time for lovemaking.

For in France there was sterner work awaiting Fulke d’Arnaye, and he set about it: through seven dreary years he and Rougemont and Dunois managed, somehow, to bolster up the cause of the fat-witted King of Bourges (as the English then called him), who afterward became King Charles VII of France. But in the February of 1429—four days before the Maid of Domremy set forth from her voice-haunted Bois Chenu to bring about a certain coronation in Rheims Church and in Rouen Square a flamy martyrdom—four days before the coming of the good Lorrainer, Fulke d’Arnaye was slain at Rouvray-en-Beausse in that encounter between the French and the English which history has commemorated as the Battle of the Herrings.

===Chinese===
于是，事情便这样成了：阿德莱丝随蒂比尔丝·达尔奈的曾孙去了法兰西；而且据说，富尔克（Fulke）做她的丈夫还算像样——就照一般丈夫的标准。不过自然，他没有多少工夫谈情说爱。

因为在法兰西，有更严酷的功业等着富尔克·达尔奈（Fulke d'Arnaye），他便着手做去：整整七个黯淡的年头里，他同鲁热蒙（Rougemont）与迪努瓦（Dunois）勉力支撑着那位蠢钝的「布尔日国王」——当年英国人这样称呼他——的大业；此人后来成了法兰西国王查理七世。然而在1429年2月——早于栋雷米的少女（the Maid of Domremy）从她那萦回着人声的舍努林（Bois Chenu）启程四天，去兰斯大教堂里促成一场加冕、在鲁昂广场上促成一场烈焰殉道——早于那位善良的洛林姑娘驾临四天，富尔克·达尔奈已战死在博斯的鲁夫赖（Rouvray-en-Beausse），死于英法两军的那场遭遇战；史册纪念它，名之曰「鲱鱼之战」（the Battle of the Herrings）。

===Original===
Adelais was wooed by, and betrothed to, the powerful old Comte de Vaudremont, but died just before the date set for this second marriage, in October, 1429. She left two sons: Noël, born in 1425, and Raymond, born in 1426, who were reared by their uncle, Olivier d’Arnaye. It was said of them that Noël was the handsomest man of his times, and Raymond the most shrewd, concerning that you will judge hereafter. Both of these d’Arnayes, on reaching manhood, were identified with the Dauphin’s party in the unending squabbles between Charles VII and the future Louis XI.

Now you may learn how Noël d’Arnaye came to be immortalized by a legacy of two hundred and twenty blows from an osierwhip—since (as the testator piously affirms), “*chastoy est une belle aulmosne.*”

===Chinese===
阿德莱丝被权势煊赫的沃德雷蒙老伯爵（Comte de Vaudremont）求婚并订了亲，却在第二度婚期将至之前溘然长逝，时在1429年10月。她留下两个儿子：诺埃尔（Noël），生于1425年；雷蒙（Raymond），生于1426年——由他们的叔父奥利维耶·达尔奈（Olivier d'Arnaye）抚育成人。据人们说，诺埃尔是他那个时代最美的男子，雷蒙则是最精明的人——此话如何，且待诸位后文自行评判。这两位达尔奈家的男儿一到成年，便都归附了王储一党，投身于查理七世与未来的路易十一之间无休无止的龃龉倾轧。

如今诸位可以领教，诺埃尔·达尔奈（Noël d'Arnaye）如何因一笔遗产而不朽——遗产是柳条鞭抽打的二百二十下：因为（正如立遗嘱人虔敬地申言），「chastoy est une belle aulmosne」（惩戒是一桩美好的布施）。

### I / I

“Bon Bec de Paris” / 「巴黎巧嘴」

===Original===
There went about the Rue Saint Jacques a notable shaking of heads on the day that Catherine de Vaucelles was betrothed to François de Montcorbier.

“Holy Virgin!” said the Rue Saint Jacques; “the girl is a fool. Why has she not taken Noël d’Arnaye—Noël the Handsome? I grant you Noël is an ass, but, then, look you, he is of the nobility. He has the Dauphin’s favor. Noël will be a great man when our exiled Dauphin comes back from Geneppe to be King of France. Then, too, she might have had Philippe Sermaise. Sermaise is a priest, of course, and one may not marry a priest, but Sermaise has money, and Sermaise is mad for love of her. She might have done worse. But François! Ho, death of my life, what is François? Perhaps—he, he!—perhaps Ysabeau de Montigny might inform us, you say? Doubtless Ysabeau knows more of him than she would care to confess, but I measure the lad by other standards. François is inoffensive enough, I dare assert, but what does Catherine see in him? He is a scholar?—well, the College of Navarre has furnished food for the gallows before this. A poet?—rhyming will not fill the pot. Rhymes are a thin diet for two lusty young folk like these. And who knows if Guillaume de Villon, his foster-father, has one *sou* to rub against another? He is canon at Saint Benoit-le-Bétourné yonder, but canons are not Midases. The girl will have a hard life of it, neighbor, a hard life, I tell you, if—but, yes!—if Ysabeau de Montigny does not knife her some day. Oh, beyond doubt, Catherine has played the fool.”

===Chinese===
卡特琳·德·沃塞勒（Catherine de Vaucelles）与弗朗索瓦·德·蒙科尔比耶（François de Montcorbier）订婚那天，圣雅克街（Rue Saint Jacques）上下起了一场可观的大摇头。

「圣处女呀！」圣雅克街说道；「这姑娘是个傻子。她怎么不嫁给诺埃尔·达尔奈——美男子诺埃尔？我承认诺埃尔是头蠢驴，可是，你瞧，他到底是贵族出身。他蒙王储的恩宠。等我们流亡的王储从热纳普（Geneppe）回来做了法兰西国王，诺埃尔便是大人物了。再者，她原可以得到菲利普·塞尔莫瓦斯（Philippe Sermaise）。塞尔莫瓦斯当然是教士，教士嫁不得，可塞尔莫瓦斯有钱，而且塞尔莫瓦斯为她爱得发疯。她原可以嫁得更糟。可偏偏是弗朗索瓦！嗬，我的命呀，弗朗索瓦算个什么东西？兴许——嘻，嘻！——兴许伊莎博·德·蒙蒂尼（Ysabeau de Montigny）肯赐教，你说是不是？伊莎博对他的底细，无疑比她乐意招认的知道得多，可我衡量这小子另有尺子。弗朗索瓦本人倒无伤大雅，我敢说，可卡特琳看中他哪一点呢？他是个学子？——得了吧，纳瓦尔学院（College of Navarre）从前也不是没给绞架供过货。是个诗人？——押韵可填不饱锅。对他们这样两个血气方刚的年轻人，韵是太稀薄的口粮。再说，谁知道他养父纪尧姆·德·维永（Guillaume de Villon）兜里有没有一个铜子儿（sou）好跟另一个碰一碰？他是那边圣伯努瓦-勒贝图尔内（Saint Benoit-le-Bétourné）的议事司铎，可司铎又不是弥达斯。这姑娘往后有苦日子呢，街坊，苦日子，我告诉你，要是——哦，可不！——要是伊莎博·德·蒙蒂尼哪天不给她一刀的话。哦，毫无疑问，卡特琳干了件天大的蠢事。」

===Original===
Thus far the Rue Saint Jacques.

This was on the day of the *Fête-Dieu*. It was on this day that Noël d’Arnaye blasphemed for a matter of a half-hour and then went to the Crowned Ox, where he drank himself into a contented insensibility; that Ysabeau de Montigny, having wept a little, sent for Gilles Raguyer, a priest and aforetime a rival of François de Montcorbier for her favors; and that Philippe Sermaise grinned and said nothing. But afterward Sermaise gnawed at his under lip like a madman as he went about seeking for François de Montcorbier.

===Chinese===
这一席话，便是圣雅克街的全部高见。

这一天是圣体节（Fête-Dieu）。正是在这一天，诺埃尔·达尔奈亵渎神明达半个钟头之久，随后进了加冕牛酒店（the Crowned Ox），喝到心满意足、不省人事；伊莎博·德·蒙蒂尼洒了几滴泪，便差人去请吉尔·拉居耶（Gilles Raguyer）——一个神父，从前也曾与弗朗索瓦·德·蒙科尔比耶争夺过她的芳心；而菲利普·塞尔莫瓦斯咧嘴笑笑，一言不发。可后来，塞尔莫瓦斯四处寻访弗朗索瓦·德·蒙科尔比耶时，像疯子一般咬噬着自己的下唇。

### II / II

“Deux estions, et n’avions qu’ung Cueur” / 「我们本是两个人，却只共一颗心」

===Original===
It verged upon nine in the evening—a late hour in those days—when François climbed the wall of Jehan de Vaucelles’ garden.

A wall!—and what is a wall to your true lover? What bones, pray, did the Sieur Pyramus, that ill-starred Babylonish knight, make of a wall? did not his protestations slip through a chink, mocking at implacable granite and more implacable fathers? Most assuredly they did; and Pyramus was a pattern to all lovers. Thus ran the meditations of Master François as he leapt down into the garden.

===Chinese===
晚间将近九点——在那个年月已是很晚的时辰——弗朗索瓦翻上了让·德·沃塞勒（Jehan de Vaucelles）家的花园墙头。

一堵墙！——在一颗真正的情人心里，墙算得了什么？请问，那位倒霉的巴比伦骑士皮拉摩斯大人（Sieur Pyramus），几曾被一堵墙难倒过？他的山盟海誓不也曾从墙缝里溜过去，嘲笑着铁面无情的花岗岩和更铁面无情的父亲们？千真万确，正是如此；而皮拉摩斯是一切情人的楷模。弗朗索瓦大师跳进花园时，心里盘旋的便是这般高见。

===Original===
He had not, you must understand, seen Catherine for three hours. Three hours! three eternities rather, and each one of them spent in Malebolge. Coming to a patch of moonlight, François paused there and cut an agile caper, as he thought of that approaching time when he might see Catherine every day.

“Madame François de Montcorbier,” he said, tasting each syllable with gusto. “Catherine de Montcorbier. Was there ever a sweeter juxtaposition of sounds? It is a name for an angel. And an angel shall bear it—eh, yes, an angel, no less. O saints in Paradise, envy me! Envy me,” he cried, with a heroical gesture toward the stars, “for François would change places with none of you.”

===Chinese===
您得知道，他已有三个钟头没见着卡特琳了。三个钟头！不如说是三度永恒，而且每一度都是在但丁地狱的恶囊（Malebolge）里熬过的。走到一片月光里，弗朗索瓦停住脚，灵巧地打了个雀跃——他想到了那个日渐临近的日子，到那时他便能天天看见卡特琳了。

「弗朗索瓦·德·蒙科尔比耶夫人，」他说，有滋有味地品咂着每一个音节。「卡特琳·德·蒙科尔比耶。世上可有过比这更悦耳的音节毗邻么？这是给天使起的名字。而顶着这名字的，也必是一位天使——嗯，是的，一位天使，如假包换。天堂里的众圣徒啊，羡慕我吧！羡慕我吧，」他朝繁星打出一个豪迈的手势，喊道，「因为弗朗索瓦同你们哪一位换位置都不干。」

===Original===
He crept through ordered rows of chestnuts and acacias to a window wherein burned a dim light. He unslung a lute from his shoulder and began to sing, secure in the knowledge that deaf old Jehan de Vaucelles was not likely to be disturbed by sound of any nature till that time when it should please high God that the last trump be noised about the tumbling heavens.

It was good to breathe the mingled odor of roses and mignonette that was thick about him. It was good to sing to her a wailing song of unrequited love and know that she loved him. François dallied with his bliss, parodied his bliss, and—as he complacently reflected—lamented in the moonlight with as tuneful a dolor as Messire Orpheus may have evinced when he carolled in Hades.

Sang François:

===Chinese===
他蹑手蹑脚穿过成行齐整的栗树与洋槐，来到一扇透出昏黄灯光的窗下。他从肩上解下一张鲁特琴，开口唱了起来，心里笃定得很：耳背的老让·德·沃塞勒断不至于被任何性质的声响搅扰，除非到了高上帝高兴让末日号角在崩塌的诸天之间吹响的那个时辰。

呼吸着他四周浓得化不开的玫瑰与木樨草的交叠香气，是桩美事。向她唱一支单相思的哀歌、而明知她爱着他，也是桩美事。弗朗索瓦同他的幸福厮混，把他的幸福打趣，并且——正如他自己得意地反省的那样——在月光里哀吟得声调凄婉，不亚于俄耳甫斯大人（Messire Orpheus）当年在地府放歌时展露的风采。

弗朗索瓦唱道：

===Original===
“O Beauty of her, whereby I am undone!
O Grace of her, that hath no grace for me!
O Love of her, the bit that guides me on
To sorrow and to grievous misery!
O felon Charms, my poor heart’s enemy!
O furtive murderous Pride! O pitiless, great
Cold Eyes of her! have done with cruelty!
Have pity upon me ere it be too late!

“Happier for me if elsewhere I had gone
For pity—ah, far happier for me,
Since never of her may any grace be won,
And lest dishonor slay me, I must flee.
‘Haro!’ I cry, (and cry how uselessly!)
‘Haro!’ I cry to folk of all estate,
For I must die unless it chance that she
Have pity upon me ere it be too late.

“*M’amye*, that day in whose disastrous sun
Your beauty’s flower must fade and wane and be
No longer beautiful, draws near—whereon
I will nor plead nor mock;—not I, for we
Shall both be old and vigorless! *M’amye*,
Drink deep of love, drink deep, nor hesitate
Until the spring run dry, but speedily
Have pity upon me—ere it be too late!

“Lord Love, that all love’s lordship hast in fee,
Lighten, ah, lighten thy displeasure’s weight,
For all true hearts should, of Christ’s charity,
Have pity upon me ere it be too late.”

===Chinese===
「哦，她的美，我因它而倾覆！
哦，她的优雅，于我却不肯施舍半分！
哦，她的爱，是引导我的衔铁，
引我走向悲伤，走向凄惨的苦境！
哦，奸邪的魅惑，我这可怜心的仇敌！
哦，鬼祟而凶残的骄傲！哦，无情的、
冰冷的她的眼睛！收起残忍吧！
趁为时未晚，垂怜于我吧！

「倘若我当初去别处乞求怜悯，
我会更幸福——啊，远比现在幸福，
既然从她那里永远赢不来半分恩典，
而为了不叫耻辱杀死我，我唯有逃走。
『哈罗！』我呼喊（喊得何等徒劳！）——
『哈罗！』我向各色人等呼喊，
因为我必死无疑，除非碰巧她
趁为时未晚，垂怜于我。

「我的爱人（m'amye），那一天正步步逼近——
在它灾殃的烈日之下，你美貌之花
必将凋残、枯萎，不再美丽——到那时
我不哀恳，也不嘲弄；我不，因为你我
都将老迈无力！我的爱人，
畅饮爱之酒吧，畅饮，莫要踌躇，
直到泉源枯竭；但请你快快
垂怜于我——趁为时未晚！

「爱的主上，爱的全部疆土由你领有，
减轻些，啊，减轻你震怒的分量，
因为一切真心都该本着基督的仁慈，
趁为时未晚，垂怜于我。」

===Original===
Then from above a delicate and cool voice was audible. “You have mistaken the window, Monsieur de Montcorbier. Ysabeau de Montigny dwells in the Rue du Fouarre.”

“Ah, cruel!” sighed François. “Will you never let that kite hang upon the wall?”

“It is all very well to groan like a bellows. Guillemette Moreau did not sup here for nothing. I know of the verses you made her—and the gloves you gave her at Candlemas, too. Saint Anne!” observed the voice, somewhat sharply; “she needed gloves. Her hands are so much raw beef. And the headdress at Easter—she looks like the steeple of Saint Benoit in it. But every man to his taste, Monsieur de Montcorbier. Good night, Monsieur de Montcorbier.” But, for all that, the window did not close.

===Chinese===
随后，头顶上传来一个清雅而凉爽的嗓音。「您找错窗户了，蒙科尔比耶先生。伊莎博·德·蒙蒂尼住在福阿尔街（Rue du Fouarre）。」

「啊，狠心的人！」弗朗索瓦叹道。「您就永远不肯让那只鸢鸟挂在墙上了么？」

「像风箱一样唉声叹气倒容易。吉耶梅特·莫罗（Guillemette Moreau）那顿晚饭可不是白吃的。我知道你为她写的诗——还有圣烛节送她的手套。圣安娜！」那嗓音略带尖刻地说；「她倒正需要手套。她那双手跟生牛肉差不多。还有复活节那顶头巾——她戴上活像圣伯努瓦的尖塔。不过各有所好嘛，蒙科尔比耶先生。晚安，蒙科尔比耶先生。」话虽如此，那扇窗却没有关上。

===Original===
“Catherine—!” he pleaded; and under his breath he expressed uncharitable aspirations as to the future of Guillemette Moreau.

“You have made me very unhappy,” said the voice, with a little sniff.

“It was before I knew you, Catherine. The stars are beautiful, *m’amye*, and a man may reasonably admire them; but the stars vanish and are forgotten when the sun appears.”

“Ysabeau is not a star,” the voice pointed out; “she is simply a lank, good-for-nothing, slovenly trollop.”

“Ah, Catherine—!”

“You are still in love with her.”

“Catherine—!”

“Otherwise, you will promise me for the future to avoid her as you would the Black Death.”

“Catherine, her brother is my friend—!”

“René de Montigny is, to the knowledge of the entire Rue Saint Jacques, a gambler and a drunkard and, in all likelihood, a thief. But you prefer, it appears, the Montignys to me. An ill cat seeks an ill rat. Very heartily do I wish you joy of them. You will not promise? Good night, then, Monsieur de Montcorbier.”

“Mother of God! I promise, Catherine.”

From above Mademoiselle de Vaucelles gave a luxurious sigh. “Dear François!” said she.

===Chinese===
「卡特琳——！」他哀告道；随即压低嗓音，对吉耶梅特·莫罗的前途发出了几句不厚道的祝愿。

「你害我好不伤心，」那嗓音说着，轻轻吸了吸鼻子。

「那是我认识你之前的事呀，卡特琳。星星是美的，我的爱人（m'amye），人尽可以合情合理地仰慕它们；可太阳一出来，星星便消隐，便被忘却。」

「伊莎博可不是星星，」那嗓音指正道；「她不过是个瘦长条、一无是处、邋里邋遢的荡妇。」

「啊，卡特琳——！」

「你还爱着她。」

「卡特琳——！」

「要不然，你就向我应承，往后像躲黑死病一样躲开她。」

「卡特琳，她哥哥是我的朋友——！」

「勒内·德·蒙蒂尼（René de Montigny），全圣雅克街都知道，是个赌棍加酒鬼，十有八九还是个贼。可你呀，看来宁可要蒙蒂尼家的人，也不要我。歪猫专寻歪老鼠。我衷心祝您同他们厮混愉快。你不肯应承？那么，晚安，蒙科尔比耶先生。」

「天主的圣母啊！我应承，卡特琳。」

楼上，沃塞勒小姐心满意足地叹了口气。「亲爱的弗朗索瓦！」她说。

===Original===
“You are a tyrant,” he complained. “Madame Penthesilea was not more cruel. Madame Herodias was less implacable, I think. And I think that neither was so beautiful.”

“I love you,” said Mademoiselle de Vaucelles, promptly.

“But there was never anyone so many fathoms deep in love as I. Love bandies me from the postern to the frying-pan, from hot to cold. Ah, Catherine, Catherine, have pity upon my folly! Bid me fetch you Prester John’s beard, and I will do it; bid me believe the sky is made of calfskin, that morning is evening, that a fat sow is a windmill, and I will do it. Only love me a little, dear.”

“My king, my king of lads!” she murmured.

===Chinese===
「你是个暴君，」他抱怨道。「潘忒西勒亚女王也不见得比你更狠。希罗底夫人呢，我想还没有你这样斩钉截铁。我还想，她们俩谁也没有你这样美。」

「我爱你，」沃塞勒小姐应声便答。

「可爱得像我这样深——六个、七个测寻都探不到底的，天下再没有第二人。爱情把我从后门甩进油锅，又从火热抛进冰凉。啊，卡特琳，卡特琳，可怜可怜我的痴愚吧！你叫我去取祭司王约翰的胡须，我就去取；你叫我相信天是牛皮做的，早晨就是晚上，肥母猪就是风车，我也照信不误。只要稍稍爱我一点，亲爱的。」

「我的王，小伙子里的王！」她喃喃道。

===Original===
“My queen, my tyrant of unreason! Ah, yes, you are all that is ruthless and abominable, but then what eyes you have! Oh, very pitiless, large, lovely eyes—huge sapphires that in the old days might have ransomed every monarch in Tamerlane’s stable! Even in the night I see them, Catherine.”

“Yet Ysabeau’s eyes are brown.”

“Then are her eyes the gutter’s color. But Catherine’s eyes are twin firmaments.”

And about them the acacias rustled lazily, and the air was sweet with the odors of growing things, and the world, drenched in moonlight, slumbered. Without was Paris, but old Jehan’s garden-wall cloistered Paradise.

“Has the world, think you, known lovers, long dead now, that were once as happy as we?”

“Love was not known till we discovered it.”

===Chinese===
「我的女王，我的蛮不讲理的暴君！啊，是的，你狠心透顶、可恶透顶，可是你长了怎样一双眼睛！哦，好不无情、又大又可爱的眼睛——两颗巨大的蓝宝石，搁在往昔，足够赎清帖木儿（Tamerlane）马厩里圈着的全体君主！便在黑夜里我也看得见它们，卡特琳。」

「可伊莎博的眼睛是褐色的。」

「那她的眼睛便是阴沟的颜色。而卡特琳的眼睛，是一双天空。」

他们四周，洋槐懒懒地沙沙作响，空气里满是草木生长的甜香，浸透月光的世界沉沉睡着。墙外是巴黎，而老让的花园围墙之内，自成一个与世隔绝的乐园。

「你说，这世上可曾有过那样的情人——早已亡故，却一度同我们一样幸福？」

「在我们发现爱情之前，世上根本不晓得有爱情。」

===Original===
“I am so happy, François, that I fear death.”

“We have our day. Let us drink deep of love, not waiting until the spring run dry. Catherine, death comes to all, and yonder in the churchyard the poor dead lie together, huggermugger, and a man may not tell an archbishop from a ragpicker. Yet they have exulted in their youth, and have laughed in the sun with some lass or another lass. We have our day, Catherine.”

“Our day wherein I love you!”

“And wherein I love you precisely seven times as much!”

So they prattled in the moonlight. Their discourse was no more overburdened with wisdom than has been the ordinary communing of lovers since Adam first awakened ribless. Yet they were content, who, were young in the world’s recaptured youth.

Fate grinned and went on with her weaving.

===Chinese===
「我太幸福了，弗朗索瓦，幸福得害怕死亡。」

「我们有我们的日子。且畅饮爱之酒，莫等泉源枯竭。卡特琳，人皆有死，那边教堂墓园里，可怜的死者乱七八糟挤作一团躺着，谁也分不清哪位是大主教、哪位是捡破烂的。可他们也都在青春里欢腾过，也曾同这个姑娘、那个姑娘在太阳底下笑过。我们有我们的日子，卡特琳。」

「我们的日子，其中我爱着你！」

「其中我爱着你，不多不少，恰恰是七倍于你的爱！」

他们就这样在月光里絮语。这番谈论并不比自亚当头一遭缺着肋骨醒来以后天下情人的寻常对谈更多几分智慧。然而他们心满意足——他们正年轻，置身于这世界重拾的青春里。

命运咧嘴一笑，继续织她的布。

### III / III

“Et Ysabeau, Qui Dit: Enné!” / 「还有伊莎博，她喊：哼！」

===Original===
Somewhat later François came down the deserted street, treading on air. It was a bland summer night, windless, moon-washed, odorous with garden-scents; the moon, nearing its full, was a silver egg set on end—(“Leda-hatched,” he termed it; “one may look for the advent of Queen Heleine ere dawn”); and the sky he likened to blue velvet studded with the gilt nailheads of a seraphic upholsterer. François was a poet, but a civic poet; then, as always, he pilfered his similes from shopwindows.

===Chinese===
后来不多时，弗朗索瓦走下空无一人的长街，脚下像踩着云。这是一个温和的夏夜，无风，月光如洗，满溢着园圃的芬芳；月亮将近圆满，是一枚竖立着的银蛋——（「勒达（Leda）孵出来的，」他如此形容；「天亮之前，且等着海伦王后（Queen Heleine）光降吧」）；天空呢，他比作蓝丝绒，上头钉着某位天使级装潢匠的镀金圆钉。弗朗索瓦是个诗人，却是个市井诗人；那时一如往常，他的譬喻都是从铺面橱窗里顺手牵来的。

===Original===
But the heart of François was pure magnanimity, the heels of François were mercury, as he tripped past the church of Saint Benoit-le-Bétourné, stark snow and ink in the moonlight. Then with a jerk François paused.

On a stone bench before the church sat Ysabeau de Montigny and Gilles Raguyer. The priest was fuddled, hiccuping in his amorous dithyrambics as he paddled with the girl’s hand. “You tempt me to murder,” he was saying. “It is a deadly sin, my soul, and I have no mind to fry in Hell while my body swings on the Saint Denis road, a crow’s dinner. Let François live, my soul! My soul, he would stick little Gilles like a pig.”

===Chinese===
可弗朗索瓦的心是纯粹的宽宏，弗朗索瓦的脚步轻捷如水银，一路蹦跳着走过月光下黑白如雪如墨的圣伯努瓦-勒贝图尔内教堂。蓦地，他猛然收步。

教堂前的一张石凳上，坐着伊莎博·德·蒙蒂尼和吉尔·拉居耶。神父已经醉了，一面把玩姑娘的手，一面打着酒嗝，高诵他情意绵绵的酒神颂。「你这是引我行凶啊，」他正说着。「那是死罪，我的魂儿，我可不想身子在圣但尼大道上晃荡、当乌鸦的一餐，魂儿却在地狱里挨油煎。让弗朗索瓦活着吧，我的魂儿！我的魂儿，他准会把小吉尔当猪一样捅了。」

===Original===
Raguyer began to blubber at the thought.

“Holy Macaire!” said François; “here is a pretty plot a-brewing.” Yet because his heart was filled just now with loving-kindness, he forgave the girl. “*Tantaene irae?*” said François; and aloud, “Ysabeau, it is time you were abed.”

She wheeled upon him in apprehension; then, with recognition, her rage flamed. “Now, Gilles!” cried Ysabeau de Montigny; “now, coward! He is unarmed, Gilles. Look, Gilles! Kill for me this betrayer of women!”

===Chinese===
想到这一层，拉居耶顿时咧嘴要哭。

「圣马凯尔！」弗朗索瓦说；「这儿正酿着一出好戏呢。」不过此刻他心里盛满了仁爱，便饶过了那姑娘。「竟有这般怒气？（Tantaene irae?）」弗朗索瓦说；随即扬声道：「伊莎博，你该上床睡觉了。」

她惊惶地猛然转身；认出是他，怒火轰然烧起。「上啊，吉尔！」伊莎博·德·蒙蒂尼喊道；「上啊，胆小鬼！他没带家伙，吉尔。瞧啊，吉尔！替我杀了这个玩弄女人的负心汉！」

===Original===
Under his mantle François loosened the short sword he carried. But the priest plainly had no mind to the business. He rose, tipsily fumbling a knife, and snarling like a cur at sight of a strange mastiff. “Vile rascal!” said Gilles Raguyer, as he strove to lash himself into a rage. “O coward! O parricide! O Tarquin!”

François began to laugh. “Let us have done with this farce,” said he. “Your man has no stomach for battle, Ysabeau. And you do me wrong, my lass, to call me a betrayer of women. Doubtless, that tale seemed the most apt to kindle in poor Gilles some homicidal virtue: but you and I and God know that naught has passed between us save a few kisses and a trinket or so. It is no knifing matter. Yet for the sake of old time, come home, Ysabeau; your brother is my friend, and the hour is somewhat late for honest women to be abroad.”

===Chinese===
弗朗索瓦在外袍底下松开了随身短剑的鞘口。可神父显然无意应这份差。他站起身，醉醺醺地摸索一把刀，像小癞狗望见生人大犬似的低吼。「下流胚！」吉尔·拉居耶一面给自己鼓着火气一面咆哮。「哦懦夫！哦弑亲者！哦塔昆！」

弗朗索瓦笑了起来。「这出闹剧收场吧，」他说。「你这位汉子没有打仗的胃口，伊莎博。你骂我玩弄女人的负心汉，也冤枉了我，姑娘。毫无疑问，那个说法最能点起可怜吉尔的一点杀人义勇：可你我加上上帝都知道，我们之间除过几个吻、一两件小玩意儿，什么也不曾有过。这犯不着动刀子。不过念着旧日情分，回家去吧，伊莎博；你哥哥是我的朋友，这个时辰正经女人家还在外头游荡，未免太晚了些。」

===Original===
“Enné?” shrilled Ysabeau; “and yet, if I cannot strike a spark of courage from this clod here, there come those who may help me, François de Montcorbier. ’Ware Sermaise, Master François!”

François wheeled. Down the Rue Saint Jacques came Philippe Sermaise, like a questing hound, with drunken Jehan le Merdi at his heels. “Holy Virgin!” thought François; “this is likely to be a nasty affair. I would give a deal for a glimpse of the patrol lanterns just now.”

He edged his way toward the cloister, to get a wall at his back. But Gilles Raguyer followed him, knife in hand. “O hideous Tarquin! O Absalom!” growled Gilles; “have you, then, no respect for churchmen?”

===Chinese===
「哼，是么？」伊莎博尖声道；「可要是从这块木头疙瘩身上敲不出半点火星，自有能帮我的人来，弗朗索瓦·德·蒙科尔比耶。『留神塞尔莫瓦斯』，弗朗索瓦大师！」

弗朗索瓦霍然回身。菲利普·塞尔莫瓦斯顺着圣雅克街大步而来，像条四处寻猎的猎犬，脚后跟着醉醺醺的让·勒梅尔迪（Jehan le Merdi）。「圣处女！」弗朗索瓦暗想；「瞧这架势，怕是桩不干净的营生。这会儿哪怕瞧一眼巡夜的灯笼，我肯出大价钱。」

他侧身朝回廊蹭过去，好让背后有堵墙。可吉尔·拉居耶提着刀跟了上来。「哦无耻的塔昆！哦押沙龙！」吉尔咆哮道；「你难道对教士没有半分敬畏么？」

===Original===
With an oath, Sermaise ran up. “Now, may God die twice,” he panted, “if I have not found the skulker at last! There is a crow needs picking between us two, Montcorbier.”

Hemmed in by his enemies, François temporized. “Why do you accost me thus angrily, Master Philippe?” he babbled. “What harm have I done you? What is your will of me?”

But his fingers tore feverishly at the strap by which the lute was swung over his shoulder, and now the lute fell at their feet, leaving François unhampered and his sword-arm free.

===Chinese===
塞尔莫瓦斯咒骂一声，抢步上前。「好啊，愿上帝再死一回，」他喘着气说，「我可算逮着这躲躲闪闪的家伙了！咱俩之间有一只乌鸦等着拔毛，蒙科尔比耶。」

腹背受敌，弗朗索瓦虚与委蛇。「您为什么这样怒气冲冲地拦我，菲利普大师？」他结结巴巴地说。「我几时伤过您？您要我怎么样？」

可他的手指正发狂似的撕扯着肩上挂琴的背带，诗琴随即落在众人脚边，弗朗索瓦脱开了羁绊，持剑的手臂自由了。

===Original===
This was fuel to the priest’s wrath. “Sacred bones of Benoit!” he snarled; “I could make a near guess as to what window you have been caterwauling under.”

From beneath his gown he suddenly hauled out a rapier and struck at the boy while François was yet tugging at his sword.

Full in the mouth Sermaise struck him, splitting the lower lip through. François felt the piercing cold of the steel, the tingling of it against his teeth, then the warm grateful spurt of blood; through a red mist, he saw Gilles and Ysabeau run screaming down the Rue Saint Jacques.

===Chinese===
这话往神父的怒火里添了柴。「圣伯努瓦的圣骨！」他咆哮道；「你方才在哪扇窗底下叫春，我不猜也猜得八九不离十。」

他猛地从法衣底下抽出一把细剑，趁弗朗索瓦还在拔剑，朝少年劈面便刺。

剑锋正中口部，塞尔莫瓦斯把他下唇整个豁穿。弗朗索瓦觉出钢铁刺骨的寒意，觉出它磕在牙齿上的震颤，随后是温热的、令人快慰的鲜血涌出；透过一层红雾，他看见吉尔和伊莎博尖叫着沿圣雅克街逃远了。

===Original===
He drew and made at Sermaise, forgetful of le Merdi. It was shrewd work. Presently they were fighting in the moonlight, hammer-and-tongs, as the saying is, and presently Sermaise was cursing like a madman, for François had wounded him in the groin. Window after window rattled open as the Rue Saint Jacques ran nightcapped to peer at the brawl. Then as François hurled back his sword to slash at the priest’s shaven head—Frenchmen had not yet learned to thrust with the point in the Italian manner—Jehan le Merdi leapt from behind, nimble as a snake, and wrested away the boy’s weapon. Sermaise closed with a glad shout.

“Heart of God!” cried Sermaise. “Pray, bridegroom, pray!”

But François jumped backward, tumbling over le Merdi, and with apish celerity caught up a great stone and flung it full in the priest’s countenance.

===Chinese===
他拔剑扑向塞尔莫瓦斯，把勒梅尔迪忘在了脑后。这一场恶斗毫不留情。不多时，两人已在月光下短兵相接，正应了俗话说的，大刀阔斧；又不多时，塞尔莫瓦斯咒骂得像个疯子，因为弗朗索瓦伤了他的下阴。圣雅克街的窗子一扇接一扇哐啷推开，人人戴着睡帽探头张望这场恶斗。随后，正当弗朗索瓦抡圆了剑、要劈那神父剃得精光的脑袋——法兰西人那时还没学会意大利式的刺击剑法——让·勒梅尔迪从背后跃出，灵巧如蛇，夺走了少年的剑。塞尔莫瓦斯欢呼一声，扑了上来。

「上帝的心啊！」塞尔莫瓦斯喊道。「祷告吧，新郎倌，祷告吧！」

可弗朗索瓦向后一跃，绊倒了勒梅尔迪，又以猿猴般的迅捷抄起一块大石，正掷在神父的脸上。

===Original===
The rest was hideous. For a breathing space Sermaise kept his feet, his outspread arms making a tottering cross. It was curious to see him peer about irresolutely now that he had no face. François, staring at the black featureless horror before him, began to choke. Standing thus, with outstretched arms, the priest first let fall his hands, so that they hung limp from the wrists; his fingernails gleamed in the moonlight. His rapier tinkled on the flagstones with the sound of shattering glass, and Philippe Sermaise slid down, all a-jumble, crumpling like a broken toy. Afterward you might have heard a long, awed sibilance go about the windows overhead as the watching Rue Saint Jacques breathed again.

===Chinese===
往后的事惨不忍睹。有一口气的工夫，塞尔莫瓦斯还立着，双臂张开，搭成一个摇摇欲坠的十字。他没有了脸，却还在那里犹犹豫豫地四下张望，那模样古怪至极。弗朗索瓦瞪着眼前这团漆黑无五官的恐怖，开始哽住。神父就这样双臂直伸地立着，先是垂下了手，两只手软塌塌地吊在腕子上；指甲在月光里闪着微光。他的细剑叮的一声落在石板上，声如碎玻璃，菲利普·塞尔莫瓦斯随之瘫滑下去，七零八落，像一件摔坏的玩偶。随后你或许听得见头顶一扇扇窗子四周响起一片悠长的、敬畏的咝声——那是看热闹的圣雅克街重又喘出了气。

===Original===
François de Montcorbier ran. He tore at his breast as he ran, stifling. He wept as he ran through the moon-washed Rue Saint Jacques, making animal-like and whistling noises. His split lip was a clammy dead thing that napped against his chin as he ran.

“François!” a man cried, meeting him; “ah, name of a name, François!”

It was René de Montigny, lurching from the Crowned Ox, half-tipsy. He caught the boy by the shoulder and hurried François, still sobbing, to Fouquet the barber-surgeon’s, where they sewed up his wound. In accordance with the police regulations, they first demanded an account of how he had received it. René lied uphill and down-dale, while in a corner of the room François monotonously wept.

Fate grinned and went on with her weaving.

===Chinese===
弗朗索瓦·德·蒙科尔比耶跑了。他一面跑一面撕扯胸口，窒闷难当。他哭着一路跑过月光如洗的圣雅克街，发出野兽般的、吹哨似的呜咽。豁开的下唇成了一片又冷又湿的死肉，随着奔跑一下一下拍打着他的下巴。

「弗朗索瓦！」迎面一个人喊道；「哎呀，以名之名起誓，弗朗索瓦！」

是勒内·德·蒙蒂尼，从加冕牛酒店里歪歪斜斜出来，半醉。他抓住少年的肩头，把还在抽噎的弗朗索瓦匆匆送到外科理发师傅富凯（Fouquet）那里，缝好了伤口。依照警察章程，他们先盘问这伤是怎么落的。勒内满口谎言，一路撒得山响；屋子一角，弗朗索瓦一声接一声地呜咽。

命运咧嘴一笑，继续织她的布。

### IV / IV

“Necessité Faict Gens Mesprende” / 「困迫使人行差踏错」

===Original===
The Rue Saint Jacques had toothsome sauce for its breakfast. The quarter smacked stiff lips over the news, as it pictured François de Montcorbier dangling from Montfaucon. “Horrible!” said the Rue Saint Jacques, and drew a moral of suitably pious flavor.

Guillemette Moreau had told Catherine of the affair before the day was aired. The girl’s hurt vanity broke tether.

“Sermaise!” said she. “Bah, what do I care for Sermaise! He killed him in fair fight. But within an hour, Guillemette—within a half-hour after leaving me, he is junketing on church-porches with that trollop. They were not there for holy-water. Midnight, look you! And he swore to me—chaff, chaff! His honor is chaff, Guillemette, and his heart a bran-bag. Oh, swine, filthy swine! Eh, well, let the swine stick to his sty. Send Noël d’Arnaye to me.”

===Chinese===
圣雅克街的早饭有了津津有味的作料。这街区咂着发僵的嘴唇咀嚼这消息，一面想象弗朗索瓦·德·蒙科尔比耶在蒙福孔（Montfaucon）绞架上晃荡的模样。「可怕呀！」圣雅克街说，随即引出一条口味相当虔敬的教训。

天还没透亮，吉耶梅特·莫罗已把这件事告诉了卡特琳。姑娘受伤的虚荣挣断了缰绳。

「塞尔莫瓦斯！」她说。「呸，塞尔莫瓦斯关我什么事！他是堂堂正正决斗里杀的。可离开我还不到一个钟头，吉耶梅特——不到半个钟头，他就在教堂门廊上同那个荡妇调笑取乐了。他们到那儿去，可不是为了取圣水。半夜三更，你瞧！他还对着我发誓来着——糠，全是糠！他的名誉是糠，吉耶梅特，他的心是只麸皮口袋。哦，猪，肮脏的猪！好得很，让猪守着他的猪圈去吧。去把诺埃尔·达尔奈给我叫来。」

===Original===
The Sieur d’Arnaye came, his head tied in a napkin.

“Foh!” said she; “another swine fresh from the gutter? No, this is a bottle, a tun, a walking wine-barrel! Noël, I despise you. I will marry you if you like.”

He fell to mumbling her hand. An hour later Catherine told Jehan de Vaucelles she intended to marry Noël the Handsome when he should come back from Geneppe with the exiled Dauphin. The old man, having wisdom, lifted his brows, and returned to his reading in *Le Pet au Diable*.

===Chinese===
达尔奈大人来了，脑袋上还扎着餐巾。

「呸！」她说；「又是一头刚从阴沟里爬出来的猪？不对，这是一只瓶，一只大桶，一桶会走路的酒！诺埃尔，我看不起你。你要愿意，我就嫁你。」

他立刻捧起她的手乱吻一气。一个钟头后，卡特琳告诉让·德·沃塞勒，说她打算等美男子诺埃尔随流亡的王储从热纳普回来，便嫁给他。老人颇有识见，扬了扬眉毛，回身继续读他的《Le Pet au Diable》（《魔鬼的屁》）去了。

===Original===
The patrol had transported Sermaise to the prison of Saint Benoit, where he lay all night. That day he was carried to the hospital of the Hôtel Dieu. He died the following Saturday.

Death exalted the man to some nobility. Before one of the apparitors of the Châtelet he exonerated Montcorbier, under oath, and asked that no steps be taken against him. “I forgive him my death,” said Sermaise, manly enough at the last, “by reason of certain causes moving him thereunto.” Presently he demanded the peach-colored silk glove they would find in the pocket of his gown. It was Catherine’s glove. The priest kissed it, and then began to laugh. Shortly afterward he died, still gnawing at the glove.

===Chinese===
巡夜队已把塞尔莫瓦斯抬进圣伯努瓦的监狱，他在那里躺了一夜。当天，他被送进了主宫医院（Hôtel Dieu）。随后的星期六，他死了。

死亡把这人抬举出了几分高贵。他在夏特莱法院（Châtelet）一名执达吏面前立誓为蒙科尔比耶开脱，并请求不要对他采取任何手段。「我饶恕他害我死命，」塞尔莫瓦斯说，临终倒颇有丈夫气，「鉴于促使他出此下策的种种缘由。」随后他索要那只他们会在法衣口袋里找到的桃红色丝手套。那是卡特琳的手套。神父吻了它，随即大笑起来。不多时他咽了气，嘴里还啃着那只手套。

===Original===
François and René had vanished. “Good riddance,” said the Rue Saint Jacques. But Montcorbier was summoned to answer before the court of the Châtelet for the death of Philippe Sermaise, and in default of his appearance, was subsequently condemned to banishment from the kingdom.

The two young men were at Saint Pourçain-en-Bourbonnais, where René had kinsmen. Under the name of des Loges, François had there secured a place as tutor, but when he heard that Sermaise in the article of death had cleared him of all blame, François set about procuring a pardon.2 It was January before he succeeded in obtaining it.

===Chinese===
弗朗索瓦和勒内已经逃得无影无踪。「走了倒干净。」圣雅克街说。可蒙科尔比耶仍被传唤到夏特莱法院，为菲利普·塞尔莫瓦斯之死对簿公堂；因他缺席未到，随后被判驱逐出王国。

两个年轻人躲在波旁奈的圣普尔桑（Saint Pourçain-en-Bourbonnais），勒内在那里有亲戚。弗朗索瓦化名德洛日（des Loges），在当地谋得一个教书先生的位子；可一听说塞尔莫瓦斯弥留之际为他洗清了全部分干系，弗朗索瓦便着手设法求得赦免。² 直到一月里，他才把赦免弄到手。

===Original===
Meanwhile he had learned a deal of René’s way of living. “You are a thief,” François observed to Montigny the day the pardon came, “but you have played a kindly part by me. I think you are Dysmas, René, not Gestas. Heh, I throw no stones. You have stolen, but I have killed. Let us go to Paris, lad, and start afresh.”

Montigny grinned. “I shall certainly go to Paris,” he said. “Friends wait for me there—Guy Tabary, Petit Jehan and Colin de Cayeux. We are planning to visit Guillaume Coiffier, a fat priest with some six hundred crowns in the cupboard. You will make one of the party, François.”

===Chinese===
这期间，他把勒内的营生摸了个透。「你是个贼，」赦免下来的那天，弗朗索瓦对蒙蒂尼说，「可你待我倒是一片好心。我想你是狄斯玛斯（Dysmas），勒内，不是杰斯塔斯（Gestas）。嘿，我可不扔石头。你偷过，我杀过。咱们去巴黎吧，小子，从头来过。」

蒙蒂尼咧嘴一笑。「巴黎我是一定要去的，」他说。「有朋友在那儿等我——居伊·塔巴里（Guy Tabary）、小让（Petit Jehan），还有科兰·德·卡约（Colin de Cayeux）。我们正打算去拜访纪尧姆·夸菲耶（Guillaume Coiffier），一个碗柜里存着六百来克朗的胖神父。你也来入伙，弗朗索瓦。」

===Original===
“René, René,” said the other, “my heart bleeds for you.”

Again Montigny grinned. “You think a great deal about blood nowadays,” he commented. “People will be mistaking you for such a poet as was crowned Nero, who, likewise, gave his time to ballad-making and to murdering fathers of the Church. Eh, dear Ahenabarbus, let us first see what the Rue Saint Jacques has to say about your recent gambols. After that, I think you will make one of our party.”

===Chinese===
「勒内，勒内，」另一位说，「我真为你心痛啊。」

蒙蒂尼又咧嘴一笑。「你如今倒满脑子想着血，」他评点道。「人家要把你错当成那类加过冕的诗人——尼禄了，他当年也一样，一边忙着编歌谣，一边忙着谋杀教父。嗳，亲爱的阿赫诺巴尔布斯（Ahenabarbus），咱们且先瞧瞧，圣雅克街对你近来的放浪形骸怎么说。到那时，我想你就肯入伙了。」

### V / V

“Yeulx sans Pitié!” / 「无情的眼睛！」

===Original===
There was a light crackling frost under foot the day that François came back to the Rue Saint Jacques. Upon this brisk, clear January day it was good to be home again, an excellent thing to be alive.

“Eh, Guillemette, Guillemette,” he laughed. “Why, lass—!”

“Faugh!” said Guillemette Moreau, as she passed him, nose in air. “A murderer, a priest-killer.”

Then the sun went black for François. Such welcoming was a bucket of cold water, full in the face. He gasped, staring after her; and pursy Thomas Tricot, on his way from mass, nudged Martin Blaru in the ribs.

“Martin,” said he, “fruit must be cheap this year. Yonder in the gutter is an apple from the gallows-tree, and no one will pick it up.”

Blaru turned and spat out, “Cain! Judas!”

===Chinese===
弗朗索瓦回到圣雅克街那天，脚下踩着一层薄薄的、咯吱作响的霜。在这样一个爽朗明净的一月天里，回家是桩美事，活着更是桩妙事。

「嗳，吉耶梅特，吉耶梅特，」他笑道。「哎呀，姑娘——！」

「呸！」吉耶梅特·莫罗擦身而过，鼻子翘上了天。「杀人犯，杀教士的凶手。」

弗朗索瓦眼前的太阳顿时黑了。这样的迎接，等于迎面泼来一桶冷水。他倒抽一口气，怔怔望着她走远；刚望完弥撒、肥得气喘的托马·特里科（Thomas Tricot）用胳膊肘捅了捅马丁·布拉吕（Martin Blaru）的肋骨。

「马丁，」他说，「今年的果子想必便宜。那边阴沟里躺着一枚从绞架树上落下来的苹果，谁也不肯捡。」

布拉吕转过身，啐出一口：「该隐！犹大！」

===Original===
This was only a sample. Everywhere François found rigid faces, sniffs, and skirts drawn aside. A little girl in a red cap, Robin Troussecaille’s daughter, flung a stone at François as he slunk into the cloister of Saint Benoit-le-Bétourné. In those days a slain priest was God’s servant slain, no less; and the Rue Saint Jacques was a respectable God-fearing quarter of Paris.

“My father!” the boy cried, rapping upon the door of the Hôtel de la Porte-Rouge; “O my father, open to me, for I think that my heart is breaking.”

Shortly his foster-father, Guillaume de Villon, came to the window. “Murderer!” said he. “Betrayer of women! Now, by the cauldron of John! how dare you show your face here? I gave you my name and you soiled it. Back to your husks, rascal!”

===Chinese===
这不过是个样品。弗朗索瓦到处撞见铁板的脸、嗤嗤的鼻子和撩开避让的裙裾。罗班·特鲁斯凯耶（Robin Troussecaille）家戴红帽的小丫头，在他溜进圣伯努瓦-勒贝图尔内的回廊时，朝他扔了一块石头。那个年月，被杀的神父便是被杀的天主之仆，一点不含糊；而圣雅克街，是巴黎一处体面的、敬畏上帝的街区。

「父亲哪！」少年敲着红门府（Hôtel de la Porte-Rouge）的大门喊道；「哦，父亲，给我开门吧，我觉得我的心要碎了。」

不多时，他的养父纪尧姆·德·维永来到窗前。「杀人犯！」他说。「玩弄女人的负心汉！好哇，以圣约翰的油锅起誓！你还有什么脸到这儿来？我把我的名字给了你，你却把它糟蹋了。滚回去吃你的豆荚吧，恶棍！」

===Original===
“O God, O God!” François cried, one or two times, as he looked up into the old man’s implacable countenance. “You, too, my father!”

He burst into a fit of sobbing.

“Go!” the priest stormed; “go, murderer!”

It was not good to hear François’ laughter. “What a world we live in!” he giggled. “You gave me your name and I soiled it? Eh, Master Priest, Master Pharisee, beware! *Villon* is good French for *vagabond*, an excellent name for an outcast. And as God lives, I will presently drag that name through every muckheap in France.”

Yet he went to Jehan de Vaucelles’ home. “I will afford God one more chance at my soul,” said François.

===Chinese===
「哦上帝，哦上帝！」弗朗索瓦仰望着老人那张铁面无情的脸，喊了一两声。「连您也一样，父亲！」

他放声抽噎起来。

「滚！」神父怒喝；「滚，杀人犯！」

弗朗索瓦的笑声，叫人不忍卒听。「我们活在怎样一个世道啊！」他嘻嘻笑道。「你把你的名字给了我，是我糟蹋了它？咦，神父大人，法利赛人大人，仔细着！Villon 在地道的法语里就是「流浪汉」（vagabond），给逐出家门的人做名字，再合适不过。上帝在上，我不久就要把这名字拖过法兰西的每一堆粪土。」

可他还是去了让·德·沃塞勒家。「我且再给上帝一次机会，救一救我的灵魂。」弗朗索瓦说。

===Original===
In the garden he met Catherine and Noël d’Arnaye coming out of the house. They stopped short. Her face, half-muffled in the brown fur of her cloak, flushed to a wonderful rose of happiness, the great eyes glowed, and Catherine reached out her hands toward François with a glad cry.

His heart was hot wax as he fell before her upon his knees. “O heart’s dearest, heart’s dearest!” he sobbed; “forgive me that I doubted you!”

And then for an instant, the balance hung level. But after a while, “Ysabeau de Montigny dwells in the Rue du Fouarre,” said Catherine, in a crisp voice—“having served your purpose, however, I perceive that Ysabeau, too, is to be cast aside as though she were an old glove. Monsieur d’Arnaye, thrash for me this betrayer of women.”

===Chinese===
花园里，他迎面撞见卡特琳和诺埃尔·达尔奈正从宅子里出来。两人猛地站住。她的脸半掩在斗篷的棕色皮毛里，涨起一片奇妙的幸福红晕，大眼睛灼灼放光，卡特琳朝弗朗索瓦伸出双手，欢叫出声。

他的心滚烫如蜡，双膝一软，跪倒在她面前。「哦，心肝，心肝！」他呜咽道；「饶恕我曾疑心你！」

有那么一瞬，天平悬得平平的。可过了一会儿，「伊莎博·德·蒙蒂尼住在福阿尔街，」卡特琳说，声音干脆利落——「不过我看，伊莎博既已遂了你的用场，也要像只旧手套一样被扔到一边了。达尔奈大人，替我狠狠揍这玩弄女人的负心汉。」

===Original===
Noël was a big, handsome man, like an obtuse demigod, a foot taller than François. Noël lifted the boy by his collar, caught up a stick and set to work. Catherine watched them, her eyes gemlike and cruel.

François did not move a muscle. God had chosen.

After a little, though, the Sieur d’Arnaye flung François upon the ground, where he lay quite still for a moment. Then slowly he rose to his feet. He never looked at Noël. For a long time François stared at Catherine de Vaucelles, frost-flushed, defiant, incredibly beautiful. Afterward the boy went out of the garden, staggering like a drunken person.

===Chinese===
诺埃尔身材高大英俊，像一尊迟钝的半神，比弗朗索瓦高出一英尺。诺埃尔拎起少年的领子，抄起一根棍子，动手便打。卡特琳在一旁看着，眼睛如宝石，冷酷。

弗朗索瓦一动不动。上帝已作出了选择。

不过打了一会儿，达尔奈大人把弗朗索瓦掼在地上，他一动不动地躺了片刻。随后他缓缓爬起身。他始终没看诺埃尔一眼。弗朗索瓦久久地盯着卡特琳·德·沃塞勒——霜染般绯红、倔强、美得叫人不敢信。随后，少年走出花园，像醉汉一样踉踉跄跄。

===Original===
He found Montigny at the Crowned Ox. “René,” said François, “there is no charity on earth, there is no God in Heaven. But in Hell there is most assuredly a devil, and I think that he must laugh a great deal. What was that you were telling me about the priest with six hundred crowns in his cupboard?”

René slapped him on the shoulder. “Now,” said he, “you talk like a man.” He opened the door at the back and cried: “Colin, you and Petit Jehan and that pig Tabary may come out. I have the honor, messieurs, to offer you a new Companion of the Cockleshell—Master François de Montcorbier.”

But the recruit raised a protesting hand. “No,” said he—“François Villon. The name is triply indisputable, since it has been put upon me not by one priest but by three.”

===Chinese===
他在加冕牛酒店找到了蒙蒂尼。「勒内，」弗朗索瓦说，「世上没有仁慈，天上没有上帝。可地狱里确确实实有个魔鬼，而且我想，他一定常常大笑。你先前跟我说的那位碗柜里存着六百克朗的神父，是怎么回事来着？」

勒内一巴掌拍上他的肩膀。「这，」他说，「才像句人话。」他打开后门，喊道：「科兰，你和小让，还有那头猪塔巴里，都出来吧。诸位先生，我有幸向你们引荐一位贝壳帮（Companion of the Cockleshell）的新兄弟——弗朗索瓦·德·蒙科尔比耶大师。」

可那新入伙的举起一只手，表示抗议。「不，」他说——「弗朗索瓦·维永（François Villon）。这名字三重地无可辩驳，因为它不是一位神父给我安上的，是三位。」

### VI / VI

“Volia l’Estat Divers d’entre Eulx” / 「这便是他们各自的下场」

===Original===
When the Dauphin came from Geneppe to be crowned King of France, there rode with him Noël d’Arnaye and Noël’s brother Raymond. And the longawaited news that Charles the Well-Served was at last servitor to Death, brought the exiled Louis posthaste to Paris, where the Rue Saint Jacques turned out full force to witness his triumphal entry. They expected, in those days, Saturnian doings of Louis XI, a recrudescence of the Golden Age; and when the new king began his reign by granting Noël a snug fief in Picardy, the Rue Saint Jacques applauded.

“Noël has followed the King’s fortunes these ten years,” said the Rue Saint Jacques; “it is only just. And now, neighbor, we may look to see Noël the Handsome and Catherine de Vaucelles make a match of it. The girl has a tidy dowry, they say; old Jehan proved wealthier than the quarter suspected. But death of my life, yes! You may see his tomb in the Innocents’ yonder, with weeping seraphim and a yard of Latin on it. I warrant you that rascal Montcorbier has lain awake in half the prisons in France thinking of what he flung away. Seven years, no less, since he and Montigny showed their thieves’ faces here. La, the world wags, neighbor, and they say there will be a new tax on salt if we go to war with the English.”

===Chinese===
当王储从热纳普回来加冕为法兰西国王时，随驾骑行的有诺埃尔·达尔奈和诺埃尔的弟弟雷蒙。而久候的音讯——「善得其臣的查理」（Charles the Well-Served）终于也去伺候死神了——让流亡的路易火速赶回了巴黎，圣雅克街倾巢而出，观看他凯旋入城。那些日子，人们指望路易十一有萨图恩治世的作为、黄金时代的重光；新王登基的头一件事，便是赐给诺埃尔皮卡第（Picardy）一块殷实的封地，圣雅克街不由鼓起掌来。

「诺埃尔追随国王的时运已有十年了，」圣雅克街说；「这是他应得的。如今，街坊，我们就要看着美男子诺埃尔和卡特琳·德·沃塞勒结成一家了。姑娘有一份可观的嫁妆，据说；老让的家底，比这街区猜想的要厚实。可是我的命呀，可不是！他的坟就在那边无辜者公墓（the Innocents'），坟上刻着哭泣的炽天使，还有一码长的拉丁文。我敢担保，那恶棍蒙科尔比耶在法兰西半数的监狱里都彻夜难眠，翻来覆去想着他亲手扔掉的是什么。算来七年了，自打他和蒙蒂尼在这儿露出他们那两张贼脸。嗐，世道就这么转着，街坊；据说要是同英国人开战，盐上又要添新税喽。」

===Original===
Not quite thus, perhaps, ran the meditations of Catherine de Vaucelles one still August night as she sat at her window, overlooking the acacias and chestnuts of her garden. Noël, conspicuously prosperous in blue and silver, had but now gone down the Rue Saint Jacques, singing, clinking the fat purse whose plumpness was still a novelty. That evening she had given her promise to marry him at Michaelmas.

This was a black night, moonless, windless. There were a scant half-dozen stars overhead, and the thick scent of roses and mignonette came up to her in languid waves. Below, the treetops conferred, stealthily, and the fountain plashed its eternal remonstrance against the conspiracy they lisped of.

===Chinese===
然而八月里一个静谧的夜晚，卡特琳·德·沃塞勒坐在窗前，俯瞰园中的洋槐与栗树，她心里转的念头，或许同这些话大不一样。诺埃尔一身蓝银两色，招摇阔绰，方才唱着歌沿圣雅克街而去，一路把那只鼓囊囊的钱袋抖得叮当响——那份鼓囊囊，对他说来还是件新鲜事。就在那天傍晚，她应允了米迦勒节（Michaelmas）嫁给他。

这是个漆黑的夜，无月无风。头顶上稀稀落落挂着六颗不到的星星，玫瑰与木樨草的浓香一阵阵懒洋洋地向她涌来。园子底下，树梢们鬼鬼祟祟地密谈，泉水淙淙作响，发出它永恒的抗议，抗议它们絮絮低语的那个阴谋。

===Original===
After a while Catherine rose and stood contemplative before a long mirror that was in her room. Catherine de Vaucelles was now, at twenty-three, in the full flower of her comeliness. Blue eyes the mirror showed her—luminous and tranquil eyes, set very far apart; honey-colored hair massed heavily about her face, a mouth all curves, the hue of a strawberry, tender but rather fretful, and beneath it a firm chin; only her nose left something to be desired—for that feature, though well-formed, was diminutive and bent toward the left, by perhaps the thickness of a cobweb. She might reasonably have smiled at what the mirror showed her, but, for all that, she sighed.

“*O Beauty of her, whereby I am undone*,” said Catherine, wistfully. “Ah, God in Heaven, forgive me for my folly! Sweet Christ, intercede for me who have paid dearly for my folly!”

Fate grinned in her weaving. Through the open window came the sound of a voice singing.

===Chinese===
过了一会儿，卡特琳站起身，若有所思地立在房中那面长镜前。卡特琳·德·沃塞勒如今二十三岁，正当容颜的全盛。镜中映给她一双蓝眼睛——澄澈而安宁，生得极开；蜜色的头发沉沉堆拥着脸庞；一张嘴满是曲线，色泽如草莓，温柔之中却带几分嗔意；唇下是一方坚定的下巴；唯独鼻子略嫌不足——那件五官虽生得端正，却嫌小巧，还微微向左倾，倾幅大约不过一根蛛丝的厚度。镜中这一切，本可让她嫣然一笑，然而她却叹了口气。

「哦，她的美，我因它而倾覆，」卡特琳怅然念道。「天上的上帝啊，饶恕我的痴愚！亲爱的基督，为我这为痴愚付出高价的人代求吧！」

命运在她的织机上咧嘴一笑。敞开的窗口，飘进来一阵歌声。

===Original===
Sang the voice:

“O Beauty of her, whereby I am undone!

O Grace of her, that hath no grace for me!

O Love of her, the bit that guides me on

To sorrow and to grievous misery!

O felon Charms, my poor heart’s enemy—”

and the singing broke off in a fit of coughing.

===Chinese===
那嗓音唱道：

「哦，她的美，我因它而倾覆！
哦，她的优雅，于我却不肯施舍半分！
哦，她的爱，是引导我的衔铁，
引我走向悲伤，走向凄惨的苦境！
哦，奸邪的魅惑，我这可怜心的仇敌——」

歌声在一阵咳嗽中戛然而止。

===Original===
Catherine had remained motionless for a matter of two minutes, her head poised alertly. She went to the gong and struck it seven or eight times.

“Macée, there is a man in the garden. Bring him to me, Macée—ah, love of God, Macée, make haste!”

Blinking, he stood upon the threshold. Then, without words, their lips met.

“My king!” said Catherine; “heart’s emperor!”

“O rose of all the world!” he cried.

There was at first no need of speech.

===Chinese===
卡特琳一动不动地立了约莫两分钟，头颅机警地侧昂着。随后她走到铜锣前，敲了七八下。

「玛赛（Macée），园子里有个男人。把他带到我这儿来，玛赛——啊，上帝的爱啊，玛赛，快些！」

他眨着眼站在门槛上。随后，不待言语，他们的唇相遇了。

「我的王！」卡特琳说；「心儿的皇帝！」

「哦，举世无双的玫瑰！」他喊道。

起初，无须言语。

===Original===
But after a moment she drew away and stared at him. François, though he was but thirty, seemed an old man. His bald head shone in the candlelight. His face was a mesh of tiny wrinkles, wax-white, and his lower lip, puckered by the scar of his wound, protruded in an eternal grimace. As Catherine steadfastly regarded him, the faded eyes, half-covered with a bluish film, shifted, and with a jerk he glanced over his shoulder. The movement started a cough tearing at his throat.

“Holy Macaire!” said he. “I thought that somebody, if not Henri Cousin, the executioner, was at my heels. Why do you stare so, lass? Have you anything to eat? I am famished.”

In silence she brought him meat and wine, and he fell upon it. He ate hastily, chewing with his front teeth, like a sheep.

===Chinese===
可过了一会儿，她抽身退开，怔怔打量他。弗朗索瓦虽只三十岁，看上去却已是个老人。秃顶在烛光里发亮。他的脸是一张细密皱纹织成的网，白得像蜡；下唇被伤疤扯得皱起，向外噘着，凝成一副永恒的怪相。卡特琳定定注视他时，那双蒙着淡蓝薄翳的失色眼睛躲闪了一下，他猛然回头朝肩后望去。这一动，引得一阵咳嗽撕扯他的喉咙。

「圣马凯尔！」他说。「我还当有什么人跟在我脚后——若不是刽子手亨利·库赞（Henri Cousin）的话。你这么直勾勾瞧什么，姑娘？有什么吃的没有？我饿坏了。」

她默默给他端来肉和酒，他扑上去便吃。他吃得很急，用门牙咀嚼，像一只羊。

===Original===
When he had ended, Catherine came to him and took both his hands in hers and lifted them to her lips. “The years have changed you, François,” she said, curiously meek.

François put her away. Then he strode to the mirror and regarded it intently. With a snarl, he turned about. “The years!” said he. “You are modest. It was you who killed François de Montcorbier, as surely as Montcorbier killed Sermaise. Eh, Sovereign Virgin! that is scant cause for grief. You made François Villon. What do you think of him, lass?”

She echoed the name. It was in many ways a seasoned name, but unaccustomed to mean nothing. Accordingly François sneered.

===Chinese===
他吃完了，卡特琳走到他跟前，把他双手握进自己手里，举到唇边。「岁月把你变了，弗朗索瓦，」她说，出奇地温顺。

弗朗索瓦把她推开。随后他大步走到镜子跟前，凝神逼视。他一声咆哮，转过身来。「岁月！」他说。「你说得倒客气。是你要了弗朗索瓦·德·蒙科尔比耶的命，正如蒙科尔比耶要了塞尔莫瓦斯的命，一样确凿。咦，至高无上的圣母！这可算不得伤心的理由。是你造出了弗朗索瓦·维永。你看他怎么样，姑娘？」

她跟着念了一遍这名字。这名字在许多方面都已饱经风霜，却不惯于一无所值。于是弗朗索瓦冷笑了一声。

===Original===
“Now, by all the fourteen joys and sorrows of Our Lady! I believe that you have never heard of François Villon! The Rue Saint Jacques has not heard of François Villon! The pigs, the gross pigs, that dare not peep out of their sty! Why, I have capped verses with the Duke of Orleans. The very street-boys know my Ballad of the Women of Paris. Not a drunkard in the realm but has ranted my jolly Orison for Master Cotard’s Soul when the bottle passed. The King himself hauled me out of Meung gaol last September, swearing that in all France there was not my equal at a ballad. And you have never heard of me!”

Once more a fit of coughing choked him midcourse in his indignant chattering.

She gave him a woman’s answer: “I do not care if you are the greatest lord in the kingdom or the most sunken knave that steals ducks from Paris Moat. I only know that I love you, François.”

===Chinese===
「好哇，凭圣母的十四般欢喜与忧愁起誓！我看你压根儿没听说过弗朗索瓦·维永！圣雅克街也没听说过弗朗索瓦·维永！那群猪，那群粗鄙的猪，缩在猪圈里连头都不敢探一探！嗐，我同奥尔良公爵斗过韵。街上的顽童都会唱我的《巴黎女人谣曲》。王国里没有一个酒鬼，酒瓶传到手上时不扯着嗓子念我那篇快活的《科塔尔大师之魂祷文》。去年九月，国王亲自把我从默恩（Meung）监狱里提了出来，赌咒说全法兰西写谣曲没人比得上我。而你竟然没听说过我！」

一阵咳嗽又把他满腔愤懑的滔滔话语噎在半途。

她给了他一个女人的回答：「你便是王国里最显赫的爵爷，或是从巴黎城壕里偷鸭子的最下作的贼，我也不在乎。我只知道我爱你，弗朗索瓦。」

===Original===
For a long time he kept silence, blinking, peering quizzically at her lifted face. She did love him; no questioning that. But presently he again put her aside, and went toward the open window. This was a matter for consideration.

The night was black as a pocket. Staring into it, François threw back his head and drew a deep, tremulous breath. The rising odor of roses and mignonette, keen and intolerably sweet, had roused unforgotten pulses in his blood, had set shame and joy adrum in his breast.

The woman loved him! Through these years, with a woman’s unreasoning fidelity, she had loved him. He knew well enough how matters stood between her and Noël d’Arnaye; the host of the Crowned Ox had been garrulous that evening. But it was François whom she loved. She was well-to-do. Here for the asking was a competence, love, an ingleside of his own. The deuce of it was that François feared to ask.

===Chinese===
他沉默了好久，眨着眼，狡黠地打量她仰起的脸。她确实爱他；这毋庸置疑。可不久他又把她轻轻推开，朝敞开的窗口走去。这是件需要斟酌的事。

夜黑得像只口袋。弗朗索瓦凝望黑暗，仰起头，深深吸了一口颤巍巍的气。升腾上来的玫瑰与木樨草的气息，浓烈得叫人消受不起，唤醒了他血脉中不曾遗忘的搏动，使羞愧与欢喜在他胸中擂起鼓来。

那女人爱他！这些年里，凭着女人那种不问缘由的忠贞，她一直爱着他。她与诺埃尔·达尔奈之间的事，他知道得一清二楚；加冕牛酒店的掌柜那晚很是健谈。可她爱的是他弗朗索瓦。她家境殷实。一份温饱、一份爱、一个自己的炉边，都唾手可得。糟糕的是，弗朗索瓦不敢去取。

===Original===
“—Because I am still past reason in all that touches this ignorant, hotheaded, Pharisaical, rather stupid wench! That is droll. But love is a resistless tyrant, and, Mother of God! has there been in my life a day, an hour, a moment when I have not loved her! To see her once was all that I had craved—as a lost soul might covet, ere the Pit take him, one splendid glimpse of Heaven and the Nine Blessed Orders at their fiddling. And I find that she loves me—me! Fate must have her jest, I perceive, though the firmament crack for it. She would have been content enough with Noël, thinking me dead. And with me?” Contemplatively he spat out of the window. “Eh, if I dared hope that this last flicker of life left in my crazy carcass might burn clear! I have but a little while to live; if I dared hope to live that little cleanly! But the next cup of wine, the next light woman?—I have answered more difficult riddles. Choose, then, François Villon—choose between the squalid, foul life yonder and her well-being. It is true that starvation is unpleasant and that hanging is reported to be even less agreeable. But just now these considerations are irrelevant.”

===Chinese===
「——因为在一切牵涉这个无知、急躁、法利赛气、相当愚蠢的丫头的事情上，我至今仍理智尽失！这可好笑。可爱是个无法抗拒的暴君，而且，圣母啊！我一生里可曾有过一天、一个时辰、一个瞬间不在爱她！见她一面，本是我贪求的全部——正如一个沉沦的魂灵，在深渊吞没他之前，贪求看一眼天堂的壮丽，看一眼九品天神奏乐的模样。而我发觉她爱我——爱我！我看明白了，命运非开她这个玩笑不可，哪怕苍穹为之迸裂。只当我死了，她同诺埃尔本可以过得心满意足。那么同我呢？」他若有所思地朝窗外啐了一口。「咳，倘若我敢指望，我这疯疯癫癫的皮囊里剩下的最后一线生命，能烧得清清亮亮！我活不了多久了；倘若我敢指望，把这点时日活得干干净净！可是下一杯酒、下一个轻贱的女人呢？——比这更难的谜，我也解开过。那么选吧，弗朗索瓦·维永——在那边污浊卑贱的活法与她的幸福之间，选一样。不错，饿肚子不好受，据说吊绞架的滋味更差。可眼下这些考虑都不相干。」

===Original===
Staring into the darkness he fought the battle out. Squarely he faced the issue; for that instant he saw François Villon as the last seven years had made him, saw the wine-sodden soul of François Villon, rotten and weak and honeycombed with vice. Moments of nobility it had; momentarily, as now, it might be roused to finer issues; but François knew that no power existent could hearten it daily to curb the brutish passions. It was no longer possible for François Villon to live cleanly. “For what am I?—a hog with a voice. And shall I hazard her life’s happiness to get me a more comfortable sty? Ah, but the deuce of it is that I so badly need that sty!”

He turned with a quick gesture.

===Chinese===
他凝望着黑暗，把这场仗打到了底。他把症结端端正正摆在眼前；那一瞬，他看清了这七年把弗朗索瓦·维永造就成了什么，看清了弗朗索瓦·维永那被酒泡透的灵魂——腐朽、软弱、被罪恶蛀成了蜂窝。它自有高贵的时刻；一时之间，比如眼下，也能被唤醒，趋向更高的事；可弗朗索瓦知道，世间再没有任何力量，能天天给它打气，去驾驭那些兽性的情欲。弗朗索瓦·维永再不可能活得干净了。「我算什么？——一头会出声的猪。难道要我拿她一生的幸福作孤注，给自己换一个舒服些的猪圈？啊，可糟就糟在，我偏偏那么需要那个猪圈！」

他霍然转身。

===Original===
“Listen,” François said. “Yonder is Paris—laughing, tragic Paris, who once had need of a singer to proclaim her splendor and all her misery. Fate made the man; in necessity’s mortar she pounded his soul into the shape Fate needed. To king’s courts she lifted him; to thieves’ hovels she thrust him down; and past Lutetia’s palaces and abbeys and taverns and lupanars and gutters and prisons and its very gallows—past each in turn the man was dragged, that he might make the ‘Song of Paris.’ He could not have made it here in the smug Rue Saint Jacques. Well! the song is made, Catherine. So long as Paris endures, François Villon will be remembered. Villon the singer Fate fashioned as was needful: and, in this fashioning, Villon the man was damned in body and soul. And by God! the song was worth it!”

===Chinese===
「听着，」弗朗索瓦说。「那边是巴黎——笑着又悲着的巴黎，她曾需要一个歌人，来宣扬她全部的荣光与全部的苦难。命运造出了这个人；在『必要』的臼里，她把他的灵魂捣成命运所需的形状。她把他举上宫廷；又把他摔进贼窝；卢泰西亚（Lutetia）的宫殿、修道院、酒馆、妓院、阴沟、监狱，还有它那座绞架——这人被拖着一一走过，好让他写出《巴黎之歌》。这歌，在自鸣得意的圣雅克街上是写不出来的。好了！歌写成了，卡特琳。只要巴黎还在，弗朗索瓦·维永就不会被忘记。歌人维永，命运按所需把他锻造了出来：而在这一锻造里，作为人的维永，身与魂都被断送了。上帝在上！这歌值得！」

===Original===
She gave a startled cry and came to him, her hands fluttering toward his breast. “François!” she breathed.

It would not be good to kill the love in her face.

“You loved François de Montcorbier. François de Montcorbier is dead. The Pharisees of the Rue Saint Jacques killed him seven years ago, and that day François Villon was born. That was the name I swore to drag through every muckheap in France. And I have done it, Catherine. The Companions of the Cockleshell—eh, well, the world knows us. We robbed Guillamme Coiffier, we robbed the College of Navarre, we robbed the Church of Saint Maturin—I abridge the list of our gambols. Now we harvest. René de Montigny’s bones swing in the wind yonder at Montfaucon. Colin de Cayeux they broke on the wheel. The rest—in effect, I am the only one that justice spared—because I had diverting gifts at rhyming, they said. Pah! if they only knew! I am immortal, lass. *Exegi monumentum.* Villon’s glory and Villon’s shame will never die.”

===Chinese===
她惊呼一声，朝他奔来，双手颤颤地伸向他的胸口。「弗朗索瓦！」她轻声道。

她脸上那份爱，不忍掐灭。

「你爱过的是弗朗索瓦·德·蒙科尔比耶。弗朗索瓦·德·蒙科尔比耶已经死了。七年前，圣雅克街的法利赛人杀死了他，就在那一天，弗朗索瓦·维永诞生了。那便是我发誓要拖过法兰西每一堆粪土的名字。而我做到了，卡特琳。贝壳帮的弟兄——嗐，世人都知道我们。我们抢过纪尧姆·夸菲耶，抢过纳瓦尔学院，抢过圣马蒂兰教堂——我们那些『丰功伟绩』，我就不一一列举了。如今开始收割。勒内·德·蒙蒂尼的骨头在那边蒙福孔的风里晃荡。科兰·德·卡约被处以轮刑。其余的人——说起来，唯独我一人得王法豁免——据说是因为我编韵的本领颇具消遣之效。呸！他们哪里知道！我是不朽的，姑娘。Exegi monumentum（我已筑起我的纪念碑）。维永的光荣与维永的耻辱都不会死。」

===Original===
He flung back his bald head and laughed now, tittering over that calamitous, shabby secret between all-seeing God and François Villon. She had drawn a little away from him. This well-reared girl saw him exultant in infamy, steeped to the eyes in infamy. But still the nearness of her, the faint perfume of her, shook in his veins, and still he must play the miserable comedy to the end, since the prize he played for was to him peculiarly desirable.

“A thief—a common thief!” But again her hands fluttered back. “I drove you to it. Mine is the shame.”

“Holy Macaire! what is a theft or two? Hunger that causes the wolf to sally from the wood, may well make a man do worse than steal. I could tell you—For example, you might ask in Hell of one Thevenin Pensete, who knifed him in the cemetery of Saint John.”

===Chinese===
他仰起秃头放声笑了，为自己与洞悉一切的上帝之间那桩又惨又寒碜的秘密吃吃而笑。她已从他身边稍稍退开。这位家教良好的姑娘看见他在恶名里欢腾得意，恶名浸到了眉眼。可她的亲近、她的幽香仍在他血管里震颤，他仍须把这出悲惨的喜剧演到底，因为他押上的那份彩头，对他格外要紧。

「贼——一个下贱的贼！」可她的手又颤颤地伸了回去。「是我把你逼到这一步的。耻辱归我。」

「圣马凯尔！偷一两回算得了什么？饥饿既能引得饿狼窜出树林，自然也能叫人干出比偷更糟的事。我倒可以告诉你——比如，你不妨到地狱里去问一位泰夫南·庞塞特（Thevenin Pensete），问是谁在圣约翰墓园里捅了他一刀。」

===Original===
He hinted a lie, for it was Montigny who killed Thevenin Pensete. Villon played without scruple now.

Catherine’s face was white. “Stop,” she pleaded; “no more, François—ah, Holy Virgin! do not tell me any more.”

But after a little she came to him, touching him almost as if with unwillingness. “Mine is the shame. It was my jealousy, my vanity, François, that thrust you back into temptation. And we are told by those in holy orders that the compassion of God is infinite. If you still care for me, I will be your wife.”

Yet she shuddered.

He saw it. His face, too, was paper, and François laughed horribly.

===Chinese===
他暗示的是句谎话，因为杀死泰夫南·庞塞特的其实是蒙蒂尼。维永此刻使起手段来，已毫无顾忌。

卡特琳的脸白了。「别说下去了，」她哀求道；「别再说了，弗朗索瓦——啊，圣母！别再告诉我了。」

可过了一会儿，她又走近他，触了触他，几乎像是出于不情愿。「耻辱归我。是我的妒忌、我的虚荣，弗朗索瓦，把你推回了诱惑里。神职人员告诉我们，上帝的怜悯是无穷的。倘若你还在意我，我愿做你的妻子。」

然而她打了个寒噤。

他看见了。他自己的脸也白得像纸，弗朗索瓦笑得可怕。

===Original===
“If I still love you! Go, ask of Denise, of Jacqueline, or of Pierrette, of Marion the Statue, of Jehanne of Brittany, of Blanche Slippermaker, of Fat Peg—ask of any trollop in all Paris how François Villon loves. You thought me faithful! You thought that I especially preferred you to any other bedfellow! Eh, I perceive that the credo of the Rue Saint Jacques is somewhat narrow-minded. For my part I find one woman much the same as another.” And his voice shook, for he saw how pretty she was, saw how she suffered. But he managed a laugh.

“I do not believe you,” Catherine said, in muffled tones. “François! You loved me, François. Ah, boy, boy!” she cried, with a pitiable wail; “come back to me, boy that I loved!”

===Chinese===
「我还在不在爱你！去问德妮丝（Denise）、雅克利娜（Jacqueline）、皮埃蕾特（Pierrette），问石像玛里翁（Marion the Statue）、布列塔尼的让娜（Jehanne of Brittany）、鞋匠娘布朗什（Blanche Slippermaker）、胖子佩格（Fat Peg）——去问全巴黎随便哪个荡妇，看弗朗索瓦·维永是怎么爱的。你还当我是忠心的！你还当我偏偏把你看得比任何别的同床人重！咦，我看圣雅克街的信经也未免太褊狭。依我看，一个女人同另一个女人差不太多。」他的声音发着颤，因为他看见她多么可爱，看见她多么受苦。可他还是挤出了一笑。

「我不信，」卡特琳闷声说。「弗朗索瓦！你爱过我，弗朗索瓦。啊，少年，少年！」她喊道，声音哀得可怜；「回到我这儿来吧，我爱过的少年！」

===Original===
It was a difficult business. But he grinned in her face.

“He is dead. Let François de Montcorbier rest in his grave. Your voice is very sweet, Catherine, and—and he could refuse you nothing, could he, lass? Ah, God, God, God!” he cried, in his agony; “why can you not believe me? I tell you Necessity pounds us in her mortar to what shape she will. I tell you that Montcorbier loved you, but François Villon prefers Fat Peg. An ill cat seeks an ill rat.” And with this, tranquillity fell upon his soul, for he knew that he had won.

Her face told him that. Loathing was what he saw there.

“I am sorry,” Catherine said, dully. “I am sorry. Oh, for high God’s sake! go, go! Do you want money? I will give you anything if you will only go. Oh, beast! Oh, swine, swine, swine!”

He turned and went, staggering like a drunken person.

===Chinese===
这是桩难办的差事。可他冲着她的脸咧嘴笑了。

「他死了。让弗朗索瓦·德·蒙科尔比耶在他的坟里安息吧。你的嗓音很甜，卡特琳，而——而他什么也不会拒绝你，对不对，姑娘？啊，上帝，上帝，上帝！」他痛苦地喊道；「你为什么就不能信我？我告诉你，『必要』在她的臼里，把我们捣成她所要的任何形状。我告诉你，蒙科尔比耶爱的是你，弗朗索瓦·维永中意的是胖子佩格。歪猫专寻歪老鼠。」说完这话，他的灵魂归于安宁，因为他知道自己赢了。

她的脸告诉了他。他看见的是憎恶。

「对不起，」卡特琳木然说。「对不起。哦，看在至高上帝的份上！走吧，走吧！你要钱么？只要你肯走，我什么都给。哦，畜生！哦，猪，猪，猪！」

他转身走了，像醉汉一样踉踉跄跄。

===Original===
Once in the garden he fell prone upon his face in the wet grass. About him the mingled odor of roses and mignonette was sweet and heavy; the fountain plashed interminably in the night, and above him the chestnuts and acacias rustled and lisped as they had done seven years ago. Only he was changed.

“O Mother of God,” the thief prayed, “grant that Noël may be kind to her! Mother of God, grant that she may be happy! Mother of God, grant that I may not live long!”

And straightway he perceived that triple invocation could be, rather neatly, worked out in ballade form. Yes, with a separate prayer to each verse. So, dismissing for the while his misery, he fell to considering, with undried cheeks, what rhymes he needed.

===Chinese===
一进花园，他便脸朝下扑倒在湿草里。四周，玫瑰与木樨草交叠的香气又甜又沉；泉水在夜里没完没了地淙淙作响，头顶上，栗树和洋槐像七年前一样沙沙低语。只是他变了。

「哦，圣母啊，」这贼祷告道，「求你叫诺埃尔善待她！圣母啊，求你叫她幸福！圣母啊，求你叫我活不长久！」

随即他便察觉，这三重祈愿大可相当巧妙地铺排成一首谣曲体。是的，每节各配一段祷词。于是，他暂且把悲苦撇在一旁，带着未干的脸颊，琢磨起自己需要哪些韵脚来。

## VI / VI

The Episode Called the Conspiracy of Arnaye / 所谓阿尔奈阴谋

===Original===
July 17, 1484

“Et puis il se rencontre icy une avanture merveilleuse, c’est que le fils de Grand Turc ressemble à Cléonte, à peu de chose prés.”

===Chinese===
1484年7月17日

（法语）
“Et puis il se rencontre icy une avanture merveilleuse, c’est que le fils de Grand Turc ressemble à Cléonte, à peu de chose prés.”
（意译）
况且此处又恰巧遇上一桩奇妙的冒险：那土耳其大皇帝的公子，竟与克雷昂特生得相似，所差不过毫厘。

===Original===
Noël d’Arnaye and Catherine de Vaucelles were married in the September of 1462, and afterward withdrew to Noël’s fief in Picardy. There Noël built him a new Château d’Arnaye, and through the influence of Nicole Beaupertuys, the King’s mistress (who was rumored in court byways to have a tenderness for the handsome Noël), obtained large grants for its maintenance. Madame d’Arnaye, also, it is gratifying to record, appears to have lived in tolerable amity with Sieur Noël, and neither of them pried too closely into the other’s friendships.

Catherine died in 1470, and Noël outlived her but by three years. Of the six acknowledged children surviving him, only one was legitimate—a daughter called Matthiette. The estate and title thus reverted to Raymond d’Arnaye, Noël’s younger brother, from whom the present family of Arnaye is descended.

Raymond was a far shrewder man than his predecessor. For ten years’ space, while Louis XI, that royal fox of France, was destroying feudalism piecemeal—trimming its power day by day as you might pare an onion—the new Sieur d’Arnaye steered his shifty course between France and Burgundy, always to the betterment of his chances in this world however he may have modified them in the next. At Arras he fought beneath the orifiamme, at Guinegate you could not have found a more staunch Burgundian: though he was no warrior, victory followed him like a lapdog. So that presently the Sieur d’Arnaye and the Vicomte de Puysange—with which family we have previously concerned ourselves—were the great lords of Northern France.

But after the old King’s death came gusty times for Sieur Raymond. It is with them we have here to do.

===Chinese===
诺埃尔·达尔奈（Noël d'Arnaye）与卡特琳·德·沃塞勒（Catherine de Vaucelles）于1462年9月成婚，随后退居到诺埃尔在皮卡第（Picardy）的封地。诺埃尔在那里给自己新建了一座阿尔奈城堡（Château d'Arnaye），又借着国王的情妇妮科尔·博珀蒂伊（Nicole Beaupertuys）的关照——宫闱僻径上风传，她对英俊的诺埃尔颇有几分柔情——为城堡的维系讨得了大宗的赏赐。同样令人欣慰的是，据载达尔奈夫人与诺埃尔大人相处得还算和睦，两口子对彼此的情谊，都不曾追根究底。

卡特琳卒于1470年，诺埃尔也只比她多活了三年。他身后留下六名公开承认的子女，其中唯有一个是合法婚生——一个名叫玛蒂埃特（Matthiette）的女儿。于是家业与爵号归于诺埃尔的胞弟雷蒙·达尔奈（Raymond d'Arnaye），如今的阿尔奈一族便是他的后裔。

雷蒙比他的前人精明得多。整整十年间，当法兰西那只王家狐狸路易十一（Louis XI）正把封建制度一鳞半爪地拆毁——日复一日地削它的权势，好比削一只洋葱——新任达尔奈大人便驾着他那见风转舵的航路，周旋于法兰西与勃艮第（Burgundy）之间，无论来世的前程如何改动，今生的际遇总归蒸蒸日上。在阿拉斯（Arras），他战于奥里弗拉姆王旗（oriflamme）之下；到了基内加特（Guinegate），你再找不出比他更铁杆的勃艮第人：虽说并非将才，胜利却像条哈巴狗似的尾随着他。于是不多时，达尔奈大人与皮桑日子爵（Vicomte de Puysange）——那一家人我们先前已经讲过——便成了北法兰西最大的领主。

但老王驾崩之后，雷蒙大人的日子便风雨飘摇起来。我们要讲的正是这段时局。

### I / I

Policy Tempered with Singing / 政略掺着歌声

===Original===
“And so,” said the Sieur d’Arnaye, as he laid down the letter, “we may look for the coming of Monsieur de Puysange tomorrow.”

The Demoiselle Matthiette contorted her features in an expression of disapproval. “So soon!” said she. “I had thought—”

“*Ouais*, my dear niece, Love rides by ordinary with a dripping spur, and is still as arbitrary as in the day when Mars was taken with a net and amorous Jove bellowed in Europa’s kail-yard. My faith! if Love distemper thus the spectral ichor of the gods, is it remarkable that the warmer blood of man pulses rather vehemently at his bidding? It were the least of Cupid’s miracles that a lusty bridegroom of some twenty-and-odd should be pricked to outstrip the dial by a scant week. For love—I might tell you such tales—”

===Chinese===
「这么说来，」达尔奈大人放下信，说道，「我们可以指望德·皮桑日先生明天就到了。」

玛蒂埃特小姐把五官拧成一副不以为然的表情。「这么快！」她说，「我还以为——」

「可不是嘛（Ouais），我亲爱的侄女，爱情素来是打着湿淋淋的马刺赶路的，而且它至今依旧任性如初，同当年玛尔斯（Mars）被罗网捉住、多情的朱庇特（Jove）在欧罗巴（Europa）家菜园里吼叫的那个时代不相上下。我的天！倘若爱情连诸神那种幽冥的灵液都搅扰得动，那么人身上更热的血受它驱遣而搏动得颇为激烈，又何足为奇呢？一个二十好几的健壮新郎，被它鞭策着比日晷上标定的日子提前了整整一个星期——这在丘比特（Cupid）的诸般奇迹里，要算最不足道的一桩了。说起爱情——我本可以给你讲几桩这样的故事——」

===Original===
Sieur Raymond crossed his white, dimpled hands over a well-rounded paunch and chuckled reminiscently; had he spoken doubtless he would have left Master Jehan de Troyes very little to reveal in his Scandalous Chronicle: but now, as if now recalling with whom Sieur Raymond conversed, d’Arnaye’s lean face assumed an expression of placid sanctity, and the somewhat unholy flame died out of his green eyes. He was like no other thing than a plethoric cat purring over the follies of kittenhood. You would have taken oath that a cultured taste for good living was the chief of his offences, and that this benevolent gentleman had some sixty well-spent years to his credit. True, his late Majesty, King Louis XI, had sworn *Pacque Dieu!* that d’Arnaye loved underhanded work so heartily that he conspired with his gardener concerning the planting of cabbages, and within a week after his death would be heading some treachery against Lucifer; but kings are not always infallible, as his Majesty himself had proven at Peronne.

===Chinese===
雷蒙大人把白皙、带着酒窝的双手交叠在滚圆的肚皮上，追怀往事似的呵呵而笑；他若真讲出来，只怕要让让·德·特鲁瓦（Jehan de Troyes）师傅在那部《丑闻编年史》里无甚可揭的了：不过此刻，仿佛忽然想起了自己在同谁说话，达尔奈那张清瘦的脸换上一副恬静圣洁的表情，绿眼睛里那一点不甚圣洁的火光也随之熄灭了。他活像一只脑满肠肥的猫，为幼年的荒唐事打着呼噜。你大可以起誓说，此人最大的罪过无非是讲究饮食的雅趣，这位慈祥的绅士攒下了六十来年清清白白的好光阴。诚然，先王路易十一陛下曾指「帕克·迪厄（Pacque Dieu，天主在上）」而誓，说达尔奈太爱偷偷摸摸的勾当，连栽个白菜都要同园丁密谋一番，死后不出一星期，保准又去领头对路西法（Lucifer）图谋不轨；可是君王未必永远圣明无误，这一点陛下本人在佩罗讷（Péronne）就已经亲身验证过了。

===Original===
“—For,” said the Sieur d’Arnaye, “man’s flesh is frail, and the devil is very cunning to avail himself of the weaknesses of lovers.”

“Love!” Matthiette cried. “Ah, do not mock me, my uncle! There can be no pretence of love between Monsieur de Puysange and me. A man that I have never seen, that is to wed me of pure policy, may look for no Alcestis in his wife.”

“You speak like a very sensible girl,” said Sieur Raymond, complacently. “However, so that he find her no Guinevere or Semiramis or other loose-minded trollop of history, I dare say Monsieur de Puysange will hold to his bargain with indifferent content. Look you, niece, he, also, is buying—though the saying is somewhat rustic—a pig in a poke.”

===Chinese===
「——因为，」达尔奈大人说，「人的血肉是软弱的，魔鬼又极其狡猾，最会利用情人们的弱点。」

「爱情！」玛蒂埃特叫道，「啊，叔父，别嘲弄我了！德·皮桑日先生和我之间，谈不上什么爱情。一个我从未谋面的男人，一门纯粹出于权谋的亲事，就休想在新娘身上寻出什么阿尔刻斯提斯（Alcestis）来。」

「你这话说得像个十分明白事理的姑娘，」雷蒙大人沾沾自喜地说。「不过，只要他发现她不是桂内维尔（Guinevere），不是塞弥拉弥斯（Semiramis），也不是史册上任何别的水性杨花的荡妇，我料想德·皮桑日先生总会守着这桩买卖，心满意足——或者说，不咸不淡地知足。你瞧，侄女，他也是在买——话虽粗鲁了些——隔着口袋买猪。」

===Original===
Matthiette glanced quickly toward the mirror which hung in her apartment. The glass reflected features which went to make up a beauty already be-sonneted in that part of France; and if her green gown was some months behind the last Italian fashion, it undeniably clad one who needed few adventitious aids. The Demoiselle Matthiette at seventeen was very tall, and was as yet too slender for perfection of form, but her honey-colored hair hung heavily about the unblemished oval of a countenance whose nose alone left something to be desired; for this feature, though well shaped, was unduly diminutive.
