“Take care!” I cried﻿—but too late, for my horse swerved under me as I spoke, and my lord marquis’ steed caught foot in a pile of lumber and fell heavily. He was up in a moment, unhurt, but the horse was lamed.

“You!” cried my Cousin Stephen. “Oh, but what fiend sends me this burr again!”

I said: “My fellow-madmen, it is all one if I have a taste for night-riding and the shedding of noble blood. Alack, though, that I have left my brave bauble at Tiverton! Had I that here, I might do such deeds! I might show such prowess upon the person of Monsieur de Puysange as your Nine Worthies would quake to hear of! For I have the honor to inform you, my doves, that we are captured.”

Indeed, we were in train to be, for even the two sound horses were well-nigh foundered: Blaise, the idle rogue, had not troubled to provide fresh steeds, so easy had the flitting seemed; and it was conspicuous that we would be overtaken in half an hour.

“So it seems,” said Stephen Allonby. “Well! one can die but once.” Thus speaking, he drew his sword with an air which might have been envied by Captain Leonidas at Thermopylae.

“Together, my heart!” she cried.

“Madonna,” said I, dismounting as I spoke, “pray you consider! With neither of you, is there any question of death; ’tis but that Monsieur de Puysange desires you to make a suitable match. It is not yet too late; his heart is kindly so long as he gets his will and profit everywhere, and he bears no malice toward my lord marquis. Yield, then, to your father’s wishes, since there is no choice.”

She stared at me, as thanks for this sensible advice. “And you﻿—is it you that would enter into the Castle of Content?” she cried, with a scorn that lashed.

===Chinese===
可是追兵稳稳地逼近：等我们停下来，寻路越过横跨埃克斯河的那座危桥时，他们的喧嚣已经很近了。

「当心！」我喊道——却迟了，话音未落，我的马已在我身下一歪，侯爵大人的坐骑在一堆木料里绊了蹄，沉沉摔倒。他转眼便跳了起来，人无恙，马却瘸了。

「是你！」斯蒂芬堂弟叫道，「嗐，是哪个魔鬼又把这芒刺给我送来了！」

我说：「我亲爱的疯子同伴们，就算我爱好夜驰、爱好流贵族的血，也都不打紧。可惜呀，我把好样的戏杖落在蒂弗顿了！有它在此，我该能立下怎样的功业啊！我该能在皮桑日先生身上施展怎样的武勇，保管九大英雄（Nine Worthies）听了都要发抖！因为我有幸通知各位，我的鸽子们：咱们被擒了。」

的确，被擒已是在所难免：连那两匹完好的马都快跑残了：布莱兹这懒骨头无赖，觉得私奔这事实在轻松，竟懒得备换脚力；而追兵不出半个钟头便要赶上我们，这是明摆着的。

「看来是这么回事，」斯蒂芬·阿隆比说，「好吧！人只能死一回。」说着他拔出剑来，那气概，温泉关的列奥尼达斯队长见了也要眼红。

「一同死，我的心肝！」她喊道。

「小姐，」我说着下了马，「请您三思！你们两位谁都没有性命之忧；不过是皮桑日先生盼您结一门合适的亲事。现在还不算晚；只要处处如了他的意、得了他的利，他的心肠是和善的，他对侯爵大人也并无恶意。既然别无选择，那就顺从您父亲的意愿吧。」

她对我瞪目而视，权当是对这句明白话的谢意。「还有你——想踏进知足城堡的，原来是你么？」她喊道，那轻蔑像鞭子一样抽人。

===Original===
I said: “Madonna, bethink you, you know naught of this man your father desires you to wed. Is it not possible that he, too, may love﻿—or may learn to love you, on provocation? You are very fair, madonna. Yours is a beauty that may draw a man to Heaven or unclose the gates of Hell, at will; indeed, even I, in my poor dreams, have seen your face as bright and glorious as is the lighted space above the altar when Christ’s blood and body are shared among His worshippers. Men certainly will never cease to love you. Will he﻿—your husband that may be﻿—prove less susceptible, we will say, than I? Ah, but, madonna, let us unrein imagination! Suppose, were it possible, that he﻿—even now﻿—yearns to enter into the Castle of Content, and that your hand, your hand alone, may draw the bolt for him﻿—that the thought of you is to him as a flame before which honor and faith shrivel as shed feathers, and that he has loved you these many years, unknown to you, long, long before the Marquis of Falmouth came into your life with his fair face and smooth sayings. Suppose, were it possible, that he now stood before you, every pulse and fibre of him racked with an intolerable ecstasy of loving you, his heart one vast hunger for you, Adeliza, and his voice shaking as my voice shakes, and his hands trembling as my hands tremble﻿—ah, see how they tremble, madonna, the poor foolish hands! Suppose, were it possible﻿—”

“Fool! O treacherous fool!” my cousin cried, in a fine rage.

She rested her fingertips upon his arm. “Hush!” she bade him; then turned to me an uncertain countenance that was half pity, half wonder. “Dear Will,” said she, “if you have ever known aught of love, do you not understand how I love Stephen here?”

But she did not any longer speak as a lord’s daughter speaks to the fool that makes mirth for his betters.

“In that case,” said I﻿—and my voice played tricks﻿—“in that case, may I request that you assist me in gathering such brushwood as we may find hereabout?”

===Chinese===
我说：「小姐，请您想想，您对令尊要您嫁的这个男人一无所知。他会不会也爱着您——或者，一经点拨，便会学着爱您，这难道不可能么？您生得极美，小姐。您这份美貌，随意之间，能把一个男人引上天堂，也能把地狱的大门豁然打开；真的，连我，在我卑微的梦里，也见过您的脸明亮荣光，一如信众们分领基督的血与身体时，祭坛上方那片灯火通明的所在。男人们对您的爱，自然永远不会止息。他——您未来的丈夫——的感受力，我们不妨这样说，难道会不如我么？啊，小姐，让咱们给想象松开缰绳吧！假如——倘若这可能——他也在此时渴望踏进知足城堡，而您的手，唯独您的手，能为他拔开门闩——假如对您的思念于他是一团烈焰，荣誉与信义在它面前如脱落的羽毛般蜷缩，假如他爱了您这么多年，您却一无所知，远在法尔茅斯侯爵带着漂亮脸蛋和甜言蜜语走进您生活之前很久很久。假如——倘若这可能——他此刻正站在您面前，每一脉搏、每一纤维都为爱您的狂喜而震裂，他的心是一片对您的无边饥渴，阿德莉扎，他的嗓音像我此刻的嗓音一样发颤，他的手像我此刻的手一样发抖——啊，瞧它们抖得多厉害，小姐，这双可怜的痴愚的手！假如——倘若这可能——」

「傻瓜！噢，奸猾的傻瓜！」我堂弟怒喝，怒得好不气派。

她把指尖搭上他的手臂。「嘘！」她止住他；随即向我转过一张惶惑的脸，半是怜悯，半是诧异。「亲爱的威尔，」她说，「倘若你也曾识得爱情的分毫，你难道不明白我多么爱这里的斯蒂芬么？」

只是她说话的口吻，已不再是贵人之女对以滑稽侍奉主子的傻瓜说话的口吻了。

「既然如此，」我说——我的嗓音也耍起了花招——「既然如此，可否请您助我一臂之力，把这附近寻得着的柴薪拾拢来？」

===Original===
They both stared at me now. “My lord,” I said, “the Exe is high, the bridge is of wood, and I have flint and steel in my pocket. The ford is five miles above and quite impassable. Do you understand me, my lord?”

He clapped his hands. “Oh, excellent!” he cried.

Then, each having caught my drift, we heaped up a pile of broken boughs and twigs and brushwood on the bridge, all three gathering it together. And I wondered if the moon, that is copartner in the antics of most rogues and lovers, had often beheld a sight more reasonless than the foregathering of a marquis, a peer’s daughter, and a fool at dead of night to make fagots.

When we had done I handed him the flint and steel. “My lord,” said I, “the honor is yours.”

“Udsfoot!” he murmured, in a moment, swearing and striking futile sparks, “but the late rain has so wet the wood that it will not kindle.”

I said, “Assuredly, in such matters a fool is indispensable.” I heaped before him the papers that made an honest woman of my mother and a marquis of me, and seizing the flint, I cast a spark among them that set them crackling cheerily. Oh, I knew well enough that patience would coax a flame from those twigs without my paper’s aid, but to be patient does not afford the posturing which youth loves. So it was a comfort to wreck all magnificently: and I knew that, too, as we three drew back upon the western bank and watched the writhing twigs splutter and snap and burn.

The bridge caught apace and in five minutes afforded passage to nothing short of the ardent equipage of the prophet Elias. Five minutes later the bridge did not exist: only the stone arches towered above the roaring waters that glistened in the light of the fire, which had, by this, reached the other side of the river, to find quick employment in the woods of Tiverton. Our pursuers rode through a glare which was that of Hell’s kitchen on baking-day, and so reached the Exe only to curse vainly and to shriek idle imprecations at us, who were as immune from their anger as though the severing river had been Pyriphlegethon.

===Chinese===
两人此刻都直瞪着我。「大人，」我说，「埃克斯河水位正高，桥是木头的，我兜里又有火石火镰。渡口在上游五英里外，完全过不去。您明白了么，大人？」

他一拍双手。「哦，妙极了！」他喊道。

随后，三人既都心领神会，便一齐动手，在桥上堆起一大垛断枝、细条和柴草。我不禁寻思：那轮月亮——大多数无赖与情人的胡闹都有它一份合伙——可曾见过比这更没道理的景象：一位侯爵、一位贵族之女、一个傻瓜，深更半夜聚在一处捆柴。

活计做完，我把火石火镰递给他。「大人，」我说，「这份光荣归您。」

「上帝的脚！」顷刻间他咕哝起来，一面赌着咒，一面打着无用的火星，「方才那场雨把柴禾淋得透湿，点不着啊。」

我说：「这种事上，傻瓜自然是少不了的。」我把那叠能还我母亲清白、封我侯爵的文书，在他面前堆作一堆，夺过火石，往里打出一粒火星，火星噼啪欢快地烧了起来。噢，我心里雪亮：单凭耐心，不用我文书帮衬，也能从那些枝条里哄出一簇火苗来；可是耐心这回事，摆不出青年人钟爱的那种漂亮架势。所以，痛痛快快地毁掉一切，反倒是一种安慰：这一点我也是明白的，当我们三人退到西岸，看着蜷曲的枝条噼啪爆响、断裂、燃烧。

火势迅速窜上桥身，不出五分钟，这桥便足可供先知以利亚（Elias）那驾烈火战车通行了。又过五分钟，桥已不复存在：只剩石拱高高耸立在咆哮的河水之上，河水在火光里闪烁；此时大火已烧到对岸，在蒂弗顿的树林里立刻找到了新的活计。我们的追兵穿过一片白炽的光焰——那是地狱厨房烤饼日的光景——赶到埃克斯河边，也只能徒然咒骂，朝我们尖声空嚎几句无用的毒誓；而我们任凭他们发怒，毫发无伤，仿佛隔开我们的不是一条河，而是火焰河皮利弗莱格通（Pyriphlegethon）。

===Original===
“My lord,” I presently suggested, “it may be that your priest expects you?”

“Indeed,” said he, laughing, “it is possible. Let us go.” Thereupon they mounted the two sound horses. “Most useful burr,” said he, “do you follow on foot to Teignmouth; and there﻿—”

“Sir,” I replied, “my home is at Tiverton.”

He wheeled about. “Do you not fear﻿—?”

“The whip?” said I. “Ah, my lord, I have been whipped ere this. It is not the greatest ill in life to be whipped.”

He began to protest.

“But, indeed, I am resolved,” said I. “Farewell!”

He tossed me his purse. “As you will,” he retorted, shortly. “We thank you for your aid; and if I am still master of Allonby﻿—”

“No fear of that!” I said. “Farewell, good cousin marquis! I cannot weep at your going, since it brings you happiness. And we have it on excellent authority that the laughter of fools is as the crackling of thorns under a pot. Accordingly, I bid you Godspeed in a discreet silence.”

I stood fumbling my cousin’s gold as he went forward into the night; but she did not follow.

“I am sorry﻿—” she began. She paused and the lithe fingers fretted with her horse’s mane.

I said: “Madonna, earlier in this crowded night, you told me of love’s nature: must my halting commentary prove the glose upon your text? Look, then, to be edified while the fool is delivered of his folly. For upon the maternal side, love was born of the ocean, madonna, and the ocean is but salt water, and salt water is but tears; and thus may love claim love’s authentic kin with sorrow. Ay, certainly, madonna, Fate hath ordained for her diversion that through sorrow alone we lovers may attain to the true Castle of Content.”

There was a long silence, and the wind wailed among the falling, tattered leaves. “Had I but known﻿—” said Adeliza, very sadly.

===Chinese===
「大人，」过了一会儿我提醒道，「您那位教士恐怕在等着您了吧？」

「可不是，」他笑着说，「很有可能。走吧。」说罢两人跨上两匹完好的马。「最有用的芒刺，」他说，「你步行随后来廷茅斯；到了那里——」

「老爷，」我答道，「我的家在蒂弗顿。」

他拨转马头。「你不怕——？」

「怕鞭子？」我说，「啊，大人，我从前也挨过鞭子。挨鞭子并不是人生里最大的祸事。」

他刚要分辩。

「可是，实话说，我主意已定，」我说，「别了！」

他把钱袋抛给我。「随你的便，」他没好气地回了一句，「多谢你的援手；倘若我日后还是阿隆比的主人——」

「这倒不必担心！」我说，「别了，好堂弟侯爵！您这一走，我不能洒泪，因为它带给您的是幸福。何况我们有极好的权威作证：傻瓜的笑声，如同锅下烧荆棘的爆响。因此，我以一种谨慎的沉默，祝您一路平安。」

他向夜色深处去了，我站在原地，摆弄着堂弟的金子；她却没有跟上。

「我很抱歉——」她开口道，随即停住，纤细的手指烦躁地拨弄着马鬃。

我说：「小姐，在这诸事纷纭的一夜里，您先前已对我讲过爱情的品性：如今莫非轮到我这段结结巴巴的注疏，来给您那段经文作注了？那么请听好，好领受教益，趁傻瓜分娩他的痴愚。因为从母系论起来，爱情生于海洋，小姐，而海洋不过是咸水，咸水不过是眼泪；所以爱情尽可认悲伤为本家骨肉。是啊，千真万确，小姐，命运女神为了自遣，早有钦定：我们这些恋人，唯有穿过悲伤，方能抵达真正的知足城堡。」

久久一片沉默，风在飘零的破叶间呜咽。「我若早知道——」阿德莉扎悲声说。

===Original===
I said: “Madonna, go forward and God speed you! Yonder your lover waits for you, and the world is exceedingly fair; here is only a fool. As for this new Marquis of Falmouth, let him trouble you no longer. ’Tis an Eastern superstition that we lackbrains are endowed with peculiar gifts of prophecy: and as such, I predict, very confidently, madonna, that you will see and hear no more of him in this life.”

I caught my breath. In the moonlight she seemed God’s master-work. Her eyes were big with half-comprehended sorrow, and a slender hand stole timorously toward me. I laughed, seeing how she strove to pity my great sorrow and could not, by reason of her great happiness. I laughed and was content. “As surely as God reigns in Heaven,” I cried aloud, “I am content, and this moment is well purchased with a marquisate!”

Indeed, I was vastly uplift and vastly pleased with my own nobleness, just then, and that condition is always a comfort.

More alertly she regarded me; and in her eyes I saw the anxiety and the wonder merge now into illimitable pity. “That, too!” she said, smiling sadly. “That, too, O son of Thomas Allonby!” And her mothering arms were clasped about me, and her lips clung and were one with my lips for a moment, and her tears were wet upon my cheek. She seemed to shield me, making of her breast my sanctuary.

“My dear, my dear, I am not worthy!” said Adeliza, with a tenderness I cannot tell you of; and presently she, too, was gone.

I mounted the lamed horse, who limped slowly up the river bank; very slowly we came out from the glare of the crackling fire into the cool darkness of the autumn woods; very slowly, for the horse was lamed and wearied, and patience is a discreet virtue when one journeys toward curses and the lash of a dog-whip: and I thought of many quips and jests whereby to soothe the anger of Monsieur de Puysange, and I sang to myself as I rode through the woods, a nobleman no longer, a tired Jack-pudding whose tongue must save his hide.

Sang I:

“The towers are fallen; no laughter rings
Through the rafters, charred and rent;
The ruin is wrought of all goodly things
In the Castle of Content.

“Ei ho! Ei ho! the Castle of Content,
Rased in the Land of Youth, where mirth was meant!
Nay, all is ashes there; and all in vain
Hand-shadowed eyes turn backward, to regain
Disastrous memories of that dear domain—
Ei ho! the vanished Castle of Content!”

===Chinese===
我说：「小姐，往前走吧，愿上帝送您一程！那边您的情郎等着您，这世界也美极了；这里只有一个傻瓜。至于这位新任的法尔茅斯侯爵，别再让他搅扰您了。东方有种迷信，说我们这些缺心眼的，偏偏得天独厚，有预言的异禀：既有此说，我便满怀自信地预言，小姐：今生今世，您再见不到他，再听不到他的音讯。」

我屏住了呼吸。月光里，她俨然是上帝的杰作。她的眼睛睁得大大的，盛着似懂非懂的悲伤，一只纤手怯生生地向我伸来。我笑了——我看得出，她多么想怜悯我的大悲伤，却办不到，只因为她的幸福太大。我笑着，心满意足。「上帝在上天为王，千真万确，」我高声道，「我心满意足，这一刻，拿一个侯爵位来换，是笔好买卖！」

的确，那一刻我大大地飘飘然，又大大地为自己的高尚得意，而这种心境，从来都是一种安慰。

她更专注地端详我；在她眼里，我看见焦虑与诧异此刻融成了无边的怜悯。「还有这个！」她凄然笑着说，「还有这个，噢，托马斯·阿隆比的儿子！」她的双臂慈母般搂住了我，她的唇贴上来，与我的唇合了一会儿，她的泪湿在我的脸颊上。她仿佛在护庇我，把她的胸膛作我的圣所。

「我亲爱的，我亲爱的，我不配！」阿德莉扎说，那柔情我无法向你们言传；转眼间，她也去了。

我跨上那匹瘸马，它一瘸一拐、缓缓走上河岸；我们缓缓地走出爆响的火光，走进秋日树林清凉的黑暗；走得极慢，因为马又瘸又乏，也因为当一个人此行的前程是咒骂与狗鞭的时候，耐心是一种识趣的美德：一路上我盘算着许多俏皮话和笑话，好平息皮桑日先生的怒气；我穿林而行，对自己唱着——贵族是做不成了，只剩一个疲惫的滑稽艺人，全凭这条舌头保住他那张皮。

我唱道：

「塔楼已倾颓；再没有笑声
穿过烧焦撕裂的椽木；
一切美好的事物俱已毁弃，
在知足的城堡。

「唉呵！唉呵！知足城堡，
夷平在青春之原，那本为欢乐而设的地角！
不，那里尽是灰烬；徒然地
手搭凉棚的眼睛回望，想追回
那片可爱疆土的种种劫毁的记忆——
唉呵！那湮没了的知足城堡！」

## VIII / VIII

The Episode Called in Ursula’s Garden / 所谓厄休拉的花园

===Original===
May 27, 1559

“‘O welladay!’ said Beichan then,

‘That I so soon have married thee!

For it can be none but Susie Pie,

That sailed the sea for love of me.’”

===Chinese===
1559年5月27日

（英格兰边区古谣《少年贝肯》）
“‘O welladay!’ said Beichan then,
‘That I so soon have married thee!
For it can be none but Susie Pie,
That sailed the sea for love of me.’”
（意译）
「『唉，苦也！』贝肯当时说道，
『我竟这样快便娶了你！
因为渡海来爱我的人儿，
除了苏茜·派，再无别个。』」

===Original===
How Will Sommers encountered the Marchioness of Falmouth in the Cardinal’s house at Whitehall, and how in Windsor Forest that noble lady died with the fool’s arms about her, does not concern us here. That is matter for another tale.

You are not, though, to imagine any scandal. Barring an affair with Sir Henry Rochford, and another with Lord Norreys, and the brief interval in 1525 when the King was enamored of her, there is no record that the marchioness ever wavered from the choice her heart had made, or had any especial reason to regret it.

So she lived and died, more virtuously and happily than most, and found the marquis a fair husband, as husbands go, and bore him three sons and a daughter.

But when the ninth Marquis of Falmouth died long after his wife, in the November of 1557, he was survived by only one of these sons, a junior Stephen, born in 1530, who at his father’s demise succeeded to the title. The oldest son, Thomas, born 1531, had been killed in Wyatt’s Rebellion in 1554, the second, George, born 1526, with a marked look of the King, was, in February, 1556, stabbed in a disreputable tavern brawl.

Now we have to do with the tenth Marquis of Falmouth’s suit for the hand of Lady Ursula Heleigh, the Earl of Brudenel’s co-heiress. You are to imagine yourself at Longaville Court, in Sussex, at a time when Anne Bullen’s daughter was very recently become Queen of England.

===Chinese===
威尔·萨默斯（Will Sommers）如何在白厅枢机主教的宅邸里同法尔茅斯侯爵夫人相遇，那位高贵的夫人又如何在温莎森林里、在弄臣的怀抱中咽气，都不干我们这里的事。那是另一则故事的题材。

不过，诸位却切莫就此想象出什么丑闻来。除了与亨利·罗奇福德爵士（Sir Henry Rochford）的一段私情，与诺里斯勋爵（Lord Norreys）的另一段，再加上 1525 年国王为她倾倒的那段短短时光，再无任何记载表明，侯爵夫人曾背弃她心中作下的抉择，或有什么特别的缘由去追悔。

于是她生前死后，都比大多数人更有德行、更快活，也觉得侯爵做丈夫还算像样——就照一般丈夫的标准——为他生下三个儿子和一个女儿。

但第九代法尔茅斯侯爵比亡妻晚去世多年，于 1557 年 11 月辞世时，几个儿子中仅存一位在世：生于 1530 年的小斯蒂芬，父亲一死便承袭了爵位。长子托马斯生于 1531 年，死于 1554 年的怀亚特叛乱；次子乔治生于 1526 年，眉眼间明显带着国王的模样，1556 年 2 月在一场声名狼藉的酒店斗殴中被人刺死。

现在我们要讲的，是第十代法尔茅斯侯爵向布鲁德内尔伯爵（Earl of Brudenel）的共同继承人厄休拉·希利小姐（Lady Ursula Heleigh）求婚的事。诸位不妨想象自己身处萨塞克斯的朗加维尔府（Longaville Court），其时安妮·博林（Anne Bullen）的女儿新登英格兰王位未久。

### I / I

Love, and Love’s Mimic / 爱与爱的拟态

===Original===
Her three lovers had praised her with many canzonets and sonnets on that May morning as they sat in the rose-garden at Longaville, and the sun-steeped leaves made a tempered aromatic shade about them. Afterward they had drawn grass-blades to decide who should accompany the Lady Ursula to the summer pavilion, that she might fetch her viol and sing them a song of love, and in the sylvan lottery chance had favored the Earl of Pevensey.

===Chinese===
那个五月的早晨，她的三位爱慕者坐在朗加维尔的玫瑰园里，用许多短歌与十四行诗赞美她，沐满阳光的树叶在他们四周织成一片温润芬芳的浓荫。随后他们抽草茎占签，决定谁该陪厄休拉小姐去夏亭，好让她取来维奥尔琴，给他们唱一支情歌；在这场林间的抽签里，运气眷顾了佩文西伯爵（Earl of Pevensey）。

===Original===
Left to themselves, the Marquis of Falmouth and Master Richard Mervale regarded each the other, irresolutely, like strange curs uncertain whether to fraternize or to fly at one another’s throat. Then Master Mervale lay down in the young grass, stretched himself, twirled his thin black mustachios, and chuckled in luxurious content.

“Decidedly,” said he, “your lordship is past master in the art of wooing; no university in the world would refuse you a degree.”

The marquis frowned. He was a great bluff man, with wheat-colored hair, and was somewhat slow-witted. After a little he found the quizzical, boyish face that mocked him irresistible, and he laughed, and unbent from the dignified reserve which he had for a while maintained portentously.

===Chinese===
单独留下之后，法尔茅斯侯爵与理查德·默维尔先生（Master Richard Mervale）面面相觑，犹豫不决，像两条生分的狗，拿不准该亲热一番，还是扑上去咬断对方的喉咙。随后默维尔先生往嫩草里一躺，伸了个懒腰，捻着他那两撇细细的黑髭，惬意无比地咯咯直笑。

「毫无疑问，」他说，「大人在求爱的艺术上已是炉火纯青的宗师；世上没有一所大学肯拒授您一个学位。」

侯爵皱起眉头。他是个魁伟粗豪的汉子，一头麦色头发，头脑有些迟钝。过了一小会儿，他发觉那张揶揄他的、孩子气的促狭面孔叫人无法抗拒，便笑了，从他那煞有介事地端了一阵子的庄严矜持里松弛了下来。

===Original===
“Master Mervale,” said the marquis, “I will be frank with you, for you appear a lad of good intelligence, as lads run, and barring a trifle of affectation and a certain squeamishness in speech. When I would go exploring into a woman’s heart, I must pay my way in the land’s current coinage of compliments and high-pitched protestations. Yes, yes, such sixpenny phrases suffice the seasoned traveler, who does not ostentatiously display his gems while traveling. Now, in courtship, Master Mervale, one traverses ground more dubious than the Indies, and the truth, Master Mervale, is a jewel of great price.”

===Chinese===
「默维尔先生，」侯爵说，「我愿对您坦诚相待，因为就少年们的成色而论，您看来是个脑子灵光的小子——撇开几分做作、以及说话时某种假清高的忸怩不算。当我要深入勘探一个女人的心，我就得用这片国土上通行的钱币——恭维话和高声赌咒的表白——来付我的路费。是的，是的，这种六便士一枚的辞令，对老练的旅人尽够用了；老练的旅人上路，是不会招摇显摆自己的宝石的。要知道，在求婚这一路上，默维尔先生，人行过的地界比印度群岛还凶险叵测，而真理呢，默维尔先生，是一件重价的珠宝。」

===Original===
Master Mervale raised his eyebrows. “The truth?” he queried, gently. “Now how, I wonder, did your lordship happen to think of that remote abstraction.” For beyond doubt, Lord Falmouth’s wooing had been that morning of a rather florid sort.

However, “It would surely be indelicate,” the marquis suggested, “to allow even truth to appear quite unclothed in the presence of a lady?” He smiled and took a short turn on the grass. “Look you, Master Mervale,” said he, narrowing his pale-blue eyes to slits, “I have, somehow, a disposition to confidence come upon me. Frankly, my passion for the Lady Ursula burns more mildly than that which Antony bore the Egyptian; it is less a fire to consume kingdoms than a candle wherewith to light a contented home; and quite frankly, I mean to have her. The estates lie convenient, the families are of equal rank, her father is agreed, and she has a sufficiency of beauty; there are, in short, no obstacles to our union save you and my lord of Pevensey, and these, I confess, I do not fear. I can wait, Master Mervale. Oh, I am patient, Master Mervale, but, I own, I cannot brook denial. It is I, or no one. By Saint Gregory! I wear steel at my side, Master Mervale, that will serve for other purposes save that of opening oysters!” So he blustered in the spring sunlight, and frowned darkly when Master Mervale appeared the more amused than impressed.

===Chinese===
默维尔先生扬起眉毛。「真理？」他轻轻地疑问道，「我倒纳闷，大人您怎么会偏偏想起这件遥远得近乎抽象的东西。」因为毫无疑问，法尔茅斯勋爵那天早晨的求爱，是相当浓艳华丽的一路。

不过，「话说回来，」侯爵提议道，「让真理在一位女士面前一丝不挂地露面，总归有失体统吧？」他微微一笑，在草上踱了个短圈。「您瞧着，默维尔先生，」他说，淡蓝的眼睛眯成两道细缝，「不知怎的，我心里忽然生出一股倾吐的兴致。坦白说，我对厄休拉小姐的情火，烧得比安东尼对那埃及女人的温和些；它算不上一场焚毁王国的烈焰，倒更像一支点亮安乐人家的蜡烛；而且十分坦白地说，我打定主意要娶她。田产毗连相宜，两家门第相当，她父亲已经应允，她的美貌也尽够了；简而言之，我们的结合没有任何阻碍，除了您和佩文西勋爵两位——而这两位，我承认，我并不惧怕。我等得起，默维尔先生。哦，我有的是耐性，默维尔先生，不过，我承认，我受不得拒绝。非我莫属，否则谁也别想。以圣格列高利起誓！我腰间佩的这钢，默维尔先生，除了起牡蛎，还派得上别的用场！」他就这样在春日的阳光里咋咋呼呼，见默维尔先生似乎受用的少、觉得好玩的多，便又阴沉沉皱起了眉头。

===Original===
“Your patience shames Job the Patriarch,” said Master Mervale, “yet, it seems to me, my lord, you do not consider one thing. I grant you that Pevensey and I are your equals neither in estate nor reputation; still, setting modesty aside, is it not possible the Lady Ursula may come, in time, to love one of us?”

“Setting common sense aside,” said the marquis, stiffly, “it is possible she may be smitten with the smallpox. Let us hope, however, that she may escape both of these misfortunes.”

===Chinese===
「大人的耐性，连族长约伯也要自愧不如，」默维尔先生说，「不过依我看，大人，您有一层没有虑到。我承认，佩文西和我，论门第论声名都不及您；可是，把谦虚撇开不谈，厄休拉小姐难道就没有可能，久而久之，爱上我们中的一个么？」

「把常识撇开不谈，」侯爵板着脸说，「她也有可能染上天花。不过，我们还是指望她这两桩灾祸都能幸免吧。」

===Original===
The younger man refrained from speech for a while. Presently, “You liken love to a plague,” he said, “yet I have heard there was once a cousin of the Lady Ursula’s﻿—a Mistress Katherine Beaufort﻿—”

“Swounds!” Lord Falmouth had wheeled about, scowled, and then tapped sharply upon the palm of one hand with the nail-bitten fingers of the other. “Ay,” said he, more slowly, “there was such a person.”

“She loved you?” Master Mervale suggested.

“God help me!” replied the marquis; “we loved each other! I know not how you came by your information, nor do I ask. Yet, it is ill to open an old wound. I loved her; let that suffice.” With a set face, he turned away for a moment and gazed toward the high parapets of Longaville, half-hidden by pale foliage and very white against the rain-washed sky; then groaned, and glared angrily into the lad’s upturned countenance. “You talk of love,” said the marquis; “a love compounded equally of youthful imagination, a liking for fantastic phrases and a disposition for caterwauling i’ the moonlight. Ah, lad, lad!﻿—if you but knew! That is not love; to love is to go mad like a star-struck moth, and afterward to strive in vain to forget, and to eat one’s heart out in the loneliness, and to hunger﻿—hunger﻿—” The marquis spread his big hands helplessly, and then, with a quick, impatient gesture, swept back the mass of wheat-colored hair that fell about his face. “Ah, Master Mervale,” he sighed, “I was right after all﻿—it is the cruelest plague in the world, and that same smallpox leaves less troubling scars.”

===Chinese===
年轻人一时缄口不言。过了一会儿，「您把爱比作瘟疫，」他说，「可我听说，厄休拉小姐从前有一位表姐——一位凯瑟琳·博福特小姐（Mistress Katherine Beaufort）——」

「该死！」法尔茅斯勋爵霍地转过身，横眉立目，随即用一只手那咬秃了指甲的手指，笃笃地敲着另一只手的掌心。「唉，」他放慢了声调，「是有这么一个人。」

「她爱过您？」默维尔先生提点道。

「上天饶恕我！」侯爵答道，「是我们彼此相爱！您这消息从何而来，我不知道，也不问。只是，揭旧伤疤终归不是好事。我爱过她；有这一句尽够了。」他板起面孔，掉头片刻，望向朗加维尔府高高的雉堞——那雉堞半掩在灰白的树叶后，衬着雨洗过的天空，白得晃眼；随后他呻吟一声，怒冲冲地瞪着少年仰起的脸。「您谈论爱，」侯爵说，「那种爱，是少年的想象、对浮夸辞令的癖好、外加在月光下学猫叫春的天性，三者均等调和而成。啊，小子，小子！——您若知道就好了！那不是爱；爱，是像扑向星辰的飞蛾一样发狂，过后又徒劳地竭力忘却，在孤独里把心一点点熬干，是饥饿——饥饿——」侯爵无可奈何地摊开他那双大手，随即一个急躁的手势，把垂在脸上的麦色头发统统拂了回去。「啊，默维尔先生，」他叹道，「我到底还是说对了——那是世上最狠的瘟疫，就是天花留下的疤，都没它恼人。」

===Original===
“Yet,” Master Mervale said, with courteous interest, “you did not marry?”

“Marry!” His lordship snarled toward the sun and laughed. “Look you, Master Mervale, I know not how far y’are acquainted with the business. It was in Cornwall yonder years since; I was but a lad, and she a wench﻿—Oh, such a wench, with tender blue eyes, and a faint, sweet voice that could deny me nothing! God does not fashion her like every day﻿—*Dieu qui la fist de ses deux mains*, saith the Frenchman.” The marquis paced the grass, gnawing his lip and debating with himself. “Marry? Her family was good, but their deserts outranked their fortunes; their crest was not the topmost feather in Fortune’s cap, you understand; somewhat sunken i’ the world, Master Mervale, somewhat sunken. And I? My father﻿—God rest his bones!﻿—was a cold, hard man, and my two elder brothers﻿—Holy Virgin, pray for them!﻿—loved me none too well. I was the cadet then: Heaven helps them that help themselves, says my father, and I ha’n’t a penny for you. My way was yet to make in the world; to saddle myself with a dowerless wench﻿—even a wench whose least ‘Good morning’ set a man’s heart hammering at his ribs﻿—would have been folly, Master Mervale. Utter, improvident, shiftless, bedlamite folly, lad!”

===Chinese===
「可是，」默维尔先生彬彬有礼、饶有兴味地问道，「您没有娶她？」

「结婚！」爵爷朝着太阳狺狺地笑了一声。「您瞧，默维尔先生，我不知道这事您知道多少。那是多年以前，在康沃尔；我还是个毛头小子，她是个姑娘——哦，那样一个姑娘，一双温柔的蓝眼睛，一副轻柔甜美的嗓音，对我从来拒绝不了什么！上帝不是每天都造得出她这样的人——*Dieu qui la fist de ses deux mains*（上帝用双手造了她），法国人是这么说的。」侯爵在草上来回踱着，咬着嘴唇，心里自己同自己交战。「结婚？她家门第不坏，可他们的功德胜过家财；他们家的纹章，够不上命运女神帽顶上最高的那根翎毛，您懂的；在世道里有些败落了，默维尔先生，有些败落了。而我呢？我父亲——愿他的骨头安息！——是个冷面硬心肠的人，我的两个哥哥——圣母啊，为他们祈祷吧！——都不怎么疼我。我那时是幼子：自助者天助，我父亲说，我可一个子儿也不给你。我的前程还得自己去挣；给自己套上一个没有嫁妆的姑娘——哪怕是一个连她最寻常的一句『早安』都能叫男人心头撞鹿的姑娘——那就是愚蠢了，默维尔先生。彻头彻尾、不顾将来、没出息、疯人院式的愚蠢，小子！」

===Original===
“H’m!” Master Mervale cleared his throat, twirled his mustachios, and smiled at some unspoken thought. “We pay for our follies in this world, my lord, but I sometimes think that we pay even more dearly for our wisdom.”

“Ah, lad, lad!” the marquis cried, in a gust of anger; “I dare say, as your smirking hints, it was a coward’s act not to snap fingers at fate and fathers and dare all! Well! I did not dare. We parted﻿—in what lamentable fashion is now of little import﻿—and I set forth to seek my fortune. Ho, it was a brave world then, Master Mervale, for all the tears that were scarce dried on my cheeks! A world wherein the heavens were as blue as a certain woman’s eyes﻿—a world wherein a likely lad might see far countries, waggle a good sword in Babylon and Tripolis and other ultimate kingdoms, beard the Muslim in his mosque, and at last fetch home﻿—though he might never love her, you understand﻿—a sultan’s daughter for his wife﻿—

===Chinese===
「哼！」默维尔先生清了清嗓子，捻着髭须，为某个没说出口的念头莞尔一笑。「我们在这世上为荒唐付出代价，大人；只是我有时想，我们为自己的明智付的代价更高。」

「啊，小子，小子！」侯爵一阵怒气冲冲地喊道，「照您那皮笑肉不笑的暗示，我敢说，不肯向命运和父亲们弹弹指头、不肯豁出去一搏，是懦夫行径了！好吧！我没敢。我们分了手——以怎样可悲的方式分的，如今已无关紧要——我便动身去闯天下。嚯，那可真是个美妙的世界，默维尔先生，尽管我颊上的泪痕还没干透！一个天蓝得同某一个女人眼睛一般的世界——一个有出息的小伙子在其中可以远游异邦，在巴比伦和的黎波里以及其他极远的国度里挥舞一柄好剑，闯进清真寺去捋穆斯林的胡子，临了还迎回家来——您要明白，尽管他永远不能爱她——一位苏丹的女儿做妻子——

===Original===
“With more gay gold about her middle

Than would buy half Northumberlee.”

His voice died away. He sighed and shrugged. “Eh, well!” said the marquis; “I fought in Flanders somewhat﻿—in Spain﻿—what matter where? Then, at last, sickened in Amsterdam, three years ago, where a messenger comes to haul me out of bed as future Marquis of Falmouth. One brother slain in a duel, Master Mervale; one killed in Wyatt’s Rebellion; my father dying, and﻿—Heaven rest his soul!﻿—not overeager to meet his Maker. There you have it, Master Mervale﻿—a right pleasant jest of Fortune’s perpetration﻿—I a marquis, my own master, fit mate for any woman in the kingdom, and Kate﻿—my Kate who was past human praising!﻿—vanished.”

===Chinese===
「她腰间缠着的鲜亮黄金，
只怕半个诺森伯兰都买得下来。」

他的声音渐渐低哑下去。他叹了口气，耸耸肩。「唉，也罢！」侯爵说，「我在佛兰德斯打过些仗——在西班牙——在哪里打又有什么要紧？后来，三年前，我在阿姆斯特丹病倒了，一个信使跑来把我从床上拖起来，说我成了法尔茅斯侯爵的继承人。一个哥哥死于决斗，默维尔先生；一个死在怀亚特叛乱里；我父亲垂死——愿他的灵魂安息！——却不大急着去见他的造物主。事情就是这样，默维尔先生——命运女神亲手炮制的一出妙极的恶作剧——我成了侯爵，自己做了自己的主，配得上王国里任何女子，而凯特——我的凯特，那是人间赞语都追不上的！——不见了。」

===Original===
“Vanished?” The lad echoed the word, with wide eyes.

“Vanished in the night, and no sign nor rumor of her since! Gone to seek me abroad, no doubt, poor wench! Dead, dead, beyond question, Master Mervale!” The marquis swallowed, and rubbed his lips with the back of his hand. “Ah, well!” said he; “it is an old sorrow!”

===Chinese===
「不见了？」少年睁大眼睛，重复了这个词。

「夜里不见了，从此没有一点踪迹、一句传闻！多半是到海外找我去啦，可怜的姑娘！死了，死了，毫无疑问，默维尔先生！」侯爵咽了口唾沫，用手背擦了擦嘴唇。「唉，也罢！」他说，「那是桩旧日的伤心事了！」

===Original===
The male animal shaken by strong emotion is to his brothers an embarrassing rather than a pathetic sight. Master Mervale, lowering his eyes discreetly, rooted up several tufts of grass before he spoke. Then, “My lord, you have known of love,” said he, very slowly; “does there survive no kindliness for aspiring lovers in you who have been one of us? My lord of Pevensey, I think, loves the Lady Ursula, at least, as much as you ever loved this Mistress Katherine; of my own adoration I do not speak, save to say that I have sworn never to marry any other woman. Her father favors you, for you are a match in a thousand; but you do not love her. It matters little to you, my lord, whom she may wed; to us it signifies a life’s happiness. Will not the memory of that Cornish lass﻿—the memory of moonlit nights, and of those sweet, vain aspirations and foiled daydreams that in boyhood waked your blood even to such brave folly as now possesses us﻿—will not the memory of these things soften you, my lord?”

===Chinese===
被强烈情感震撼的雄性动物，在同性兄弟眼里，与其说惹人怜悯，不如说令人尴尬。默维尔先生知趣地垂下眼睛，连根拔起几丛青草，方才开口。然后他说：「大人，您是懂得爱的；您既曾是我们中的一员，可还对满怀热望的恋人们存不下一点恻隐之心么？依我看，佩文西勋爵爱厄休拉小姐，至少不亚于您当年爱那位凯瑟琳·博福特小姐；至于我自己的倾慕，我不便自夸，只说一句：我发过誓，此生绝不另娶旁人。她父亲属意于您，因为您是千里挑一的良配；可您并不爱她。她嫁给谁，大人，于您无关痛痒；于我们，却系着一生一世的幸福。那位康沃尔姑娘的旧忆——那些月夜的记忆，还有那些甜美的、虚妄的渴望，那些在少年时代把您的血唤醒、直至燃成今日这般壮烈痴愚的破灭的白日梦——这些记忆，大人，就不能叫您心软一软么？」

===Original===
But Falmouth by this time appeared half regretful of his recent outburst, and somewhat inclined to regard his companion as a dangerously plausible young fellow who had very unwarrantably wormed himself into Lord Falmouth’s confidence. Falmouth’s heavy jaw shut like a trap.

“By Saint Gregory!” said he; “if ever such notions soften me at all, I pray to be in hell entirely melted! What I have told you of is past, Master Mervale; and a wise man does not meditate unthriftily upon spilt milk.”

“You are adamant?” sighed the boy.

“The nether millstone,” said the marquis, smiling grimly, “is in comparison a pillow of down.”

===Chinese===
可这会儿，法尔茅斯对自己方才的失态已有些懊悔，也有些把他这位同伴看作一个危险地能言善道的少年——这小子好没来由地花言巧语，套出了法尔茅斯勋爵的心事。法尔茅斯沉重的下颚像捕兽夹一样扣紧了。

「以圣格列高利起誓！」他说，「这样的念头若有一日真能叫我心软，我祈祷干脆到地狱里去熔个稀烂！我告诉您的事都过去了，默维尔先生；聪明人不对着泼翻的牛奶不知节俭地空想。」

「您是冥顽不化了？」少年叹道。

「比起您来，」侯爵狞笑着说，「磨盘底下那扇石也算得上鹅绒枕头了。」

===Original===
“Yet﻿—yet the milk was sweet, my lord?” the boy suggested, with a faint answering smile.

“Sweet!” The marquis’ voice had a deep tremor.

“And if the choice lay between Ursula and Katherine?”

“Oh, fool!﻿—Oh, pink-cheeked, utter ignorant fool!” the marquis groaned. “Did I not say you knew nothing of love?”

“Heigho!” Master Mervale put aside all glum-faced discussion, with a little yawn, and sprang to his feet. “Then we can but hope that somewhere, somehow, Mistress Katherine yet lives and in her own good time may reappear. And while we speak of reappearances﻿—surely the Lady Ursula is strangely tardy in making hers?”

The marquis’ jealousy when it slumbered slept with an open ear. “Let us join them,” he said, shortly, and he started through the gardens with quick, stiff strides.

===Chinese===
「可是——可是那牛奶原是甜的，大人？」少年含着一丝相应的浅笑试探道。

「甜的！」侯爵的声音深处一阵颤动。

「倘若要在厄休拉与凯瑟琳之间作一抉择呢？」

「哦，傻子！——哦，粉脸的、彻头彻尾无知的傻子！」侯爵呻吟道。「我不是早说过，您对爱一窍不通么？」

「哎嗨！」默维尔先生打个小小的呵欠，把一切哭丧着脸的讨论撂在一旁，一跃而起。「那我们只好指望，凯瑟琳不管在哪里、以何种方式，还活在人间，且在她方便的时候重新露面。说到重新露面——厄休拉小姐这一位，也未免露得太慢了吧？」

侯爵的妒意睡去时也竖着一只耳朵。「我们找他们去，」他简短地说，迈开又快又僵的步子，穿过花园而去。

### II / II

Song-Guerdon / 歌之酬报

===Original===
They went westward toward the summer pavilion. Presently the marquis blundered into the green gloom of the maze, laid out in the Italian fashion, and was extricated only by the superior knowledge of Master Mervale, who guided Falmouth skilfully and surely through manifold intricacies, to open daylight.

Afterward they came to a close-shaven lawn, where the summer pavilion stood beside the brook that widened here into an artificial pond, spread with lily-pads and fringed with rushes. The Lady Ursula sat with the Earl of Pevensey beneath a burgeoning maple-tree. Such rays as sifted through into their cool retreat lay like splotches of wine upon the ground, and there the taller grass-blades turned to needles of thin silver; one palpitating beam, more daring than the rest, slanted straight toward the little head of the Lady Ursula, converting her hair into a halo of misty gold, that appeared out of place in this particular position. She seemed a Bassarid who had somehow fallen heir to an aureole; for otherwise, to phrase it sedately, there was about her no clamant suggestion of saintship. At least, there is no record of any saint in the calendar who ever looked with laughing gray-green eyes upon her lover and mocked at the fervor and trepidation of his speech. This the Lady Ursula now did; and, manifestly, enjoyed the doing of it.

===Chinese===
他们朝西边的夏亭走去。不多时，侯爵一头撞进迷园绿沉沉的幽暗里——那园子是照意大利式样布置的——全亏默维尔先生识途的本领更胜一筹，引着法尔茅斯七绕八绕、又稳又巧地穿出重重盘径，重见天光。

随后他们走到一片修剪得溜平的草坪，夏亭便立在溪畔；溪水流到这里拓宽成一泓人工池塘，浮满荷叶，四缘长着灯芯草。厄休拉小姐同佩文西伯爵坐在一棵新叶初绽的枫树下。筛落进他们清凉幽居的光线，像一摊摊酒渍铺在地上，较高的草茎在那里化作根根细银针；有一道光比其余的都大胆，颤巍巍地直斜向厄休拉小姐的小脑袋，把她的头发变成一圈朦胧金色的光环——在这般所在，这光环显得格格不入。她仿佛一位巴萨里德狂女，不知怎的继承了一圈圣光；因为，说得庄重些，她周身实在没有半点昭彰的圣徒气象。至少，历书上还从无记载，哪位圣女曾用一双灰绿色的笑眼望着她的恋人，讥笑他言语的热切与惶恐。厄休拉小姐此刻正这样做着；而且显而易见，她做得津津有味。

===Original===
Within the moment the Earl of Pevensey took up the viol that lay beside them, and sang to her in the clear morning. He was sunbrowned and very comely, and his big, black eyes were tender as he sang to her sitting there in the shade. He himself sat at her feet in the sunlight.

Sang the Earl of Pevensey:

“Ursula, spring wakes about us﻿—

Wakes to mock at us and flout us

That so coldly do delay:

When the very birds are mating,

Pray you, why should we be waiting﻿—

We that might be wed today!

“‘Life is short,’ the wise men tell us;﻿—

Even those dusty, musty fellows

That have done with life﻿—and pass

Where the wraith of Aristotle

Hankers, vainly, for a bottle,

Youth and some frank Grecian lass.

“Ah, I warrant you;﻿—and Zeno

Would not reason, now, could he know

One more chance to live and love:

For, at best, the merry May-time

Is a very fleeting playtime;﻿—

Why, then, waste an hour thereof?

“Plato, Solon, Periander,

Seneca, Anaximander,

Pyrrho, and Parmenides!

Were one hour alone remaining

Would ye spend it in attaining

Learning, or to lips like these?

“Thus, I demonstrate by reason

Now is our predestined season

For the garnering of all bliss;

Prudence is but long-faced folly;

Cry a fig for melancholy!

Seal the bargain with a kiss”

===Chinese===
不多一刻，佩文西伯爵拿起放在两人身边的维奥尔琴，在清朗的早晨对她唱了起来。他晒得黑红，十分俊美；他坐在那荫影中的她面前歌唱时，那双黑色的大眼睛里满是柔情。他自己则坐在她脚边的阳光里。

佩文西伯爵唱道：

「厄休拉，春天在我们四周苏醒——
醒来讥笑我们、奚落我们
竟这样冷冷地迁延时日：
连鸟儿都在交颈成双，
请问，我们又何必观望——
我们本可以今天就成婚！

「『人生苦短』，智者们这样告诉我们；——
连那些尘满面、霉满身、
早已了却此生的家伙也是这样，
他们去的那地方，亚里士多德的幽灵
正徒然渴念着一瓶美酒、
一个少年和某个坦爽的希腊姑娘。

「啊，我担保；——就连芝诺，
倘若他知道还能有一次机会
去活去爱，
如今也不会再讲推理：
因为，五月这欢乐的时光，
说到底不过一场倏忽的嬉戏；——
那又何苦枉费其中一个钟头？

「柏拉图、梭伦、佩里安德、
塞内加、阿那克西曼德、
皮浪，还有巴门尼德！
倘若只剩一个钟头可活，
你们可愿把它用来求取
学问，还是这样的嘴唇？

「所以，我以道理证明：
如今正是我们命定的季节，
正好收割一切的至福；
审慎不过是拉着长脸的愚蠢；
给忧郁一个无花果吧！
用一个吻把这桩买卖敲定」

===Original===
When he had ended, the Earl of Pevensey laughed and looked up into the Lady Ursula’s face with a long, hungry gaze; and the Lady Ursula laughed likewise and spoke kindly to him, though the distance was too great for the eavesdroppers to overhear. Then, after a little, the Lady Ursula bent forward, out of the shade of the maple into the sun, so that the sunlight fell upon her golden head and glowed in the depths of her hair, as she kissed Pevensey, tenderly and without haste, full upon the lips.

===Chinese===
唱罢，佩文西伯爵大笑起来，久久地、如饥似渴地仰望着厄休拉小姐的脸；厄休拉小姐也笑了，亲切地对他说话，只是距离太远，窃听的两人听不真切。随后，过了一小会儿，厄休拉小姐俯身向前，从枫荫里探进阳光中，阳光落在她金色的头上，在她的发间深处灼灼发光——她吻了佩文西，温存而不慌不忙，正吻在唇上。

### III / III

Falmouth Furens / 法尔茅斯之怒

===Original===
The Marquis of Falmouth caught Master Mervale’s arm in a grip that made the boy wince. Lord Falmouth’s look was murderous, as he turned in the shadow of a white-lilac bush and spoke carefully through sharp breaths that shook his great body.

“There are,” said he, “certain matters I must immediately discuss with my lord of Pevensey. I desire you, Master Mervale, to fetch him to the spot where we parted last, so that we may talk over these matters quietly and undisturbed. For else﻿—go, lad, and fetch him!”

===Chinese===
法尔茅斯侯爵一把攥住默维尔先生的胳膊，攥得少年直龇牙。法尔茅斯勋爵的眼色凶得要杀人，他在一丛白丁香的荫影里转过身，从震动着他庞大身躯的急促喘息中，一字一顿地说：

「有几桩事，」他说，「我必须立即同佩文西勋爵了结。我请您，默维尔先生，把他带到我们方才分手的地方去，好让我们安安静静、不受搅扰地谈谈这几桩事。否则——去吧，小子，把他带来！」

===Original===
For a moment the boy faced the half-shut pale eyes that were like coals smouldering behind a veil of gray ash. Then he shrugged his shoulders, sauntered forward, and doffed his hat to the Lady Ursula. There followed much laughter among the three, many explanations from Master Mervale, and yet more laughter from the lady and the earl. The marquis ground his big, white teeth as he listened, and he appeared to disapprove of so much mirth.

“Foh, the hyenas! the apes, the vile magpies!” the marquis observed. He heaved a sigh of relief, as the Earl of Pevensey, raising his hands lightly toward heaven, laughed once more, and departed into the thicket. Lord Falmouth laughed in turn, though not very pleasantly. Afterward he loosened his sword in the scabbard and wheeled back to seek their rendezvous in the shadowed place where they had made sonnets to the Lady Ursula.

===Chinese===
少年迎着那双半阖的浅色眼睛对视了片刻——那眼睛像灰烬的面纱后闷烧的炭火。随后他耸耸肩，信步上前，向厄休拉小姐脱帽致意。接着三人一阵大笑，默维尔先生说了许多解释，小姐和伯爵又笑了许多。侯爵听着，咬得那口大白牙咯咯作响，似乎很不以为然，嫌他们笑得太多。

「呸，这群鬣狗！这群猴儿，这群恶嘴的喜鹊！」侯爵评价道。佩文西伯爵朝天轻轻举起双手，又笑了一声，走进树丛深处去了，侯爵这才如释重负地舒了口气。法尔茅斯勋爵也笑了，只是笑得不很愉快。随后他松了松鞘中的剑，转身回去找他们约定的相会之地——就在他们方才为厄休拉小姐作十四行诗的那片荫处。

===Original===
For some ten minutes the marquis strode proudly through the maze, pondering, by the look of him, on the more fatal tricks of fencing. In a quarter of an hour he was lost in a wilderness of trim yew-hedges which confronted him stiffly at every outlet and branched off into innumerable gravelled alleys that led nowhither.

“Swounds!” said the marquis. He retraced his steps impatiently. He cast his hat upon the ground in seething desperation. He turned in a different direction, and in two minutes trod upon his discarded headgear.

“Holy Gregory!” the marquis commented. He meditated for a moment, then caught up his sword close to his side and plunged into the nearest hedge. After a little he came out, with a scratched face and a scant breath, into another alley. As the crow flies, he went through the maze of Longaville, leaving in his rear desolation and snapped yew-twigs. He came out of the ruin behind the white-lilac bush, where he had stood and had heard the Earl of Pevensey sing to the Lady Ursula, and had seen what followed.

===Chinese===
约莫十分钟里，侯爵昂首阔步穿行迷园，看那神色，是在揣摩剑术上更致命的招数。一刻钟后，他已迷失在一片修剪齐整的紫杉树篱的荒野里：每个出口都有树篱冷着脸拦住去路，又分岔出无数条铺着砾石的小径，条条不知通向何处。

「该死！」侯爵说。他不耐烦地循原路折回，又愤懑欲狂地把帽子掼在地上，换个方向再走，两分钟后一脚踏在自己丢弃的帽子上。

「圣格列高利！」侯爵评点道。他默想片刻，随即把剑紧紧收在胁边，一头扎进最近的树篱。不多时，他顶着一脸抓痕、气喘吁吁地钻出来，到了另一条小径。他照乌鸦飞的直线横穿了朗加维尔的迷园，身后留下一片狼藉与折断的紫杉枝。他从那丛白丁香后面破阵而出——正是在那里，他站着听佩文西伯爵对厄休拉小姐唱歌，也看见了后来那一幕。

===Original===
The marquis wiped his brow. He looked out over the lawn and breathed heavily. The Lady Ursula still sat beneath the maple, and beside her was Master Mervale, whose arm girdled her waist. Her arm was about his neck, and she listened as he talked eagerly with many gestures. Then they both laughed and kissed each other.

“Oh, defend me!” groaned the marquis. Once more he wiped his brow, as he crouched behind the white-lilac bush. “Why, the woman is a second Messalina!” he said. “Oh, the trollop! the wanton! Oh, holy Gregory! Yet I must be quiet﻿—quiet as a sucking lamb, that I may strike afterward as a roaring lion. Is this your innocence, Mistress Ursula, that cannot endure the spoken name of a spade? Oh, splendor of God!”

===Chinese===
侯爵抹了抹额头。他望着草坪那头，呼吸粗重。厄休拉小姐还坐在枫树下，身边是默维尔先生，一条胳膊搂着她的腰。她的胳膊勾着他的脖颈，听他连说带比划、兴致勃勃地讲着什么。随后两人一同大笑，互相亲吻。

「哦，护佑我吧！」侯爵呻吟道。他蹲在白丁香丛后，又抹了一把额头。「嚯，这女人是个梅萨利娜再世！」他说。「哦，这荡妇！这淫娃！哦，圣格列高利啊！可我得沉住气——沉静得像吃奶的羔羊，回头才好如怒吼的雄狮般扑击。这就是你的冰清玉洁么，厄休拉小姐，连把铁锹直唤作铁锹都受不住？哦，天主的荣光！」

===Original===
Thus he raged behind the white-lilac bush while they laughed and kissed under the maple-tree. After a space they parted. The Lady Ursula, still laughing, lifted the branches of the rearward thicket and disappeared in the path which the Earl of Pevensey had taken. Master Mervale, kissing his hand and laughing yet more loudly, lounged toward the entrance of the maze.

The jackanapes (as anybody could see), was in a mood to be pleased with himself. Smiles eddied about the boy’s face, his heels skipped, disdaining the honest grass; and presently he broke into a glad little song, all trills and shakes, like that of a bird ecstasizing over the perfections of his mate.

Sang Master Mervale:

“Listen, all lovers! the spring is here

And the world is not amiss;

As long as laughter is good to hear,

And lips are good to kiss,

As long as Youth and Spring endure,

There is never an evil past a cure

And the world is never amiss.

“O lovers all, I bid ye declare

The world is a pleasant place;﻿—

Give thanks to God for the gift so fair,

Give thanks for His singular grace!

Give thanks for Youth and Love and Spring!

Give thanks, as gentlefolk should, and sing,

‘The world is a pleasant place!’﻿”

===Chinese===
他就这样在白丁香丛后义愤填膺，他们就这样在枫树下又笑又吻。过了一阵，两人分手了。厄休拉小姐犹自笑着，掀开后头树丛的枝条，顺着佩文西伯爵方才走的小径消失了。默维尔先生吻着自己的手，笑得越发响亮，懒洋洋地朝迷园入口踱去。

那猴崽子（谁都看得出来）正是一副自我感觉良好的做派。笑容在少年脸上打着旋儿，他的脚跟雀跃着，不屑沾踏老实的青草；不多时他唱起一支欢快的小曲，满是颤音与花腔，像一只对着配偶的种种完美如痴如醉的鸟儿。

默维尔先生唱道：

「听着，恋人们！春天来了，
这世界并没有什么不好；
只要笑声还入耳动听，
只要嘴唇还值得亲吻，
只要青春与春天长在，
便没有一桩灾祸治不好，
这世界永远不会不好。

「恋人们啊，我请你们宣告：
这世界是个可喜的地方；——
为这如此美好的馈赠感谢上主，
为他独一无二的恩典感谢！
为青春、爱与春天感谢吧！
要像淑女君子那样道谢，还要歌唱：
『这世界是个可喜的地方！』」

===Original===
In mid-skip Master Mervale here desisted, his voice trailing into inarticulate vowels. After many angry throes, a white-lilac bush had been delivered of the Marquis of Falmouth, who now confronted Master Mervale, furiously moved.

===Chinese===
默维尔先生蹦跳到半中间便停了下来，嗓音拖成几声含混的元音。那丛白丁香经过多番恼怒的阵痛，终于「分娩」出了法尔茅斯侯爵；侯爵此刻拦在默维尔先生面前，怒不可遏。

### IV / IV

Love Rises from un-Cytherean Waters / 爱自非库忒瑞亚之水中升起

===Original===
“I have heard, Master Mervale,” said the marquis, gently, “that love is blind?”

The boy stared at the white face, that had before his eyes veiled rage with a crooked smile. So you may see the cat, tense for the fatal spring, relax and with one paw indolently flip the mouse.

“It is an ancient fable, my lord,” the boy said, smiling, and made as though to pass.

“Indeed,” said the marquis, courteously, but without yielding an inch, “it is a very reassuring fable: for,” he continued, meditatively, “were the eyes of all lovers suddenly opened, Master Mervale, I suspect it would prove a red hour for the world. There would be both tempers and reputations lost, Master Mervale; there would be sword-thrusts; there would be corpses, Master Mervale.”

===Chinese===
「我听说，默维尔先生，」侯爵温和地说，「爱是盲目的？」

少年盯着那张苍白的脸——那脸在他眼前用一抹歪斜的微笑，把怒意遮掩了起来。正如你可以看到一只蓄势待发、准备致命一扑的猫松弛下来，用一只爪子懒洋洋地拨弄老鼠。

「那是个古老的寓言，大人，」少年微笑着说，作势要走过去。

「的确，」侯爵客客气气地说，脚下却寸步不让，「是个非常叫人宽心的寓言：因为，」他若有所思地续道，「倘若所有恋人的眼睛突然睁开，默维尔先生，我疑心那对世界将是一个见血的时辰。要赔上脾气，也要赔上名誉，默维尔先生；要出剑；要出人命，默维尔先生。」

===Original===
“Doubtless, my lord,” the lad assented, striving to jest and have done; “for all flesh is frail, and as the flesh of woman is frailer than that of man, so is it, as I remember to have read, the more easily entrapped by the gross snares of the devil, as was over-well proved by the serpent’s beguiling deceit of Eve at the beginning.”

“Yet, Master Mervale,” pursued the marquis, equably, but without smiling, “there be lovers in the world that have eyes?”

“Doubtless, my lord,” said the boy.

“There also be women in the world, Master Mervale,” Lord Falmouth suggested, with a deeper gravity, “that are but the handsome sepulchres of iniquity﻿—ay, and for the major part of women, those miracles which are their bodies, compact of white and gold and sprightly color though they be, serve as the lovely cerements of corruption.”

“Doubtless, my lord. The devil, as they say, is homelier with that sex.”

“There also be swords in the world, Master Mervale?” purred the marquis. He touched his own sword as he spoke.

“My lord﻿—!” the boy cried, with a gasp.

===Chinese===
「想必如此，大人，」少年附和着，力图玩笑两句就此收场；「因为凡血肉之躯都软弱，而女人的血肉既比男人软弱，据我记得在什么书里读到过，也就更容易落进魔鬼粗劣的罗网，当初蛇以巧诈诱骗夏娃，便把这一点证明得太充分了。」

「然而，默维尔先生，」侯爵不紧不慢地追问，脸上却没了笑，「世上毕竟还有长着眼睛的恋人？」

「想必如此，大人。」少年说。

「世上也还有那样的女人，默维尔先生，」法尔茅斯勋爵意味深长地提点道，语调愈发郑重，「不过是罪恶装扮得漂亮的坟茔——是啊，就大多数女人而言，她们的肉体纵然是白色、金色与鲜活色彩织成的奇迹，也不过是裹着腐朽的可爱殓布。」

「想必如此，大人。魔鬼，正如人们所说，跟那个性别更熟络些。」

「世上也还有剑，默维尔先生？」侯爵喉咙里咕噜作响地说。他一边说，一边碰了碰自己的剑。

「大人——！」少年倒抽一口气，喊道。

===Original===
“Now, swords have at least three uses, Master Mervale,” Falmouth continued. “With a sword one may pick a cork from a bottle; with a sword one may toast cheese about the Twelfth Night fire; and with a sword one may spit a man, Master Mervale﻿—ay, even an ambling, pink-faced, lisping lad that cannot boo at a goose, Master Mervale. I have no inclination, Master Mervale, just now, for either wine or toasted cheese.”

“I do not understand you, my lord,” said the boy, in a thin voice.

“Indeed, I think we understand each other perfectly,” said the marquis. “For I have been very frank with you, and I have watched you from behind this bush.”

The boy raised his hand as though to speak.

“Look you, Master Mervale,” the marquis argued, “you and my lord of Pevensey and I be brave fellows; we need a wide world to bustle in. Now, the thought has come to me that this small planet of ours is scarcely commodious enough for all three. There be purgatory and Heaven, and yet another place, Master Mervale; why, then, crowd one another?”

“My lord,” said the boy, dully, “I do not understand you.”

===Chinese===
「要知道，剑至少有三种用处，默维尔先生，」法尔茅斯续道，「用剑可以挑出瓶塞；用剑可以在十二夜节的炉火边烤干酪；用剑还可以把人串起来，默维尔先生——是啊，哪怕是那种迈着碎步、粉脸、咬着舌头、连对一只鹅也喝不出一声吓的少年，默维尔先生。而我眼下，默维尔先生，对酒也罢、烤干酪也罢，都毫无胃口。」

「我不明白您的意思，大人。」少年声音发细地说。

「您明白得很，我想我们是彼此心照的。」侯爵说。「因为我已对您坦诚相待过了，而且我方才就在这丛树后，看着您来着。」

少年举起手，似乎要说话。

「您瞧，默维尔先生，」侯爵条分缕析，「您、佩文西勋爵和我，都是好汉；我们需要一个广阔的世界供我们驰骋。如今我忽然想到，咱们这颗小小的行星，实在不够宽敞，容不下咱们三个。世上有炼狱，有天堂，另外还有一处地方，默维尔先生；那又何苦彼此挤着呢？」

「大人，」少年木然地说，「我不明白您的意思。」

===Original===
“Holy Gregory!” scoffed the marquis; “surely my meaning is plain enough! it is to kill you first, and my lord of Pevensey afterward! Y’are phoenixes, Master Mervale, Arabian birds! Y’are too good for this world. Longaville is not fit to be trodden under your feet; and therefore it is my intention that you leave Longaville feet first. Draw, Master Mervale!” cried the marquis, his light hair falling about his flushed, handsome face as he laughed joyously, and flashed his sword in the spring sunshine.

The boy sprang back, with an inarticulate cry; then gulped some dignity into himself and spoke. “My lord,” he said, “I admit that explanation may seem necessary.”

“You will render it, if to anybody, Master Mervale, to my heir, who will doubtless accord it such credence as it merits. For my part, having two duels on my hands today, I have no time to listen to a romance out of the Hundred Merry Tales.”

Falmouth had placed himself on guard; but Master Mervale stood with chattering teeth and irresolute, groping hands, and made no effort to draw. “Oh, the block! the curd-faced cheat!” cried the marquis. “Will nothing move you?” With his left hand he struck at the boy.

Thereupon Master Mervale gasped, and turning with a great sob, ran through the gardens. The marquis laughed discordantly; then he followed, taking big leaps as he ran and flourishing his sword.

“Oh, the coward!” he shouted; “Oh, the milk-livered rogue! Oh, you paltry rabbit!”

So they came to the bank of the artificial pond. Master Mervale swerved as with an oath the marquis pounced at him. Master Mervale’s foot caught in the root of a great willow, and Master Mervale splashed into ten feet of still water, that glistened like quicksilver in the sunlight.

===Chinese===
「圣格列高利！」侯爵讥笑道，「我的意思还不够明白么！就是先杀了您，再杀佩文西勋爵！你们是凤凰，默维尔先生，阿拉伯的神鸟！你们好得叫这世界配不上。朗加维尔都不配给您踩在脚下；所以我打算，让您脚朝前地被抬出朗加维尔。拔剑，默维尔先生！」侯爵喊道，浅色的头发披散在他涨红英俊的脸上，他欢畅地大笑着，剑在春日的阳光里闪着寒光。

少年发出一声含混的喊叫，向后跳开；随后强咽下一口气，撑起几分尊严，开口道：「大人，」他说，「我承认，解释或许是必要的。」

「您要说，也只说给我的继承人听吧，默维尔先生，他无疑会按这段解释应得的可信度予以采信。至于我，今天手头有两场决斗，可没有工夫听一段《百篇趣谈》里的传奇。」

法尔茅斯已摆开架势；可默维尔先生牙齿打战，双手犹疑地摸索着，并不拔剑。「哦，这块木头！这张凝着奶皮脸的滑头！」侯爵喊道。「什么都打动不了您么？」他用左手朝少年打了过去。

默维尔先生当即倒抽一口气，转身抱以一声大哭，一路穿过花园跑去。侯爵笑声刺耳；随即追了上去，一路大步腾跃，挥舞着剑。

「哦，这懦夫！」他喊道，「哦，这奶肝的泼皮！哦，你这不值钱的兔子！」

就这样，他们跑到了人工池塘的岸边。侯爵咒骂着扑来，默维尔先生侧身一闪，脚绊在一株大柳树的树根上，扑通一声跌进十英尺深的静水里，池水在阳光下亮得像水银。

===Original===
“Oh, Saint Gregory!” the marquis cried, and clasped his sides in noisy mirth; “was there no other way to cool your courage? Paddle out and be flogged, Master Hare-heels!” he called. The boy had come to the surface and was swimming aimlessly, parallel to the bank. “Now I have heard,” said the marquis, as he walked beside him, “that water swells a man. Pray Heaven, it may swell his heart a thousandfold or so, and thus hearten him for wholesome exercise after his ducking﻿—a friendly thrust or two, a little judicious bloodletting to ward off the effects of the damp.”

The marquis started as Master Mervale grounded on a shallow and rose, dripping, knee-deep among the lily-pads. “Oh, splendor of God!” cried the marquis.

Master Mervale had risen from his bath almost clean-shaven; only one sodden half of his mustachios clung to his upper lip, and as he rubbed the water from his eyes, this remaining half also fell away from the boy’s face.

“Oh, splendor of God!” groaned the marquis. He splashed noisily into the water. “O Kate, Kate!” he cried, his arms about Master Mervale. “Oh, blind, blind, blind! O heart’s dearest! Oh, my dear, my dear!” he observed.

===Chinese===
「哦，圣格列高利！」侯爵拍着两肋、放声大笑地喊道，「就没有别的法子，给您那点勇气降降温么？划水出来领鞭子吧，兔蹄少爷！」他喊道。少年已浮上水面，正沿岸漫无目的地游着。「我方才听说，」侯爵沿岸边跟着他走，说道，「水能把人泡胀。求上天保佑，把他的胆气也泡胀个一千倍上下，好叫他在挨了这顿水浸之后有精神做点有益的运动——友好地刺上一两剑，放掉一点恰到好处的血，祛一祛潮气。」

默维尔先生在一片浅水处站住了脚，滴着水、膝深地立在荷叶间站起身来，侯爵猛然一惊。「哦，天主的荣光！」侯爵喊道。

默维尔先生洗完这一澡，胡子几乎剃了个干净；只剩浸透了的半撇髭须还粘在上唇，而当他揉去眼中的池水时，剩下的半撇也从少年脸上脱落了。

「哦，天主的荣光！」侯爵呻吟道。他扑通扑通踩着水下了池塘。「哦，凯特，凯特！」他喊道，双臂抱住默维尔先生。「哦，瞎了眼，瞎了眼，瞎了眼！哦，心肝儿！哦，我的宝贝，我的宝贝！」他说。

===Original===
Master Mervale slipped from his embrace and waded to dry land. “My lord﻿—” he began, demurely.

“My lady wife﻿—” said his lordship of Falmouth, with a tremulous smile. He paused, and passed his hand over his brow. “And yet I do not understand,” he said. “Y’are dead; y’are buried. It was a frightened boy I struck.” He spread out his strong arms. “O world! O sun! O stars!” he cried; “she is come back to me from the grave. O little world! small shining planet! I think that I could crush you in my hands!”

“Meanwhile,” Master Mervale suggested, after an interval, “it is I that you are crushing.” He sighed﻿—though not very deeply﻿—and continued, with a hiatus: “They would have wedded me to Lucius Rossmore, and I could not﻿—I could not﻿—”

“That skinflint! that palsied goat!” the marquis growled.

“He was wealthy,” said Master Mervale. Then he sighed once more. “There seemed only you﻿—only you in all the world. A man might come to you in those far-off countries: a woman might not. I fled by night, my lord, by the aid of a waiting-woman; became a man by the aid of a tailor; and set out to find you by the aid of such impudence as I might muster. But luck did not travel with me. I followed you through Flanders, Italy, Spain﻿—always just too late; always finding the bird flown, the nest yet warm. Presently I heard you were become Marquis of Falmouth; then I gave up the quest.”

===Chinese===
默维尔先生从他怀里滑脱，蹚水上了干岸。「大人——」她娴静地开口。

「我的夫人——」法尔茅斯爵爷说着，嘴唇发颤地笑了。他顿了顿，用手抹了抹前额。「可我还是不明白，」他说，「你已经死了；你已经下葬了。我打的那个，是个受了惊的少年。」他张开强壮的双臂。「哦，世界！哦，太阳！哦，星辰！」他喊道，「她从坟墓里回到我身边了。哦，小小的世界！小小的、亮晶晶的行星！我想我能把你捏碎在这双手里！」

「眼下，」过了一阵，默维尔先生提醒道，「您捏着的可是我。」她叹了口气——叹得不算太深——又顿了一顿，续道：「他们要把我嫁给卢修斯·罗斯莫尔（Lucius Rossmore），而我不能——我不能——」

「那吝啬鬼！那瘫手瘫脚的老公羊！」侯爵咆哮道。

「他有钱。」默维尔先生说。随即又叹了口气。「这世上仿佛只有您——只有您一个人。在那些遥远的国度，男人还可以寻到您跟前：女人却不能。我连夜逃了出来，大人，靠一个贴身侍女帮忙；又靠一位裁缝帮忙，变成了男人；再靠我能搜罗出的那点厚脸皮，动身来找您。可运气没有随我同行。我跟着您穿行佛兰德斯、意大利、西班牙——总是迟了一步；总是发现鸟已飞走，巢还温热。后来我听说您做了法尔茅斯侯爵；我便断了这个念想。」

===Original===
“I would suggest,” said the marquis, “that my name is Stephen;﻿—but why, in the devil’s name, should you give up a quest so laudable?”

“Stephen Allonby, my lord,” said Master Mervale, sadly, “was not Marquis of Falmouth; as Marquis of Falmouth, you might look to mate with any woman short of the Queen.”

“To tell you a secret,” the marquis whispered, “I look to mate with one beside whom the Queen﻿—not to speak treason﻿—is but a lean-faced, yellow piece of affectation. I aim higher than royalty, heart’s dearest﻿—aspiring to one beside whom empresses are but common hussies.”

“And Ursula?” asked Master Mervale, gently.

“Holy Gregory!” cried the marquis, “I had forgot! Poor wench, poor wench! I must withdraw my suit warily﻿—firmly, of course, yet very kindlily, you understand, so as to grieve her no more than must be. Poor wench!﻿—well, after all,” he hopefully suggested, “there is yet Pevensey.”

===Chinese===
「我倒想提醒一句，」侯爵说，「我的名字是斯蒂芬；——可是，看在魔鬼份上，这样一桩可嘉的追寻，您为什么偏要断念呢？」

「斯蒂芬·阿隆比（Stephen Allonby），大人，当年还不是法尔茅斯侯爵，」默维尔先生忧伤地说，「而身为法尔茅斯侯爵的您，除女王之外，同哪位女子结亲都不在话下。」

「告诉您一个秘密，」侯爵耳语道，「我指望结亲的那一位，在她身旁，女王——这可不是叛国的话——不过是个瘦脸黄肤、装腔作势的货色。我的眼光高过王室，心肝儿——我所仰慕的那一位，在她身旁，女皇们也不过是个寻常的粗使丫头。」

「那厄休拉呢？」默维尔先生轻声问。

「圣格列高利！」侯爵喊道，「我把她忘了！可怜的姑娘，可怜的姑娘！我得小心翼翼地把求婚撤回——当然要坚决，却也要非常和气，您懂的，好叫她伤心伤得越少越好。可怜的姑娘！——不过，话说回来，」他抱着希望地提点道，「不是还有佩文西么。」

===Original===
“O Stephen! Stephen!” Master Mervale murmured; “Why, there was never any other but Pevensey! For Ursula knows all﻿—knows there was never any more manhood in Master Mervale’s disposition than might be gummed on with a playactor’s mustachios! Why, she is my cousin, Stephen﻿—my cousin and good friend, to whom I came at once on reaching England, to find you, favored by her father, pestering her with your suit, and the poor girl well-nigh at her wits’ end because she might not have Pevensey. So,” said Master Mervale, “we put our heads together, Stephen, as you observe.”

“Indeed,” my lord of Falmouth said, “it would seem that you two wenches have, between you, concocted a very pleasant comedy.”

“It was not all a comedy,” sighed Master Mervale﻿—“not all a comedy, Stephen, until today when you told Master Mervale the story of Katherine Beaufort. For I did not know﻿—I could not know﻿—”

“And now?” my lord of Falmouth queried.

“H’m!” cried Master Mervale, and he tossed his head. “You are very unreasonable in anger! you are a veritable Turk! you struck me!”

===Chinese===
「哦，斯蒂芬！斯蒂芬！」默维尔先生喃喃道，「哪里还有过什么别人，从来就只有佩文西！因为厄休拉什么都知道——知道默维尔先生的性情里，从来就没有一星半点比戏子用胶粘上的髭须更多的男子气概！要知道，她是我表妹，斯蒂芬——我的表妹，我的好朋友，我一到英国就直奔她这里，却发现您受着她父亲的属意，拿求婚纠缠着她，把那可怜的姑娘急得几乎没了主张，只因她得不到佩文西。所以，」默维尔先生说，「斯蒂芬，你也看出来了，我们两个凑在一处合计了一番。」

「可不是，」法尔茅斯爵爷说，「看来你们两个丫头，合谋炮制了一出非常有趣的喜剧。」

「这不全是喜剧，」默维尔先生叹道，「——不全是喜剧，斯蒂芬，直到今天，您把凯瑟琳·博福特的故事讲给默维尔先生听之前。因为我并不知道——我不可能知道——」

「那么如今呢？」法尔茅斯爵爷问道。

「哼！」默维尔先生喊道，把头一扬。「您发起怒来蛮不讲理！您活脱脱是个土耳其人！您还打了我！」

===Original===
The marquis rose, bowing low to his former adversary. “Master Mervale,” said the marquis, “I hereby tender you my unreserved apologies for the affront I put upon you. I protest I was vastly mistaken in your disposition and hold you as valorous a gentleman as was ever made by barbers’ tricks; and you are at liberty to bestow as many kisses and caresses upon the Lady Ursula as you may elect, reserving, however, a reasonable sufficiency for one that shall be nameless. Are we friends, Master Mervale?”

Master Mervale rested his head upon Lord Falmouth’s shoulder, and sighed happily. Master Mervale laughed﻿—a low and gentle laugh that was vibrant with content. But Master Mervale said nothing, because there seemed to be between these two, who were young in the world’s recaptured youth, no longer any need of idle speaking.

===Chinese===
侯爵站起身，向他昔日的对手深深一揖。「默维尔先生，」侯爵说，「我为方才加诸您的冒犯，在此向您奉上毫无保留的歉意。我声明，我对您的胆气实在看走了眼，并且认定，您是理发师的巧手历来造就得出的最勇敢的绅士；您尽可以在厄休拉小姐身上随意施加亲吻与爱抚，悉听尊便——只是请留出合理的一份，给一位不便说出名姓的人。我们还是朋友么，默维尔先生？」

默维尔先生把头靠在法尔茅斯勋爵的肩上，幸福地叹了口气。默维尔先生笑了——一声低柔的笑，因满足而微微震颤。可默维尔先生什么也没有说，因为在这两个人之间——在这重拾青春的世界里年轻着的两个人之间——似乎已不再需要任何闲话了。

## IX / IX

The Episode Called Porcelain Cups / 所谓瓷杯

===Original===
June 1, 1593

“She was the admirablest lady that ever lived: therefore, Master Doctor, if you will do us that favor, as to let us see that peerless dame, we should think ourselves much beholding unto you.”

===Chinese===
1593 年 6 月 1 日

「她是古往今来最可赞叹的一位贵妇：所以，博士先生，倘蒙您赏脸，容我们一睹那位绝世无双的夫人，我们必将感念您的大德。」

===Original===
There was a double wedding some two weeks later in the chapel at Longaville: and each marriage appears to have been happy enough.

The tenth Marquis of Falmouth had begotten sixteen children within seventeen years, at the end of which period his wife unluckily died in producing a final pledge of affection. This child, a daughter, survived, and was christened Cynthia: of her you may hear later.

Meanwhile the Earl and the Countess of Pevensey had propagated more moderately, and Pevensey had played a larger part in public life than was allotted to Falmouth, who did not shine at Court. Pevensey, indeed, has his sizable niche in history: his Irish expeditions, in 1575, were once notorious, as well as the circumstances of the earl’s death in that year at Triloch Lenoch. His more famous son, then a boy of eight, succeeded to the title, and somewhat later, as the world knows, to the hazardous position of chief favorite to Queen Elizabeth.

“For Pevensey has the vision of a poet,”—thus Langard quotes the lonely old Queen—“and to balance it, such mathematics as add two and two correctly, where you others smirk and assure me it sums up to whatever the Queen prefers. I have need of Pevensey: in this parched little age all England has need of Pevensey.”

That is as it may have been: at all events, it is with this Lord Pevensey, at the height of his power, that we have now to do.

===Chinese===
两个星期以后，朗加维尔（Longaville）的小教堂里举行了一场双喜临门的婚礼：两桩婚姻，看来都相当美满。

第十代法尔茅斯侯爵在十七年间养育了十六个子女；到这一期限的末尾，他的妻子不幸在产下一枚最后的恩爱凭据时去世。这个孩子是个女儿，活了下来，受洗取名辛西娅：她的后话，诸位稍后自会听闻。

与此同时，佩文西（Pevensey）伯爵与伯爵夫人的生养则节制得多；而佩文西在公共生活中所扮演的角色，也比那位在宫廷里并不光彩照人的法尔茅斯分得的大得多。佩文西在历史上确实占着一个不小的壁龛：他 1575 年的爱尔兰远征曾一度声名狼藉，同年伯爵在特里洛赫·莱诺赫（Triloch Lenoch）殒命的情形亦然。他那位名声更盛的儿子——当时不过八龄童子——承袭了爵位，又过了些年，正如世人所知，接着登上了伊丽莎白女王头号宠臣那个凶险的位子。

「因为佩文西有诗人的远见，」兰加德（Langard）引述那位孤独的老女王的话说，「而与之相配的，是二加二必得四的算学，不像你们旁人，堆着笑向我担保，得数凭女王高兴是什么便是什么。我需要佩文西：在这个干渴的小小时代，整个英格兰都需要佩文西。」

实情是否如此，姑且不论：无论如何，我们如今要叙说的，正是这位权势鼎盛的佩文西勋爵。

### I / I

Of Greatness Intimately Viewed / 近观伟大

===Original===
“Ah, but they are beyond praise,” said Cynthia Allonby, enraptured, “and certainly you should have presented them to the Queen.”

“Her majesty already possesses a cup of that ware,” replied Lord Pevensey. “It was one of her New Year’s gifts, from Robert Cecil. Hers is, I believe, not quite so fine as either of yours; but then, they tell me, there is not the like of this pair in England, nor indeed on the hither side of Cataia.”

He set the two pieces of Chinese pottery upon the shelves in the south corner of the room. These cups were of that sea-green tint called celadon, with a very wonderful glow and radiance. Such oddities were the last vogue at Court; and Cynthia could not but speculate as to what monstrous sum Lord Pevensey had paid for this his last gift to her.

Now he turned, smiling, a really superb creature in his blue and gold. “I had today another message from the Queen—”

===Chinese===
「啊，它们真是赞美不尽，」辛西娅·阿隆比（Cynthia Allonby）心醉神迷地说，「您实在应当把它们献给女王才对。」

「陛下已经拥有一只那种窑口的杯子了，」佩文西勋爵答道，「那是她的一件新年礼物，罗伯特·塞西尔（Robert Cecil）进献的。不过据我看，她那一只不及您这两只中的任何一只精美；可话说回来，人家告诉我，像这样的一对，英格兰找不到第二对，老实说，便是契丹（Cataia）以近的整个天下也没有。」

他把这两件中国瓷器安放到房间南角的搁架上。这对杯子是那种名为青瓷（celadon）的海碧色泽，宝光内蕴，莹彩四射。这类奇物正是宫廷里眼下的风尚；佩文西勋爵为他这最新的一件赠礼花了怎样一笔惊人的巨款，辛西娅不禁暗自揣度。

此刻他转过身来，微笑着，一身蓝金相间的衣装，着实是位仪表堂堂的人物。「我今天又接到女王的一道谕示——」

===Original===
“George,” Cynthia said, with fond concern, “it frightens me to see you thus foolhardy, in tempting alike the Queen’s anger and the Plague.”

“Eh, as goes the Plague, it spares nine out of ten,” he answered, lightly. “The Queen, I grant you, is another pair of sleeves, for an irritated Tudor spares nobody.”

But Cynthia Allonby kept silence, and did not exactly smile, while she appraised her famous young kinsman. She was flattered by, and a little afraid of, the gay self-confidence which led anybody to take such chances. Two weeks ago it was that the terrible painted old Queen had named Lord Pevensey to go straightway into France, where, rumor had it, King Henri was preparing to renounce the Reformed Religion, and making his peace with the Pope: and for two weeks Pevensey had lingered, on one pretence or another, at his house in London, with the Plague creeping about the city like an invisible incalculable flame, and the Queen asking questions at Windsor. Of all the monarchs that had ever reigned in England, Elizabeth Tudor was the least used to having her orders disregarded. Meanwhile Lord Pevensey came every day to the Marquis of Falmouth’s lodgings at Deptford: and every day Lord Pevensey pointed out to the marquis’ daughter that Pevensey, whose wife had died in childbirth a year back, did not intend to go into France, for nobody could foretell how long a stay, as a widower. Certainly it was all very flattering. …

===Chinese===
「乔治，」辛西娅又疼又忧地说，「看你这样有勇无谋，同时招惹女王的雷霆与瘟疫，真叫我害怕。」

「呃，说到瘟疫，十个人里它也只收一个，」他轻快地答道，「至于女王，我承认，那可就是另一码事了，都铎家的人一旦动怒，是谁也不肯放过的。」

可辛西娅·阿隆比只默不作声，也说不上是在微笑，一面打量着她这位名满天下的年轻亲戚。那种驱使人去冒此大险的飞扬自信，让她受用，也让她有几分害怕。两个星期以前，正是那位可怕的、涂脂抹粉的老女王钦命佩文西勋爵即刻赴法——据传，法兰西国王亨利正准备背弃新教，与教皇言归于好：而佩文西却以这样那样的托辞，在伦敦的宅邸里一拖便是两个星期，任凭瘟疫像一团无形无准的火焰在城市里潜行，任凭女王在温莎连连追问。古往今来君临英格兰的君主之中，伊丽莎白·都铎最不习惯自己的旨意被人置之不理。与此同时，佩文西勋爵天天到法尔茅斯侯爵位于德特福德（Deptford）的寓所来：天天向侯爵的女儿指明，佩文西这个一年前妻子死于产褥的鳏夫，并不打算前往那个归期难料的法兰西。这自然叫人受宠若惊。……

===Original===
“Yes, and you would be an excellent match,” said Cynthia, aloud, “if that were all. And yet, what must I reasonably expect in marrying, sir, the famous Earl of Pevensey?”

“A great deal of love and petting, my dear. And if there were anything else to which you had a fancy, I would get it for you.”

Her glance went to those lovely cups and lingered fondly. “Yes, dear Master Generosity, if it could be purchased or manufactured, you would get it for me—”

“If it exists I will get it for you,” he declared.

===Chinese===
「是啊，倘若单看这些，你倒是一门极好的亲事，」辛西娅出声说道，「可是，嫁给您这位大名鼎鼎的佩文西伯爵，我合情合理地又能指望些什么呢，先生？」

「许许多多的爱怜与疼宠，亲爱的。倘若您还看上了别的什么，我自会替您弄来。」

她的目光转向那对可爱的杯子，流连不舍。「是啊，亲爱的慷慨老爷，只要那东西买得到、造得出，您就会替我弄来——」

「只要它存在，我就一定替您弄来。」他断言道。

===Original===
“I think that it exists. But I am not learned enough to know what it is. George, if I married you I would have money and fine clothes and gilded coaches, and an army of maids and pages, and honor from all men. And you would be kind to me, I know, when you returned from the day’s work at Windsor—or Holyrood or the Louvre. But do you not see that I would always be to you only a rather costly luxury, like those cups, which the Queen’s minister could afford to keep for his hours of leisure?”

He answered: “You are all in all to me. You know it. Oh, very well do you know and abuse your power, you adorable and lovely baggage, who have kept me dancing attendance for a fortnight, without ever giving me an honest yes or no.” He gesticulated. “Well, but life is very dull in Deptford village, and it amuses you to twist a Queen’s adviser around your finger! I see it plainly, you minx, and I acquiesce because it delights me to give you pleasure, even at the cost of some dignity. Yet I may no longer shirk the Queen’s business—no, not even to amuse you, my dear.”

===Chinese===
「我想它是存在的。只是我学识浅陋，说不出它是什么。乔治，我若嫁给你，便有钱财、华服、鎏金的马车，有一大群侍女和侍童，受尽世人的敬重。我也知道，等你从温莎——或是荷里路德、卢浮宫——操劳一天回来，你会待我很好。可你难道看不出来么？在你眼里，我永远只是一件颇为昂贵的奢侈品，就像那对杯子一样，供女王的重臣在闲暇的时辰里赏玩得起？」

他答道：「你就是我的一切。你是知道的。哦，你清楚得很，还一味滥用这份权力，你这招人疼的、可爱的小滑头——害我随侍听命吊了整整两个星期，却始终不肯给我一句老老实实的可否。」他打着手势。「唉，德特福德这村子里日子实在乏味，把女王的谋臣玩弄于股掌之上，很叫你开心罢！我看得明明白白，你这小妖精，我姑且由你去，因为让你高兴便是我的乐事，哪怕搭上几分体面。然而女王的公务，我再也不能怠慢了——不，即便为了逗你开心，也不能了，亲爱的。」

===Original===
“You said you had heard from her—again?”

“I had this morning my orders, under Gloriana’s own fair hand, either to depart tomorrow into France or else to come tomorrow to Windsor. I need not say that in the circumstances I consider France the more wholesome.”

Now the girl’s voice was hurt and wistful. “So, for the thousandth time, is it proven the Queen’s business means more to you than I do. Yes, certainly it is just as I said, George.”

===Chinese===
「你说你又接到她的谕示了？」

「今天早晨我领了敕令，出自格罗丽亚娜（Gloriana）那纤纤玉手亲书：要么明日启程赴法兰西，要么明日前往温莎。不必我说，处此境地，我自认为法兰西于养生更为相宜。」

此刻姑娘的嗓音里带着委屈与怅惘。「于是，第一千次证明了：女王的公务在你心里比我重。是啊，千真万确，正如我所说，乔治。」

===Original===
He observed, unruffled: “My dear, I scent unreason. This is a high matter. If the French King compounds with Rome, it means war for Protestant England. Even you must see that.”

She replied, sadly: “Yes, even I! oh, certainly, my lord, even a half-witted child of seventeen can perceive as much as that.”

“I was not speaking of half-witted persons, as I remember. Well, it chances that I am honored by the friendship of our gallant Bearnais, and am supposed to have some claim upon him, thanks to my good fortune last year in saving his life from the assassin Barriere. It chances that I may perhaps become, under providence, the instrument of preserving my fellow countrymen from much grief and trumpet-sounding and throat-cutting. Instead of pursuing that chance, two weeks ago—as was my duty—I have dangled at your apron-strings, in the vain hope of softening the most variable and hardest heart in the world. Now, clearly, I have not the right to do that any longer.”

===Chinese===
他不动声色地说：「亲爱的，我嗅到了不讲道理的味道。这是件大事。法王若与罗马妥协，新教的英格兰便要面临战争。连你也一定看得出来。」

她悲哀地答道：「是啊，连我也看得出来！哦，自然了，大人，连一个十七岁的半傻孩子也看得懂这一点。」

「我记得，我方才说的可不是半傻的人。唉，事有凑巧，我忝获我们那位英勇的贝亚恩人的友谊，多亏去年我运气好，从刺客巴里埃尔（Barriere）剑下救了他的性命，据说他对我颇领几分情。事有凑巧，托上天保佑，我或许能成为一件工具，使我的同胞免遭许多悲恸、许多号角齐鸣与割喉断颈。而我不去追逐这个机会，反而一如过去两个星期——这本有违我的职责——垂手系于你的裙裾之下，妄想着软化世上最反复无常、也最硬的一颗心。如今，很显然，我再也没有权利这样做了。」

===Original===
She admired the ennobled, the slightly rapt look which, she knew, denoted that George Bulmer was doing his duty as he saw it, even in her disappointment. “No, you have not the right. You are wedded to your statecraft, to your patriotism, to your self-advancement, or christen it what you will. You are wedded, at all events, to your man’s business. You have not the time for such trifles as giving a maid that foolish and lovely sort of wooing to which every maid looks forward in her heart of hearts. Indeed, when you married the first time it was a kind of infidelity; and I am certain that poor, dear mouse-like Mary must have felt that often and over again. Why, do you not see, George, even now, that your wife will always come second to your real love?”

“In my heart, dear sophist, you will always come first. But it is not permitted that any loyal gentleman devote every hour of his life to sighing and making sonnets, and to the general solacing of a maid’s loneliness in this dull little Deptford. Nor would you, I am sure, desire me to do so.”

“I hardly know what I desire,” she told him ruefully. “But I know that when you talk of your man’s business I am lonely and chilled and far away from you. And I know that I cannot understand more than half your fine high notions about duty and patriotism and serving England and so on,” the girl declared: and she flung wide her lovely little hands, in a despairing gesture. “I admire you, sir, when you talk of England. It makes you handsomer—yes, even handsomer!—somehow. But all the while I am remembering that England is just an ordinary island inhabited by a number of ordinary persons, for the most of whom I have no particular feeling one way or the other.”

===Chinese===
她钦慕那升华了的、微微出神的表情——她知道，那表情表明，即便在她失望之际，乔治·布尔默（George Bulmer）仍在按他的理解尽他的本分。「是啊，你没有这个权利。你同你的权术结了婚，同你的爱国心结了婚，同你的功名前程结了婚，随你给它行什么洗礼、取什么名字都好。无论如何，你同你男人的事业结了婚。你没有工夫去理会那些琐事，比如给一位姑娘那一份又痴又美的追求——那可是每一位姑娘在心底里暗暗盼望的。说真的，你头一回结婚时，那便已经是一种负心了；我敢肯定，那位可怜的、亲爱的小耗子般的玛丽，对此一定一次又一次地深有体会。唉，乔治，事到如今你还不明白么？你的妻子永远要屈居第二，排在你真正的爱人之后。」

「在我心里，亲爱的诡辩家，你永远排第一。只是任何一位忠心的绅士，都不获准把毕生每一个钟头都耗费在叹息与做十四行诗上，耗费在这乏味的小小德特福德里宽慰一位姑娘的寂寞上。而且我确信，你也决不愿我这样做。」

「我自己都说不清想要什么，」她悻悻地告诉他。「可我知道，每当你谈起你男人的事业，我便觉得孤单、心冷，离你远远的。我还知道，你那一套关于责任呀、爱国呀、报效英格兰呀等等的高妙见解，我至多懂得一半，」姑娘宣称道，一面把她那双可爱的小手绝望地摊开。「先生，每当你谈起英格兰，我真是倾慕你。那让你更俊美了——是啊，不知怎的，居然更俊美了！可与此同时，我心里总惦着：英格兰不过是一座普普通通的海岛，住着一群普普通通的人，他们中的大多数，无论好歹，我都谈不上有什么特别的感情。」

===Original===
Pevensey looked down at her for a while with queer tenderness. Then he smiled. “No, I could not quite make you understand, my dear. But, ah, why fuddle that quaint little brain by trying to understand such matters as lie without your realm? For a woman’s kingdom is the home, my dear, and her throne is in the heart of her husband—”

“All this is but another way of saying your lordship would have us cups upon a shelf,” she pointed out—“in readiness for your leisure.”

He shrugged, said “Nonsense!” and began more lightly to talk of other matters. Thus and thus he would do in France, such and such trinkets he would fetch back—“as toys for the most whimsical, the loveliest, and the most obstinate child in all the world,” he phrased it. And they would be married, Pevensey declared, in September: nor (he gaily said) did he propose to have any further argument about it. Children should be seen—the proverb was dusty, but it particularly applied to pretty children.

===Chinese===
佩文西带着一种古怪的温柔，俯视了她片刻。随即他笑了。「不，我实在没法让你完全明白，亲爱的。可是，啊，何苦绞那颗玲珑小脑袋的汁，去弄懂那些超出你疆域的事情呢？因为女人的王国是家，亲爱的，她的宝座安在她丈夫的心坎里——」

「这一切不过是换个说法，说白了，大人您是想把我们变成搁架上的杯子，」她指出，「随时恭候您的闲暇。」

他耸耸肩，说了声「胡说！」便轻快地谈起别的事情来。到了法兰西他将如此这般，回来时要带这样那样的小玩意儿——用他的话说，「送给全世界最任性、最可爱、也最执拗的孩子的玩具」。他们将在九月成婚，佩文西宣称：而且（他喜滋滋地说）他不打算再就此事多费唇舌。孩子家只许看着、不许出声——这谚语虽已积了灰，用在漂亮孩子身上却格外合适。

===Original===
Cynthia let him talk. She was just a little afraid of his self-confidence, and of this tall nobleman’s habit of getting what he wanted, in the end: but she dispiritedly felt that Pevensey had failed her. Why, George Bulmer treated her as if she were a silly infant; and his want of her, even in that capacity, was a secondary matter: he was going into France, for all his petting talk, and was leaving her to shift as she best might, until he could spare the time to resume his lovemaking. …

===Chinese===
辛西娅任他说去。她对他那份自信，对这位高挑贵爵到头来总能如愿以偿的习惯，多少有点发怵：可她仍旧沮丧地觉得，佩文西辜负了她。唉，乔治·布尔默待她，就像待一个傻里傻气的婴孩；而且即便充作这个角色，他对她的需要也只算次要：任凭他说得再疼人，他终究要去法兰西，撇下她自谋生计，等他腾出工夫，再来续他的求爱。……

### II / II

What Comes of Scribbling / 舞文弄墨的下场

===Original===
Now when Pevensey had gone the room seemed darkened by the withdrawal of so much magnificence. Cynthia watched from the window as the tall earl rode away, with three handsomely clad retainers. Yes, George was very fine and admirable, no doubt of it: even so, there was relief in the reflection that for a month or two she was rid of him.

Turning, she faced a lean, dishevelled man, who stood by the Magdalen tapestry scratching his chin. He had unquiet bright eyes, this out-at-elbows poet whom a marquis’ daughter was pleased to patronize, and his red hair was unpardonably tousled. Nor were his manners beyond reproach, for now, without saying anything, he, too, went to the window. He dragged one foot a little as he walked.

“So my lord Pevensey departs! Look how he rides in triumph! like lame Tamburlaine, with Techelles and Usumcasane and Theridamas to attend him, and with the sunset turning the dust raised by their horses’ hoofs into a sort of golden haze about them. It is a beautiful world. And truly, Mistress Cyn,” the poet said, reflectively, “that Pevensey is a very splendid ephemera. If not a king himself, at least he goes magnificently to settle the affairs of kings. Were modesty not my failing, Mistress Cyn, I would acclaim you as strangely lucky, in being beloved by two fine fellows that have not their like in England.”

===Chinese===
佩文西走后，这么多富丽堂皇的气象一撤，房间似乎随之暗淡了。辛西娅从窗口望着那位高挑的伯爵策马而去，身后跟着三名衣着鲜丽的随从。是啊，乔治确实仪表非凡、令人叹服，这毫无疑问：即便如此，一想到有一两个月可以摆脱他，心头仍觉宽慰。

一转身，她面对着一个精瘦的、衣冠不整的男人，他正站在那幅抹大拉挂毯旁搔着下巴。这位衣肘磨穿的诗人——蒙一位侯爵千金降贵纡尊地加以照拂的诗人——有一双不安分的亮眼，一头乱得不可原谅的红发。他的举止也说不上无可指摘：这会儿他一言不发，也自顾自走向窗前。他走路时一条腿微微拖曳。

「于是佩文西大人去了！瞧他何等得意地纵马而行！活像跛足的帖木儿，有特切勒斯（Techelles）、乌苏姆卡萨内（Usumcasane）和瑟里达马斯（Theridamas）随侍左右，夕阳把他们马蹄扬起的尘土，化作一袭笼身的金色烟霭。这真是个美丽的世界。说实在的，辛小姐，」诗人若有所思地说，「那位佩文西是一只非常辉煌的蜉蝣。他自己纵然不是君王，至少也风风光光地去裁度君王们的事务了。若非谦逊是我的痼疾，辛小姐，我真要赞叹您运气好得出奇，同时得到两位英格兰绝无仅有的好男儿的爱慕。」

===Original===
“Truly, you are not always thus modest, Kit Marlowe—”

“But, Lord, how seriously Pevensey takes it all! and takes himself in particular! Why, there departs from us, in befitting state, a personage whose opinion as to every topic in the world is written legibly in the carriage of those fine shoulders, even when seen from behind and from so considerable a distance. And in not one syllable do any of these opinions differ from the opinions of his great-great-grandfathers. Oho, and hark to Deptford! now all the oafs in the Corn-market are cheering this bulwark of Protestant England, this rising young hero of a people with no nonsense about them. Yes, it is a very quaint and rather splendid ephemera.”

The daughter of a marquis could not quite approve of the way in which this shoemaker’s son, however talented, railed at his betters. “Pevensey will be the greatest man in these kingdoms some day. Indeed, Kit Marlowe, there are those who say he is that much already.”

===Chinese===
「说真的，你可不总是这么谦虚的，基特·马洛（Kit Marlowe）——」

「可是，天哪，佩文西把这一切看得多认真啊！尤其把他自己看得多认真！瞧，一位何等体面的大人物从我们眼前起驾了，他对世上每一个题目的高见，都清清楚楚写在那对漂亮肩膀的架势里，哪怕从背后、隔着这么远看去也一目了然。而这些高见，没有一音一节不与他曾祖们的曾祖们如出一辙。哦嗬，听听德特福德这动静！谷物市场里的蠢汉们这会儿正欢呼这座新教英格兰的干城呢，欢呼这位新起的少年英雄——他那民族可是最讲实际、绝不容空谈的。是啊，一只非常古怪、也相当辉煌的蜉蝣。」

侯爵的千金对于这个鞋匠之子——纵然才华横溢——出言讪辱尊长的做派，无论如何难以苟同。「佩文西有朝一日会成为这两大王国里最伟大的人物。真的，基特·马洛，已经有人说他现在就是了。」

===Original===
“Oh, very probably! Still, I am puzzled by human greatness. A century hence what will he matter, this Pevensey? His ascent and his declension will have been completed, and his foolish battles and treaties will have given place to other foolish battles and treaties, and oblivion will have swallowed this glistening bluebottle, plumes and fine lace and stately ruff and all. Why, he is but an adviser to the queen of half an island, whereas my Tamburlaine was lord of all the golden ancient East: and what does my Tamburlaine matter now, save that he gave Kit Marlowe the subject of a drama? Hah, softly though! for does even that very greatly matter? Who really cares today about what scratches were made upon wax by that old Euripides, the latchet of whose sandals I am not worthy to unloose? No, not quite worthy, as yet!”

And thereupon the shabby fellow sat down in the tall leather-covered chair which Pevensey had just vacated: and this Marlowe nodded his flaming head portentously. “Hoh, look you, I am displeased, Mistress Cyn, I cannot lend my approval to this over-greedy oblivion that gapes for all. No, it is not a satisfying arrangement, that I should teeter insecurely through the void on a gob of mud, and be expected by and by to relinquish even that crazy foothold. Even for Kit Marlowe death lies in wait! and it may be, not anything more after death, not even any lovely words to play with. Yes, and this Marlowe may amount to nothing, after all: and his one chance of amounting to that which he intends may be taken away from him at any moment!”

He touched the breast of a weather-beaten doublet. He gave her that queer twisted sort of smile which the girl could not but find attractive, somehow. He said: “Why, but this heart thumping here inside me may stop any moment like a broken clock. Here is Euripides writing better than I: and here in my body, under my hand, is the mechanism upon which depend all those masterpieces that are to blot the Athenian from the reckoning, and I have no control of it!”

===Chinese===
「哦，很有可能！不过，人类的伟大总叫我犯迷糊。百年之后，这佩文西又算得了什么？他的攀升与跌落俱已画上句号，他那些愚蠢的战役与条约，已让位于别的一些愚蠢的战役与条约，遗忘之口早已把这只油光锃亮的绿头苍蝇吞下了肚——羽毛、细花边、神气的高皱领，一股脑儿。嘿，他不过是半个海岛的女王的谋臣，而我的帖木儿，却是整个金色的古老东方之主：可我的帖木儿如今又算得了什么？无非是给基特·马洛提供了一部戏的题材罢了。哈，且慢！便是这件事，又当真要紧么？那位老欧里庇得斯（Euripides）——我连给他解凉鞋带子都不配——当年在蜡板上刻下的那些道道，今天又有谁当真放在心上？是啊，眼下还不配！」

说罢，这个衣衫寒酸的家伙一屁股坐进佩文西方才让出的那张高高的皮面座椅：这位马洛煞有介事地点着他那颗火红的头颅。「嗬，您瞧，我不痛快，辛小姐：这张朝着万物张开的、贪得无厌的遗忘大口，我无法赞同。不，这可不是一桩叫人满意的安排：我竟然得踩着一团泥丸，摇摇欲坠地穿越虚空，而过不了多久，连这点发疯的立足处也指望我拱手让出。连基特·马洛，死亡也在半路埋伏着呢！而且说不定，死了之后便什么都没有了，连一个可以摆弄的漂亮字眼都没有。是啊，这位马洛说不定终究成不了气候：而他成就心中所愿的那唯一的机会，随时可能被人夺走！」

他按了按那件饱经风霜的紧身上衣的胸口。他朝她绽出那种歪扭古怪的微笑——不知怎的，姑娘偏偏觉得它迷人。他说：「唉，我这胸腔里怦怦跳动的心，随时可能像一只坏钟似的停摆。欧里庇得斯写得比我好：而在我这皮囊里、在我手掌底下，安着那副机枢——我未来的全部杰作，那些要把雅典人从名册上勾销的杰作，全系于它一身——而我竟对它毫无驾驭之力！」

===Original===
“Indeed, I fear that you control few things,” she told him, “and that least of all do you control your taste for taverns and bad women. Oh, I hear tales of you!” And Cynthia raised a reproving forefinger.

“True tales, no doubt.” He shrugged. “Lacking the moon he vainly cried for, the child learns to content himself with a penny whistle.”

“Ah, but the moon is far away,” the girl said, smiling—“too far to hear the sound of human crying: and besides, the moon, as I remember it, was never a very amorous goddess—”

“Just so,” he answered: “also she was called Cynthia, and she, too, was beautiful.”

“Yet is it the heart that cries to me, my poet?” she asked him, softly, “or just the lips?”

===Chinese===
「说真的，恐怕你能驾驭的东西寥寥无几，」她对他说，「而最驾驭不住的，是你对酒馆和坏女人的嗜好。哦，我可是听说了你不少的事！」辛西娅竖起一根嗔责的食指。

「想必都是真事。」他耸耸肩。「孩子哭月亮而不可得，便学会了拿一支便士小哨聊以自慰。」

「啊，可月亮远在天边，」姑娘微笑着说，「远得听不见人间的哭声：再说，据我所知，月亮从来不曾是一位多情的女神——」

「正是如此，」他答道，「况且她的名字就叫辛西娅，而且她也是美的。」

「可在我耳边哭喊的，我的诗人，是心，还是单单那张嘴？」她轻声问他。

===Original===
“Oh, both of them, most beautiful and inaccessible of goddesses.” Then Marlowe leaned toward her, laughing and shaking that disreputable red head. “Still, you are very foolish, in your latest incarnation, to be wasting your rays upon carpet earls who will not outwear a century. Were modesty not my failing, I repeat, I could name somebody who will last longer. Yes, and—if but I lacked that plaguey virtue—I would advise you to go a-gypsying with that nameless somebody, so that two manikins might snatch their little share of the big things that are eternal, just as the butterfly fares intrepidly and joyously, with the sun for his torchboy, through a universe wherein thought cannot estimate the unimportance of a butterfly, and wherein not even the chaste moon is very important. Yes, certainly I would advise you to have done with this vanity of courts and masques, of satins and fans and fiddles, this dallying with tinsels and bright vapors; and very movingly I would exhort you to seek out Arcadia, travelling hand in hand with that still nameless somebody.” And of a sudden the restless man began to sing.

Sang Kit Marlowe:

“Come live with me and be my love,

And we will all the pleasures prove

That hills and valleys, dales and fields,

Woods or steepy mountain yields.

“And we will sit upon the rocks,

And see the shepherds feed their flocks

By shallow rivers, to whose falls

Melodious birds sing madrigals—”

===Chinese===
「哦，两者皆是，最美丽也最不可亲近的女神。」随即马洛朝她俯过身去，笑着，摇着那颗不成体统的红脑袋。「不过，您今生这副化身实在犯傻，竟把清辉浪费在活不过一个世纪的地毯伯爵身上。我再重复一遍：若非谦逊是我的痼疾，我本可以点出一个人的名字，那人将流传得更久。是啊，而且——但愿我能去掉那恼人的美德——我还要奉劝您同那位无名氏一起去流浪，好教两个小小的人形，也捞取一份永恒的大事物，正如蝴蝶携着太阳作执炬的仆童，无畏而欢畅地飞过这大千世界——在这世界里，纵使思想也无法估量一只蝴蝶何等微不足道，而连那贞洁的月亮，也说不上多么要紧。是啊，我一定还要奉劝您，趁早同这宫廷与假面剧的虚荣、这绸缎、羽扇与提琴的虚荣一刀两断，别再同金箔和亮闪闪的雾气调情；然后声情并茂地劝您寻访阿卡狄亚（Arcadia）去，与那位依然无名的人儿携手同游。」说着，这闲不住的人忽然唱了起来。

基特·马洛唱道：

「来吧，与我同住，做我的爱人，

我们将把一切欢愉一一亲证：

那山峦与幽谷、平芜与田野，

那林莽与陡峭峰峦的馈赠。

「我们要并肩坐在岩石上，

看牧人在浅浅的河边

放牧羊群，水声潺潺处，

鸣禽正唱着悠悠的情歌——」

===Original===
But the girl shook her small, wise head decisively. “That is all very fine, but, as it happens, there is no such place as this Arcadia, where people can frolic in perpetual sunlight the year round, and find their food and clothing miraculously provided. No, nor can you, I am afraid, give me what all maids really, in their heart of hearts, desire far more than any sugar-candy Arcadia. Oh, as I have so often told you, Kit, I think you love no woman. You love words. And your seraglio is tenanted by very beautiful words, I grant you, though there is no longer any Sestos builded of agate and crystal, either, Kit Marlowe. For, as you may perceive, sir, I have read all that lovely poem you left with me last Thursday—”

She saw how interested he was, saw how he almost smirked. “Aha, so you think it not quite bad, eh, the conclusion of my ‘Hero and Leander?’”
